Having thus realized the unborn and immovable God, Who is uncontaminated by material elements, one becomes free from all bondage.”**

**Комментарий:**

Here the author presents the philosophical view of Vyāsadeva. According to the Śridhara-kośa, the word ‘kintu‘ is used in the sense of will and deliberation, and here it means that the subject matter that will be now described is the one desired. The word ‘tasyai’ means ‘for bringing about the ultimate attainment of happiness and disappearance of distress’. The author here quotes the śruti to illustrate that knowledge about the Supreme Lord of all gives liberation. By knowing (jnātva) from the Vedas about God (devam), Lord Hari, Who is the Lord of all and is to be worshipped by all, one determined to obtain liberation has all the bonds (pāśa) of body and bodily egotism destroyed (apahāniḥ). When the distresses (kleśaiḥ) caused by such bonds vanish (kṣiṇaiḥ), the birth and death (janma-mṛtyu) of a particular person who is being repeatedly born for the completion of the unripe results of one’s past activities also vanish (prahāṇiḥ). The meaning is that one is not afflicted by birth and death, just as a baby cat is not afflicted by the touch of the teeth of the she-cat. By meditating (abhidhyānāt) on God more and more, upon breaking out (bhede) of the subtle body (deha), one who knows Him then attains His divine abode, which is the third (tṛtiyam) abode in relation to the abode of the moon-god and that of Lord Brahma. This is the rest of the sentence. What is this third abode like ? It has unlimited and eternal divine opulence’s (viśvaiśvaryam). The word ‘kevalam‘ means that it is not even slightly tainted by material nature. Then, one who knows God has all desires fulfilled (āpta-kāmaḥ). The author again quotes the śruti to illustrate that accompanied by knowledge about the individual soul, knowledge of the Supreme Lord of all gives liberation. Being endowed (yuktaḥ) with self-knowledge (ātma-tattvena), which is like a lamp (dipopamena), when a person who desires liberation contemplates (prapaśyet) the Supreme Absolute Truth (brahma-tattvam), having thus realized (jnātvā) God (devam), he becomes free (mucyate) from all bondage (sarva-paśaiḥ). This is the syntax here. How is God? He is devoid of the six kinds of transformations starting with birth (ajam). He is immovable (dhruvam), complete, distinct from all material elements (sarva-tattvaiḥ) starting with pradhāna, and not tainted by them (viśuddham).

### Text 12

**Перевод:**

**Someone may inquire, “What is the svarūpa (original nature) of the Supreme Person? What are His qualities? And what is the nature of such qualities, knowing which one attains liberation?” In response, we say that His svarūpa is knowledge and bliss, as stated in śruti passages such as: vijnānam ānandam brahma rātir dātuḥ parāyaṇam (Bṛhad-āraṇyaka Upaniṣad, 3.9.28), “The Supreme Lord is knowledge and bliss personified, a generous giver, and the ultimate goal of the sacrificer.” ānando brahmeti vyajānāt (Taittiriya Upaniṣad, 3.6), “Bhṛgu then realized that the Supreme Lord is bliss personified.” raso vai saḥ (Taittiriya Upaniṣad, 2.7), “The Supreme Lord is rasa personified.” This transcendental nature itself must be considered His body, and not that His body is something else besides His nature. It is so stated in the śruti, such as: yad-ātmako bhagavāṁs tad-ātmikā vyaktiḥ, kim-ātmako bhagavān jnānātmaka aiśvaryātmakaḥśaktyātmakaḥ, “The Supreme Lord‘s form consists of the same substance as His very Self. What does He consist of? The Supreme Lord consists of consciousness, opulence, and energies.” buddhi-mano-‘ṅga-pratyaṅgavattāṁ bhagavato lakṣayāmahe, buddhimān manovān aṅga-pratyaṅgavān, “We perceive the Supreme Lord as endowed with intelligence, mind, body, and limbs. Indeed, He has mind, intelligence, body, and limbs.” tam ekaṁ govindaṁ sac-cid-ananda-vigraham (Gopāla-tāpani Upaniṣad, 1.33), “I satisfy Lord Govinda, Whose form is eternity, knowledge, and bliss personified.” ardha-mātrātmako rāmo brāhmaṇandaika-vigrahaḥ (Ramottara-tāpani Upaniṣad, 2.2), “Lord Rāma, Whose unique form is spiritual bliss personified, is the half syllable in the praṇava.” This is also corroborated by the smṛti, such as: vedānte prathitaṁ tejas tava cedaṁ vicaryate (Hari-vaṁśa, Bhaviṣya-parva, 111.39), “O Lord, the spiritual power declared in the Upaniṣads is considered to be Your very body.” In this way, the claim that the Supreme Lord‘s body is a manifestation of the material mode of goodness and the claim that He is mere knowledge devoid of consciousness are both refuted. Therefore, since the Supreme Lord‘s body is itself the Supreme Absolute Truth, the belief that His body is material, based on statements from scriptures like the Viṣṇu Purāna, is created by māyā to bewilder the demons. This is also understood from verses like: rājan parasya tanu-bhṛj-jananāpyayehā māyā-viḍambanam avehi yathā naṭasya (Śrimad-Bhāgavatam, 11.31.11), “O King, you should know that the activities of the Supreme Lord, His appearance and disappearance like those of embodied beings, are an illusory display like that of a magician.”**

**Комментарий:**

Here someone inquires about the nature of the Supreme Lord. According to the Amara-kośa (3-3.248), the word ‘nanu‘ is employed in the sense of inquiry, certainty, request, respect, and as a vocative. The author then replies to the inquiry. The word ‘dātuḥ‘ means ‘of the sacrificer’, and ‘rātiḥ‘ means ‘one Who gives the results’. The passage starting with the words ‘yad-ātmakaḥ‘ is from the Paiṅgi-śruti. The very same śruti deliberates — The Supreme Lord‘s form consists of the same substance as His very Self. The Supreme Lord is His very form, thus there is also no internal difference (svagata-bheda) in Him. As corroborated by the scriptures, such as: neha nānāsti kihcana (Bṛhad-āraṇyaka Upaniṣad, 4.4.1, “Within the Supreme Lord there is no difference whatsoever.” svagata-bheda-vivarjitātmā (Mahā-bhārata-tātparya-nirṇaya, l.ll), "The Supreme Soul is devoid of any internal difference. ” The idea is that it is on the mere strength of viśeṣathat difference should be assumed in the Supreme Lord‘s transcendental activities. The same śruti raises and replies to the question regarding His nature by stating that the Supreme Lord consists of consciousness (jnānātmakaḥ), or in other words, He has consciousness for His sole substance. Is the Supreme mere consciousness devoid of attributes ? No, for the Supreme Lord has six opulences which are intrinsic to Himself (aiśvaryātmakaḥ). The word ‘aiśvarya‘ is employed here in the sense of six opulences. Wherefrom do these opulences emanate?

It is said, “The Supreme Lord is replete with potencies (śakty-ātmakaḥ)”. The energy referred to here is the one called parā śakti, His superior energy. Those opulences belong to His superior energy, for they are intrinsic to it, just as heat is to fire. Thus, the Supreme Lord‘s form is His very Self and consists of consciousness, opulences, and His superior energy. The word ‘iti‘ at the end of the sentence is used in the sense of reason. The meaning is, “Because the Supreme Lord‘s essential nature is such, we, the omniscient śruties, perceive Him as endowed with intelligence and so on by dint of His intrinsic inconceivable energy». Giving a clear explanation for this, the śruti declares that the Supreme Lord indeed has intelligence (buddhimān) and so on. The meaning of the rest is clear. In the Hari-vaṁśa (Bhaviṣya-parva, 111.39-41), Durvāsā speaks the following words to Lord Kṛṣṇa: vedante prathitaṁ tejas tava cedaṁ vibhāvyate, yena vijnāna-tṛptās tu yogino vita-kalmasāḥ, paśyanti hṛt-saroje hi tad evedam vapuḥ prabho, vede yat kirtyate tejo brahmeti pravibhajya vai, tad evedaṁ vijāne‘ham rūpam aiśvaram iśvara, “O Lord, the spiritual power declared in the Upaniṣads is considered to be Your very body, which the sinless yogis contemplate in the lotus of their hearts, and by which they become satisfied by spiritual knowledge. О Supreme Lord, I know that Your divine form is the spiritual power described in the Vedas, where it is classified as Brahman itself». Having thus demonstrated that without any doubt the Supreme Lord‘s body is eternal on account of its very spiritual nature, in order to remove any further doubt from those of poor intellect, the author says, “Therefore, etc.” In the Viṣṇu Purāṇa (5-37.54-5; 74; 38.1-3) it is stated: caṁkramyamāṇau tau rāmaṁ vṛkṣa-mūle kṛtāsanam, dadrśāte mukhāc cāsya niṣkrāmantaṁ mahoragam, niṣkramya sa mukhāt tasya mahā-nāgo bhujaṅgamaḥ, prayayāv arṇavaṁ siddhaiḥ stūyamāno mahoragaiḥ, “ While Kṛṣṇa and Dāruka were roaming about, they saw Balarāma sitting under a tree and a large serpent coming out from His mouth. Having come out from His mouth, that large serpent went towards the ocean, while being praised by the Siddhas and Nāgas». gate tasmin sa bhagavān saṁyojyātmānam ātmani, tatyāja mānuṣaṁ deham atitya tri-vidhaṁ gatim, “When the hunter departed to heaven, the Supreme Lord became absorbed in His own Self. Surpassing the three modes of nature, He then gave up His human body”. arjuno‘pi tadānviṣya kṛṣṇa-rāma-kalevare, saṁskāraṁ lambhayām āsa tathānyeṣām anukramāt, aṣṭau mahiṣyaḥ kathitā rukmiṇi-pramukhās tu yāḥ, upaguhya harer dehaṁ viviśus tā hutāsanam, revati caiva rāmasya dehaṁ aslisya sattama, viveśa jvalitaṁ vahniṁ tat-saṅgād atiśitalam, “After searching for the bodies of Kṛṣṇa and Balarāma, Arjuna performed the last rites for Both. In due order, he performed the funeral rites for others as well. Embracing Lord Hari‘s body, the eight aforementioned queens, headed by Rukmiṇi, entered the fire. The most virtuous Revati also embraced Lord Balarāma‘s body and entered the blazing fire, which felt very cool due to the contact with His body». Similarly, in the Mausala-parva of the Mahā-bhārata (8.31) it is said: tataḥ śarire rāmasya vāsudevasya cobhayoḥ, anviṣya dāhayām āsa puruṣair āpta-kāribhiḥ, “Then, after searching for the bodies of Vāsudeva and Balarāma, he burnt Both along with Their retinue». It must be understood from the context that Arjuna is the subject in this verse. In the Śrimad-Bhāgavatam (l. 15-34-35; 3-4.28) it is also stated so: yayāharad bhuvo bhāraṁ tāṁ tanuṁ vijahāv ajaḥ, kaṇṭakaṁ kaṇṭakeneva dvayaṁ cāpiśituḥ samam, yathā matsyādi-rūpāṇi dhatte jahyād yathā naṭaḥ, bhū-bhāraḥ kṣapito yena jahau tac ca kalevaram, “The unborn Lord gave up that same body with which He removed the burden from the earth, just as one removes a thorn by a thorn. In fact, both are the same for the Supreme Lord. In the same way as He assumes forms like a fish and so on, the Lord gives them up, just like an actor. He thus gave up that body by which the burden of the earth planet was destroyed». harir api tatyāja ākṛtiṁ try-adhiśaḥ”, Hari, the Lord of the three worlds, also gave up His body”. Just as in the aforementioned texts of the Viṣṇu Purāṇa and Mahā-bhārata, Lord Kṛṣṇa‘s final pastimes are also mentioned in the eleventh book of the Śrimad-Bhāgavatam. Similarly, in the story of the sixteen kings (Mahā-bhārata, 12.29.46), Nārada Muni also spoke about Lord Rama’s demise: rāmaṁ dāśarathiṁ caiva mṛtam śuśruma sṛnjaya, “O Sṛnjaya, we also hear that Lord Rāma, the son of Daśaratha, died. ” Those of poor intellect say that in these statements it is clear that the Lord‘s body is not eternal, since its destruction is mentioned. The author refutes this by saying, “that is a mere display of māyā to bewilder the demons”. In order to teach detachment from the world, the Supreme Lord makes such a show through His illusory potency, and those of a demoniac nature accept it as fact. While actually remaining in his own state, a magician is seen to make us believe that he is cutting off his own head, etc., so how much greater an illusionist is the Supreme Lord? Why is that belief illusory ? The reason for this is explained in the verse “rājan, etc. ” “Parikṣit is exclusively devoted to Lord Kṛṣṇa, so may he not become too dejected after hearing about His departure. ” Thinking thus, Śukadeva Gosvāmi explained that His departure was just illusory. “O King, you should know that the activities of the Supreme Lord described by me, such as birth and death like an embodied being, are an illusory show, like that of a magician. Hearing this, you should not become dejected. ” This is the meaning. Before this, the Lord had told Dāruka that the mausala-lilā was just illusory (Śrimad-Bhāgavatam, 11.30.49): tvaṁ tu mad-dharmam āsthāya jnāna-niṣṭha upekṣakaḥ, man-māyā-racitām etāṁ vijnāyopaśamaṁ vraja, “Becoming fixed in devotion to Me, you should remain steady in knowledge and indifferent. Understanding that this is an arrangement of My illusory potency, be peaceful. ” By the word ‘etc’. (ādi), other scriptures are also meant, such as the Skanda Purāṇa:  asaṅgaś cāvyayo‘bhedyo’nigrahyo‘śoṣya eva ca, viddho‘sṛgacito baddha iti viṣṇuḥ pradṛśyate, asurān mohayan devaḥ kriḍaty eṣa sureṣv api, mānuṣān madhyayā dṛṣṭya na mukteṣu kathancana, avijnāya paraṁ deham ānandātmānam avyayam, āropayanti janimat pancā-bhūtatmakaṁ jaḍam, “Lord Viṣṇu is certainly unobstructed, imperishable, and cannot be pierced, punished, or dried up. Yet He is seen pierced, covered with blood, and tied up. Deluding the demons, the Supreme Lord play is this way. On account of His human form, even amongst the demigods He is seen as medium sized, but never amongst the liberated souls. Not knowing that His body is transcendental, blissful, spiritual, and imperishable, the ignorant impose on Him a material body made of five elements and subject to birth. ”In the Nārayāṇiya (Mahā-bhārata, 12.326.42-43), it is said: etat tvayā na vijneyaṁ rūpavān iti dṛśyate, icchan muhūrtān naśyeyam iśo‘ham jagato guruḥ, māyā hy eṣā mayā sṛṣṭā yan māṁ paśyasi nārada, sarva-bhūta-guṇair yuktaṁ naivaṁ tvaṁ jnātum arhasi, “You should not consider that I have a material form because it so appears. I am the Supreme Lord, the Master of the universe, and desiring so, I can disappear in a moment. О Nārada, the illusory energy by which you see Me in this way was created by Me. Thus, you should not consider that I am subjected to the modes of material nature.” The word ‘nasyeyam‘ here means ‘I can become invisible’. In the Varāha Purāṇa (75-44) it is said: na tasya prākṛtā mūrtir meda-majjāsthi-sambhavā, na yogitvād iśvaratvāt satya-rūpo‘cyuto vibhuḥ, “The Lord‘s body is not material, made of fat, marrow, and bones. Nor was it achieved by yoga practice, for being the Supreme Lord, He is imperishable, all-pervading and His form is real. ” The meaning is that His form was not assumed according to His will because He is a great yogi, but it is eternal because He is the Supreme Lord. In order to clarify that the pastime of the Lord‘s departure is an illusory display, at the end of the narration, it is described in this way. In the Viṣṇu Purāṇa (5-38.10) it is stated: nātikrāntum alaṁ brahmaṁs tad adyāpi mahodadhiḥ, nityaṁ sannihitas tatra bhagavān keśavo yataḥ, “O brāhmana, even today the ocean is not able to cross over Lord Keśava’s residence in Dvārakā because He is eternally present there». In the Śrimad-Bhāgavatam (l 1.31.23-24) it is corroborated: dvārakāṁ hariṇā tyaktāṁ samudro‘plāvayat kṣaṇāt, varjayitvā mahā-rāja śrimad-bhagavad-ālayam, nityaṁ sannihitas tatra bhagavān madhusūdanaḥ, “O King, when Lord Hari abandoned it, the ocean flooded Dvārakā in a moment, except the residence of the transcendental Supreme Lord, for Lord Madhusūdana is eternally present there». Before the verse ‘rājan, etc.,‘ it was described how Lord Kṛṣṇa, Whose body had not been burnt, entered His own abode, but the demigods could not perceive His movements, which were like lightning. Then subsequently, by narrations such as His bringing the son of His guru from Yamaloka, it is also shown how much more capable He must be to protect His own body. In the Śrimad-Bhāgavatam (3.2.11), Uddhava clearly declared so: ādāyāntaradhād yas tu sva-bimbaṁ loka-locanam, “Taking away His body, which was visible to common people, Lord Kṛṣṇa disappeared. ”In the Ramayana (7.110.12-13), it is said that at the time of His departure, Lord Rama’s two arms became four: yām icchasi mahā-virya tāṁ tanuṁ praviśa svakām, pitāmaha-vacaḥ śrutvā viniścitya matiṁ tataḥ, viveśa vaiṣṇavam tejaḥ saśariraḥ sahānujaḥ, “‘O most powerful Lord, now enter whatever personal form You wish’. Hearing Lord Brahma’s words and making up His mind, Lord Rama entered the powerful form of Viṣṇu in that same body, being accompanied by His brothers. ” His demise mentioned in the story of the sixteen kings is merely meant to convey the transience of the material world in order to remove the lamentation for a dead son. The sentence describing Lord Rama’s death must either mean that He disappeared, or it is meant to instigate renunciation of the world, since His death is actually not possible.

### Text 13

**Перевод:**

**It is only by the śruti that one can understand that the Supreme Lord has a body of knowledge and bliss, and not by logic, for this subject is inconceivable and transcendental. In the Brahma-sūtra (3.2.14-17), its author, the revered Vyāsadeva, says this: arūpavad eva hi tat-pradhānatvāt, “The Supreme Lord is said to be bodiless because His very self is His body. ” prakāśavac cāvaiyarthyam, “ Similarly to the sun having a form, the principle that He has a body is not meaningless.” āha ca tan-mātram, “The scriptures say that His body is He Himself;” darśayati cātho api smaryate, “Both the śruti and the smṛti teach this.” In this Adhikaraṇa, the same thing is thus stated — the Supreme Lord and His body are one and the same.**

**Комментарий:**

If someone argues that a body made of consciousness and bliss contradicts logic, the author here says that this is true. The Lord‘s body cannot be understood by logical inference. Vyāsadeva also says this (tad idam), i.e., that His body is knowledge and bliss. The word ‘rūpa‘ means ‘body’. Because Brahman, the Supreme Lord, is not different from His body, He is said to be bodiless (arūpavad). Why ? This is because His body is His very Self (pradhānatvāt). This means that His body is both the possessor of attributes and the very attributes like omnipresence, omniscience, and so on. Therefore, Brahman and His form are non-different. Someone may argue, “By meditating on Brahman, Who is knowledge and bliss personified, prakṛti, which is the opposite of that because it consists of dull matter and suffering, would disappear like darkness in the presence of light. Thus, it would be meaningless for Brahman to have a body. "In response, it is said that since the sun is a uniform agglomerate of light, the personal form of the sun-god is not meaningless, for it serves the purpose of meditation on the sun. Similarly, the same is true in the case of meditation on Brahman, Who is knowledge and bliss. Being so, the author shows evidence from the Brahma-sūtra. According to the Amara-kośa (3-3.178), the word ‘mātra‘ is used in the sense of entirety and restriction. The śruti says that the Lord is His very body and is therefore known as the Supreme Soul. As stated in the Gopāla-tāpani Upaniṣad (1.9): sat-puṇḍarika-nayanaṁ meghābhaṁ vaidyutāmbaram, dvi-bhujaṁ mauna-mudrāḍhyaṁ vana-mālinam iśvaram, “One should meditate on Lord Govinda, the Supreme Lord, Who has beautiful lotus eyes and two hands. His complexion is like that of a cloud, and His dress is effulgent like lightning. He wears a garland of forest flowers and is in a posture of meditative silence. ” It is clear that His body, endowed with the attributes described here such as having beautiful lotus eyes, is the Supreme Lord Himself. Both the śruti and the smṛti teach this. The śruti says: sākṣāt prakṛti-paro‘yam ātmā gopālaḥ (Gopāla-tāpani Upaniṣad, 2.22), “This cowherd boy is Himself the Supreme Soul, beyond material nature. ” The smṛti also states that His body is the Supreme Soul: satya-jnānānantānanda-mātraikarasa-mūrtayaḥ (Śrimad-Bhāgavatam, 10.13.54), “The immutable bodies of the Supreme consist exclusively of eternity, knowledge, and unlimited bliss». Thus, it is proved that the Lord has a form. In the last two quoted aphorisms, there is a grammatical interchange of subject, object, and verb.

### Text 14

**Перевод:**

**One inquisitive about the Supreme Soul should not take to logic. As stated in the śruti, such as the Kaṭha Upaniṣad, (1.2.9): naiṣā tarkeṇa matir āpaneyā proktānyena sujnānāya preṣṭha, “Dear Naciketā, this knowledge cannot be attained by logic. When this is taught by another person, it leads to the realization of the Supreme.” In the smṛti, such as the Mahā-bhārata (6.6.11), it is said: acintyāḥ khalu ye bhāvā na tāṁs tarkeṇa yojayet, “Transcendental things are certainly inconceivable. One cannot approach them by logic.” Someone may argue, “The śruti says that the Supreme Soul should be thought upon. This is an admission to logic.” This is true. Logic that is conformable to the import of the Upaniṣads is not rejected. In the Kūrma Purāṇa it is stated: pūrvottarāvirodhena ko‘trartho ‘bhimato bhavet, ity ādyam ūhanaṁ tarkaḥ śuṣka-tarkaṁ tu varjayet “ ‘Without contradicting the previous and subsequent statements, what must be the intended meaning in this scriptural passage? ‘— Such deliberation is real logic. Mere dry logic should be given up.” In this way, it is proved that the Supreme Lord‘s nature is non-different from His body in the form of knowledge and bliss.**

**Комментарий:**

The quoted śruti starting with ‘naiṣa‘ is from the Kaṭha Upaniṣad. The vocative ‘O dear‘ (preṣṭha) is addressed to Naciketā. “This knowledge (matiḥ) that is appropriate for worshipping the Supreme cannot be attained (āpaneyā) by you by means of logic (tarkeṇa). When it is taught (proktā) by another person (anyena), a Vedic guru, then it leads to the realization (sujnānāya) of the Supreme». The verse starting with ‘acintyāh‘ is from the Mahā-bhārata. The word ‘asau‘ (that) in response to the raised objection refers to logic. The verse starting with ‘pūrvottarāviroddhena‘ is from the Kūrma Purāṇa.

### Text 15

**Перевод:**

**The Supreme Lord‘s qualities, such as knowledge, bliss, realizing all desires, realizing all thoughts, and so on are all eternal. They are also not different from His svarūpa. The śruti describes Him as such: eṣa ātmāpahata-pāpmā vijaro vimṛtyur viśoko vijighatso ‘pipāsaḥ satya-kāmaḥ satya-saṅkalpaḥ (Chāndogya Upaniṣad, 8.1.5), “The Supreme Soul is free from sins, free from old age, free from death, free from sorrow, free from hunger, free from thirst, fulfills all His desires, and brings all thoughts into reality.” yaḥ sarvajnaḥ sarvavid yasya jnāna-mayaṁ tapaḥ (Muṇḍaka Upaniṣad, 1.1.9), “The Supreme Lord is omniscient, all-knowing, and His austerity consists of knowledge.” ānandaṁ brahmaṇo vidvān (Taittiriya Upaniṣad, 2.4.1). “A wise person knows the blissfulness of the Supreme Lord.” It is also heard from the śruti that one who sees the Lord as different from His qualities is condemned: yathodakaṁ durge vṛṣṭam parvateṣu vidhāvati, evaṁ dharmān pṛthak paśyaṁs tān evānuvidhāvati (Kaṭha Upaniṣad, 2.4.14), “Just as rain water on the hills runs down into impassable places, similarly one who sees the Lord‘s attributes as different from Him falls down.” brāhmaṇas tad-guṇānāṁ ca bheda-darśy adhamaṁ tamaḥ, bhedābheda-pradarśi tu madhyamaṁ tu tamo vrajet,  “One who sees difference between the Supreme Lord and His qualities goes down to the lowest hell, and one who sees difference and non-difference goes to an intermediate hell». Therefore, the śruti merely denies difference between the Lord and His qualities in the statement ‘neha nānāsti kincana ‘ (Bṛhad-āraṇyaka Upaniṣad, 4.4.19), “Within the Supreme Lord there is no difference whatsoever.”**

**Комментарий:**

The author here gives evidence of the Lord‘s qualities by first quoting the Chāndogya Upaniṣad. The word ‘vijighatsaḥ‘ means ‘without hunger’. The author then gives evidence from the Kaṭha Upaniṣad that the Lord’s qualities are non-different from His own nature. Just as rain water (vṣṛṭam) on the hills (parvateṣu) runs down (vidhāvati) into an impassable lower place (durge), similarly (evam) one who sees (paśyan) the Supreme Lord‘s attributes (dharmān) as different (pṛthak) from Him falls down (anuvidhāvati) into the well-known hellish planets. Thus, the quoted śruti passages have denied the difference between the Lord and His qualities and so on.

### Text 16

**Перевод:**

**In the smṛti, the attributes are also expressed by words denoting the possessor of those attributes: jňāna-śakti-balaiśvarya-virya-tejāṁsy aśeṣataḥ, bhagavac-chabda-vācyāni vinā heyair guṇādibhiḥ (Viṣṇu Purāṇa, 6.5.79), “Absolute knowledge, power, strength, opulence, heroism, and splendor without any negative quality are expressed by the word ‘bhagavān’”. It is further stated that there is no difference between the attributes and their possessor: ananta-kalyāṇa-guṇātmako’sau sva-śakti-leśāvṛta-bhūta-sargaḥ (Viṣṇu Purāṇa, 6.5-84), “The Supreme Lord consists of unlimited auspicious qualities, and all created elements are spread over a mere fraction of His energy.” guṇātmanas te‘pi guṇān vimātuṁ hitāvatirṇasya ka iśire’sya (Śrimad-Bhāgavatam, 10.14.7), “Who would be able to measure the transcendental qualities of Yours, Whose very Self consists of those qualities, and Who have descended for the welfare of this universe?” Thus, although there is non-difference, designations expressing difference should be observed, as in the case of the water and the waves. This is what is stated in the following Adhikaraṇa of the Brahma-sūtra (3.2.28) by its author, the revered Vyāsadeva: ubhaya-vyapadeśāt tv ahikuṇḍalavat, “The Supreme Lord is both the attributes and their possessor, like a serpent and its coils, for the scriptures declare so.”**

**Комментарий:**

By quoting the verse starting with ‘jnāna-śakti’, the author now states that there is no difference in the interchange between the qualities and their possessor. This will be explained in the sixth chapter. The verse starting with ‘guṇātmanaḥ‘ is from the tenth book of the Śrimad-Bhāgavatam and was spoken by Lord Brahma. Vyāsadeva also says this (tad idam), i.e., that there is no duality between the qualities and their possessor. Since the śruti makes both kinds of the statements, it must be considered that the Supreme is both knowledge and bliss personified, and is knowledgeable and blissful: vijnānam ānandaṁ brahma (Bṛhad-āraṇyaka Upaniṣad 3-9.28), “The Supreme Lord is knowledge and bliss personified». yah sarvajnah sarvavit (Muṇḍaka Upaniṣad, 1.1.9), “The Supreme Lord is omniscient, all-knowing.” ānandaṁ brahmaṇo vidvān (Taittiriya Upaniṣad, 2.4.1), “A wise person knows the blissfulness of the Supreme Lord. ” This is akin to coils being an attribute of the snake, which may be seen simultaneously as made of coils.

### Text 17

**Перевод:**

**Someone may argue, “It is not correct to say that the Supreme Lord‘s svarūpa is non-different from His body of eternity, knowledge, and bliss, and that His qualities such as knowledge, bliss, and so on, are non-different from Him, and thus the Supreme is non-different from His qualities and nature. The reason is that since there are different semantic causes in the words ‘eternity, ‘‘knowledge, ‘and ‘bliss, ‘whatever is expressed by these words must also be unavoidably different.”  This is a foolish idea, for logic has no scope in transcendental, inconceivable subject matters. The Supreme Absolute Truth is certainly not perceptible by logic. The śruti and the smṛti corroborate this by statements such as: naiṣa tarkeṇa matir āpaneyā (Kaṭha Upaniṣad, 1.2.9), “This knowledge cannot be attained by logic.” Rather, the Supreme can be attained only by the śruti, as stated by Vyāsadeva, the revered compiler of the Brahma-sūtra: śrutes tu śabda-mūlatvāt (2.1.27), “There is no defect in the Supreme Lord, for there is evidence of this in the scriptures, since they are the only basis of knowledge about Him.”**

**Комментарий:**

Here the grammarians and logicians raise an objection. The word ‘nirbhinnam‘ means ‘non-different’. According to the Amara-kośa (3.3.253), the prefix ‘nir‘ is used in the sense of certainty and negation. Just as on account of the difference in the semantic causes in words such as ‘cow, ‘‘pot, ‘and so on, there is difference in what is expressed by these words, so it must be the case with the Supreme. According to the Amara-kośa (3.4.12), the words ‘cet‘ and ‘yadi‘ mean ‘if‘ and are used when another proposition is put forth. The author refutes this idea calling it foolish. The word ‘parāmaṁ‘ means ’supremely opulent’. The word ‘tattvam‘ means ‘the Supreme Lord’. The author then quotes Vyāsadeva’s opinion on this matter. The word ‘na‘ (no) is supplied from a previous sūtra. Although the Supreme Lord appears as the qualities and their possessor, the body and its possessor, He is also devoid of internal difference. Why? This is because the śruti says so: neha nānāsti kincana (Bṛhad-āraṇyaka Upaniṣad, 4.4.19), “Within the Supreme Lord there is no difference whatsoever.” Someone may object, “How can the śruti say something contradictory ? ” The answer is that in inconceivable matters, the words of the scriptures are the only evidence (śabda-mūlatvāt). This is also seen in the case of scriptural prescriptions of mantras and medicines.

### Text 18

**Перевод:**

**It is with the intent to express that there is no internal difference in the Supreme Lord that the śruti says: neha nānāsti kincana (Bṛhadāraṇyaka Upaniṣad, 4.4.19). “Within the Supreme Lord there is no difference whatsoever.” In the smṛti it is also said: nirdoṣa-pūrṇa-guṇa-vigraha ātma-tantro niścetanātmaka-śarira-guṇaiś ca hinaḥ, ānanda-mātra-kara-pāda-mukhodarādiḥ sarvatra ca svagata-bheda-vivarjitātmā, “The Supreme Soul is independent. His body is composed of faultless and fully transcendental qualities, devoid of the qualities of a body made of insentient matter. His self is totally free of internal differences, and His hands, feet, face, belly, and so on consist exclusively of blissfulness.” Nevertheless, on account of the Supreme Lord‘s inconceivable and distinguished potencies, which are compared to the vaidHrya gem, there is no contradiction when even the learned use those words to describe Him. It is also not true that the Lord is different and non-different from His body and attributes, for this contradicts the scriptural statements that deny difference. Therefore, one should be satisfied with the concept that the Supreme has inconceivable potencies.**

**Комментарий:**

The author now clarifies the previously mentioned absence of internal difference in the Supreme Lord. In the quoted śruti, the word ‘iha‘ means ‘in the Supreme Soul’, and the word ‘kincana‘ refers to His body and so on, which are non-different (nānā)from Him but are rather intrinsic to His own nature. According to the Amara-kośa (3.3.247) and the Śridhara-kośa, the word ‘nānā‘ is used in the sense of ‘many‘ and ‘both’. If the absence of attributes were meant, the śruti would have said that there is nothing in the Supreme Soul. The verse starting with ‘nirdoṣa’ is seen in the Nārada-pancarātra. One who is devoid of defects such as ignorance, and whose body is full of qualities such as omniscience, He is the Supreme Soul. Is His body made of pure material goodness as proposed by the Māyāvādis? In response, the smṛti says that His body is devoid (hinaḥ) of the qualities of a body (śarira-guṇaiḥ) made of insentient matter (niścetanātmaka). This means that His body consists of consciousness and is manifest as having the quality of consciousness on account of viśeṣa. Is His body made only of consciousness for its single element as proposed by the Sāṁkhya philosophers? The smṛti says that His body consists exclusively of blissfulness (ānanda-mātra). The meaning is that His body consists of consciousness and bliss. Is His soul different from His body as proposed by some tridaṇḍi sannyāsis?  The smṛti says that although the concept of body and the possessor of the body, and the concept of qualities and the possessor of the qualities exist in the Supreme Lord, His self is also totally (sarvatra) free of internal differences (svagata-bheda-vivarjitātmā). ‘A mango tree is not a Neem tree’ — this is an example of difference amongst things of the same class. ‘A mango tree is not a stone’ — this is an example of difference amongst things of a different class. ‘A mango-bud is not a mango tree ‘— this is an example of internal difference. Just as the different qualities of the vaidūrya gem, such as its yellow color, blue color, and so on, are not different from it but are still treated as different from each other, similarly blissfulness and knowledge, which are the actual form of the Supreme Lord, as well as qualities such as omniscience, are non-different from Him but are still treated as different from each other on account of viśeṣa. Otherwise, there would be fault by contradicting the śruti that denies internal difference: evaṁ dharmān pṛthak paśyaṁs tān evānuvidhāvati (Kaṭha Upaniṣad, 2.4.14), “One who sees the Lord‘s attributes as different from Him falls down. ”

### Text 19

**Перевод:**

**The concept of viśeṣa must be certainly accepted. Viśeṣa is something akin to difference, which conveys the concept of difference where it actually does not exist, expresses the concept of the qualities and their possessor, and conveys the meaning of words such as truth, knowledge, and bliss without synonymy. Otherwise, the use of the following expressions would not be possible without absurdity — ‘there is existence, ‘‘the difference is different, ‘‘time always exists, ‘and ‘the place is everywhere’. Even the opponents must unavoidably accept the concept of viśeṣa, otherwise there would be a flawed synonymy of words such as knowledge, bliss, and eternity, which merely express the Supreme Lord‘s nature in the śruti, such as: vijnānam ānandaṁ brahma (Bṛhad-āraṇyaka, 3-9-28), ‘The Supreme Lord is knowledge and bliss personified’. satyaṁ jnānam anantaṁ brahma (Taittiriya Upaniṣad, 2.1), ‘The Supreme Lord is truth, knowledge, and eternity personified’. Moreover, since consciousness is the basis upon which delusion happens, although consciousness is always perceived, its non-different attributes such as bliss and knowledge are not manifest at present, and this would not be possible without viśeṣa. Inherent nature is actually viśeṣa.**

**Комментарий:**

Having demonstrated, on the strength of viśeṣa, the existence of qualities and their possessor even in an object devoid of difference, the author continues to speak further so that everyone may also accept viśeṣa, even unwillingly. Although the Supreme Lord is devoid of difference, qualities like truth, knowledge, and bliss are manifest in Him on account of viśeṣa, and thus the non-synonymy of words such as truth, knowledge, and bliss is proved, as they express different meanings. Synonymy happens when what is being expressed and the words expressing it are one in meaning. The monists speak of only one object devoid of qualities by using words such as truth, knowledge, and bliss while excluding anything different from that However, in this philosophical view, it is hard to get away with the synonymy of those words. In the sixth chapter, it will be clarified how the object being expressed and the words expressing it are one. The word ‘itarathā‘ means to say, ‘otherwise, in case one does not accept viśeṣa, which is the cause that defines the relation as qualities and their possessor, as seen in an object that is devoid of difference. \*In the sentence ‘there is existence, \* it is only due to viśeṣa that existence is perceived as a substratum of existence. When it is said that ‘an earthen pot is not a cloth’, the difference from the earthen pot is perceived in the cloth. Although such a difference is nothing but the cloth, it is only due to viśeṣa that there is a perception of its difference from the cloth in the statement \*a cloth possesses a difference from an earthen pot’. Hence, in the sentence ‘time always exists/ time as a substratum of time is understood, and in the sentence ‘the place is everywhere’, place as a substratum of place is also understood. The opponents (pareṣām) meant here are the monists. In the next sentence, the word ‘itarathā’ means to say, ‘otherwise, apprehending flaw in seeing difference in the Supreme, if one does not accept viśeṣa, which reveals different qualities, when the Supreme Lord, Whose form is knowledge, bliss, and so on, is described in the śruti by words such as knowledge and bliss without semantic causes but just on account of the inconceivable power of words/ Another fault is stated: Just as in the illusory perception of silver in a shell there is factual perception of a shell, which is the basis upon which such illusion happens, similarly in the illusory perception of the material world, there is factual perception of consciousness. At present, at the stage of worldly life, bliss and knowledge are not manifest like consciousness, although they are non-different from it. The reason for this is only viśeṣa situated on consciousness. If viśeṣa is not accepted, then bliss would also be manifest just as consciousness is. Someone may object, “Although there is nothing different than existence within existence and so on, it is by the very inherent nature of the subject that the usage of sentences like ‘there is existence, ‘and the like, is possible». In response, the author says that this very inherent nature is what we refer to by the word ‘viśeṣa.3 Therefore, enough disagreement on mere names!

### Text 20

**Перевод:**

**Nor can it be said that, “By words such as knowledge and bliss, which specifically express attributes like knowledge and bliss, the Primary meaning expresses the qualified Brahman, while the secondary meaning expresses the pure and undivided Brahman,” for a secondary meaning cannot be applied to something that is not expressed by words. Even in that case, neither difference nor difference and non-difference of the oneness in the meaning of the sentence ‘you are that ‘(tat tvam asi), which is derived from the purified meaning of its words, would be agreeable to you (the opponent). If it were so, there would be flaws such as falsity of the oneness. If there is no viśeṣa in Brahman, then how is oneness not manifest, although the self-luminous consciousness is manifest? Even though the manifestation of consciousness does not obstruct the illusion of difference, the manifestation of oneness, which is non-different from consciousness, obstructs such illusion. Therefore, viśeṣa must be certainly admitted.**

**Комментарий:**

According to the Navya-mata, because something that cannot be expressed by words cannot possibly have a secondary meaning in sentences like ‘you are that ‘(tat tvam asi), the consciousness devoid of attributes should be understood through the inconceivable power of words, without any secondary meaning. In the previous paragraph, the author has refuted this view by pointing out the flaw of synonymy and has thus made them accept the concept of viśeṣa. Now, refuting the grammarians who propound a secondary meaning (lakṣaṇā-vāda), in a different manner, the author explains the concept of viśeṣa. The meaning here is that the opponent cannot say that, “There is indeed no synonymy between words such as ‘knowledge ‘and ‘bliss, ‘for they express attributes such as knowledge and bliss, which are different from each other. Yet, if having rejected all attributes, the secondary meaning expresses the pure spiritual nature of Brahman, then what is wrong in our philosophy?” The reason for this is stated, “If on the basis of śruti passages like ‘yato vāco nivartante, ‘ in your view Brahman cannot be expressed by words, then how can it be expressed by a secondary meaning? Just as in sentences like ‘This is that same Devadatta‘ it is seen that the secondary meaning, Devadatta’s mere physical body, is certainly expressed by words. In order to refute another objection, the author says, “Even in that case, ” which means, “even if there would be no objection to a secondary meaning in connection with Brahman, which is not expressed by words». The word ‘purified’ (śodhitāt) means ‘devoid of qualities like omniscience, ignorance, and so on.’ “If it would be so ” means “if either difference or difference and non-difference of the oneness in the meaning of the sentence, which is derived from the purified meaning of its words, would be agreeable to you, then there is a flaw in your philosophical conclusion, according to which everything different from Brahman is false because it is an imposition on it». The word ‘tatra’ means ‘in the unique, pure Brahman/ Although Consciousness is manifest, the illusion of difference does not cease to exist, therefore its manifestation does not obstruct illusion. When oneness becomes manifest, the illusion of difference ceases to exist, therefore its manifestation obstructs illusion. Since you (the opponent) speak of difference as an effect, although the ultimate truth is non-different, you have swallowed the concept of viśeṣa. By saying “Therefore, etc.,’’ the author means that viśeṣa is not a mere conjecture, as corroborated by the words of Bhiṣma in the Bhiṣma-stava-raja (Mahā-bhārata, 12.47.50): avyakta-buddhy-ahahkāra-mano-bhūtendriyāni ca, tan-mātrāṇi viśeṣaś ca tasmai tattvātmane namaḥ, “Pradhāna, the intelligence, the false ego, the mind, the five gross elements, the senses, the sense objects, and their peculiar attributes (viśeṣa) — I offer my humble obeisances unto the Supreme Soul of all these elements. ”

### Text 21

**Перевод:**

**Viśeṣa is non-different from its object and is self-descriptive, and thus there is no regressus ad infinitum. Its being so is proved by evidences that reveal an attribute as also possessing attributes.**

**Комментарий:**

Someone may argue, “It was said that it is on account of viśeṣa that qualities such as being the knower and being blissful are perceived in the Supreme Lord‘s original nature, which consists of knowledge and bliss. Then, on account of what is viśeṣa itself perceived in the Supreme Lord? If it is on account of another viśeṣa, then there will be regressus ad infinitum». If thus challenged by the monists, the author gives the response here. “Its being so" means, “viśeṣa, being self-descriptive and non-different from its object.” By which evidence is this understood? The śruti says: evaṁ dharmān prthak paśyaṁs tan evanuvidhavati (Kaṭha Upaniṣad, 2.4.14), ‘One who sees the Lord‘s attributes as different from Him falls down.” Viśeṣa’s non-difference from its object is proved either by the evidence of this śruti or by postulation (arthapatti), through which viśeṣa — which reveals the nature of both the qualities and their possessor — is understood as the possessor of attributes itself (dharmi-grāhaka-pramāṇa), even though the object itself is devoid of difference. This is because otherwise, there would be the flaw of regressus ad infinitum. Similarly, its self-descriptiveness, or its revealing the nature of both the qualities and their possessor on its own, as well as its inconceivability, are understood by the same evidence, for without inconceivability there cannot be a manifestation of both the qualities and their possessor in an object devoid of difference. Justas according to the Nyāya philosophy, the universe in the form of earth, oceans, and so on was made by God. Being impossible for the individual living entities to create it, and because the universe is an effect, by inference it is thus proved that God is its creator, and in this way it is also proved that since He used knowledge, will, and effort in order to carry out the creation, God possesses attributes, just as it is seen in this world that those who perform work also act through knowledge, will, and effort. By this inference, which reveals the Lord as the possessor of attributes, His attributes like knowledge and bliss are similarly understood. As for the universe being an effect, this is something accepted by all, since it consists of parts.

### Text 22

**Перевод:**

**Someone may argue, “The conventional designation of Brahman as God, the individual soul, and material nature must exist only on account of viśeṣa” It is not so. Viśeṣa, which is alike to difference, is accepted only where the absence of difference and the effect of difference are proved to exist, and not amongst God, the individual soul, and material nature, where difference itself is proved. Upon acquiring the Soma plant, the Pūtika plant is certainly not to be considered a substitute. Therefore, it is proved that on the strength of viśeṣa, conventional difference is possible even in an object devoid of difference.**

**Комментарий:**

Delighted that the concept of viśeṣa is acceptable to the author, some unprecedented Vaiṣṇavas, who experience dullness on account of their faith in the non-difference within Brahman, propose, “The conventional designation, etc.” The author refutes them herein. The mutual difference between material nature, the individual soul, and God as respectively the enjoyable, the enjoyer, and the controller is proved in the Śvetāśvatara Upaniṣad (1.9; 1.12): jnājnau dvāv ajav iśāniśāv ajā hy ekā bhoktṛ-bhogārtha-yuktā, “Both the Supreme Lord and the jiva are never born, but the Lord is omniscient, while the jiva is ignorant. He is the controller, but the jiva is not a controller. A single unborn material nature is engaged in providing enjoyable objects for the enjoyer, the jiva». bhoktā bhogyaṁ preritāraṁ ca matvā sarvaṁ proktaṁ tri-vidhaṁ brahmam etat, "Considering the enjoyer, the enjoyable, and the ruler, one should understand that all these are said to be the threefold Brahman. ”

### Text 23

**Перевод:**

**In this respect, some tridaṇḍi sannyāsis speak as follows: “His body and His qualities are different from His svarūpa. Otherwise, the self-evidence of the following śruti and smṛti would be rejected: yad-ātmako bhagavāṁs tad-ātmikā vyaktiḥ, kim-ātmako bhagavān jnānātmaka aiśvaryātmakaḥ śaktyātmakaḥ, ‘The Supreme Lord‘s form consists of the same substance as His very Self. What does He consist of? The Supreme Lord consists of consciousness, opulences, and energies’. mama dehe guḍākeśa yac cānyad draṣṭum icchasi (Bhagavad-gita, 11.7), ‘O Arjuna, now behold in My body whatever you wish to see’. harer guṇākṣipta-matiḥ (Śrimad-Bhāgavatam, 1.7.11), ‘Śukadeva’s mind was enthralled by Lord Hari’s qualities’. Nor can it be said that, ‘A body constituted of consciousness and so on would be purposeless, ‘for since there is scriptural evidence of this difference, the purpose of difference is not a matter of consideration.**

**Комментарий:**

“In this respect, ” which means, “upon describing the nature of the Supreme Lord, ” some tridaṇḍi sannyāsis speak in this way. “Otherwise, ” that means, “if the Lord‘s body and qualities are not taken as being different from His svarūpa. ” "The śruf says that the Supreme Lord consists of consciousness and His body consists of consciousness, thus there is similarity in consciousness, but there is no non-difference. The quoted verse from the Bhagavad-gitā corroborates this. The analogy given is that a small water-pot is similarly made of the same material as a large pitcher — clay. The śruti itself submits a question about the Lord‘s nature and responds to it — He consists of consciousness and so on. Being a substantive, the possessor of attributes here consists of consciousness. Since consciousness is proved as an attribute inseparable from His opulences and energies, the Supreme Lord consists of all three of them. Otherwise, the possessive case would be contradicted. ” The word ‘iti‘ is used in the sense of reason. The meaning of this śruti is, “Because the Lord is eternally endowed with such opulences and energies, therefore we perceive His attributes such as intelligence and so on — which are eternally accomplished by those — as inseparable from Him. ” The tridaṇḍis mean to say that, “It cannot be said that if the Lord‘s self in the form of consciousness, opulences, and energies accomplishes results such as His manifestation, then it is useless to describe His body and so on as real. On the other hand, it is appropriate to say that His body consists of pure material goodness. The scriptural evidence here applies in consideration of the nature of the object, and not of the result thereof.”

### Text 24

**Перевод:**

**“The connection of the Supreme Lord‘s body with His svarūpa, the substantive, is eternal, for His attributes are inseparable. The scriptural statements that express non-difference from His svarūpa refer to that eternal connection.**

**Комментарий:**

“If someone argues that the Lord‘s body cannot ever be separated from His svarūpa as if it were different from it, just as we are different from our bodies, then here is our reply, ‘The connection, etc’. If someone argues that if His body is different from His self, how there can be coherence in the scriptural statements that express non-difference in Him ? For instance: neha nānāsti kincana (Bṛhad-āraṇyaka Upaniṣad, 4.4.19), ‘Within the Supreme Lord there is no difference whatsoever’. ananta-kalyāna-guṇātmako’sau (Viṣṇu Purāṇa, 6.5.84), ‘The Supreme Lord consists of unlimited auspicious qualities’. In response, we say, ‘The scriptural statements, etc. ‘”

### Text 25

**Перевод:**

**“Nor can it be said that, ‘Difference would not be possible if we accept that the substantive and the attributes have a form of consciousness, opulences, and energies, as well as an eternal connection, ‘for there is indeed difference between substantive and attributes. One is the possessor of consciousness and so on; the other is consciousness itself and so forth. Otherwise, there would be non-difference between God and the jiva.**

**Комментарий:**

Here the tridaṇḍis declare that the attributes make the difference between the Supreme Lord‘s svarūpa and His body. “It cannot be said that just as there is non-difference between Lord Nārāyaṇa and His quadruple expansions starting with Vāsudeva on account of Their form as consciousness, opulences, and energies, as well as Their eternal connection, similarly for the reason stated here there must not be non-difference between Lord Nārāyaṇa, His body, and His qualities». Their reasoning is thus stated, “One thing is the substantive — the Supreme Lord‘s svarūpa; another thing is the attribute — His body and qualities. The former is the one who is conscious; the latter is the consciousness. Between the Supreme Lord and His quadruple expansions there is no such a thing as substantive and attribute, for the quadruple expansions are manifested by the Supreme Lord Himself. Thus, there is no flaw whatsoever». “Otherwise, ” i.e., “If we do not accept difference between the substantive and its attributes because both have a form of consciousness, opulences, and energies, as well as an eternal connection, then the śruti statements that express the difference between God and the jlva would be contradicted, such as: dvā suparṇā sayujā sakhāyā samānaṁ vṛkṣaṁ pariṣasvajāte, tayor anyaḥ pippalaṁ svādy atty anaśnann anyo‘bhicākaśiti (Muṇḍaka Upaniṣad, 3.1.1), ‘Two birds are sitting together on the same tree as friends. One of them is eating the sweet fruits of sense enjoyment, while the other one just observes without eating.

### Text 26

**Перевод:**

**“The śruti condemns one who advocates separateness in the Supreme: yathodakaṁ durge vṣṛṭaṁ parvateṣu vidhāvati, evaṁ dharmān prthak paśyaṁs tān evānuvidhāvati (Kaṭha Upaniṣad, 2.4-l4), ‘Just as rain water on the hills runs down into impassable places, similarly one who sees the Lord‘s attributes as different from Him falls down’. Therefore, there is indeed difference between the Supreme Lord‘s svarūpa and His body and qualities.”**

**Комментарий:**

“Someone may argue, ‘Then how to reconcile the śruti which instructs those who advocate difference between the Lord and His qualities that there is no difference between them ? ‘In response, we say that this śruti does not state that the qualities are non-different from the Supreme Lord, but rather it condemns those who say that the qualities are separate and material. According to the Sāṁkhya philosophers, qualities like being the knower and so on belong to prakṛti, and not to puruṣa, yet on account of the contact with prakṛti, they appear on puruṣa, just as the redness of a China rose flower appears on a crystal. ”

### Text 27

**Перевод:**

**This is not very reasonable, for actually in this way the natural meaning of the aforementioned43 śruti and smṛti would be rejected. Our position is stated in the Brahma-sūtra (1.1.7): śrutes tu śabda-mūlatvāt, “The evidence of this is the scriptures, since they are the only basis of knowledge about the Supreme Lord.”**

**Комментарий:**

Here the author refutes the views presented in paragraphs 23-26. Their views are not correct for the following reason: The direct meaning is understood from the śruti statement, “The Supreme Lord‘s form consists of the same substance as His very Self, ” just as the quadruple expansions starting with Vāsudeva are understood to be directly the Supreme Lord Nārāyaṇa. In the statement, “The Supreme Lord consists of unlimited auspicious qualities” (Viṣṇu Purāṇa, 6.5.84), the meaning is also directly understood — the Supreme Soul is non-different from His qualities. This meaning would be rejected if a forced imagination should be applied to this statement. Moreover, when the śruti declares that, “We perceive the Supreme Lord as endowed with intelligence, mind, body, and limbs. Indeed, He has mind, intelligence, body, and limbs, ‘‘here, intelligence and mind are related to the Lord‘s self, while body and limbs are related to His form. Because it is heard from the scriptures that all these are related to the Supreme Lord‘s self, the oneness between His body and self must be correct. The author means to say that, “The Buddhists state that consciousness is momentary, and the Māyāvādis say that consciousness is formless. On the strength of the śruti, you must say that consciousness is eternal and has form. ”

### Text 28

**Перевод:**

**“There is no meaning in sentences that express saguṇa-brahma if they are taken as expressing the Supreme’s qualities and body, for they are actually just a common description of qualities, and therefore the ultimate spiritual reality is mere consciousness devoid of attributes, and not saguṇa-brahma ,” — This foolish statement of the monists will be refuted afterwards.**

**Комментарий:**

Showing disdain for the doubts raised by the monists, the author says, “There is no meaning, etc. ” The word ‘guṇa‘ here means ‘body ‘and ‘attributes’. There are scriptural sentences that express Brahman as qualified by both’. Afterwards ‘means ‘in the fourth chapter’.

### Text 29

**Перевод:**

**Moreover, although there is no factual difference between the Supreme Lord‘s svarūpa and His body and qualities, there is conventional difference on account of viśeṣa. We accept that difference in meaning on account of different semantic causes pertains to worldly matters. The same cannot be accepted regarding the Supreme Absolute Truth, which is transcendental and apprehended only through the śruti.**

**Комментарий:**

Here the author concludes the current topic’. Worldly matters ‘are those discussed in texts on grammar, etc., such as in descriptions like, ‘So-and-so is a cook. He is dark-complexioned and is a brāhmaṇa’. The idea is that the etymology in Vedānta, which is like an ocean of nectar, has nothing to benefit from the etymology in grammar and other disciplines, which are like well water’. The same ‘means, ‘different meanings on account of different semantic causes’. It must be understood that different semantic causes exist in the Supreme on the strength of viśeṣa.

### Text 30

**Перевод:**

**In this way, it is proved that it is only by knowledge about such a Supreme Lord that the living entity can succeed in acquiring both the ultimate happiness and removal of distress, and not by any other means. This is corroborated by the scriptures in the following way: tam eva viditvā atimṛtyum eti nānyaḥ panthā vidyate anāya (Puruṣa-sukta, 18), “Only by knowing the Supreme Lord can one transcend death. There is no other way to cross over it.” vidyaiva tu tan-nirdhāraṇāt (Brahma-sūtra, 3.3.48), “It is only by knowledge of God that one can attain liberation, for this is ascertained in the scriptures.” plava hy ete adṛḍhā yajna-rūpāḥ (Muṇḍaka Upaniṣad, 1.2.7), “Ritualistic sacrifices are like unstable boats for crossing over birth and death.” nasty akṛtaḥ kṛtena (Muṇḍaka Upaniṣad, 1.2.12), “The Supreme Lord‘s abode cannot be attained by Vedic rituals.” na karmaṇā na prajayā dhanena tyāgenaike amṛtatvam ānaśuḥ (Taittiriya Āraṇyaka, 10.12), “It is not by ritualistic activities, or by producing good progeny, or by acquiring and giving wealth that one may attain liberation. This is possible only by knowledge of God accompanied by renunciation.” yathā-dṛḍhaiḥ karma-mayaiḥ kratubhir nāma-nau-nibhaiḥ, vidyām ānvikṣikiṁ hitvā tirtiṣanti bhavārṇavam (Śrimad-Bhāgavatam, 10.25.4), “Giving up spiritual knowledge, foolish people wish to cross the ocean of material existence by the performance of Vedic sacrifices, which are like unstable, so-called boats.”**

**Комментарий:**

Here the author concludes the main topic. “Both” means “acquiring the ultimate happiness and removal of distress.” “Not by any other means” such as by Vedic rituals. The mantra starting with ‘tam eva‘ also appears in the Puruṣa-sūkta of the Yajur Veda. Knowing (viditvā) and worshipping the Supreme Lord (tam), one attains liberation (atimṛtyum). Except for knowledge of God, there is no other (anyaḥ) means (panthāh) to attain liberation (anāya). The elision of the syllable ‘ya‘ in ‘anāya‘ is peculiar to the Vedas. The aphorism ‘vidyaiva tu tan-nirdhāraṇāt‘ is from the Brahma-sūtra (3.3.48). Only knowledge of God (vidyā) accompanied by His worship is the cause of liberation. Why ? Because in the śruti starting with ‘tam eva‘ is has been ascertained that knowledge of God is the cause of liberation. Corroborating that there is no other means, the author quotes the Muṇḍaka Upaniṣad. The word ‘adṛḍhā‘ means ‘decayed‘, ‘unstable’. The Supreme Lord‘s abode (akṛtaḥ) cannot be attained by Vedic rituals (kṛtena), for there is incongruity between the goal and the means. Yet that can be attained by knowledge of God, as stated in the rest of the sentence: tad-vijnānārthaṁ sa gurum evābhigacchet (Muṇḍaka Upaniṣad, 1.2.12), “In order to know the Supreme Lord, one should approach a spiritual master. ” The text starting with ‘na karmaṇā‘ is from the Taittiriya Āraṇyaka (10.12). Those who are detached attain liberation by knowledge of God accompanied by renunciation. The verse starting with ‘yathā-dṛḍhaiḥ ‘is from the Śrimad-Bhāgavatam (10.25.4) and was spoken by Indra. The words ‘vidyām ānvikṣikim‘ mean ’spiritual knowledge’. Similarly, Jaimini’s view has also been refuted, according to which only Vedic rituals are the cause for attaining the heavenly planets and liberation, while knowledge is just a means to perform them.

### Text 31

**Перевод:**

**Someone may argue,“ It may be so. Yet the Supreme Lord speaks of three causes of liberation: yogās trayo mayā proktā nṛṇāṁ śreyo-vidhitsayā, jnānaṁ karma ca bhaktiś ca nopāyo‘nyo’sti karhicit (Śrimad-Bhāgavatam, 11.20.6), ‘Desiring to bestow liberation upon men, I have spoken about three kinds of yoga — jnāna-yoga, karma-yoga, and bhakti-yoga, besides which there is no other means whatsoever’. How do you claim that knowledge alone is the cause of liberation?” In response, we say that Vedic rituals (karma) are not a direct cause of liberation, for this is evinced by the aforementioned scriptures. However, Vedic rituals are a means of purifying the mind, and hence are subordinate to jnāna and bhakti. As stated in the Bṛhad-āraṇyaka Upaniṣad (4.4.22): tam etaṁ vedānuvacanena brāhmaṇā vividiṣanti yajnena dānena tapasānāśakena, “The brāhmaṇas desire to know the Supreme Lord by means of studying the Vedas, performing sacrifices, giving charity, performing austerities, and fasting.” The Viṣṇu Purāṇa (1.6.12-13) says: yathecchāvāsa-niratāḥ sarva-bādhā-vivarjitaḥ, śuddhāntaḥkaraṇāḥ śuddhāḥ karmānuṣṭhāna-nirmalāḥ, śuddhe ca tāsāṁ manasi śuddhe‘ntaḥ-saṁsthite harau, śuddhaṁ jnānaṁ prapaśyanti viṣṇy-ākhyaṁ yena tat padam, “Free from all impediments, satisfied to live at any place according to their own will, those purified by the performance of Vedic rituals are pure and their consciousness is pure. When their minds are thus purified by the Holy Lord Hari situated in their hearts, they realise pure knowledge by which they attain the ultimate object, namely Lord Viṣṇu Himself.” However, jnāna and bhakti are direct causes of liberation.**

**Комментарий:**

“What you have said may be right, that knowledge alone is the cause of acquiring the ultimate happiness and removal of distress, but only if what I say is not correct. ” — this is the rest of the sentence. If someone asks the opponent, “What do you say?” In response, he quotes the above verse from the Śrimad-Bhāgavatam (11.20.6). The word ‘vidyā ‘or ‘jnāna‘ means ‘knowledge’. Vedic rituals are subordinate to jnāna and bhakti, which means they serve as a support to both. The passage starting with the Words ‘tam etam ‘is from the Bṛhad-āraṇyaka Upaniṣad (4.4.22). Here, ‘tam‘ means the Supreme Lord, Whose part and parcel is the living entity. The brahmacāris attain knowledge about Him by studying the Vedas; the grhasthas by performing sacrifices and giving charity; the vanaprasthas by performing austerities; and the sannyāsis by fasting. The verses starting with ‘yathecchayā’ are from the Viṣṇu Purāṇa (1.6.12-13). It should be understood that all living entities have been created by Lord Brahma, and by pure knowledge (śuddhaṁ jnānam) they (tāsām) can attain the ultimate object (padam), named Lord Viṣṇu Himself According to the Amara-kośa (3.3-93), the word ‘pada‘ means ‘business, ‘‘protection’ ‘abode,3 ‘characteristic,9 ‘foot, ‘and ‘object’. Jnāna and bhakti are direct causes of liberation, without any intervening factor.

### Text 32

**Перевод:**

**Bhakti is also a kind of knowledge, for its general characteristic as knowledge is so designated in the above quoted Śvetāśvatara Upaniṣad (3.8) and Brahma-sūtra (3-3-48). Taking into consideration a general class, oneness is designated among many. As stated by Bharata Muni: rasādinām anantatvād bheda eko hi gaṇyate, “Since there are unlimited rasas and so on, only one variety is counted.” This has also been explained in the following way — “Taking into consideration a general class without any specific order, rasas and so on are counted as only one. Otherwise, it is not possible to count them, for each rasa has so many varieties.” The employment of the word ‘bhakti ‘in the sense of a particular kind of knowledge should be understood like the word ‘pāṇḍava‘ as a particular group of Kauravas.**

**Комментарий:**

Someone may argue, “From the Lord‘s words, both jnāna and bhakti appear to be causes of liberation, but not from the quoted śruti and sūtra». In response, the author says, “Bhakti is also a kind of knowledge, etc. ” The meaning is clear. Both jnāna and bhakti are generally designated as knowledge, among many of its varieties. It should be understood that the explanation of Bharata Muni’s verse is from Baladeva Vidyābhūṣaṇa’s Sāhitya-kaumudi (4.18). The words ‘tad-gaṇanam‘ mean ‘to count the rasas and so on.

### Text 33

**Перевод:**

**The conclusion here is this — jnāna (knowledge) is a synonym of the words ‘vidyā ‘and ‘vedana, ‘and it is twofold. One kind is in the form of realization of the meaning of the words ‘tat ‘and ‘tvam ‘in the sentence ‘tat tvam asi’ (you are that), and it is like an unblinking glance. The other kind is in a variegated form of devotion, and it is like a side-glance. By meditating on the pure meaning of the word ‘tvam although a jiva attains the spiritual world, being devoid of the fortune of having the Supreme Lord‘s favour, its liberation is characterized by isolation, just like a wife given up by her husband. As stated by the Bṛhad-āraṇyaka Upaniṣad (4.4-12): atmānaṁ ced vijāniyād ayam asmiti pūruṣaḥ, kirn icchan kasya kāmāya śariram anusanjvaret, “If the living entity knows its own self in this way — ‘I am this, ‘then desiring which object and to fulfil which desire would one mortify the body?”**

**Комментарий:**

Here the author explains the twofold knowledge. Those who know the Pancāgni-vidyā meditate on the jiva’s svarūpa as a part and parcel of the Supreme Lord, Who is both knowledge and knower. While meditating on oneself there is also meditation on the Supreme Lord, and thus there is no contradiction with the above quoted śruti and sūtra. This is also understood by an illustration in the following way — a wife who was given up by her husband certainly has knowledge about him. Being purified, such a liberated soul dwells in the spiritual abode but has no perception of it. The text starting with ‘ātmānaṁ ced ‘is from the Bṛhad-āraṇyaka Upaniṣad (4.4.12). "If the living entity (pūrusah) knows (vijāniyāt) one’s own self (ātmānam). ”In what way ? When one knows that, “Let myself, the 7, ‘be part and parcel of the Supreme Lord, Whose form is both knowledge and knower, ‘‘then one attains liberation. Thereupon, desiring (icchan) which object and to fulfil which (kasya) desire (kāmāya) would one continuously mortify (anusanjvaret) the body (śariram)? By such knowledge one becomes free from distress. Such a self-realized soul has no desires. One who is part and parcel of the Supreme Lord also becomes fully satisfied like Him and has nothing to desire. According to the Śridhara-kośa, the word ‘kāma‘ is used in the sense of love and desire.

### Text 34

**Перевод:**

**By the right knowledge of the meaning of the word ‘tat, ‘one who is fortunate to receive the Supreme Lord‘s favor attains one out of four kinds of liberation starting with sālokya-mukti, just as one who receives the favor of the king becomes a minister, commander in chief, and the like. In the Praśna Upaniṣad (5.2) we find a passage starting with the following words: etad vai satyakāma paraṁ cāparaṁ ca brahma yad oṁkāras tasmād vidvān etenaivāyatanenaikataram anveti, “O Satyakāma, this higher Brahman and lower Brahman is indeed the syllable Oṁ. Therefore, knowing this, one attains either of these by this very means». In the same paragraph it is stated: yaḥ punar etaṁ tri-mātreṇaum ity anenaivākṣareṇa paraṁ puruṣam abhidhyāyita sa tejasi sūrye sampanno yathā pādodaras tvacā vinirmucyate, evaṁ ha vai sa pāpmabhir vinirmuktaḥ sa sāmabhir unniyate brahma-lokam, sa etasmaj jiva-ghanāt parāt paraṁ puri-śayaṁ puruṣam ikṣate, “One who Meditates on the Supreme Person by the three letters of the syllable Om attains the shining sun planet. One then becomes free from all sins, just as a serpent becomes free from its skin. One is thereafter carried by the hymns of the Sāma Veda to the abode of Lord Brahma. After receiving instructions from Lord Brahma, the embodiment of all jtvas, one them attains the Supreme Lord Who is situated in the spiritual sky.” It must be accepted that scriptural knowledge is the means to knowledge of both ‘tat tvam asi ‘and bhakti, for otherwise there would be no inclination towards either.**

**Комментарий:**

“By the right knowledge of the meaning of the word ‘tat, ” ‘i.e., “by realizing the Supreme Lord by exclusively devoted consciousness». The four kinds of liberation are: having perception of a spiritual planet (sālokya), having a form similar to the Supreme Lord‘s form (sārūpya), having the Supreme Lord‘s close association (sāmipya), and having opulence like the Supreme Lord (sārṣṭi). The passage starting with the words ‘etad vai‘ is from the Praśna Upaniṣad (5.2), and was spoken by the great sage Pippalāda. “О Satyakāma, the syllable Om is the higher (param) Brahman named Lord Nārāyaṇa, and the lower (aparam) Brahman named Lord Brahma. ” From this it is understood that the syllable Om is the Para-brahman, for it is an avatāra in the form of a syllable, just like the mantra ‘hamsa‘ and so on. The lower Brahman is called so on account of being an effect of the higher Brahman. Therefore, knowing that the syllable Om is the twofold Brahman, by having meditated on the higher or lower Brahman, one attains (anveti) either (ekataram) according to one’s meditation. The word ‘padodarah‘ means ’serpent’. After reaching Lord Brahma, who is identified as the embodiment of all jivas (jiva-ghanāt), one attains (ikṣate) the Supreme (param), Lord Nārāyaṇa, Who is situated (śayam) in the spiritual sky (puri). It is understood that by having meditated on Him and attained His favour, one obtains sālokya, sāmipya, sārūpya, or sārṣṭi. Scriptural knowledge is the means to knowledge of both ‘tat tvam asi‘ and bhakti, for otherwise, without scriptural knowledge, there would be no inclination towards either.

### Text 35

**Перевод:**

**The goal of human life, characterized as the joy of serving the Supreme Lord‘s feet, is attained by one who becomes an object of His favour and thus brings Him under one’s control by means of a particular kind of knowledge in the form of bhakti, just as the best of damsels adorned with qualities like affection and beauty controls her husband. As stated in the Māṭhara-śruti: bhaktir evainaṁ nayati bhaktir evainaṁ darśayati bhakti-vaśaḥ puruṣo bhaktir eva bhūyasi, “Bhakti alone brings one towards the Supreme Lord; bhakti alone reveals Him. The Supreme Person is under the control of bhakti. Bhakti is indeed greater than any other means.” In the Gopāla-tāpani Upaniṣad (2.97) it is said: mānuṣi vijnāna-ghanānanda-ghanā sac-cid-ānandaikarase bhakti-yoge tiṣṭhati, “My human-like form is the embodiment of transcendental knowledge and bliss, and it is always present in unalloyed devotional service, which also consists exclusively of consciousness, eternity, and bliss.” Smṛtis like the Śrimad-Bhāgavatam (9.4.63,66) also corroborate this: aham bhakta-paradhino hy asvatantra iva dvija, sadhubhir grasta-hrdayo bhaktair bhakta-jana-priyah, “O brāhmaṇa, I am dependent on My devotees as if I had no independence. Since I am so dear to My saintly devotees, My heart has been taken away by them.” mayi nirbaddha-hrdayah sadhavah sama-darsinah, vase kurvanti mam bhaktya sat-striyah sat-patim yatha, “The saintly devotees of equal vision, whose hearts are fixed upon Me, control Me by devotional service, just as chaste women control a good husband.”**

**Комментарий:**

It should be understood that ‘by bhakti ‘means ‘by bhakti without any material desires’. The word ‘varivasya ‘means’service’. According to the Amara-kośa (2.7.35), the words ‘varivasya, ‘‘susrūsa, ‘‘paricarya, ‘and ‘upāsana ‘are synonyms of’service’. The text starting with ‘bhaktir evainam ‘is from the Maṭhara-śruti. Bhakti brings (nayati) the devotee to Lord Hari’s abode and reveals Him, the controller of that abode. Why is it so ? Because Lord Hari (puruṣah) is under the control of bhakti (bhakti-vasah). Why is He under its control? Because bhakti is greater and better (bhuyast) than all other means to attain Him. Indicating the nature of bhakti, the author quotes the Gopāla-tāpani Upaniṣad (2.97) stating how the Lord is under its control. The twelfth form of Lord Kṛṣṇa is situated in bhakti-yoga, which means He is under its control. What is His form like ? It is the embodiment of transcendental knowledge and bliss (vijnāna-ghanananda-ghana). In the Astadhyayi (3-3.77), Panini states: mūrtau ghanah, “The word ‘ghana ‘is used in the sense of form.” According to Sanskrit grammar, the affix ‘ap ‘comes after the verbal root ‘han, ‘which is then substituted by ‘ghan ‘when it is used in the sense of solidity. Examples are ’saindhava-ghana ‘(a lump of salt) and ‘dadhi-ghana, ‘(solid curd). How is that bhakti-yoga in which He is situated ? It also consists exclusively of consciousness, eternity, and bliss, i.e., it is the essence ofhlādini, sandhini, and samvit. The verses starting with the words ‘aham bhakta-paradhinah ‘are from the Śrimad-Bhāgavatam (9-4-63,66) and were spoken by the Lord Himself “Although I am the Supreme Lord, I am still dependent on My devotees, as if I had no independence (asvatantrah) like a jiva subordinated to a superior (paradhinah)». By the illustration of a chaste wife who controls her husband, the Lord states that His dependence on His devotees is meant for His own happiness.

### Text 36

**Перевод:**

**Those engaged in selfless devotional service have no desire for any kind of liberation such as salokya and so on, for that occurs on its own. As stated in the Śrimad-Bhāgavatam (9-4.67) ‘mat-sevaya pratitam te salokyadi-catustayam, necchanti sevaya purnah kuto ‘nyat kala-viplutam, “Being fully satisfied in My service, My devotees do not aspire even for the four kinds of liberation starting with salokya, which are consequently attained by serving Me, so what to speak of happiness in the heavenly planets, which is lost by the effect of time.” In the Gopāla-tāpani Upaniṣad (1.15), the following is mentioned regarding the characteristics of bhakti: bhaktir asya bhajanam tad ihamutropadhi-nairasyenamusmin manah-kalpanam etad eva naiskarmyam, “Bhakti is the worship of the Supreme Lord, rejecting all material designations in this world and the next. It is concentrating the mind on Him. This alone is freedom from karma.” It is also said in the Nārada-pancaratra: sarvopadhi-vinirmuktam tat-paratvena nirmalam, hrsikena hrsikesa-sevanam bhaktir ucyate, “Utilizing the senses in Lord Hrsikesa’s pure service, free from all material designations and with full devotion unto Him, is called bhakti.”**

**Комментарий:**

Here the author demonstrates the selfless nature of pure devotional service. The word ‘iha‘ in the beginning of this paragraph means ‘in knowledge manifest in the form of selfless devotional service’. “On its own ” (svatah) means just as one automatically obtains satisfaction by eating. The evidence and reason to this is found in the Srimad-Bhāgavatam (9.4.67) in the words spoken by the Supreme Lord. In this connection, it should be understood that the earlier stage of service is sadhana-bhakti, while the higher stage is prema-bhakti. The word ‘pratitam ‘means ‘consequently attained’. The word ‘anyat ‘means ‘happiness in the heavenly planets’. Someone may argue, “It is not appropriate for the devotees to be indifferent towards liberation starting with salokya-mukti, for that is a transcendental stage and hence desirable. ” True. They do not desire the opulence and happiness that are the natural result of liberation if that happens to be contrary to the Lord‘s service. On the other hand, they desire liberation if it is not contrary to His service. Without the Supreme Lord‘s service, liberation is just happiness, but with His service, it is the supreme happiness, for it is itself a form of the Lord. The devotees ‘expertise in attaining their own interests has been described in this way. As stated by Indra in the Śrimad-Bhāgavatam (6.18.74). aradhanam bhagavata ihamana nirasisah, ye tu necchanty apiparam te svartha-kusalah smrtah, Those who wish to worship the Supreme Lord have no further desires. Those who do not aspire even for liberation are said to be experts in their own interest. ” Pure Bhakti is similarly understood from the Srimad-Bhagavatam (3.29.12-13) as causeless and uninterrupted, pursuing which one does not care for any kind of liberation. In the Purāṇas, such bhakti is described by those who perform it as being devoid of any further interest: na yesam bhajanad anyac ciktrsitam abhipsitam, jijnasitath ca kincit te jneya niskincana budhaih, “Those who worship the Supreme Lord without any further intention, desire, or question are known by the wise as free from all possessions (niskincana)». The text starting with ‘bhaktir asya ‘is from the Gopāla-tāpani Upaniṣad (1.15). Lord Kṛṣṇa’s (asya) worship (bhaktih) is performed in a favourable way by hearing, chanting, and so forth. Favourable performance of worship is that which is appreciated by Him. The mind (manah) should also be concentrated (kalpanam) on Lord Kṛṣṇa(amusmin). The etymological explanation of the word ‘kalpanam ‘is ‘that by which the mind becomes absorbed’. This refers to the stage when the mind is attached, which is caused by chanting, hearing, and so on. In order to demonstrate the superiority of pure devotional service, it is stated that in this world (iha) and in the next (amutra) it is performed rejecting (nairasyena) all material designations (upadhi). This means rejecting desires for any result other than Kṛṣṇa. The meaning is that such devotional service is performed with the sole desire for Kṛṣṇa. This itself is freedom from all karma (naiskarmyam), regular, occasional, optional, or forbidden. The affix ‘ya ‘is employed in the word ‘naiskarmya, ‘but the original meaning of the word ‘niskarma ‘is retained. The word ‘eva ‘(alone) is used to exclude devotional service performed because of selfish desires. Or else, it can mean that bhakti is itself freedom from karma, for it consequently removes ignorance in the form of karma, as in the maxim of longevity and ghee.  “Freedom from all material designations” (sarvopadhi-vinirmuktam) means ‘freedom from any desire other than Lord Hrsikesa’. “Pure (nirmalam) devotional service ” is that which is not contaminated by karma, yoga, jnāna, and so on. “With full devotion ” (tat-paratvena) means ‘especially favourable’. Service (sevanam) of Lord Kṛṣṇa (Hrsikesa) performed with the sense-organs, such as ears, eyes, and so forth, means full dedication with body, mind, and words.

### Text 37

**Перевод:**

**In this way, the gradation of the results expressed in the following scriptural statements is also consistent: yatha kratur asmim loke puruso bhavati, tathetah pretya bhavati (Chāndogya Upaniṣad, 3.14.1), “According to one’s worship in this world, so is his mode of worship after departing from here.” ye yatha mam prapadyante tams tathaiva bhajamy aham (Bhagavad-gita, 4.11), “As they surrender unto Me, I reward them accordingly.” nayam sukhapo bhagavan dehinam gopika-sutah, jnaninarh catma-bhutanam yatha bhaktimatam iha (Śrimad-Bhāgavatam, 10.9.21), “The Supreme Lord, the son of Mother Yasoda, does not give as much happiness in this world to neophyte jnanis who are still on the bodily platform, and to advanced jnanis who have realised the self, as He gives to those who engage in devotional service.”**

**Комментарий:**

“In this way” means “there being a twofold path of jnāna — one in the form of meditation on the meaning of the words ‘tat ‘and ‘tvam, ‘and the other in the form of love for God, which is attained by performing devotional service such as hearing, chanting, and so on”. The text starting with ‘yatha-kratuh ‘is from the Chāndogya Upaniṣad (3.14.1)’. yatha-kratuh ‘means ‘according to one’s worship’. Having departed (pretya) from this world (itah) and attained the next world, one obtains a result consequent to one’s mode of worship. The verse starting with the words ‘ye yatha ‘is from the Bhagavad-glta (4.11). The verse starting with the words ‘nayamsukhapah ‘isfrom the Śrimad-Bhāgavatam (10.9.21). This (ayam) Supreme Person (bhagavan) is Lord Kṛṣṇa, the son of Mother Yasoda (gopika-sutah).
