The Supreme Lord does not give as much happiness in this world to the neophyte jnanis (dehinam) and advanced jnanis (atma-bhūtanam) as He gives to those engaged in devotional service to Himself, the son of thegopi. This is the meaning.

### Text 38

**Перевод:**

**This topic is again discussed here in the following way — What is the nature of bhakti, which is the cause of subduing the Supreme Lord ? Is it in the form of knowledge and bliss composed of material sattva-guna ? Or is it in the form of knowledge and bliss, which are the very nature of the Supreme Lord? Or is it in the form of the jtva’s knowledge and bliss? Or is it the essence of samvit combined with the essence of hlādini? It cannot be the first, because nowhere in the scriptures do we hear that the Supreme Lord is under the control of maya, and because He is complete on His own. It cannot be the second, for this is disproved by the superiority of the bliss of bhakti over the Lord‘s nature. It also cannot be the third, for the knowledge and bliss of the jiva are minute. However, it must certainly be the fourth.**

**Комментарий:**

The Lord cannot be under the jurisdiction of maya, for in the smṛti, such as the Śrimad-Bhāgavatam (2.7.47), this is denied: māyā paraity abhimukhe ca vilajjamana, “In front of the Supreme Lord, māyā becomes embarrassed and runs away. ”Moreover, the Lord is complete on His own, as corroborated by the śruti, such as the Bṛhad-āraṇyaka Upaniṣad (5.1.1): purnam adah purnam idam, “The Supreme Lord is complete, hence this universe is complete. ” Because the jiva is not complete on its own, it desires material knowledge and happiness and strives to attain them. Although the Supreme Lord‘s nature is bliss itself, the bliss of bhakti surpasses it, for there cannot be anything superior to such bliss, as will be described later. Bhakti is the fourth kind mentioned here and consists of the essence of both hlādini and samvit.

### Text 39

**Перевод:**

**It is understood from the śruti and the smṛti that having come under the control of bhakti, the Supreme Lord remains there like a bee inside a lotus bud, or like an infatuated youth under the control of a graceful damsel. If the meaning understood from the scriptures is taken otherwise, then it would be inconclusive. Consequently, a postulation can be made based on the words of the Viṣṇu Purāṇa (1.12.69): hlādini sandhini samvit tvayy eka sarva-samsraye hlada-tapa-kari misra tvayi no guna-varjite, “One single energy in the form of hlādini, sandhini, and samvit exists within You, the resting place of everything, but the energy that causes mundane joy, distress, and a mixture of both does not exist in You, Who are untainted by the modes of nature.” By such a postulation, it is ascertained that bhakti is the essence of hlādini and samvit, which is a peculiarity in the Lord‘s nature.**

**Комментарий:**

By a worldly illustration, here the author explains that the Supreme Lord‘s subjection to bhakti is a form of happiness. According to the scriptures, the Supreme Person is under the control of bhakti and is always situated in bhakti, as stated in the $rimad-Bhāgavatam (9-4.63): aharh bhakta-paradhino hy asvatantra iva dvija, “O brāhmaṇa, I am dependent on My devotees as if I had no independence. ” If the meaning understood from the śruti and the smṛti that the Supreme Lord is under the control of bhakti is taken otherwise, i.e., if bhakti is not the essence of hlādini and samvit, which are a peculiarity in the Lord‘s nature, then the meaning would be inconclusive. “Consequently, ” i.e., by knowing that otherwise the meaning would be inconclusive, by means of postulation (arthapatti) it is understood that bhakti is the essence of both hlādini and samvit, as established in the Viṣṇu Purāṇa (1.12.69). When a given meaning is seen as inconclusive, postulation (arthapatti), the hypothesis of a different meaning, is the means to establish the correct conclusion. For example, “John is fat but does not eat during the day. ” Here, John’s fatness has not been explained, since he does not eat during the day, so the means to establish the correct conclusion is to conjecture that he eats at night. Similarly, the Lord‘s subjection to bhakti, as heard from the scriptures, has not been explained. So the means to establish the correct conclusion is to conjecture that bhakti is the essence of hlādini and samvit, which are a peculiarity in the Lord‘s svarūpa. The verse quoted from the Viṣṇu Purāṇa was spoken by Dhruva. He glorifies the Supreme Lord as different from the living entities. “О Supreme Lord, one (eka) undivided energy with three functions — hlādini, sandhini, and samvit — exists within You (tvayi), the ultimate resting place of everything (sarva-samsraye). By its portion as sattva-guna, material energy causes joy characterized by mental satisfaction; by its portion as tamo-guna, it causes distress, such as the loss of sense objects; and by its portion as rajo-guna, it is a mixture created by both. Such a threefold material nature does not exist in You. ” Why ? “This is because You are untainted by the modes of the illusory energy (guna-varjite), which exist only among the jtvas. Therefore, You are different from the living entities and possess exceeding happiness and opulence». This is the meaning.

### Text 40

**Перевод:**

**It is thus proved that bhakti is the essence of both hlādini and samvit combined. This essence is a particular desire that is favourable to the Supreme Lord and rests within His eternal servants. Holding this view about such bhakti, the śruti defines it as a homogeneity of eternity, knowledge, and bliss, and demonstrates that the Lord is under its control: manusi vijnāna-ghanananda-ghana sac-cid-anandaikarase bhakti-yoge tisthati (Gopāla-tāpani Upaniṣad, 2.97), “My human-like form is the embodiment of transcendental knowledge and bliss, and it is always present in unalloyed devotional service, which also consists exclusively of consciousness, eternity, and bliss». bhaktir evainam nayati bhaktir evainam darśayati bhakti-vasah puruso bhaktir eva bhuyasi (Maṭhara-śruti), “Bhakti alone brings one towards the Supreme Lord; bhakti alone reveals Him. The Supreme Person is under the control of bhakti. Bhakti is indeed greater than any other means.”**

**Комментарий:**

The present topic is concluded here. “Together” means “undivided, ‘‘for the energy is said to be only one. Samvit is non-different from hladini, and its essence is that portion which is propitious to the Supreme Lord. Bhakti should be understood as the essence of both, just as condensed milk is the essence of milk, or the aroma is the essence of a flower.

“A particular desire ” means “a desire exclusively for Kṛṣṇa, without desire for anything else». In other words, bhakti is a portion propitious to the Supreme Lord, which becomes one with such a desire for Him, just like milk mixed with water becomes one. Such a desire is a manifestation of the awareness the jiva has of objects other than itself(dharma-bhuta-jnāna). Such bhakti shines eternally among the Lord‘s eternal associates in the eternal abode. Just like the Ganga, it descends in this world by the intervention of His devotees. This is also described in the Govinda-bhāṣya (3.3.9). “Such bhakti ” means “whose characteristics have been described. ”

### Text 41

**Перевод:**

**Someone may argue, “You yourself have said that bhakti in the form of hlādini and samvit is non-different from the bliss and knowledge that are the Supreme Lord‘s nature, since bhakti is His svarūpa-śakti. Why are you now denying that bhakti is the bliss and knowledge of the Lord‘s nature?”  In response, we say the following: We do not accept that the nature of Brahman is mere consciousness devoid of attributes as inferred by others, but we rather accept that Brahman possesses svarūpa-śakti. Although His śakti is non-different from His svarupay it is manifest as different from it because otherwise the scriptural statements that refer to it as ‘His energy ‘would be unsubstantiated.**

**Комментарий:**

Here the opponent again raises a question. The words ’svarupa-śaktih‘ are used in the objection in the sense that śakti itself is the svarūpa of the Lord. “As inferred by others ” means “as accepted by the Māyāvādis. ” The philosophical conclusion here has been propounded by Madhvācārya». We accept that the energy (śakti) and its possessor (śaktimat) are non-different than the Supreme’s own nature (svarūpa), just as when we say ‘there is existence, ‘that which exists is non-different from the existence." This is the meaning.

### Text 42

**Перевод:**

**The concept here is like that of a part and the whole. Although limbs such as hands, feet, and so on are non-different from the whole body to which they belong, the concept of relation as a part and the whole is wisdom. The Supreme Lord and His energy should be seen in a similar way. This is certainly not a baseless speculation, for it is corroborated by the scriptures, such as: neha nānāsti kincana (Bṛhad-āraṇyaka Upaniṣad, 4.4.19), “Within the Supreme Lord there is no difference whatsoever.” pratisedhac ca (Brahma-sūtra, 3-2.31), “Because difference is denied in the scriptures.” Similarly, just as a youth’s soft, round-shaped arm placed on a damsel’s shoulder gives great pleasure to both, so also bhakti, the essence of hladini and sathvit, which are a peculiarity in the Lord‘s nature, is proved as a non-separated attribute of the Lord, as well as a separated attribute of the devotee, and gives the highest bliss to both. Thus, everything is flawless.**

**Комментарий:**

Imparting the meaning of what was stated above to the reader’s heart by means of an illustration given by the wise, the author writes the present paragraph. The meaning is clear. This is to be understood according to the view of Samkhya and Yoga, and not according to the view of Nyāya and Vaiśeṣika, for there is a difference between the view accepted by both. This topic is described in detail in the Govinda-bhāṣya on the aphorism 3-2.31, which has been quoted above. In the Śvetāśvatara Upaniṣad (6.8) it is said: parasya śaktir vividhaiva srūyate svabhaviki jnāna-bala-kriya ca, “It is heard from the scriptures that the Supreme has multifarious intrinsic superior energies in the form of knowledge, power, and action. ” The meaning is that although here it appears that śakti is different from the Lord‘s svarūpa, since the word ‘intrinsic ‘(svabhaviki) prevents difference, there is no difference between the qualities and their possessor. The author explains the conclusion by a worldly illustration. The word ‘tayoh ‘refers to both the youth and the damsel. Just as the youth’s arm is non-different from him but is different from the damsel, similarly bhakti, the essence of hladini and samvit, is non-different from the Supreme Lord but is different from the devotee. “By being a non-separated attribute ” means “by having an attribute that is non-different from the subject,” just as in the sentence ‘there is existence’. “By being a separated attribute ” means “by having an attribute that is different from the subject, ” such as in the sentence ‘hе is ornamented’. “It gives the highest bliss to both, ” that means to the Lord and His devotee.

### Text 43

**Перевод:**

**In this connection, hlādini should be known as superior to samvity and samvit as superior to sandhini. Although the Supreme Lord consists of existence (sat) as His very Self, the energy by which He maintains and gives existence, and which is the cause of His omnipresence in all places, times, and substances, is called sandhini. Although the Supreme Lord consists of knowledge, the energy by which He knows and imparts knowledge is called samvit. Although the Supreme Lord consists of bliss, the energy by which He feels and gives bliss is called hlādini. By the manifestation of predominance over one another, this single energy is known in a respective form, just like a vaidurya gem.**

**Комментарий:**

Speaking about the nature and functions of hlādini, samvit, and sandhini, here the author first speaks about their gradation for the easy understanding of how one is respectively superior to the other. Sandhini is the energy by which the Supreme Lord is expressed as ‘He is ‘in the Kaṭha Upaniṣad (6.13): astity evopalabdhavyah, “The Supreme Soul is to be understood as ‘He is’». By sandhini, He holds a form of existence expressed in the Chāndogya Upaniṣad (6.2.1): sad eva saumyedam agra asit, “Dear Svetaketu, in the beginning there was only ’sat, ‘the Supreme Lord». Also by sandhini, He gives existence and the ability to work to the living entities, nature, time, karma, and substances. Samvit is the energy by which the Supreme Lord is said to have the form of knowledge in the Taittiriya Upaniṣad (2.1): satyam jnānam anantam brahma, “The Supreme Lord is truth, knowledge, and eternity personified." By samvit, He is said to have knowledge in the Muṇḍaka Upaniṣad (1.1.9): yah sarvajnah sarvavit, “ The Supreme Lord is omniscient, all-knowing;” as in the sentence ‘the sun is shining’. Also by samvit, He makes the living entities distinguished by the awareness of objects other than themselves (dharma-bhūta-jnāna), as stated in the scriptures: prajna ca tasmat prasrta purani (Śvetāśvatara Upaniṣad, 4.18), “It is from the Supreme Lord that primeval knowledge becomes manifest.” tvatto jnānam hi jivanam (Śrimad-Bhāgavatam, 11.22.28), “It is from You that the knowledge of the living entities is manifest”. Hladini is the energy by which the Supreme Lord‘s nature is said to be bliss itself: vijnānam ānandam brahma (Bṛhad-āraṇyaka Upaniṣad, 3.9.28), “The Supreme Lord is knowledge and bliss personified. ”By hlādini, He is said to have bliss: ānandam brahmano vidvan na bibheti kutascaneti (Taittiriya Upaniṣad, 2.4.1), “One who knows the Supreme Lord‘s blissfulness never fears». Hladini itself, being non-different from samvit, takes a particular form such as hearing, chanting, etc., and is the very means to propitiate Him, by which He delights the living entities who turn towards Him and makes them recipients of the highest bliss, as described in the Taittiriya Upaniṣad (2.7): raso vai sah, rasath hy evayam labdhvanandi bhavati, “The Supreme Lord is rasa personified. One who obtains this rasa becomes blissful». The author explains through an illustration how a single energy appears threefold: just as a single vaidūrya gem appears as blue, yellow, and so on depending on its proximity to a particularly colored object, a single superior energy appears as hlādini, samvit, or sandhini depending on its function.

### Text 44

**Перевод:**

**Bhakti, whose form is the essence of both hlādini and samvit, is called ‘rati ‘or ‘prema’. Placing itself in the heart of the Supreme Lord and the devotee, it makes both delight each other. As stated in the Śrimad-Bhāgavatam: sadhavo hrdayarh mahyam sadhunam hrdayam tv aham, mad-anyat te na jananti naham tebhyo manag api (9.4.68), “The saintly devotees are My heart, and I am their heart. They do not know anything but Me, nor do I know anything but them.” visrjati hrdayaib na yasya saksad dharir avasabhihito‘py aghaugha-na£ah, pranaya-rāsanaya dhrtanghri-padmah sa bhavati bhagavata-pradhana uktah (ll.2.55), “One who has bound His lotus feet by the ropes of love is said to be the foremost of His devotees. Lord Hari Himself, the destroyer of heaps of sins, never leaves the heart of such a devotee, even if called upon unintentionally.” The Supreme Lord‘s devotion for His devotees is also shown: evam sva-bhaktayo rājan bhagavan bhakta-bhaktiman, usitvadisya san-margam punar dvaravatim agat (10.86.59), “O King, in this way, having stayed some time in the company of both of His devotees, and having taught them about the spiritual path, the Supreme Lord, the devotee of His devotees, again returned to Dvārakā.”**

**Комментарий:**

Bhakti is placed in the hearts of the Lord and His devotees because of viśeṣa, whose factual effect is difference. The verse starting with the words ’sadhavo hrdayam‘ is from the Srimad-Bhāgavatam (9.4.68) and was spoken by the Supreme Lord. The verse starting with the words ‘visrjati hrdayam ‘is also from the Śrimad-Bhāgavatam (11.2.55) and was spoken by the yogi Havi. The Lord‘s bhakti for His devotees consists of bliss and knowledge and is characterized by His favour to them. The verse starting with the words ‘evam sva-bhaktayoh ‘is from the Srimad-Bhāgavatam (10.86.59) and was spoken by Śukadeva. “Both of His devotees” refer to Srutadeva, the best of saintly brāhmaṇas, and Bahulasva, the best of saintly kings, both residents of Mithila. Having stayed (usitva) some time in the company of both of them and having taught (adisya) them about the spiritual path (san-margam), the Lord returned to Dvārakā.

### Text 45

**Перевод:**

**Therefore, the Supreme Lord tries to give happiness to His devotees in various ways, as He Himself says: muhurtenapi samhartum sakto yady api danavan, mad-bhaktanam vinodartham karomi vividhah kriyah, darsana-dhyana-samsparsair matsya-kurma-vihangamah, svany apatyani pusnanti tathaham api padmaja, “Although I am able to annihilate the demons even in a single moment, I perform a variety of pastimes just for the pleasure of My devotees. О Brahma, just as the fish, the tortoise, and the bird respectively maintain their offspring by watching them, by meditating on them, and by touching them, so do I maintain My devotees.”**

**Комментарий:**

“Therefore” means “because the Supreme Lord (tasya) has devotion for His devotees». The quoted verse is from the Padma Purāṇa. The figure of expression used here is called ‘yatha-samkhya ‘(according to numerical sequence). The meaning is that just as the fish maintains its offspring by watching them, the tortoise by meditating on them, and the bird by touching them, similarly the Lord maintains His devotees.

### Text 46

**Перевод:**

**Therefore, although the Supreme Lord Himself consists of supreme bliss, by bhakti He becomes filled with excelling bliss, experiences His own nature as bliss, and makes His devotees experience different varieties of bliss. How can such bhakti be the happiness made of the material mode of goodness? Thus, bhakti is proved to be the ultimate goal of human life. The fruit of knowledge is God, but the fruit of bhakti is bhakti itself, whose sole object is He, for bhakti itself is the most intense bliss.**

**Комментарий:**

Here the author concludes the present topic. The word ‘yaya ‘means ‘by the described bhakti’. “The result of knowledge ” means “the goal of the knowledge in the form of meditation on the meaning of the word ‘tat’». The words ‘tad-visaya ‘mean ‘whose object is the Supreme Lord’.

Bhakti, which is the essence of hlādini and samvit and whose object is the Lord, is itself its own result because bhakti is the highest form of happiness. As spoken by the Supreme Lord in the Nārada Purāṇa: sarva-mangala-murdhanya pūrnananda-mayi sada, dvijendra tava mayy astu bhaktir avyabhicarini, “O best ofbrāhmaṇas, may you always have undeviating devotion for Me, which is full bliss and the highest auspiciousness. ”

### Text 47

**Перевод:**

**Bhakti, whose form is the essence of bliss and knowledge, and also appears in the form of activities, which are to be understood as limbs, just as a transcendental body constituted of bliss and knowledge has limbs like hairs and so on. As a person suffering from a bile disorder cannot feel the sweetness of sugar, one who has been continuously transmigrating in the material world at first cannot experience the bliss and knowledge of bhakti. However, by serving the Supreme Lord, one is gradually able to experience it as such.**

**Комментарий:**

Someone may argue, “How can bhakti in the form of activities such as hearing, chanting, and so on, be understood as bliss and knowledge?” The author responds to this here. The words ‘kayadi-tadatmyena ‘mean ‘by the connection with a devotee’s body and senses’. Devotional activities are limbs of bhakti, just as hairs, nails, and so on are limbs of the body. According to the Amara-kośa (2.6.70), the words ‘ahga, ‘‘pratika, ‘and ‘avayava ‘are synonyms of ‘limb’. “Then why is it that the bliss and knowledge of bhakti are not perceived in its activities such as hearing and chanting?” In response, the author gives the illustration of a person suffering from a bile disorder. The words ‘tad-adi-rupatvam ‘mean ‘whose form is the essence of bliss and knowledge’. The word ‘purvam ‘means ‘at the stage of transmigration in the material world’. The words ‘tat-samsevaya ‘mean ‘by repeatedly performing bhakti in the form of hearing chanting, and so forth’.

### Text 48

**Перевод:**

**Hence, Vyāsadeva, the revered compiler of the Brahma-sūtras, therein says that the liberated souls engage in bhakti uninterruptedly: aprayanat tatrapi hi drstam (4.1.12), “It is seen in the scriptures that one should engage in bhakti until liberation, and even after that.” Nor does this mean that those who propound bhakti as the ultimate goal of life deny that the goal of life is the Supreme Lord, for on account of its intrinsic relation with the Lord, He is also attained through bhakti itself. Thus, the conclusion is that the Supreme Lord, the object of bhakti, is the ultimate goal of life for the devotees.**

**Комментарий:**

The word ’siddhanam ‘refers to the liberated souls. It is seen in the scriptures that there is service to the Supreme Lord until liberation and even in the stage of liberation. On account of the intrinsic relation between bhakti and the Supreme Lord, by attaining bhakti, whose object is the Lord, one also attains Him. This is the conclusion stated here.

### Text 49

**Перевод:**

**Therefore, the performance of Vedic rituals (karma) is useful for Purification of the mind. However, spiritual knowledge in the form of bhakti or jnāna is useful for directly attaining God. Thus, there is no flaw whatsoever.**

**Комментарий:**

Here the author concludes the main topic. Both karma-yoga and bhakti-yoga have been said to be causes of liberation, and by including both here, there is no flaw at all.

### Text 50

**Перевод:**

**As for those who have attained bhakti from the beginning due to the special association of saintly devotees, they are different. By bhakti itself, they attain purification of the mind and realization of the Lord. As stated in the Śrimad-Bhāgavatam (2.2.37): pibanti ye bhagavata atmanah satam kathamrtam sravana-putesu sambhrtam, punanti te visaya-vidusitasayam vrājanti tac-carana-saroruhantikam, “Those who fully drink the nectarean topics of the Supreme Lord, the soul of the devotees, through their ears purify their hearts, which are contaminated by mundane sensual pleasure, and attain His lotus feet.” Regarding them, the Praśna Upaniṣad (1.10) says: tapasa brahmacaryena sraddhaya vidyayatmanam anvisyet, “One should realise Lord Hari by austerity, celibacy, faith, and loving devotional service.”**

**Комментарий:**

Having shown the mode of sadhana accompanied by karma-yoga, as performed by the sanistha and parinisthita devotees, the author now shows the sadhana devoid of karma-yoga, as performed by the nirapeksa devotees. “Those” refers to devotees like Nārada. The words ‘te punah ‘mean ‘they do not perform karma-yoga, ‘‘they are different’. As stated in the Visva-kośa (Avyaya, 55): punar aprathame bhede, “The word ‘punah ‘is used in the sense of ‘again ‘and ‘difference’. The quoted verse is from the Srimad-Bhāgavatam (2.2.37) and was spoken by Śukadeva. One should realise (anvisyet) Lord Hari (atmanam) by austerities (tapasa) such as observing the vow of ekadasi, by celibacy (brahmacaryena), by staunch faith (sraddhaya) in the import of the scriptures such as the Srimad-Bhāgavatam, and by the ninefold process of bhakti characterized by love (vidyaya).

### Text 51

**Перевод:**

**Bhakti brings about God realization only when it is causeless, and not of any other sort. It is stated in the Śrimad-Bhāgavatam (3.29.12-13): ahaituky avyavahita ya bhaktih purusottame, salokya-sārṣṭi-samipya-sarūpyaikatvam apy uta, diyamanam na grhnanti vina mat-sevanam janah, sa eva bhakti-yogakhya atyantika udahrtah, “When their devotional service unto the Supreme Person is causeless and uninterrupted} even if given salokya, sārṣṭi, samipya, sarūpya, or kaivalya, My devotees do not accept any kind of liberation if it is devoid of My service. This is said to be the highest stage of bhakti-yoga.” The words ‘ahaituki’ (causeless), ‘niskama ‘(without any personal desire), and ‘visuddha ‘(pure) are all synonyms. “When their bhakti is continuous (avyavahita) like a stream of honey,” — this is the rest of the sentence. “If devoid of loving service unto Me (mat-sevanam), Who am bliss personified and the enjoyer of bhakti.” The different kinds of liberation starting with salokya are far behind bhakti, and therefore a devotee situated in pure devotional service has no desire for them.**

**Комментарий:**

The word ‘tam ‘means ‘God realization, bringing Him under one’s control’. The quoted verse is from the Śrimad-Bhāgavatam (3.29.12-13). For the devotees situated in causeless bhakti-yoga, service to the Lord is much more exalted than any kind of liberation. This is because they are indifferent towards liberation and fully experience the supreme bliss of the Lord while engaged in pure devotional service.

### Text 52

**Перевод:**

**Someone may argue, “It is not possible that bhakti is free from personal desire, for there is desire for the happiness given by the Supreme Lord when He is pleased by someone’s devotional service. Just as a damsel desires conjugal happiness, which is given by a lover when he is pleased by her loving gestures, blandishment, and so on. That happiness is certainly different from the lover. This is so stated by the Taittiriya Upaniṣad (2.7): esa evanandayati, raso vai sah, rasam hy evayam labdhvanandi bhavati, ‘The Supreme Lord is the one Who gives bliss. He is rasa personified. One who has obtained this rasa becomes blissful’». It is not so. If the fruit of bhakti is different than the worshipable Lord, just as conjugal love given by a lover is different from him, then such bhakti is performed with personal desires. However, in the present subject matter, the worshipable Lord Himself is the fruit of bhakti, for He is bliss personified. As stated in the Taittiriya Upaniṣad (3.6): anando brahmeti vyajanat, “One should know the Supreme Lord as bliss itself.” Moreover, the lover is just a means of happiness. The Supreme Lord, however, is unarguably the fruit of bhakti, for He is bliss personified. In this way, the scriptural statements that describe that the Lord is the ultimate goal of life and that He gives Himself to His devotees would not be contradicted. The above quoted śruti should be interpreted in this way: The Supreme Lord is the one (esah) who gives bliss (anandayati) by giving Himself. One who has obtained this rasa becomes (bhavati) blissful (labdhvanandi) by this.**

**Комментарий:**

Here an objection against the said selflessness of bhakti is raised. The words ‘tat-prasaditena ‘mean ‘being pleased by devotional service’. The meaning is that the happiness given by Him, and everything that comes with it, such as the experience of divine taste, fragrance, and so on, is different from His own nature. The opponent explains the topic with an illustration’. Loving gestures ‘(bhava) are the sprout of love, and ‘blandishment ‘(hava) is the bud of love. Their characteristics are to be understood from the treatises on poetics. The words ‘rata-sukham ‘mean ‘conjugal happiness’. That is different from the lover. If the fruit of service is different from the served, then that service is said to be done with a selfish desire. The Lord is the one Who gives bliss (anandayati) after awarding one salokya and so on. The long vowel in ‘ya ‘is peculiar to the Vedas. By having obtained salokya and so on from rasa personified, one becomes blissful. The objection that has been raised is then refuted. The meaning is clear. “In this way, ” i.e., “the Supreme Lord being bliss personified. ” According to the Sridhara-kośa, the word ‘evam ‘is used in the sense of mode and similarity. In the Srimad-Bhāgavatam (4.9.17), Dhruva declares that the Supreme Lord is the ultimate goal of life personified: satyasiso hi bhagavams tava pada-padmam asis tathanubhajatah puruṣartha-mUrteh, apy evam arya bhagavan paripati dinan vasreva vatsakam anugraha-kataro’sman, “O Supreme Lord, Whose benedictions always prove true! You Yourself are the ultimate goal of life personified, thus Your lotus feet are the real benediction for one who worships You. Yet, being eager to give Your mercy, You protect us, afflicted souls, as a cow protects her calf, О worshipable Lord’. ‘How the Lord gives Himself to His devotees is seen in the words of King Nimi in the Srimad-Bhāgavatam (11.2.31): dadaty atmanam apy ajah, “The unborn Supreme Lord gives even Himself to a surrendered devotee. ” Other similar statements are also found.

### Text 53

**Перевод:**

**Someone may argue, “It may be so. Yet the happiness of the Supreme Lord upon being propitiated by His devotees is a manifestation of His svarūpa, just as the waves are a manifestation of the water, as you have commented in the Ahi-kundala Adhikaraṇa of the Brahma-sūtra (3.2.31). It is also said: pritih svayam pritim agad gayasya (Śrimad-Bhāgavatam, 5.15.13), ‘The Supreme Lord, happiness personified, became the happiness of King Gaya’. sakhyuh priyasya viprarser anga-sangatinirvrtah, prito vyamuncad ab-binduri netrabhyam puskareksanah (Śrimad-Bhāgavatam, 10.80.19), ‘Being happy upon seeing His dear brāhmaṇa friend and exceedingly delighted by embracing his body, the lotus eyed Lord Kṛṣṇashed tears’. On account of the happiness experienced by the Lord, His devotee’s happiness also becomes manifest, hence it is understood that bhakti is performed with a selfish desire. So, your argument is like the maxim of the morning in the toll station.”  This idea is foolish, for pure devotees never think, ‘May I become happy by making the Lord happy/ Otherwise, the scriptural statements that call their devotion causeless and so on would be contradicted. On the other hand, the thought, ‘His happiness itself is my happiness, ‘does not hinder the selfless nature of bhakti but rather corroborates it.**

**Комментарий:**

Here again another objection is raised. The verse ‘pritih svayam, etc’. is from the Srimad-Bhāgavatam (5.15.13) and was spoken by Śukadeva. The Supreme Lord, happiness personified (pritih), became the happiness (pritim agat) of His devotee, King Gaya. The verse starting with the words ’sakhyuh priyasya ‘is from the Srimad-Bhāgavatam (10.80.19) and was also spoken by Śukadeva. The ‘brāhmaṇa friend ‘is Sudama. By embracing his body (anga-sangena), the Lord became exceedingly happy (atinirvrtah). Upon seeing him, He became happy, His heart was content. The idea here is this — “On account of the happiness experienced by the Lord when He is very delighted, His devotee’s happiness also becomes manifest, hence bhakti is self-motivated, for devotional service to Him is performed with a desire for this happiness.” Calling this idea foolish, the author refutes it. The meaning is clear. “My happiness ” means the devotee’s happiness, which is characterized by the manifestation of the devotee’s svarūpa. “Otherwise” means “if what was said is not accepted, ” i.e., if we accept that on account of the happiness experienced by the Lord when He is very delighted by being propitiated, His devotee’s happiness also becomes manifest, which is characterized by the manifestation of one’s svarūpa as a devotee, then the scriptural statements that describe devotional service as selfless would be contradicted. By this same argument, in order to remove the idea that bhakti is motivated by selfish desire if certain thoughts appear in the devotee’s mind, such as, ‘On account of the happiness experienced by the Lord by being fully pleased with my service, may I feel exceeding happiness, ‘it is possible to explain that the devotee’s desire for happiness is due to the manifestation of the devotee’s svarūpa, which arises when the Lord experiences happiness by the devotee’s service. This is the idea propounded by the respondent. “When it is said that the devotee propitiates the Lord having the desire that, ‘May I become very happy by seeing the Supreme Lord happy upon seeing me happy, ‘here again there is personal desire, ” — It should be known that this idea of the objector has also been refuted by the above argument.

### Text 54

**Перевод:**

**This bhakti appears in this world like the Ganga, proceeding from the Lord‘s eternal associates and also spreading among the contemporary devotees. Therefore, it should be obtained by the mercy of His devotees, as discussed in the Śrimad-Bhāgavatam and other scriptures. In this way, it has been proved that pure devotional service is selfless, for it is the bliss of the worshipable Lord, and that He, the object of such bhakti, is the ultimate goal of life.**

**Комментарий:**

Here the author says that this path of bhakti is not modern but beginningless. Bhakti comes in this world like the flow of the Ganga through the disciplic succession that starts with the Lord‘s eternal associates situated in His eternal abode. They themselves also descend with the Lord into this world in different forms during the time of His descent.

### Text 55

**Перевод:**

**Someone may argue, “It is not correct to say that the Supreme Lord is bliss personified, for since He has desire and attachment for Sita and others, He is eager for sensual happiness. On account of such eagerness, sensual happiness is produced by indulging in the sense objects such as sound, touch, and so on. Such happiness in this life and the next is what the Vedic scholars consider bliss, and it is designated by the word ‘heaven’. Desiring to obtain it, people engage in work, transgressing all bounds. If the Supreme Lord is happiness personified, He should not hanker for them. Everyone should desire to attain Him, but this is not the case, because one cannot obtain sensual pleasure in this way, and because it is not worth it. Some fake renunciants who are lame, blind, deaf, lazy, or old, cheat ignorant people with meaningless words such as, ‘We are happy’. Therefore, by performing auspicious Vedic rituals, one obtains sense objects, and by indulging in them, one obtains happiness. The disappearance of distress naturally follows that, just as darkness is removed by the light of a lamp.”**

**Комментарий:**

It has been established that devotional service whose aim is the Supreme Lord is selfless because it is a form of His bliss. In this regard, those dedicated to Vedic rituals raise a doubt here. The word ‘vyāsanam ‘means ‘attachment’. By the word ‘adi ‘(etc.) Satyabhama and others should also be included. His attachment for Sita and Satyabhama is respectively seen in the Ramayana and Hari-vamsa. According to some Vedic scholars, such as Kumarila Bhatta, the word ’svarga‘ (heaven) means a kind of happiness, and not a specific planet. However, it should be understood that this is not the view of Jaimini, just as pure monism is not the view of Vyāsadeva. “Desiring to obtain it,” i.e., sensual pleasure. “All bounds” means the Vedic and worldly bounds. “He should not hanker for them, ” i.e., He should not have desire and attachment for Sita and other women.

“Everyone should desire to attain Him, ” i.e., the Supreme Lord. “But this is not the case, ” i.e., “we do not see this happen. We rather see that everyone desires sense enjoyment. ” Then are the scriptures that propound knowledge and detachment useless ? If someone asks this question, they say, “Yes, because one cannot obtain sensual pleasure in this way. ” The words ‘rikta-bhasaya ‘mean ‘with meaningless words’. This refers to the scriptures that corroborate knowledge and detachment.

### Text 56

**Перевод:**

**If someone says so, we reply that being ignorant of pure transcendental bliss and addicted to sense enjoyment, you are speaking foolishly. There is certainly higher happiness beyond sense enjoyment. As it is heard from the scriptures: sukham aham asvapsam na kincid avedisam, “I slept happily and was not aware of anything.” Here, it is acknowledged that during deep sleep, there is indeed happiness without any sense object, and this is commonly known to everyone. Being proved in this way that the atomic living entity has causeless happiness, it is proved that the all-pervasive Supreme Lord has abundant causeless happiness. It is similarly stated by the śruti: yo vai bhuma tat sukham (Chāndogya Upaniṣad\> 7.23.1), “Bhuma, the Supreme Lord, is happiness itself.” anando brahmeti vyajanat (Taittiriya Upaniṣad, 3.6.1), “Bhrgu realised that the Supreme Lord is bliss.” Moreover, everyone has experience that sensual pleasure appears when the senses perceive the sense objects and disappears when there is no perception. However, the happiness in the form of the Supreme Lord becomes manifest when sense enjoyment is given up and disappears when sense enjoyment is taken up. As corroborated by the Kaṭha Upaniṣad (1.2.24): navirato duscaritan nasanto nasamahitah, nasanta-manaso vapi prajnanenainam apnuyat, “One who does not refrain from forbidden activities, whose external senses are not controlled, who is not concentrated, or whose internal senses are not controlled, cannot attain the Supreme Soul by knowledge.” The smṛti also confirms: visayavista-cittanam visnv-avesah suduratah, varuni-dig-gatam vastu vrājann aindrim kirn apnuyat, “Absorption in Lord Viṣṇu is far away for those whose minds are absorbed in sense enjoyment. Can someone going towards the eastern side find something that was lost on the western side ?”**

**Комментарий:**

Here the author rebuts the above claim. The happiness experienced in the stage of deep sleep is certainly not produced by the enjoyment of sense objects such as sound, touch, and so forth. The word ‘tadrsa ‘means causeless’. According to the Sridhara-kośa, the expression ‘kim ca‘ is used in the sense of introduction and entirety. Its function is to either complement or introduce another refutation. The word ‘vaisayikam ‘means ‘produced by the enjoyment of sense objects’. The text starting with the words ‘naviratah ‘is from the Kaṭha Upaniṣad (1.2.24). “One who does not refrain (anivrttah) from forbidden activities (duscarat), whose external senses are not controlled (asantah), who is not concentrated (asamahitah), or whose internal senses are not controlled (asanta-manasah), cannot attain the Supreme Soul (enam) by knowledge (prajnanena).” In other words, his knowledge does not arise. The words ‘varuni-dig-gatam ‘mean ‘lost in the western side’. The word ‘aindrim ‘means ‘towards the eastern side’.

### Text 57

**Перевод:**

**According to the śruti, happiness derived from sense objects is a reflection of such supreme transcendental bliss. As stated in the Bṛhad-āraṇyaka Upaniṣad (43.32): etasyaivanandasyanyani bhutani matram upajivanti, “All other living beings subsist on a small fraction of the Supreme Soul’s bliss.” Therefore, it is proved that the Supreme Lord is the cause of all kinds of happiness. Yet sense enjoyment should be neglected. In the Śrimad-Bhāgavatam (11.8.1), the Lord says: sukham aindriyakam rājan svarge naraka eva ca, dehinam yad yatha duhkham tasman neccheta tad-budhah, “O king, since embodied beings certainly derive happiness from the sensory organs both in heaven and hell, one who knows this should not desire sense enjoyment, just as one does not desire suffering.” Transgressing all bounds, people work hard for the sake of enjoyment, and this tendency is understandable, since they have experienced that. On the other hand, because they have not experienced the pure transcendental bliss of the Supreme Lord, they have no tendency to transgress all bounds for the sake of attaining Him. Yet if somehow they experience that transcendental bliss, then after giving up everything, that tendency becomes visible, as in the case of devotees such as Śukadeva, Ambarisa, and some of the damsels of Vraja.**

**Комментарий:**

Here the author refutes the Mimāṁsā philosophers who say that by performing Vedic rituals, such as sacrifices, one obtains sense objects, and by indulging in them one obtains happiness. The mode of goodness turns itself into happiness due to its proximity to the Supreme Lord and becomes manifest by enjoying the best sense objects among sound, form, and so on. Thus, as the Supreme Lord is its ultimate cause, sense enjoyment is also a portion of Him. “Therefore”, i.e. “since the Supreme Lord gives the happiness of liberation and the happiness of sense enjoyment, He is the ultimate cause of all kinds of happiness». As stated in the Kaṭha Upaniṣad (1.2.16): etad dhy evaksaram jnātvā yo yad icchati tasya tat, “Knowing that the syllable Oth is the Supreme Lord, whatever one wishes is obtained. ” It is also said in the Srimad-Bhāgavatam (10.81.19): svargapavargayoh pumsam rasayam bhuvi sampadam, sarvasam api siddhmam mūlath tac-caranarcanam, “The worship of the Lord s lotus feet is the original cause of all mystic perfections and everyone’s opulences in heaven, in hell, on earth, or in liberation’. ‘It is hence demonstrated that the Supreme Lord is to be worshipped by one and all. “Then, since even sense enjoyment is caused by Him, sense enjoyment should be hankered for, just as much as liberation». Expecting such a doubt, the author says that sense enjoyment must be given up. The quoted verse is from the Śrimad-Bhāgavatam (11.8.1). The word ‘aindriyakam ‘means ‘which is apprehended by the organs of sense perception’. Due to its uncertainty because of its very nature, sense enjoyment is not actually happiness. This is stated by the sage Parasara in the Viṣṇu Purāṇa (2.6.47-49): vastv ekam eva duhkhaya sukhayersyodbhavaya ca, kopaya ca yatas tasmad vastu vastv-atmakam kutah, tad eva pritaye bhutva punar duhkhaya jayate, tad eva kopaya yatah prasadaya ca jayate, tasmad duhkhatmakam nasti na ca kincit sukhatmakam, manasah parinamo ‘yam sukha-duhkhadi-lakṣaṇāh, “The same object is the cause of distress, happiness, envy, and anger, so how can an object have a definite nature ? The same object sometimes gives pleasure and sometimes gives distress; it produces anger and then welfare. Therefore, there is nothing like happiness and distress. What is considered as happiness, distress, and so on, is actually a transformation of the mind.” Someone may argue, “If the Supreme Lord is such, then everyone would be taking pleasure in Him, and not in sense enjoyment’. ‘Having no experience of the pure transcendental bliss of the Supreme Lord, people have no tendency to transgress all bounds (tam) for the sake of attaining Him (tasmai). “Some of the damsels of Vraja, ” i.e., those who were inside their homes when they went for the rasa-lila.

### Text 58

**Перевод:**

**Someone may argue, “Since the Supreme Lord possesses the highest sense objects, those devotees have a tendency towards the enjoyment produced by indulging in those sense objects». This is foolish, for those sense objects are non-different from the Supreme Lord‘s blissful nature. As stated in the Bṛhad-āraṇyaka Upaniṣad (4.4-19): neha nānāsti kincana, “Within the Supreme Lord there is no difference whatsoever.” The smṛti also corroborates: ananda-matra-kara-pada-mukhodaradih sarvatra ca svagata-bheda-vivarjitatma, “The Supreme Soul is totally free of internal differences, and His hands, feet, face, belly, and so on consist exclusively of blissfulness.” Hence, those sense objects are very agitating. It has been declared that the tendency of pure devotees, knowers of the Absolute Truth, to worship the Lord is devoid of selfish desires — “Bhakti is causeless and uninterrupted.”  Everyone admits that the tendency towards the means of happiness is motivated by desire, but the tendency towards happiness is devoid of further desire. Therefore, it is proved that the Supreme Lord is abundant bliss personified. It is understood that although He is so, being caught by bhakti, He gives the ultimate goal of life, just as wood is rubbed in order to produce fire. As for Sita, Satyabhama, and others, because they are the essence of hlādini and samvit personified, an aspect of the Lord‘s svarūpa-śakti, there is no fault at all if He has desire and attachment for them.**

**Комментарий:**

Here the Mimāṁsākas again object to Lord Hari’s form as bliss itself The words ‘visaya-visistatvat ‘mean ‘because He possesses the highest sense objects, such as touch, sound, and so on. “Those devotees,” i.e., Śukadeva, Ambarisa, the gopis of Vraja, and so forth. Calling it foolish, the author refutes the objection that has been raised. Those sense objects, such as sound, touch, and so forth, are attributes of the Supreme Lord‘s body, which is non-different from Him. “Hence, ” i.e., because those sense objects (tesam) are intrinsic to the Lord‘s svarūpa, they are very agitating. The word ‘tatra ‘means ‘in the worship of the Supreme Lord’. The author then explains how pure devotional service to the Supreme Lord is devoid of selfish desire. The tendency to indulge in multiple sense objects that pertain to a means to obtain happiness, such as a young lady, is motivated by desire because the indulgence is done for the sake of the happiness it produces. On the other hand, the engagement in enjoying happiness is devoid of further desire. One certainly does not enjoy happiness desiring any result other than happiness. Hence, its enjoyment is devoid of desire, and this is known to all. “Therefore, ” i.e., “because the Lord is said to be the object of selfless devotional service.” Someone may argue, “The all-pervading Supreme

Lord, supreme bliss personified, is present everywhere. Why is everyone not happy?” The words ‘bhakti-yantritah ‘mean ‘being caught by bhakti’. Someone may object, “If He is so, then why is He attached to Sita and others ?” The reply is that they are His svarūpa-śakti personified. Therefore, there is no fault at all in the view that He is bliss personified. The idea is that His being attached to them does not preclude His being self-satisfied.

### Text 59

**Перевод:**

**Someone may object, “It is not correct that the fully blissful Supreme Lord accepts services offered by devotees who know His transcendental attributes, for this is contradictory to their knowledge of Him being fully blissful and so on. As stated in the Śrimad-Bhāgavatam (7.9.11): naivatmanah prabhur ayam nija-labha-purno manam janad avidusah karuno vrnite, yad yaj jano bhagavate vidadhita manam tac catmane pratimukhasya yatha mukha-srih, ‘Being fully satisfied with His own opulence, the Supreme Lord does not long for personal worship from an ignorant person, but He accepts it because He is merciful. Whatever worship one performs for the Lord, that is actually for one’s own benefit, just as a decoration on the face also appears on its reflection in a mirror’». This is foolish, for its said in the scriptures: so’snute sarvan kaman saha brāhmaṇa vipascita (Taittiriya Upaniṣad, 2.1), “The liberated soul then enjoys all pleasures in the company of the expert Lord Hari.” bhoktaram yajna-tapasam sarva-loka-mahesvaram (Bhagavad-gita, 5.29), “Knowing Me as the Supreme Lord of all planets and the enjoyer of all sacrifices and austerities.” aham hi sarva-yajnānam bhokta ca prabhur eva ca (Bhagavad-gita, 9-24), “I alone am the Lord and enjoyer of all sacrifices.” patram puspam phalam toyam yo me bhaktya prayacchati, tad aham bhakty-upahrtam asnami prayatatmanah (Bhagavad-gita, 9-26), “When a selfless devotee offers Me a leaf, a flower, a fruit, or water with devotion, I accept that devotional offering from him.” yah kriyah samprayuktaḥ syur ekanta-gata-buddhibhih, tah sarvah sirasa devah pratigrhnati vai svayam (Mahā-bhārata,l2.332.50), “The Supreme Lord certainly takes upon His head all devotional activities performed by those whose intelligence is exclusively devoted to Him.” From these scriptures it is understood that the Supreme Lord is endowed with the capacity to enjoy and eagerly accepts the offerings made by His devotees, and therefore their engagement in services befitting their natures is appropriate. The Supreme enjoyer must eagerly accept the services offered by his best devotees. The quoted verse from the Śrimad-Bhāgavatam should be interpreted in conformity to this.**

**Комментарий:**

Someone raises another doubt, “Well, according to the said reasoning, devotional service may be selfless. Yet it should not be performed for the Supreme Lord, for being complete in Himself, it is not possible that He accepts it. How can the devotees who know the Supreme Lord‘s attributes, such as being complete in Himself, offer Him service ? Since He has qualities such as being fully blissful, to serve Him is contradictory to such knowledge about His qualities». The verse starting with the words ‘naivatmanah ‘is from the Śrimad-Bhāgavatam (7-9.11) and was spoken by Prahlada. “The Supreme Lord (prabhuh) does not long (vrnite) for worship (manam) from an ignorant person (avidusah), He does not long for something that is worth a farthing for Him because He is fully satisfied with His own opulence (nija-labha-pmnah). Yet He accepts that because He is merciful (karunah)’. By having worshipped Me, may this person become worshipable in the world’. — Thinking thus, He accepts worship from someone out of mercy. The reason is stated there: whatever (yadyat) worship one performs (vidadhita) for the Supreme Lord, that is for one’s own benefit (atmane). An illustration is given in this regard — just as a decoration like a tilaka is done on the face, so it appears on the reflection of the face in a mirror. It should be understood that the jiva is actually a reflection of Lord Hari, just as the rainbow is a reflection of the sun. ” Calling this view foolish, the author refutes it. The text starting with the words ’so’snute ‘is from the Taittiriya Upaniṣad (2.1). The word ’sah ‘means ‘a liberated soul who has attained the status of an associate of the Lord’. He enjoys all pleasures (kaman) like divine scents and so on in the company (saha) of Lord Hari (brāhmaṇa), who is expert (vipascita) in multiple varieties of enjoyment. Three quotations from the Bhagavad-glta яге given starting with ‘bhoktaramyajna-tapasam’. The verse starting with yah kriyah ‘is from the Narayanopakhyana (Mahā-bhārata, 12.332.50).

The word ‘ekanta ‘means exclusively devoted. The word ‘asu ‘means ‘in the śruti and the smṛti’. The word ’svabhava ‘means‘ befitting their love in either servitorship, friendship, and so on’. The word ‘tah ‘means ’services’. The word ’sadaram‘ means ‘eagerly’.

### Text 60

**Перевод:**

**Someone may object, “Thus, if the Lord is an enjoyer, sometimes He may feel distress for not getting enjoyable objects, and consequentially He cannot be the goal of life.” This is foolish, for the Chāndogya Upaniṣad (3.14.2) proves that His enjoyable objects are eternal: mano-mayah prana-sariro bha-rūpah satya-sankalpa akasatma sarva-karma sarva-kamah sarva-gandhah sarva-rasah sarvam idam abhyatto ‘vakya-nadarah, “The Supreme Lord, Who is apprehended by the pure mind, is the life and soul of His devotees, and His form is shining knowledge. His desires are always fulfilled. He is all-pervasive, performs unlimited pastimes, and is endowed with all enjoyable objects. He is all fragrance, He is all taste. He pervades and maintains everything. He cannot be fully expressed by words, and He does not bow down to anyone.” In this way, the scriptural statements that the Lord is complete in Himself are also not contradictory. Otherwise, the lack of capacity to enjoy objects would render Him incomplete.**

**Комментарий:**

Since Lord Hari has the capacity to enjoy, being attracted by their love, He must accept the enjoyable objects presented with love by His devotees. Having described this, the author now raises another objection in this regard in order to refute it. "Thus, accepting that the Lord has the capacity to enjoy, sometimes He may feel distress for not attaining enjoyable objects like taste, fragrance, and so on. It is seen that even kings feel great distress when they do not obtain something they desire to enjoy, as when they are engaged in hunting and the like’. ‘Calling this idea foolish, the author refutes it. The quoted text is from the Chāndogya Upaniṣad (3.14.2). The Supreme Lord is apprehended by the pure mind (mano-mayah), as stated in the Bṛhad-āraṇyaka Upaniṣad (4.4.19): manasaivanudrastavyam, “The Supreme Lord is to be perceived by the mind». The words ‘prana-sarirah ‘mean’ situated within the life air, ‘or according to others, ‘like the life and soul of His devotees’. His body consists of consciousness and light (bha-rupah). All His thoughts are fruitful (satya-sankalpah). He is all-pervasive and spotless (akasatma). He performs unlimited variegated pastimes (sarva-karma). He is endowed with all enjoyable objects (sarva-kamah). The śruti demonstrates this — He is all fragrance (sarva-gandhah) and all taste (sarva-rasah). He eternally pervades and maintains (abhyattah) everything that exists (sarvam idam), fragrance, taste, and so on. The word ‘abhyattah‘ is formed by the affix of the past participle in the impersonal passive mode (bhave) followed by the affix ‘ac, ‘as in the sentence ‘bhukta brāhmaṇah ‘(the brāhmaṇas have eaten). The Lord cannot be fully expressed by words (avakyah). As will be described later: kartsnyena najoypy abhidhatum isah (Srimad-Bhāgavatam, 12.4.39), “Not even Lord Brahma is able to fully describe Lord Nārāyaṇa’s pastimes.” He bows down to no one different than Him (nadarah), for He is the Lord of all. As stated in the Śvetāśvatara Upaniṣad (3.9): vrksa iva stabdho divi tisthaty ekah, “The only Lord is situated in the spiritual sky у immovable like a tree. ”

### Text 61

**Перевод:**

**Someone may argue, “According to the Chāndogya Upaniṣad (8.1.5), the Supreme Soul is free from hunger and thirst (vijighatso‘pipasah). Since it is therein denied that the Supreme Lord has hunger and thirst, He is eternally satisfied, and therefore it is not possible that He eagerly accepts those offerings». In response, we say that because the Lord has no life air (prana)’ which is a transformation of air (vayu)y the śruti merely denies that He has mundane hunger and thirst, which are by-products of the life air, but it does not deny His capacity to enjoy, for this is proved by the above quoted scriptures. Similarly, although devotees like Śukadeva and Bhisma were virile, they had no erotic feelings, for they had no such desires. In the same way, although the Lord is an enjoyer, because He has no life air (prana), which is a transformation of air (vayu), He has no hunger and thirst, which are byproducts of the life air. Therefore, although He is eternally satisfied, due to the devotees ‘desire, He has hunger and thirst. As stated in the Śrimad-Bhāgavatam (10.14.2): sveccha-mayasya na tu bhuta-mayasya ko ‘pi, “No one can understand the glories of Your inconceivable body, which is dependent on the desire of Your devotees, and not made of material elements.” Erotic desires may be lurking within a man’s heart, and they become manifest by the side-glances of a woman. Similarly, hunger and thirst become manifest in the Supreme Lord because His wishes, which always come true, are in conformity with the wishes of His devotees. It is seen in daily life that despite being satisfied, a person may enjoy something offered with affection. Therefore, whenever and whatever the devotee wishes to offer, at that time the Lord enjoys that and becomes satisfied with that much, and nothing more. As it is said in the smṛti: bhakta-ksana-ksano visnuh smṛtih seva sva-vesmani, sva-bhojyasyarpanam danarh phalam indradi-durlabham, “Lord Viṣṇu’s joy is the devotees ‘joy; service is to remember Him at one’s home; a gift is offering one’s food to Him. The result of these is rarely attained even by demigods like Indra.”**

**Комментарий:**

Here again another objection is raised against the concept that the Supreme Lord is an enjoyer. The Lord is free from hunger (vijighatsah) and free from thirst (apipasah). “Those offerings” means the articles offered in His service, such as taste, fragrance, and so on. The author then refutes the objection. The word ‘tatra ‘refers to the quoted śruti. The word ‘pauruse‘ means ‘virility’. “For they have no such desires, ” i.e., “because they had no longing for women, which results from virility». Similarly, hunger and thirst (te) do not exist in the Supreme Lord. Someone may argue, “Without hunger and thirst, how can He feel pleasure in objects of enjoyment?” The response is that when a devotee who knows the Lord‘s capacity to enjoy desires to offer Him some enjoyable things, at that moment the Lord feels hunger and thirst. The evidence given is a verse from the Śrimad-Bhāgavatam (10.14.2), spoken by Lord Brahma. The Lord is dependent (mayasya) on His devotees ‘desires (sveccha). The affix ‘māyā ‘is used in the sense of prevalence. Someone may argue, “Since the Lord is free from hunger and thirst, how can these become manifest by the devotee’s will?” The answer is that this is experienced even in loving exchanges in daily life. The quoted verse is from the Hari-pradipa.

### Text 62

**Перевод:**

**In fact, foodstuffs offered to the Lord have a taste given by His svartipa-śakti, for it it so established in the Brahma-samhita (5.56): sriyah kantah kantah parama-puruṣah kalpa-taravo drama bhumis cintamani-gana-mayi toyam amrtam, katha ganam natyam gamanam api vamsl priya-sakhi cid-anandarh jyotih param api tad asvadyam api ca, “In Goloka, the goddesses of fortune are the beloveds, and the Supreme Person is the husband; the trees are desire trees; the land is made of philosopher’s stone; the water is nectar; talking is singing; walking is dancing; the flute is a dear girlfriend; the luminaries are transcendental knowledge and bliss, and so is the foodstuff.” Also in the Hayasirsa-pancaratra: dravya-tattvam srnu brahman pravaksyami samasatah, sarva-bhoga-prada yatra padapah kalpa-padapah, gandha-mpam svada-rupam dravyam puspadikam ca yat, heyamsanam abhavac ca rasa-rupam bhavec ca tat, tvag bijam caiva heyamsam kathinamsam ca yad bhavet, sarvarh tad bhautikam viddhi na hy adbhuta-mayam hi tat, rasavad bhautikam dravyam atra syad rasa-rupakam, “Listen, О Brahma. I shall briefly describe the real nature of the objects in the spiritual world. There the trees are wish-fulfilling trees that yield all enjoyable things. Objects like flowers, fruits, and so on are made of fragrance, made of taste, and made of juice because they have no discardable portions in them. Whatever has discardable portions — skin, seed, and hard portions — is all material. You should know that spiritual objects are not so, for they are transcendental. Material objects have juice, but spiritual objects essentially consist of juice.”**

**Комментарий:**

Someone may argue, “If the Supreme Lord enjoys foodstuffs and other things offered with love by devotees who know that He has the factual capacity to enjoy them, an assumption may arise that such food has discardable portions, as we see in this world. This assumption is appropriate, for if He is eating it is possible that the foodstuffs have discardable portions. Even though a form constituted of consciousness, such as Nārāyaṇa, enjoys taste, the foodstuffs have no discardable portions. However, when human forms like Kṛṣṇaeat, there must be discardable portions, as said in the £rimad-Bhāgavatam (10.8.31): evam dharstyany usati kurute mehanadini vastau, ‘0 beautiful Yasoda, Kṛṣṇais doing mischievous activities, such as passing urine in our house. If such a doubt is raised, the author replies here, demonstrating that those enjoyable objects are transcendental. The goddesses of fortune (sriyah) are the Lord‘s beloveds (kantah), as stated in a previous verse in the Brahma-samhita (5.29): laksmi-sahasra-sata-sambhrama-sevyamanam, “Govinda is served with respect by thousands of Laksmis.” Therefore, the beloveds here are understood as the ladies ofVraja. The husband (patih) of all of them is the Supreme Person, Kṛṣṇa, performing pastimes as a cowherd. Because in the spiritual world the trees (padapah) are desire trees (kalpa-padapah), they are known to yield all desirable objects (sarva-bhoga-pradah). The same is also true for everything else there, such as the land, the water, and so on. The luminaries (jyotih) like the sun and the moon are the transcendental (param) essence itself— knowledge and bliss (cid-ānandam). Although the foodstuffs (asvadyam) of the Lord and His beloveds have flavour, fragrance, and so on, by dint of the power of His svarūpa-śakti, these are actually all knowledge and bliss. The meaning of the verse from the Hayaslrsa-pancaratra is clear. That is similarly stated in the Brhad-Viṣṇu Purāṇa: nityavataro bhagavan nitya-mūrtir jagat-patih, nitya-гйро nitya-gandho nityaiśvarya-sukhanubhūh, “The Supreme Lord, Whose avataras are eternal and Whose forms are eternal, is the Lord of the universe. His beauty is eternal, His fragrance is eternal, and He experiences eternal opulence and happiness." Because they are indeed eternal, those foodstuffs become absorbed within the bodies of the enjoyers as something gratifying, and not as materially digested objects. The flowers and clothes that are thrown away once again return to their original reservoir and do not become decomposed or vanish, for such characteristics are seen in the Parijata flower: pūrne samvatsare devi puspam etat tavantikat, nirvartsyate taru-varam samayena prayasyati (Hari-vamsa, 2.65.31), “Dear lady, upon being in your possession for a whole year, this flower will have consequentially grown and will turn into a Parijata tree. ” It is to be seen in the narration therein thatNārada tells Rukmint that the Parijata flower fulfils all desires, and that after one year, it turns itself into a Parijata tree.

### Text 63

**Перевод:**

**The rasas ‘form as eatables is effected because they are transcendental, just as a body of eternity, knowledge, and bliss is effected. Those foodstuffs with colour, fragrance and so on are of such transcendental nature and made of rasa, and thus it is not possible that they have discardable portions. Nor is there any doubt regarding those who serve the Supreme Brahman in human form, for their eatables being of the same nature, such a doubt does not arise. This is so accepted by those who know the truth: sruyate parimale mala-sabdo mekhaladisu khalady-abhiyogah, candanadi-rasa eva hi panko nivi-kesa-rāsanadisu bandhah, “It is heard that in the capital of Nanda Maharaja, the word ‘mala ‘(filth) is employed only in the compound word ‘parimala ‘(scent), words such as ‘khala ‘(wicked) only in the sense of ‘girdle ‘(mekhala), etc., the word ‘panka ‘(mud, sin) only in the sense of cosmetic mixtures of sandal and so on, and the word ‘bandhah ‘(imprisonment) only in the sense of a garment knot, hair, rope, and the like.”**

**Комментарий:**

Someone may argue, “If the foodstuffs are a form of rasa, then they would not have such a nature as to be chewed, sucked, and so on». The response is given here. The rasas appear in the form of eatables such as cooked rice, condiments, and the like. Those eatables are of such transcendental nature and made of rasa. It is not possible that upon being eaten, those foodstuffs with colour, touch and so on would have any discardable portion. There is also no doubt regarding those like Nanda Maharaja, who serve a human form like Kṛṣṇa. The meaning is that one should not suppose that, “Ifforms like Kṛṣṇaare often eating, how can we believe that there are no discardable portions as we see in ordinary life ?” According to the Visva-kośa (Ka, 47)\> the word’sanka ‘is used in the sense offear and supposition. The Lord‘s associates like Nanda Maharaja also have bodies constituted of consciousness and bliss, and when they eat those foodstuffs made of rasa, there is no discardable portion, hence such a doubt does not arise. Therefore, such a doubt is impossible in the case of forms like Kr?na. This is the meaning. The experience of the learned is evidence of this. "The knowers of truth ” specifically means Kavi Karnapūra, the great authority. It should be understood that the quoted verse is a description of Nanda Maharaja’s capital. The meaning is clear. Forms like Kṛṣṇahave a body constituted of consciousness, which is worshipped by all demigods and is accepted by all If even among the demigods foodstuffs have no discardable portions, then how can the Supreme Lord‘s have? It is thus ascertained that this assumption is due to either ignorance of His nature or enmity. In this way, since there are no discardable portions at all, the svarūpa of the forms of the Supreme Lord is pure, as proved by the Viṣṇu Purāṇa (1.22.51): samasta-heya-rahitam visnv-akhyam parāmaṁ padamy “ The ultimate object, named Lord Viṣṇu Himself is devoid of all inferior qualities. ”

### Text 64

**Перевод:**

**It is said in the Śrimad-Bhāgavatam (IO.8.31): evam dharstyany usati kurute mehanadlni vastau, steyopayair viracita-krtih supratiko yathaste. The meaning here is that He performs mischievous activities (dharstyani) like passing water (mehanadini) and throwing dust inside people’s houses. Or else, the word ‘ama ‘may be interpreted in the sense of the indeclinable’saha ‘(with), as it is seen in the etymological definition of the word ‘amavasya ‘(new moon day): “when the sun and the moon are in conjunction (vasatoysyam) with (ama) each other.” According to the Amara-kośa (3-3-250) also, the word camay is used in the sense of ‘together with ‘and ‘proximity’. “He performs mischievous activities with (ama) various means for stealing (steyopayaih)” Of which sort? “Like making sounds with His throat (nadini) and so on.”**

**Комментарий:**

Someone may argue, “It is seen that the above quoted sentence demonstrates that there are discardable portions, so what is meant by that ? ” The author replies herein. “The gopxs were complaining in this way, ‘0 beautiful (usati) Queen Yasoda, your son Kṛṣṇais doing (kurute) mischievous activities (dharstyani) in our house (vastau) in such and such a way.Such as what ? “Such as dropping water (mehanadini), throwing dust, and the like. ”Making the discharge of water the semantic cause, the word ‘megha ‘(cloud) is derived from the verbal root fmih ‘(to pour water), and so is the word cmehana’.  Nor is there fault in the unusual employment of this word in this sense, for the etymology in Vedānta, which is like an ocean of nectar, is indifferent to the conventions in literature, which is like well water. What is He like? “By various means for stealing, such as using chairs, mortars, and so on, He has revealed His cleverness (viracita-krtih) in thievery. Now He is sitting in front of you as if He were a good boy (supratikah).n The author then presents yet another interpretation. The word ‘ama ‘is also understood in the sense of ‘together with ‘according to the gloss on Panini’s sūtra 3-1-122: amavasyad-anyatarasyam, “The word ‘amavasya ‘is optionally spelt ‘amavasya’». After having split the words of the verse in this way, the author gives their prose order. The word ‘nadini ‘means ‘making sounds like a cuckoo or peacock to call His friends’. This interpretation is also seen in Jiva Gosvāmi’s Brhat-krama-sandarbha on this verse.

### Text 65

**Перевод:**

**Similarly, the statement about discardable portions in connection with Lord Rsabha is a explanation that is believed only by ignorant people. The Śrimad-Bhāgavatam (3.30.5) is particularly addressing those who believe so when it speaks about people bewildered by the Lord‘s illusory potency (deva-maya-vimohitah). Therein it is stated by His own mouth that His body is a form of the Supreme Absolute Truth: idam sariram mama durvibhavyam tattvam hi me hrdayam yatra dharmah, prsthe krto me yad adharma arad ato hi mam rsabham prahur aryah (Śrimad-Bhāgavatam, 5-5.19), “This body of Mine is itself the inconceivable Supreme Soul, and My heart is religion itself. I have driven irreligiosity behind, far away from Me, and therefore the virtuous call me ‘Rsabha, ‘the best.” Since He is so, discardable portions are not possible in His case. According to the scriptures, they are also not possible in the case of the perfect souls engaged in His service: jagaj-jana-mala-dhvamsi-sravana-smṛti-kirtanah, mala-mutradi-rahitah punya-sloka iti smrtah, “The glorious devotees engaged in the Supreme Lord‘s service by chanting, hearing, and remembering, which destroys everyone’s impurities, have no impure substance like faeces and urine.”**

**Комментарий:**

Someone may argue, “It is not fair to give a different interpretation to the above quoted verse by splitting the words with your literary skill, for that same meaning is acknowledged in the narrations about Rsabha’s activities, which proves that He is ajiva. ” Here, the author despises such a belief as erroneous and then quotes a verse that describes Rsabha’s body as transcendental. “That transcendental element (tattvam), the Supreme Soul, inconceivable even to the mind (durvibhavyam), is My body itself. Because I abide by the religious principles described in the Vedas and drive away everything opposed to them, I am named Rsabha." This is the meaning. According to the Sridhara-kośa, the word ‘rsabha ‘is used in the sense of best and bull. The word ‘asau ‘refers to the discardable portions. The verse starting with ‘tat-sevinam ‘is quoted to illustrate that, not to speak of the Lord, even His devotees ‘foodstuffs leave no discardable portions. “Theglorious devotees”are those like Śukadeva.

### Text 66

**Перевод:**

**Therefore, this should be interpreted as follows: The Supreme Lord gives results according to the activities of the living entities. He declares: nayam deho deha-bhajam nr-loke kastan kaman arhate vid-bhujam ye, tapo divyam putraka yena sattvam suddhyed yasmad brahma-saukhyam tv anantam (Śrimad-Bhāgavatam, 5-5-1), “Among so many embodied forms, one who has obtained this body within human society does not deserve the troubles of sense enjoyment meant for animals like hogs. My dear sons, one should practice penance so that one’s existence may become purified, from which ensues unending spiritual happiness.” This is addressed to the virtuous who have faith in the religious principles taught by the Lord Himself and who have understood that He is the Supreme Absolute Truth. The Lord awards them all desirable objects. As for the fools who have fallen down from the religious principles taught by Him and believe that He leaves discardable portions, He leads them to a hell befitting their conduct. “Let those who are averse to My religious principles believe I am so, and let them behave in such a way,” — This being the Lord‘s desire, His illusory energy makes such a display.**

**Комментарий:**

“Therefore," i.e., “because Lord Rsabha has a transcendental body." “As for the fools” refers to the Jains and others. “Befitting their conduct,” i.e., their unclean habits. The words ‘tat-kame sati ‘mean ‘this being the Supreme Lord‘s desire’.

### Text 67

**Перевод:**

**It should be considered that it is only by the Lord‘s desire to make others adopt such a conduct that He made even something rejectable appear acceptable. Otherwise, people would not have firm faith in Him as a preceptor of His own religious principles. The irreligiosity that the Supreme Lord has driven backwards is the very same adopted by the Jains — disregard for Vedic sacrifices, brāhmaṇas, and God, and lack of Vedic principles of cleanliness and so on, as clarified by the sage Śukadeva in the Śrimad-Bhāgavatam (5.6.9): yasya kilanucaritam upakarnya konka-venka-kutakanam rajarhan-namopasiksya kalav adharme..., “In the Age of Kali, having heard and learnt about the activities of Rsabhadeva, the King of the Konkas, Venkas, and Kutakas, named Arhat, being perplexed by the increase of sinful activities, will propagate a heretical and evil way of religion.” Therefore, the apparent discardable portions are displayed by the illusory energy to the heretical followers of Arhat. Nor is the highest authority, the Supreme Lord, unjust, for this is not possible, since He merely rewards everyone according to their actions.**

**Комментарий:**

“Although it was very fragrant, Rsabha did pass stool and behaved like an animal so thatArhat and others would have this impression about His behaviour. What is the purpose of this?” In case such a question is raised, the author responds here. “Foodstuff in the form of ingredients made of earth and so on turns into faeces and other excretions. There is no difference between both. Our great authority, Rsabha, became impartial to both. Therefore, although rejectable, His stool appeared acceptable and spread a fragrance for many miles. This means that it appeared fragrant, ” — this is the meaning. “To make others adopt such a conduct, ” i.e., “to make others adopt a conduct which they mistake as being performed by Rsabha, characterized by the principle of equal vision towards both foodstuff and excrement, which is the cause of falling into hell». “Otherwise, ” i.e., “if Rsabha lacked impartially regarding food and excrement by giving up the sense of rejectable and acceptable, and if His faeces lacked such a fragrance, and if thus there were no such a display». The word ‘astha ‘means ‘firm faith’. The word ‘taih ‘refers to the Jains and others. The word ‘munih ‘refers to Śukadeva. The quoted verse is from the Srimad-Bhāgavatam (5.6.9). The word ‘yasya ‘means ‘of Rsabha’. The word ‘upakarnya ‘means ‘having only heard, without actually seeing Him’. According to the Mitaksara, characteristics foreign to those prescribed in the śruti are called ‘pasanda ‘and those who bear them are called ‘pasandis ‘(heretics).

### Text 68

**Перевод:**

**Being so, when the Śrimad-Bhāgavatam (5-6.8) states that, “The forest fire burnt that forest together with Rsabha,” here ‘together ‘is to be taken along with the subject, and not with the object — “The forest fire burnt the forest, and Rsabha burnt the ignorance of the inhabitants of that forest.” As for the statement in the Śrimad-Bhāgavatam (5.5.28) that after instructing His sons, Lord Viṣṇu Himself as Rsabhadeva performed the religious principles pertaining to the highest renunciants, that was merely done so that others may follow His example, as it is understood from the context there. His mode of giving up the body is also merely apparent, so that those who hear about it may give up bodily identification. Thus, it was well said: the statement about discardable portions in connection with the Supreme Lord is an explanation believed by ignorant people. Therefore, only knowledge about the Supreme Lord accompanied by self-knowledge is a means to attain the ultimate happiness and disappearance of distress, and not any other expedient.**

**Комментарий:**

**Thus ends the first chapter of the introduction to the Govinda-bhāṣya, named Siddhānta-ratnaṁ, whose topic is the ascertainment of the means to attain the ultimate goal of life.**

Here the author refutes another mistake. “Being so, ” i.e., “having being concluded that Rsabha is the Supreme Absolute Truth». The word’saha ‘means that together with fire He was an agent of burning, and not an object of burning. The idea is that it should not be accepted that he burnt together with the forest, the object of burning, for the Supreme Absolute Truth cannot burn. Someone may argue, “What did Rsabha burn there?” He burnt the ignorance of the inhabitants of the forest. The words ‘tad-апикагапа-гйратy mean for the sake of instructing the people, ‘as it is understood from the context there. The words’samparaya-vidhiW mean ‘the mode of giving up the body’. Such great personalities give up the body in such apparent way so that those who eagerly hear such narrations may give up bodily identification. This is the meaning. With the last sentence, the author concludes the present topic. One should examine this topic on Lord Rsabha, which is found in the fifth book of the Srimad-Bhāgavatam.

Thus, the first chapter, entitled ‘Pancajanya/has been explained in this commentary on the introduction to the Govinda-bhāṣya.

## Second Chapter — Kaumodaki

### Text 1

**Перевод:**

**The Lord‘s nature as the Supreme has two aspects — aiśvarya (divine power) and mādhurya (sweetness). Therefore, jnāna, which has aiśvarya as its object, and bhakti, which has mādhurya as its object, are manifest as twofold. This will be shown in this chapter. Aisvarya is a manifestation of the Lord‘s supreme powers, irrespective of His performing pastimes in a human form. For instance, after showing His divine power to Vāsudeva and Devaki, He told thenv etad vam darsitam rūpam prag-janma-smaranaya me, nanyatha mad-bhavam jnānam martya-lūigena jayate (Śrimad-Bhāgavatam, 10.3-44), “I have shown you this four-handed form of Mine in order to remind you of your previous birth, otherwise you would not know that I have taken birth with the characteristics of a human child.” Also, when speaking to Arjuna, the Lord says: pasya me yogam aisvaram (Bhagavad-gita, 11.8), “Behold My mystic power.” As for mādhurya, whether His supreme power is manifest or not, that does not transgress His performing pastimes in a human form. For example, when He took away Putana’s life airs while acting like a human child sucking her breast. As it was also proved when**

**Комментарий:**

He made a very hard wooden cart fall to pieces by hitting it with His feet, which are soft like a leaf. Also, when He was disturbed with fear of Mother Yasoda, even though He could not be bound by a very long rope. Also, when He performed pastimes taking the calves to graze, although omniscient, to the bewilderment of Lord Brahma and other demigods. Although He has supreme divine powers, by not displaying them, He performed pastimes such as stealing yogurt and milk, and longing for the damsels of Vraja.

Jnāna and bhakti have been said to be direct means to attain the Supreme Lord, the former having aiśvarya as its object, and the latter having mādhurya as its object. Here, the author starts to explain this twofold division. Because it crushes to pieces many stone-like doubts, this chapter has been named ‘Kaumodaki ‘by the saintly author. The verse ‘etad vam ‘is from the Śrimad-Bhāgavatam (10.3.44). The word ‘etat ‘means ‘this divine four-handed form’. The words ‘mad-bhavam ‘mean ‘about Me, as previously seen’. The words ‘martya-lingena ‘mean ‘in human form’. After the word ‘iti ‘(thus) the rest of the sentence is ‘it was spoken’. The same applies to the quotation from the Bhagavad-gita.

### Text 2

**Перевод:**

**Knowledge about aiśvarya is a kind of attribute that produces unsteadiness in one’s natural mood upon deliberating on the Lord‘s supreme powers. This unsteadiness is accompanied by reverence and respect, which make one’s heart palpitate.
