Not knowing Your glories, out of affection or carelessness I have also disrespected You just for fun while we ate, sat, lay down, or walked together, either alone or in front of others. О Acyuta, I beg You to forgive me for all this, О unlimited Lord.” In these two cases, there was unsteadiness in the devotees ‘natural mood — parental love and friendship respectively. As for knowledge about mādhurya, it is a kind of attribute that makes one’s natural mood very steady, even upon deliberating on the Lord‘s supreme powers, for not even the slightest feeling of respect arises that could make one’s heart palpitate. For instance: trayya copanisadbhis ca samkhya-yogais ca satvataih, upagiyamana-mahatmyam harim samanyatatmajam (Śrimad-Bhāgavatam, 10.8.45), “Although Lord Hari’s glories are sung by the Vedas, the Upaniṣads, the Saihkhya and Yoga treatises, and the**

Vaiṣṇava-tantrasy Mother Yasoda considered Him her own son.” vandinas tam upadeva-gana ye gita-vadya-balibhih parivavruh, vandyamana-caranah pathi vrddhaih (Śrimad-Bhāgavatam, 10.35-21-22)у “Praising Him with offerings of instrumental music and songs, the hosts of Gandharvas, Apsaras, and other celestial beings have surrounded your son, Whose feet are worshipped along the path by eminent demigods.” In the case of Mother Yasoda, although there was deliberation on the Lord‘s supreme powers, there was no unsteadiness in her natural mood, namely parental love.

Having explained the object of knowledge, the author now explains knowledge. By knowing their lover’s divine powers, the gopis3 passionate love did not become unsteady, but it rather remained strong. This is the meaning.

### Text 3

**Перевод:**

**For those situated in the mādhurya mood, knowledge about aiśvarya is taken as secondary, just as the stream of the River Sarasvati within the Triveni, because for them there is no reverence and respect, which makes the heart palpitate. Nor does knowledge about aiśvarya restrict mādhurya, but it rather makes it stronger, for it is possible that one may feel delighted that ‘my son —or my friend, or my lover —is the Supreme Lord’. This should be seen in the same way as when in worldly life someone’s son, friend, or lover is the emperor, which increases parental affection, friendship, or passionate love. This feeling arises when there is surprise, separation, or adversity, just as the stream of the Sarasvati appears during a sacrifice performed during the change of the moon.2**

**Комментарий:**

The Panca-vimia Brahmana (25-10) describes three types of sacrifices to be performed on the bank of the Sarasvati. It seems that the Sarasvati may occasionally become manifest during such sacrifices, yet it remains secondary in comparison with the Ganga and Yamuna. Similarly, those situated in the mood of mādhurya may occasionally experience feelings of aiśvarya, but these are always secondary.

Among the devotees of the Lord, those who live in Vraja are the most exalted. Some ignorant individuals think that the inhabitants of Vraja because of His greatness, just as burning naturally happens due to contact with fire. Here, the author refutes this view. “Those situated in the mādhurya mood” are the inhabitants of Vraja. If someone argues that if it is accepted that they have knowledge about aiśvarya, then their mādhurya mood is ruled out, the author says, “Nor does, etc». The word ctaty refers to the knowledge about aiśvarya. Someone may argue, “Where, is their knowledge of aiśvarya seen?” The answer is — in surprise, separation, or adversity. In surprise, such as when the Queen of Vraja saw the universe within Kṛṣṇa’s mouth, and when the inhabitants of Vraja realised that He is the Supreme Lord upon hearing that He was worshipped by Varuna. In separation, such as stated in their own words expressing His powers: manaso vrttayo nah syuh kṛṣṇa-padambujasrayah, vaco ‘bhidhayinir namnam kayas tat-prahvanadisu (Śrimad-Bhāgavatam, 10.47.66), “May our thoughts be placed on Kṛṣṇa’s lotus feet; may our speech express His names; and may our bodies be engaged in bowing down and so on». The same is also seen in the words of Srxmatl Radharani and the other gopis: mrgayur iva kapindram vivyadhe (Śrimad-Bhāgavatam, 10.47.17), “He shot the King of the monkeys like a hunter, etc». In adversity, such as when the inhabitants of Vraja were in the middle of a calamity caused by Indra or others and all of them spoke famous prayers expressing Kṛṣṇas powers. Such instances are to be seen in the tenth book of the Śrimad-Bhāgavatam.

attained liberation despite not knowing the truth about the Supreme Lord

### Text 4

**Перевод:**

**Just as aiśvarya is an attribute of the Supreme, so is mādhurya. Therefore, knowledge that has mādhurya as its object is certainly knowledge about the Supreme. It is proved in the śruti and the smṛti that just as having thousands of heads is an attribute of the Supreme, so is having a two handed form and so on: sat-pundarika-nayanam meghabham vaidyutambaram, dvi-bhujam mauna-mudradhyam vana-malinam isvaram (Gopāla-tāpani Upaniṣad, 1.9), “One should meditate on Lord Govinda, the Supreme Lord, Who has beautiful lotus eyes and two hands. His complexion is like that of a cloud, and His dress is effulgent like lightning. He wears a garland of forest flowers and is in a posture of meditative silence.” yador vamsam narah srutva sarva-papaih pramucyate, yatravatirnam kṛṣṇakhyam param brahma narakrti (Viṣṇu Purāṇa, 4.11.2), “One becomes free from all sins upon hearing about the Yadu dynasty, in which the Supreme Lord named Kṛṣṇahas descended in human form.” gudham param brahma manusya-lingam (Śrimad-Bhāgavatam, 7-10.48), “Kṛṣṇais the invisible Supreme Absolute Truth with human characteristics.” Thus, because they have knowledge about both aiśvarya and mādhurya, the dust of the feet of those situated in knowledge about mādhurya are praised by personalities like Lord Brahma and Uddhava.**

**Комментарий:**

Someone may argue, “The Supreme Lord‘s aiśvarya is factual, for it is intrinsic to His nature. On the other hand, mādhurya is not factual, for it is based on a human body and its activities, which are sustained by māyā for the benefit of humanity. Hence, it is said: maya-manusyasya vadasva vidvan (Srimad-Bhāgavatam, 10.1.7), ‘0 learned Śukadeva, please tell us about the Supreme Lord, Who appeared in a human form by the power of maya’. lila-manusa-vigrahah (Garga-samhita, 5-9.53/ Gopāla-sahasra-nama, 115), ‘The Supreme Lord performs pastimes in a human form’. Therefore, those situated in knowledge about mādhurya are inferior. ” In response to this, the author writes the present paragraph. The words ‘tad-visayakam ‘mean ‘related to mādhurya’. The text starting with’sat-pundarika ‘is from the Gopāla-tapanl Upaniṣad (1.9), in which Kṛṣṇa’s mādhurya is known by the statements about His human form and His activities, despite His being God. The verse starting with ‘yador vathsam ‘is from the Viṣṇu Purāṇa (4.11.2). The verse starting with ‘gūdhamparam ‘is from the Śrimad-Bhāgavatam (7.10.48). Lord Brahma speaks as follows: tad bhUri bhagyam iha janma kim apy atavyam yad gokule ‘pi katamanghri-rajo ‘bhisekam,yaj-jtvitam tu nikhilam bhagavan mukundas tv adyapi yat-pada-rajah śruti-mrgyam eva (Śrimad-Bhāgavatam, 10.14.34), “It is a great fortune to take any kind of birth in a forest in Gokula and bathe in the dust of the feet of some of its inhabitants, for whom the Supreme Lord Mukunda is the very life and soul. Even today the dust from His feet is sought after by the Vedas personified. ” Uddhava says: asam aho carana-renu-jusam aham syam vrndavane kim api gulma-latausadhinam (Srimad-Bhāgavatam, 10.47.61), “Oh, may I be one of the bushes, creepers, or plants that enjoy the dust of the gopis ‘feet in Vṛndāvana». The words ‘maya-manusyasya ‘mean that the Supreme Lord is eternally situated in a human form by dint of His svarūpa-śakti, which is known as the superior energy. As stated in the śruti: svarUpa-bhūtaya nitya-śaktya mayakhyaya yutah, ato maya-mayam visnum pravadanti sanatanam, “The Supreme Lord is endowed with His eternal energy called maya, which is His own nature. Therefore, the wise say that Lord Viṣṇu is eternally replete with potencies.” It is also stated in the lexicon Sabda-mahodadhi: tri-gunatmikatha jnānath ca tatha cic-chaktir eva ca, maya-sabdena bhanyante śabda-tattvartha-vedibhih, “Those who know the real import of terms say that the word ‘māyā ‘expresses the three modes of nature, knowledge, and the Lord‘s conscious energy. ” In the Nighantu-kośa (3.9) it is also said that the words ‘maya, ‘‘vayuna, ‘and ‘jnāna ‘are synonyms of knowledge. A lump of gold in human form is certainly not a human. Otherwise, the śruti that describes His form as eternity, knowledge, and bliss would be contradicted. The verse ‘lila-manusa-vigrahah ‘is explained in the same way.

### Text 5

**Перевод:**

**The great authority, Srila Rūpa Gosvāmi, stated: yad aisya-jnāna-sūnyatvad esam rage pradhanata (Bhakti-rasamrta-sindhu, 1.2.288), “Among the cowherd folk of Vraja there is a predominance of spontaneous love for Kṛṣṇabecause they lack the awareness that He is God.” It should be understood that this is so, not merely because of the lack of knowledge about His powers, since such knowledge is actually displayed, but because it does not cause reverence and respect, which make one’s heart palpitate.**

**Комментарий:**

Someone may argue, “If it is thus proved that the inhabitants of Vraja are the most exalted devotees because they have knowledge about both mādhurya and aiśvarya, then why did your authority, Srila Rūpa Gosvāmi, deny that they have knowledge about aiśvarya ?” The author replies herein. The words ‘taj-jnānasya ‘mean ‘knowledge about His aiśvarya’. The meaning is as follows — The inhabitants of Vraja have spontaneous loving devotion, whose characteristics have been described by Srila Rūpa Gosvāmi: iste svarasiki ragah paramavistata bhavet, tan-mayi ya bhaved bhaktih satra ragatmikodita (Bhakti-rasamrta-sindhu, 1.2.272), “Attachment (raga) is full and spontaneous absorption in a desirable object. Devotion with such attachment for the Lord is called ragatmika.” The meaning of this verse is that attachment is the full (parama) and natural (svarasiki) absorption (avistata) in an object agreeable to oneself (iste), and its cause is the loving eagerness that is prominent in a relationship such as parent, friend, or lover. Because such attachment is the cause of increasing love, the statement that it is non-different than love is to be understood in a similar way as when ghee is called longevity. Such devotion exclusively performed for the Lord (tan-mayi) is called ragatmika. The affix ‘māyā ‘is used to express the attributes under discussion. In such devotional service to the Supreme Lord, activities are free from the feeling of respect, such as those of the inhabitants of Vraja shown in the tenth book of the Śrimad-Bhāgavatam and other texts, where it is described that without any hesitation they were engaged in embracing Kṛṣṇaand so on. Respect (sambhrama) is said to be a state of trembling accompanied by reverence, which is consequent to the mental awareness that ‘this is the Supreme Lord’. Although this awareness may be present, on account of such spontaneous devotion that has enveloped the heart, it is not able to affect the devotee, it does not create a feeling of respect. It is for this reason that RUpa Gosvāmi denied that they have knowledge about aiśvarya. The intended meaning is not that they have no such knowledge at all, for in the tenth book of the Śrimad-Bhāgavatam and other scriptures it is described that they actually have knowledge about Kṛṣṇa’s powers. As for the inhabitants of Mathura and Dvārakā, on account of the predominance of knowledge about aiśvarya, since they lack such spontaneous love, that causes such trembling.

Therefore, in their case spontaneous love is secondary. This is the opinion of the commentators.

### Text 6

**Перевод:**

**The following are some instances that the inhabitants of Vraja have knowledge about Kṛṣṇa’s powers, as spoken by them: atho amusyaiva mamarbhakasya yah kascanautpattika atma-yogah (Śrimad-Bhāgavatam, 10.8.40), “Or else, this child of mine has some natural mystic powers.” sarpo mam grasate tata prapannam parimocaya (Śrimad-Bhāgavatam, 10.34.6), “Kṛṣṇa! Kr$na! This serpent is swallowing me! My dear, please save this surrendered soul.” davagnina dahyamanan prapannams tratum arhathah (Śrimad-Bhāgavatam, Ю.19.9), “ Kṛṣṇa! It behoves You to save these surrendered souls who are being burnt by the forest fire. ” mrgayur iva kaplndram vivyadhe ‘lubdha-dharma striyam akrta virupam stri-jitah kamayanam, balim api balim attvavestayad dhvanksavad yas tad alam asita-sakhyair dustyajas tat-katharthah (Śrimad-Bhāgavatam, 10.47.17)\> “He shot the king of monkeys like a hunter, but without abiding by the principles of hunting. Being Himself under the control of a woman, He disfigured another woman who had desire for Him. Grabbing the offering of Bali, He tied him, just as the crows do. Therefore, enough of friendship with this dark complexioned boy, although it is very difficult to stop talking about Him.” varadesvara ma sma chindya (Śrimad-Bhāgavatam, 10.29.33), “O Lord, giver of benedictions! Please do not cut down our hope for You.” However, knowledge about mādhurya and knowledge about aiśvarya arise one after the other, because their simultaneity is not possible and in this way they increase the rasas. The non-appearance of a particular knowledge does not mean it is non-existent. As stated in the Brahma-sūtra (2.3.29): pumstvadivat tv asya sato ‘bhivyakti-yogat, “The soul’s consciousness exists even in deep sleep, like virility in childhood, for later it becomes manifest in the waking state.” The Supreme Lord, Who has continuous expanded knowledge similar to the sun light, is also seen as perplexed, as befitting His human pastimes: tad-abhijno ‘pi bhagavan sarvatma sarva-darsanah, prasrayavanato ‘prcchad vrddhan nanda-purogaman (Śrimad-Bhāgavatam, 10.24.2), “Although the Supreme Lord, the Supreme Soul within everyone, sees everything and knows it all, nevertheless, bending down in respect, He inquired from Nanda Maharaja and other elders. ” tato vatsan adrstvaitya puline ‘pi ca vatsapan (Śrimad-Bhāgavatam, 10.13.16), “Then, not finding the calves, Kṛṣṇawent to the bank of the Yamuna but also did not find the cowherd boys.” This perplexity should be considered of the nature of the bliss of the Lord‘s pastimes, which is the essence of His conscious energy (cit) and is non-different from His svarūpa, for other sorts of qualities do not exist in Him. As stated in the Viṣṇu-tantra: astadāsa-mahadosai rahita bhagavat-tanuh, sarvaiśvarya-mayi satya-vijnānānanda-rupini, “The Supreme Lord‘s body is devoid of the eighteen great defects. It consists of truth, knowledge, and bliss, full of all powers.” The great defects are mentioned in the Viṣṇu-yamale: mohas tandra bhramo ruksa-rasata**

**Комментарий:**

kama ulbanah, lolata mada-matsarye himsa kheda-parisramau, asatyam krodha akanksa asanka visva-vibhramah, visamatvam parapeksa dosa astadasoditah, “Confusion, sleepiness, error, attachment without love, intense lust, restlessness, intoxication, envy, violence, distress, weariness of the senses, untruthfulness, anger, longing, doubt, absorption in worldly affairs, inequality, dependency on others — these are the eighteen great defects.”

Here the author gives evidence that the inhabitants ofVraja have knowledge about the Lord‘s powers. The pastime of Lord Hari eating dirt is described in the eighth chapter of the tenth book of the Śrimad-Bhāgavatam. Therein, starting from verse 32, Kṛṣṇa’s friends inform His mother that He had eaten dirt, and caught by the hand, He is reproached by her. Being afraid of His mother, claiming that His friends’statement is not true, He opens His mouth. Thinking, “May My mother not punish Me," in order to astonish her, Kṛṣṇadisplays the universe within His mouth by dint of His a,svarya-śakti, which is always in conformity with His will. Seeing the universe, her husband, Her son, herself, and the whole Vraja within His mouth, which were actually situated outside, His mother was filled with doubt, “How is this possible?" In half a verse, she begins: kith svapna etad uta deva-māyā kith va madtyo bata buddhi-moha (Srimad-Bhāgavatam, 10.8.40), “Oh! Is this a dream? Is this God’s illusory energy or just a confusion in my mind?” Then, in the next half, she establishes that, “Because My son is the Supreme Lord, this natural attribute of His has now become manifest before my eyes: ” atho amusyaiva mamarbhakasya yah kascanautpattika atma-yogah, “Or else, this child of mine has some natural mystic powers». Lord Hari’s attributes that are intrinsic to mādhurya are always visible to the inhabitants of Vraja. On the other hand, the attributes intrinsic to aiśvarya, which are known to exist in the Lord, are visible only sometimes. This is the conclusion of those who know the truth. Then in the next half verse, she describes that, “This inconceivable attribute characterized by this astonishing vision is manifested by the Supreme Person called Nārāyaṇa, Who is a portion of my child: ” atho yathavan na vitarka-gocaram ceto-manah-karma-vacobhir ahjasa (Srimad-Bhāgavatam, 10.8.41), “Therefore, His feet cannot be properly and truly an object of reasoning, either by the intellect, by the mind, or by activities." Then, in the next half verse she says: yad-asrayam yena yatah pratiyate sudurvibhavyam pranatasmi tat-padam, “I bow down at His feet, which are the resting place of the universe, by which and from which everything is manifested, and which are very difficult to comprehend. ” This describes His feet as the cause of creation — “His feet are the resting place (yad-asrayam), whose vision is astonishing and which cannot be an object of reasoning (vitarka-gocaram) by the intellect (cetah) and so on." The word ‘yena ‘expresses His feet as the cause of manifestation. “In order to remove my doubts about His attributes, I bow down to His feet in the form of a plenary portion of the Supreme Person. Kṛṣṇais my son and is to be fondled by me. As for His plenary portion in the form of the Supreme Lord, He is worshipable. This is our mode of devotion. Desiring to get rid of fear from me, My son Kṛṣṇa, Who is the source of all plenary portions, is bewildering me after showing the universe within His mouth. May He be pleased with me and remove my doubts about His attributes. ” This is the meaning. It is shown in the next three quarters of the verse the disappearance of her doubts, followed by the steadiness of her personal mood: aham mamasau patir esa me suto vrajesvarasyakhila-vittapa sati, gopyas ca gopah saha-godhanas ca me, “I am the chaste wife of the King of Vraja, the protector of all his wealth. This is my husband, this is my son, these are my cowherd ladies and cowherd men along with my cows. ” Then in the last quarter of the verse she says: yan-mayayettham kumatih sa me gatih, “He is my shelter, by Whose illusory energy I have become bewildered in this way. ” Here, there is a request to not have doubts again, “By the illusory energy of the Supreme Person, Who is a plenary portion of My son, I have become bewildered (kumatih) in this way (ittham) by such a doubt as to whether this is a dream. May this Supreme Person be my shelter (gatih) to remove such doubts». The next verse states: ittham vidita-tattvayām gopikayam sa isvarah, vaisnavtm vyatanon mayam putra-sneha-maytm vibhuh, “As Yasoda had thus understood the truth about Him, the omnipotent Supreme Lord spread His internal potency in the form of parental affection». This is a description of the Lord‘s kindness, which befits the mādhurya mood, “Having known my real nature, may My mother not neglect fondling Me». The following verse describes how her memory about that astonishing vision just vanished and she was then again fondling her son: sadyo nasta-smṛtir gopi saropyaroham atmajam, pravrddha-sneha-kalila-hrdayasid yatha pura, “Taking her son on her lap, her memory at once vanished and her heart was filled with deep love as before». Other instances should be explained in a similar way. Having thus shown Mother Yasoda’s knowledge about Kṛṣṇa’s powers, the author shows Srimati Radha s by quoting the verse starting with ‘mrgayur iva ‘(Śrimad-Bhāgavatam, 10.47-17). These are her words towards a bumblebee: “O bumblebee, Kṛṣṇawas previously Rama, and like (iva) a hunter (mrgayuh), He killed (vivyadhe) Vali, the king of monkeys (kapindram), without abiding by the principles of hunting (alubdha-dharma). No hunter would kill a monkey, since their flesh is not eatable. Although being Himself under the control of a woman (stri-jitah), i.e., being attached to Sita, He disfigured (virupam akrta) another woman (striyam), Surpanakha, who had desire (kamayandm) for Him, by having her nose and ears cut off. As Vāmana, He previously grabbed (attva) the offering (balim) of Bali, i.e., the three planetary systems, and then bound him up (avestayat), just as the crows (dhvanksavat) eat sacrificial offerings and then surround the sacrificer. Therefore, enough (alam) of friendship (sakhyaih) with this dark complexioned (asita) boy. ” If someone says, “In this case you should remain silent, ” she adds, “Although it is very difficult to stop (dustyajah) talking about Him (tat-katharthah).” Here, Śri Yasoda s and Śri Radha s knowledge about aiśvarya is clear. The words ‘varadesvara та sma chindyaW (Śrimad-Bhāgavatam, 10.29.33) were spoken by all the gopts who were engaged in loving affairs with Kṛṣṇa. In this connection, the following verse should also be taken: dhanya aho ami alyo govindanghry-abja-renavah, yan brahmesau rama devi dadhur murdhnagha-nuttaye (ib., 10.30.29), “O friends, how fortunate is this sand touched by the lotus feet of Govinda! Lord Brahma, Lord Siva, and Goddess Laksmi take it on their heads to drive away sinful reactions. ” This knowledge that Kṛṣṇais worshipped by demigods like Lord Brahma is common to all the inhabitants ofVraja. The words \<tat-taj-jndnani\> mean €knowledge about aiśvarya and about mādhurya.y Both cannot appear simultaneously, for multiple knowledge is not possible at the same time. Someone may argue, “It is acknowledged that astavadhams can display multiple knowledge simultaneously». In response, the author says that knowledge about mādhurya and knowledge about aiśvarya arise one after the other because in this way they increase the rasas. The learned explain that even in astavadhana there is a very small fraction of time in-between one knowledge and the other, which is not perceived because it is very subtle. Someone may argue, “At the time of knowledge about mādhurya, knowledge about aiśvarya vanishes». In response, the author says, “The non-appearance of a particular knowledge does not mean it is nonexistent.” The quoted aphorism is from the Brahma-stitra (2.3.29). Knowledge factually existent in the state of deep sleep becomes manifest in the waking state, just as virility existent in childhood becomes manifest in youth. Otherwise, even an impotent man would become virile. Because of mādhurya’s intrinsic relation with the nature of the Supreme, its knowledge is called ‘knowledge about the Supreme ‘(brahma-jnāna). Thus, qualities like perplexity are limbs of mādhurya. This was explained above, “The Supreme Lord is also seen perplexed, etc. ” Perplexity means not knowing everything. The verse starting with ‘tad-abhijnah ‘is from the Śrimad-Bhāgavatam (10.24.2) and was spoken by Śukadeva. The words ‘tad-abhijnah ‘mean that Kṛṣṇaalready knew everything about the preparations to worship Indra, thus He has expanded knowledge (pratata-jnāna). The perplexity is expressed by His bending down in respect and inquiring. Someone may argue, “Feigned perplexity is no evidence of His perplexity». In response, the verse starting with ‘tato vatsan ‘is quoted from the Śrimad-Bhāgavatam (10.13.16). The word ‘tat ‘in the next sentence refers to this perplexity. One should also not mistakenly think that if such qualities are accepted as non-different from the Lord‘s svarūpa, then it is accepted that the nature of the Supreme is mixed with ignorance. Just as nails, hair, etc., are limbs of the body, similarly such qualities are not separated from knowledge of the Supreme, although they may appear to be different. Corroborating this, the author states, “For other sorts of qualities do not exist in Him. ” In other words, because ignorance, which is a function of tamo-guna, does not exist in the nature of the Supreme. The evidence of this is the verse quoted from the Vaiṣṇava-tantra. In this regard, the following verses should be pondered over: na yatra māyā kim utapare (Śrimad-Bhāgavatam, 2.9.10), “The illusory energy does not exist in Lord Hari’s abode. ” māyā paraity abhimukhe ca vilajjamana (Śrimad-Bhāgavatam, 2.7.47), “Feeling embarrassed in front of the Supreme Lord, the illusory energy runs away». The verse enumerating the eighteen great defects is from the Viṣṇu-yamala. The word ‘kledah ‘means ‘distress’. The word ‘parisrama ‘means ‘weariness of the senses’.

### Text 7

**Перевод:**

**Someone may argue, “There is an example of confusion in the verse: tato vatsan adrstvaitya puline ‘pi ca vatsapan, ubhav api vane krsno vicikaya samantatah (Śrimad-Bhāgavatam, 10.13.16), ‘Then, not finding the calves, Kṛṣṇawent to the bank of the Yamuna but also did not find the cowherd boys. He searched for the calves and the boys here and there in the forest’. There is an example of sleepiness, fatigue, and exertion in the verse: kvacit pallava-talpesu niyuddha-srama-karsitah, vrksa-mulasrayah sete gopotsangopabarhanah (Śrimad-Bhāgavatam, 10.15.16), ‘Sometimes, feeling fatigued due to the exertion in wrestling, Kṛṣṇawould sleep on couches of grass at the foot of a tree and use the lap of a cowherd boy as a pillow’. There is an example of error in the verse starting with ‘tav anghri-yugmam ‘(Śrimad-Bhāgavatam, 10.8.22), which describes how Kṛṣṇaand Balarāma would become apprehensive and run for their mothers. There is an example of restlessness in the verse starting with ‘vatsan muncan ‘(Śrimad-Bhāgavatam, 10.8.29), which describes how Kṛṣṇawould sometimes release the neighbours ‘calves at improper times and smile at their angry faces. There is an example of intoxication in the verse: mada-vighurnita-locana isan manadah sva-suhrdam vana-mali (Śrimad-Bhāgavatam, 10.35.24), ‘Kṛṣṇacomes sporting a garland of forest flowers, with eyes slightly rolling in intoxication and showing regard for His friends’. There is an example of envy in the verse: lokesa-maninam maudhyad dharisye sri-madam tamah (Śrimad-Bhāgavatam, 10.25.16), ‘Out of foolishness, the demigods consider themselves the lords of the world. I shall destroy their ignorance in the form of the pride for their prosperity’. There are instances of violence in the killing of Putana and many other demons. There are instances of untruthfulness when He denied having eaten dirt and accused His friends of making false accusations, when He deceived Jarasandha, and so on. There are also many well-known examples of anger on several occasions.**

**Комментарий:**

Having demonstrated that all defects are rejected in the Supreme Lord, here the author again refutes an objection. The verse starting with tato vatsan ‘is from the Śrimad-Bhāgavatam (10.13.16), and the illustration made here ends in the subsequent verse. The word ‘vicikaya ‘means ‘He searched for’. The complete verse illustrating error is as follows: tav anghri-yugmam anukrsya sarisrpantaughosa-praghosa-ruciram vraja-kardamesu, tan-nada-hrsta-manasav anusrtya lokam mugdha-prabhitavad upeyaturanti matroh (Śrimad-Bhāgavatam, 10.8.22), “Kṛṣṇaand Balarāma dragged their feet behind while crawling through the dirt of

Vraja, thus making a sweet sound with their ankle bells, which delighted Them. Upon looking at people outside, as if confused and scared, They would run back to Their mothers. ” Error is the perception of an object as otherwise. Upon looking at (anusrtya) people (lokam), KṛṣṇaandBalarāma would mistakenly think, “Someone is coming to catch us». The word ‘lolata ‘means ‘restlessness’. The word ‘matsarya ‘means ‘the inability to tolerate someone else’s eminence’. As for those who say that Lord Hari is not untruthful, since the whole earth planet was already within Him when He said that He did not eat dirt, they are unusual devotees, for this would violate the instances found in the deception ofjarasandha and the words of a cheatedgopi.

### Text 8

**Перевод:**

**“There is an example of longing in the verse: tam stanya-kama asadya mathnantim jananlm harih, grhitva dadhi-manthanam nyasedhat pritim avahan (Śrimad-Bhāgavatam, 10.9.4), ‘Desiring to suck His mother’s breast, Lord Hari approached her while she was churning yogurt. Showing great affection and catching the churning rod, He prevented her from churning’. There is an example of doubt in the verse: kvapy adrstvantar vipine vatsan palams ca visvavit, sarvam vidhi-krtam kṛṣṇah sahasavajagama ha (Śrimad-Bhāgavatam, 10.13-17), ‘Not being able to find the calves and cowherd boys anywhere in the forest, Kṛṣṇa, the knower of the whole universe, suddenly understood that everything was done by Lord Brahma’. There is an example of absorption in worldly affairs in the Taittiriya Upaniṣad (2.6.1): so ‘kamayata bahu syam prajayeya, ‘The Supreme Soul desired, ‘Let Me become many.” There is an example of inequality in the verse: samo ‘ham sarva-bhutesu na me dvesyo’sti na priyah, ye bhajanti tu mam bhaktya mayi te tesu capy aham (Bhagavad-gita, 9-29), ‘I am equal towards all living entities, and to Me no one is friend or enemy. Yet those who worship Me with devotion are in My heart and I am in their hearts’. An example of dependence on others is understood in the Śrimad-Bhāgavatam (9-4-63): aham bhakta-paradhlnah, ‘I am dependent on My devotees’. ‘Ruksa-rasata ‘is attachment without love’. Kama ‘is intense lust, which causes suffering. These two may not exist in the Supreme Lord. Thus, sixteen faults like confusion and so on having proved by the evidence of these verses, how can the Lord‘s body be free from defects?” This is not true. These attributes of the Lord‘s svarūpa, which are not even slightly tainted by material nature, arise in order to bring into effect His affection for His devotees, their protection, the manifestation of pastimes that sustain a variety of bliss for His devotees, and so on. Without these transcendental attributes, none of these would be accomplished, and in this case, the Supreme Lord‘s completeness would not be demonstrated. Because the taste in these attributes does not exist in all other qualities of the Lord, spontaneous devotion would not be possible. Thus, the following śruti would be contradicted: sraddha-bhakti-dhyana-yogad avaihi (Kaivalya Upaniṣad, 2), “You should understand the Supreme Soul through faith, devotion, and meditation.” Because these are actually transcendental qualities of the Lord, the statement of the smṛti is consistent: nirdosa-purna-guna-vigraha atma-tantro niscetanatmaka-sarlra-gunais ca hinah, “The Supreme Soul is independent. His body is composed of faultless and fully transcendental qualities, devoid of the qualities of a body made of insentient matter.”**

**Комментарий:**

In the first verse here, ‘akanksa ‘is Kṛṣṇa’s desire for drinking breast milk when He was hungry. The word ‘asanka ‘means doubt. The text starting with’so ‘kamayata ‘is from the Taittiriya Upaniṣad (2.6.1). The word’sah ‘refers to the Supreme Soul. The verse starting with’samo ‘ham ‘is from the Bhagavad-gita (9.29). The word ‘parapeksa ‘means ‘dependence on others’. The word ‘tan ‘refers to qualities like confusion and so on. From confusion and doubt arise the rasas of doubt and astonishment for those serving in the aiśvarya mood, while for those serving in the mādhurya mood, they give rise to rasas that awaken and increase mādhurya feelings. From restlessness and intoxication arise pastimes like wrestling with boys of the same age, but for the damsels, they produce an increase of passionate love. Sleepiness, fatigue, and exertion are specific desires of the Lord, which increase the bliss of those engaged in serving Him. Because those who experience envy, anger, and violence from the Lord are destroyed by Him, these qualities are the protection of the devotees. From error and restlessness His parents and other elders attain increasing pleasure. By sometimes speaking lies, He makes His companions and girlfriends angry, which increases their respective rasas. By absorption in worldly affairs is manifest His kindness to all living entities who are absorbed in material nature. By inequality, dependence on others, and longing is manifest His kindness to all His devotees. These qualities should be understood in this way. “Other qualities” refers to omniscience and so on. The word ‘tesam ‘refers to qualities like confusion and so on.

### Text 9

**Перевод:**

**These qualities like confusion and so on are of the nature of the bliss of the Lord‘s pastimes, which is the essence of His svarūpa-śakti and should be considered non-different from Him, just like His nails, hair, and so on. As stated in the Śrimad-Bhāgavatam (l0.77.3i): kva soka-mohau sneho va bhayarh va ye ‘jna-sambhavah, kva cakhandita-vijnāna-jnānaiśvaryah sureditah, “What is the lamentation, illusion, affection, or fear that appear among the ignorant, and what is the Supreme Lord, Whose power, knowledge, and intelligence are unbroken, and Who is praised even by the demigods.” Here, by the interrogation ‘kva ‘about qualities that appear among the ignorant, it is supposed that they appear among the wise too. In the Śrimad-Bhāgavatam (10.12.44) it is stated: tat-smaritananta-hrtakhilendriyah, “Being reminded by King Pariksit about the unlimited Lord, Śukadeva lost consciousness of all external senses.” This illustrates that confusion caused by love for God is also seen among Śukadeva and others whose ignorance has been destroyed.**

**Комментарий:**

In order to corroborate the meaning of what has been stated, the author here clarifies the matter. The verse starting with ‘kva soka-mohau ‘is from the Śrimad-Bhāgavatam (10.77.31) and was spoken byŚukadeva. “Where is confusion seen among the wise?” If someone asks this question, the author shows an example by quoting a verse from the Śrimad-Bhāgavatam (10.12.44) spoken by Sūta. Being reminded by Pariksit about the unlimited Lord Kṛṣṇa, Śukadeva thus lost consciousness of all external senses, being stupefied out of love for God. This is the meaning.

### Text 10

**Перевод:**

**Queen Kunti says: gopy adade tvayi krtagasi dama yavad ya te dāsasru-kalilanjana-sambhramaksam, vaktram niniya bhaya-bhavanaya sthitasya sa mam vimohayati bhir api yad bibheti (Śrimad-Bhāgavatam, 1.8.31), “When You committed some mischief and Yasoda took a rope, You lowered Your face out of fear and Your perturbed eyes were smeared with tears mixed with mascara, although fear personified fears You. The vision of You standing in this situation bewilders me.” Uddhava says: mantresu mam va upahuya yat tvam akunthitakhanda-sad-atma-bodhah, prccheh prabho mugdha ivapramattas tan me mano mohayativa deva (Śrimad-Bhāgavatam, 3-4.17), “O Lord, it bewilders my mind that You have unfailing, complete, and eternal spiritual knowledge, yet being confused, You have called me many times for consultation, although You are always aware of everything.” In instances like these, it is seen that those qualities of the Lord are not imitation, for they have been perceived even by personalities who know the truth, such as Kunti and Uddhava, and have also been described by Śukadeva and others who have realised them. As for words that express how these qualities deceive people, they are appropriate because being part of His divine pastimes, such qualities transcend the behaviour of common people.**

**Комментарий:**

The verse starting with ‘gopy adade ‘is from the Śrimad-Bhāgavatam (1.8.31) and was spoken by Kunti. The gopi referred to here is the Queen ofVraja, Kṛṣṇa’s mother. “The vision of You lowering (nintya) Your face (vaktram) and standing (sthitasya) with fear (bhaya-bhavanaya) that Yasoda may punish You. ” The meaning of the rest is clear. The verse starting with ‘mantresu mam ‘is from the Śrimad-Bhāgavatam (3.4.17) and was spoken by Uddhava. The word ‘upahilya ‘means ‘having called’. The two words ‘iva ‘are used as an ornament of speech, as in sentences like ‘kim iva niragasi mayi kupyasi ‘(Why are you angry with me, who am innocent?). It should also not be interpreted that the Lord is not confused but is as if confused, and that He does not bewilder but it is as if He bewilders, for in this case, there would be no contradiction, and sentences that express contradiction would be meaningless, such as: karmany anthasya bhavo ‘bhavasya te (Śrimad-Bhāgavatam, 3.4.16), “You perform activities, although You are free from action. You take birth, although You are unborn’. ‘Moreover, the word ‘iva ‘is not used in Kunti’s sentence. The word ‘te ‘refers to those sixteen qualities starting with confusion. The words

### Text 11

**Перевод:**

**In this way, because qualities like confusion and omniscience can be simultaneously manifest, it is proved that the Lord is also attentive to His servants ‘distress, praise, service, and so on. This is also stated by Bilvamangala, who knows these qualities of the Lord: sarvajnatve ca maugdhye ca sarvabhaumam idam mahah, nirvisan nayanam hanta nirvana-padam asnute (Kṛṣṇa-karnamrta, 83), “This self-effulgence called Kṛṣṇais universally known for His attributes of omniscience and confusion. Oh, entering my eye, He enjoys Gokula, the abode of liberation.”  The smṛti describes the concomitance of opposite qualities: aiśvarya-yogad bhagavan viruddhartho ‘bhidhiyate, tathapi dosah parame naivaharyah kathancana, guna viruddha apy ete samaharyah samantatah, “On account of His inconceivable potencies, the Supreme Lord is described by contradictory designations, yet defects can never approach Him. Although He has opposite qualities, they are to be taken as a complete whole.” The same is also said by Vyāsadeva, the revered author of the Brahma-sūtra (2.1.37): sarva-dharmopapattes ca, «And because it is proved that the Supreme Lord possesses all qualities.” The real nature of the Supreme Lord is to be ascertained by scriptural testimony only, and there is no provision to approach Him through logic, as stated in the Brahma-sūtra (2.1.27): srutes tu śabda-mulatvat, “Because the evidence is the scriptures, since they are the only basis of knowledge about the Supreme Lord.”**

**Комментарий:**

Someone may argue, “Certain rasas may be accomplished by Lord Hari’s confusion, but in this way He would not be able to acknowledge the requests of the distressed and there would be a great fault by this." The author replies to this objection here. At the time when confusion is displayed, omniscience is certainly present. Thus, there is not the least fault. Therefore, it is said: tad-abhijfio ‘pi (Śrimad-Bhāgavatam, 10.24.2), “Although the Lord knew it all. ” In this regard, the author gives the experience of the wise as evidence, “This is also stated, etc." The word anubhavitrbhih ‘refers to Bilvamangala, who knows about the Lord‘s confusion and omniscience. The word’sarvabhaumam ‘means ‘known in all places’. The meaning here is that the Lord is well-known everywhere for possessing both of these qualities. This self-effulgence (mahah), the supreme essence named Kṛṣṇa, enjoys the abode of liberation (nirvana-padam) named Gokula. He experiences His very self as an object of pleasure. He is the Lord ofGokula — this is the meaning of the verse. As for the objection that in the Lord‘s svarūpa there is a mixture with material qualities, this is refuted by what has been previously stated. Next, the author gives scriptural evidence by quoting a verse from the Kūrma Purāṇa starting with ‘aiśvarya-yogdt, ‘which means (on account of His inconceivable potencies’. Similarly, he states Vyāsadeva’s conclusion by quoting the Brahma-sūtra

(2.1.37). The meaning expressed in that section is that although the Lord is impartial inequality characterized by partiality towards His devotees should be considered one of His qualities, for it is proved that all mutually contradictory qualities, such as being all-pervasive and having a mediumsized body, exist in the Supreme Lord. Someone may argue, “In this way, your philosophy would be like Syadvada. ” It is not so, for in Syadvada, all substances have indefinite forms since they are taken to be in someway or other, while in our philosophy everything is certain on account of the Lord‘s transcendental attributes (viśeṣa).

### Text 12

**Перевод:**

**There are others who speak in the following way: “ Confusion and so on are material qualities imitated by the Supreme Lord for the happiness of His devotees, as stated in the Śrimad-Bhāgavatam (I0.14.37h prapancam nisprapanco ‘pi vidambayasi bhu-tale, prapanna-janatananda-sandoham prathitum prabho, ‘O Lord, although You are transcendental, You imitate worldly life on earth in order to increase the multitude of bliss of Your surrendered devotees’. Nevertheless, by such imitation there is an increase of rasa for the Lord and for those who know the truth, because by being taken up by Him, such qualities attain the same inconceivable nature, just as anything that falls within the salt ocean becomes salty. Imitation is seen in relations such as disciple, servant, and son in the form of Laksmi, Balarāma, Pradyumna, and others. There is also an imitation of qualities like the devotion shown by Kṛṣṇatowards Devarsi Nārada.”**

**Комментарий:**

Here the author describes a different understanding. The verse starting with ‘prapancam nisprapancah ‘is from the Śrimad-Bhāgavatam

(10.14.37) and was spoken by Lord Brahma. The word ‘vidambayasi ‘means ‘You imitate’. The idea here is that the Lord acts so with the intention that, “May the happiness of these people who are engaged in My service without any reservations increase.” Those qualities are also inconceivable like the attributes that belong to the Lord‘s svarūpa. The forms of Laksmi, Balarāma, and Pradyumna are to be respectively understood as playing the role of the Lord‘s disciple, servant, and son respectively.

### Text 13

**Перевод:**

**In this way, it has been shown that knowledge about the Supreme Lord is twofold, having either aiśvarya or mādhurya for its object. Hence, bhakti, which is the means to attain Him, must also be twofold. The first kind is vidhi-bhakti, devotional service performed according to rules and regulations. Those who become perfect, enlightened, and firm in devotion by this process attain the supreme abode through the path that starts with light, as described in the Chāndogya Upaniṣad (5.10.1-2). As for those afflicted souls who become drenched by an abundant shower of the Lord‘s mercy, it must be considered that they certainly enter the supreme abode, being carried by Garuda accompanied by the Supreme Lord Himself, as spoken in the last portion of the Varāha Purāṇa by Lord Varāha to the Goddess of Earth: so ‘ham asrita-mantratma smarami ca tad-asrayam, jnāna-vairagya-bhakty-adi-kriya-rQpa-prakāsanat, nayami parāmaṁ sthanam arciradi-gatim vina, garuda-skandham aropya yathestam anivaritah, “The mantras chanted by those under My shelter are My very self. By manifesting spiritual processes such as jnāna, vairagya, and bhakti, I always keep in mind their shelter. According to My own will and without any impediment, having made them climb on the back of Garuda, I take them to the supreme abode without having to go through the path that starts with light.” Regarding these souls, Kṛṣṇasaid to Arjuna: ye tu sarvani karmani mayi sannyasya mat-parah, ananyenaiva yogena mam dhyayanta upasate, tesam aham samuddharta mrtyu-samsara-sagarat, bhavami na cirat partha mayy avesita-cetasam (Bhagavad-gita, 12.6-7), “Renouncing all material activities for My sake, those devoted to Me meditate on Me and worship Me by exclusive devotion. О Partha, for those whose minds are absorbed in Me, I quickly become the deliverer from the ocean of birth and death.”**

**Комментарий:**

Here the author gives the sum and substance of this topic. “Those souls, ” i.e., “those afflicted souls who are unable to stand even a moment without Lord Hari». The verses starting with ‘ye tu ‘are from the Bhagavad-gita (12.6-7). The word ‘mayi ‘means ‘for My sake’. “They worship, ” i.e., they propitiate the Lord by glorifying (kirtana) Him, and so on. “I (aham) quickly (na cirat) become (bhavami) their deliverer (samuddharta). ”

### Text 14

**Перевод:**

**The latter is called ruci-bhakti, devotional service performed with spontaneous love. Those who become perfect by this process enter the supreme abode together with the Supreme Lord Himself. This is to be understood from the above quoted verse from the Varāha Purāṇa in the sense that how much more this is true regarding the destination of these devotees. As stated: nayam sukhapo bhagavan dehinam gopika-sutah, jnaninam catma-bhutanam yatha bhaktimatam iha (Śrimad-Bhāgavatam, 10.9.21), “The Supreme Lord, the son of Mother Yasoda, does not give as much happiness in this world to neophyte jnanis who are still in the bodily platform and to advanced jnanis who realised the self as He gives to those who engage in devotional service.” These devotees are very dear to the Lord, for they are full of spontaneous love in their feelings towards Him. As Kṛṣṇatold the gopas, who were astonished by seeing Him lifting Govardhana and suspected Him to be a demigod or so: mat-sambandhena vo gopa yadi lajja na jayate, slaghyo vaham tatah kim vo vicarena prayojanam, yadi vo’sti mayi pritih slaghyo ‘ham bhavatam yadi, tad atma-bandhu-sadrsi buddhir vah kriyatam mayi, naham devo na gandharvo na yakso na ca raksasah, aham vo bandhavo jato natas cintyam ato ‘nyatha (Viṣṇu Purāṇa, 5.13.10-12), “If you cowherd men are not ashamed of being related to Me and if I am praiseworthy, then what is the purpose in reflecting about this ? If you have affection for Me, if you think I should be praised by you, then think of Me as your relative. I am neither a demigod, nor a gandhan ‘a, nor a yaksa, nor a raksasa. I am just your kinsman, and you should not think otherwise.” Here, the rise of respect, which makes the heart palpitate, does not bring about the loss of the mādhurya mood, for they establish His status as their kinsman: devo va danavo va tvam yakso gandharva eva va, kim vasmakam vicarena bandhavo’si namo’stu te (Viṣṇu Purāṇa, 5.13.8), “What is the use of reflecting whether You are a demigod, a demon, a yaksa, or a gandharva ? You are our relative and we salute You.” Therefore, it is understood that such a doubt rising in their minds was meant to let them hear from Kṛṣṇa’s mouth about His status.**

### Text 15

**Перевод:**

**Someone may argue, “If Lord Kṛṣṇais the legitimate son of Mother Yasoda, then His relationship with the cowherd men should be that of a family member. He is not her legitimate son, for there is no such statement. Their relationship must be based on love, as stated by Him: ye yatha mam prapadyante tams tathaiva bhajamy aham (Bhagavad-gita, 4.11), ‘I reciprocate with the devotees according to the way they surrender unto Me’. Statements that Kṛṣṇais the son of Nanda and Ya£oda must be taken in a figurative sense, as in the sentence: upaguhyatmajam evam rudatya dina-dinavat (Śrimad-Bhāgavatam, 10.4.7), ‘Embracing her daughter, Devaki was crying like the most wretched woman’. ”  It is not so, for in the following verses, Śukadeva states that Kṛṣṇais Yasoda’s son: nisithe tama udblmte jayamane janardane, devakyam deva-mpinyam visnuh sarva-guhasayah, avirasid yatha pracyam disindur iva puskalah (Śrimad-Bhāgavatam, 10.3.7-8), “On a very dark night, while Kṛṣṇawas taking birth, the powerful Lord Viṣṇu, Who is situated in everyone’s heart, appeared within Devaki, the daughter of King Devaka, just as the moon appears on the eastern horizon.” yasoda nanda-patni ca jatam param abudhyata, na tal-lingam parisranta nidrayapagata-smṛtih (Śrimad-Bhāgavatam, 10.3.53), “Yasoda, the wife of Nanda Maharaja, understood that the Supreme Lord had been born, but she was not aware of the signs. Being exhausted due to the labour, and overcome by sleep, she lost her memory.” Yasoda is also called Devaki, as stated in the Adi Purāṇa: dve namni nanda-bharyaya yasoda devaklti ca, “Nanda’s wife has two names — Yasoda and Devaki.” Because it is stated that He appeared within Devaki does not mean that His also being connected with her womb is a flaw. Just as with Vāsudeva’s wife, Nanda’s wife also understood that the Supreme Lord had been born from her womb, but she was not aware of the signs, such as His having been brought by Vāsudeva, because she was exhausted and felt asleep. As it is clearly stated in the Adi Purāṇa (l6.21): nanda-gopa-grhe jato yasoda-garbha-sambhavah, “Kṛṣṇawas born from the womb of Yasoda in the house of the gopa Nanda.” In this way, names like’son of Nanda, ‘and’son of Yasoda ‘must be taken in their primary sense. Someone may argue, “Then the clear meaning of what is being expressed will be contradicted.” It is not so. As Devaki’s son is one with Yasoda’s son, this also does not contradict the clear meaning expressing His going to places like Mathura and Dvārakā, and also coming to Vraja and Kuruksetra now and then. Thus, His relationship with the cowherd men is based both on family connection and love.**

**Комментарий:**

The devotion the inhabitants ofVraja have for Kṛṣṇais filled with love for Him as a relative and is called raga-bhakti, which is said to be full of spontaneous love. In this regard, a doubt is raised here. The word ‘asau ‘refers to Kṛṣṇa. Someone may argue, “Then how can the designations of Kṛṣṇaas the son ofDevaki be meaningful?” The opponent quotes the verse starting with ‘upaguhyatmajam, ‘“Just as Yasoda’s daughter is called DevakVs daughter in a figurative sense, similarly this must also be the case here. Therefore, in the section describing Kṛṣṇa’s birth it is not understood that He is the son of Yasoda. ” It is not so, for in that section this fact is also read. For this reason, the author quotes the verse starting with ‘nisithe’. “On a night (nisithe) of wide-spread darkness (tama-udbhūte), while lila-purusottama (janardana) was taking birth (jayamane) from Yasoda (devakyam), Lord Viṣṇu appeared from the daughter of Devaka (deva-rupinyam) ” — this should be the connection of the words. His connection with DevakVs womb should not be taken as a flaw. A king does not lose his manliness because he stays in a fragrant palace made of jewels. The verse starting with ‘upaguhyatmajam ‘was spoken by the sage Śukadeva. In order to conceal her own son, Devaki told Kamsa that she had a daughter instead of a son. Kṛṣṇas relationship with the cowherd men is based both on family connection and love. In Kṛṣṇas abode situated within the material world, the love between son and mother that He has with Yasoda and others is relative to time, similar to that of common people. In His transcendental abode, that love is not similar to that of common people but is eternal, as we read in the mantras of the Gopāla-tāpani Upaniṣad (2.86; 1.10): от kṛṣṇaya devaki-nandanaya, “Obeisances unto Lord Kṛṣṇa, the son of Devaki». gopa-gopi-gavavttam, “One should meditate on Kṛṣṇasurrounded by gopas, gopts, andcows. ” Such love should be understood as beginningless and simultaneously effected like the variegated colours on a peacock’s feathers, and like a collection of small and large leaves that turn into lotusbuds.

### Text 16

**Перевод:**

**Thus, the Supreme Person named Kṛṣṇa is perceived by this twofold process —jnatia and bhakti. Manifesting His full energy, He is said to be bhagavan Himself. Kṛṣṇa, however, is the Supreme Lord Himself. ” astamas tu tayor asit svayam eva harih kila (Śrimad-Bhāgavatam, 9.24.55), “The eighth son of Vāsudeva and DevakI was Lord Hari Himself.” Someone may argue, “There is no stated arrangement by which we may understand that the meaning is that the Supreme Person is Kṛṣṇa, Who manifests His full-fledged energy, and Whose plenary portions and portions of plenary portions are Matsya and so on.” It is not so, for the word ‘tu ‘(however) means that a different section has begun. Also in the Brahma-samhita, having first mentioned Kṛṣṇa’s form, the three puruṣavataras, Rama, and others are mentioned as Kṛṣṇa’s avataras: Isvarah paramah kṛṣṇah sac-cid-ananda-vigrahah, anadir adir-govindah sarva-karana-karanam (5.1), “The Supreme Lord is Kṛṣṇa, Whose body is eternity, knowledge, and bliss. Its whorl is Kṛṣṇa’s abode, which is manifest from Ananta-sesa, His plenary portion.” Having said this, it states: yah karanarnava-jale bhajati sma yoga-nidram ananta-jagad-anda-sa-roma-kupah, adhara-śaktim avalambya param sva-murtim govindam adi-puruṣam tam aham bhajami (5.47), “I worship the primeval Lord Govinda, from Whose hair pores emanate unlimited universes. (5.48), “I worship the primeval Lord Govinda, Whose expansion is Maha-Viṣṇu, from Whose hair pores the rulers of the universe become manifest and are sustained for the time of one of His inhalations and exhalations.” diparcir eva hi dāsantaram abhyupetya dipayate vivrta-hetu-samana-dharma, yas tadrg eva hi ca visnutayavabhati govindam ... (5.46), “I worship the primeval Lord Govinda, Who appears as Lord Viṣṇu, just as the flame of a lamp contacting another wick lights another lamp, in which are manifest the same attributes as those of its cause.” ramadi-murtisu kala-niyamena tisthan nanavataram akarod bhuvanesu kintu, kṛṣṇah svayam samabhavat paramah puman yo govindam ... (5.39), “I worship the primeval Lord Govinda. **Комментарий:**

Now the author starts another topic in order to explain that Kṛṣṇa, the legitimate son of Mother Yasoda, is the Supreme Lord Himself the origin of all avataras. “Thus, ” i.e., in the mentioned way. The word ‘tat ‘in ‘akrtsna-tat ‘refers to His energy. The verse starting with ‘ete camsa ‘is from the Śrimad-Bhāgavatam (1.3-28) and was spoken by Sūta. The word ‘pumsah ‘refers to Garbodhakasayi Viṣṇu, Pradyumna, Who has thousands of heads. The word ‘tu ‘is used in the sense of the beginning of a different statement. According to the Amara-kośa (3-3-242), the word ‘tu ‘is used in the sense of difference and emphasis. Although the name of Kṛṣṇais read among the avataras that appear from Garbhodakasayi Viṣṇu, such as Matsya and Kūrma, He is not His avatara but the original source of all avataras, starting with Garbodhakasayi Viṣṇu. The verse starting with ‘astamas tu ‘is from the Śrimad-Bhāgavatam (9-24-55). “Lord Hari Himself (svayam) appeared (asit)from Vāsudeva and Devaki (tayoh). ” The words ‘yasya pumsah ‘refer to Garbhodakasayi Viṣṇu. In the verse from the

Brahma-samhita (5.1), ‘isvarah ‘is distinguished as Kṛṣṇa. What is He like ? He is the Supreme (paramah) Lord (isvarah) Himself, hence beginningless (anadih). He is the beginning of everyone different from Him, even Brahma, Siva, and so on. Someone may argue, "It is read in the scriptures that pradhana and the primordial elements are the cause of the universe». No, Govinda is the cause of all causes (sarva-karana-karanam). The eternal manifestation (sambhavam) is possible because of Ananta-sesa, a plenary portion of Baladeva. By another interpretation, Ananta-sesa, Who is an expansion (athsa) of Baladeva, has Golokafor His residence (sambhavam). The Brahma-samhita (5-47) declares that Karanodakasayi Viṣṇu is a form of Kṛṣṇa. “Reclining on (avalambya) His own form (sva-murtim), Sesa, Whose superior (param) energy supports (adhara-śaktim) everything, Kṛṣṇa(yah) enjoys (bhajati) yoga-nidra in the waters of the causal ocean (karanarnava-jale). ” The meaning is that by a portion of Himself He becomes Sahkarsana, Who is the inner Soul of material nature and has thousands of heads. What is He like ? His hair pores (roma-kUpah) are filled with (sa) unlimited universes (ananta-jagad-anda). Here, the word’saha ‘(with) is not situated in the beginning of the compound, which is poetic licence taken by the sages (arsa). “I (aham) worship (bhajami) Him. ” This is the prose order. What is He like? He is Govinda, the Lord of cows. The Brahma-samhita (5.48) declares that Garbhodakasayi Viṣṇu is a form of Kṛṣṇa. “The rulers of the universe (jagad-anda-nathah) — Brahma, Viṣṇu, and Siva — with their respective posts in this world, become manifest (jivanti) from the hair pores ofMaha-Viṣṇu and are sustained (avalambya) for the time (kalam) of one of His inhalations and exhalations (eka-nisvasitam), but even He is an expansion (kala-viśeṣah) of Kṛṣṇa». The meaning is that by a portion of Himself He becomes Pradyumna, the father °f Lord Brahma and the inner Soul of all material universes. The Brahma-samhita (5.46) declares that Ksirodakasayi Viṣṇu is a form of Kṛṣṇa. “Just as a large lamp (dipa) contacting another wick (dāsantaram) lights it up (dipayate) and appears as a very bright and small lamp». How is it? “It manifests the same attribute of light as that of its cause, the bigger lamp." The meaning is that in a similar way, by a portion of Himself Govinda becomes (avabhdti) Ksirodakasayx Viṣṇu., Aniruddha, Who is the protector of the world. The Brahma-samhita (5-39) declares that all avataras such as Rama and Nrsimha are forms of Kṛṣṇa. “By manifesting a restricted portion of His energy (kala-niyamena), the Supreme Person named Kṛṣṇamanifests (tisthan) the forms of Rama, Nrsimha, and so on, and descends in multiple forms (nanavataram akarot) in the material planets (bhuvanesu). He Himself also descended (samabhavat) as the all-powerful Lord. I worship Him». It is similarly stated by the demigods in the Śrimad-Bhāgavatam (10.2.40): matyasydsva-kacchapa-nrsimha-vardha-hamsa-rājanya-vipra-vibudhesu krtdvatarah, tvam pdsi nas tri-bhuvanam ca tathadhunesa bharam bhuvo hara yaduttama vandanam te, “You have previously descended as a fish, a horse, a tortoise, half-man half-lion, a boar, a swan, Rama, Parasurama, and Vāmana. Therefore, О Lord, now please protect us and the three worlds. Please remove the burden of the earth. О best ofYadus, we offer obeisances unto You. ”

### Text 17

**Перевод:**

**Someone may argue, “Let it be so. Kṛṣṇais the source of the puruṣavataras and other avatarasy but His source must be the Lord of Vaikuṇṭha, for nowhere is mentioned that Lord Nārāyaṇa is an avatara This is not true, for in the Brahma-samhita (5-43) Nārāyaṇa is also said to be a manifestation of Kṛṣṇa: goloka-namni nija-dhamni tale ca tasya devi-mahesa-hari-dhamasu tesu tesu, te te prabhava-nicaya vihitas ca yena govindam..., “I worship the primeval Lord Govinda, by Whom are given the multitude of powers existent in His own abode named Goloka, as well as those in the abodes of Goddess Durgā, Lord Siva, and Lord Brahma, which are situated below that.” Kṛṣṇais also understood as the source of all avataras because of the greatness of His name, which is the highest name, as stated in the Brahmanda Purāṇa (2.36.19): sahasra-namnam punyanam trir avrttya tu yat phalam, ekavrttya tu kṛṣṇasya namaikam tat prayacchati, “The result attained by reciting Lord Viṣṇu’s thousand Holy Names three times is attained by reciting Kṛṣṇa’s name once.” Therefore, it is correctly said that although Goddess Laksmi is Lord Viṣṇu’s wife and eternally enjoys with her husband, she has a desire for Kṛṣṇa’s form, as stated in the Śrimad-Bhāgavatam (10.16.36): yad-vanchaya srir lalanacarat tapo vihaya kaman suciram dhrta-vrata, “With the desire to attain Govinda’s feet, giving up all other desires, Laksmi engaged in severe vows and performed austerities for a long time.”**

**Комментарий:**

Having accepted the meaning described above, the opponent here raises another objection. The word ‘tasya ‘refers to the Lord of the spiritual sky. “Nowhere, ” i.e., neither in the Vedas nor in the Purāṇas. The word ‘tasyam ‘refers to the Brahma-samhita. After describing Kṛṣṇa’s greatness within the material creation in verse 42, the Brahma-samhita describes His greatness in His own abode in verse 43. The enumeration of the abodes of Durgā, Siva, and Viṣṇu should be understood in reverse order. They respectively succeed one another, since they are situated one above the other and consequentially have respectively higher and higher powers. The meaning is that below Goloka is Lord Hari’s abode, below which is Lord Siva 5 abode, below which is Goddess Durgā’s abode. Thus, KṛṣṇaHimself is always situated in the spiritual sky as Nārāyaṇa, hence Nārāyaṇa is His avatara. This is clearly stated. The result attained by reciting the thousand names of Lord Viṣṇu spoken by Vaisampayana is attained by reciting once a single name among the one hundred and eight names pertaining to Kṛṣṇaavatara, which are included in the Brahmanda Purāṇa (2.36.21-41). In the thousand names of Lord Viṣṇu are mentioned the names of Kṛṣṇacharacterized by all His avataras, while in the hundred and eight names of Kṛṣṇa, all the names are peculiar only to KṛṣṇaHimself. This is the difference. It is said that in comparison to the names included in the former, each name included in the latter yields more fruits, just as the words spoken by the Supreme Lord in the Bhagavad-glta are more fruitful than other words spoken by Him. “Therefore,” i.e. “because Kṛṣṇais endowed with more transcendental qualities.” This is the meaning. The verse about Laksmi was spoken by the wives ofKaliya. The words ‘yad-vanchaya ‘mean ‘with the desire to touch the dust of Your (Govinda’s)feet’. Śri is the goddess of the spiritual sky. There is a similar narration in another Purāṇa which is to be examined in the Laghu-bhāgavatāmṛta (1.5.347-352). It should be understood that since Kṛṣṇais mentioned as the source of the Lord of the spiritual sky, the view that Kṛṣṇais one of the quadruple avatara of Vāsudeva is refuted on its own, hence it is not analyzed here.

### Text 18

**Перевод:**

**This is to be understood as follows: While residing in Goloka, Kṛṣṇais eternally manifest in Vaikuṇṭha, which is a manifestation of Him, as its Lord named the Supreme Person, Srinivasa, as well as other forms like Rama. Some forms of the Lord, such as the puruṣavataras, sometimes become manifest in connection with the material world, but the Lord of Vaikuṇṭha is never in connection with it, and hence Their respective designations as avataras and not avataras. Because Kṛṣṇabecomes manifest in connection with the material world, He is also said to be an avatara. The residence of the puruṣavataras and other avataras in Vaikuṇṭha is described in the Padma Purāṇa: vaikuntha-bhuvane nitye nivasanti mahojjvalah, avatarah sada tatra matsya-kurmadayo ‘khilah, “All the highly effulgent avataras of the Lord, such as Matsya and Kūrma, always reside in the eternal abode, Vaikuṇṭha.” Thus, since Kṛṣṇadisplays His full energies and His form does not depend on anyone, He is the Supreme Lord Himself. This is the conclusion based on the views presented in scriptures such as the Śrimad-Bhāgavatam and Brahma-samhita. The same is also seen in the Gopāla-tāpani Upaniṣad, which is the basis of all scriptures (śruti). Therein the sages start to inquire in the following words: kah paramo devah (1.2), “Who is the Supreme God ?” Being thus questioned by them about the Supreme Deity, Who removes all suffering and is the cause of all causes, Brahma declares that all these attributes belong to Kṛṣṇaonly. Expecting that they might ask how such and such attributes can belong to Kṛṣṇaonly, since the scriptures describe that they belong to the Lord of Vaikuṇṭha, to Karanodakasayi Viṣṇu, and so on, Brahma confirms that Kṛṣṇaactually has all Their attributes, such as being the Lord of Vaikuṇṭha: eko ‘pi san bahudha yoVabhati (1.19), “Although one, Kṛṣṇaappears as many.” In the Bhagavad-gita (7.7) is also said: mattah parataram nanyat, “There is nothing superior than Me.”**

**Комментарий:**

Here the author tells something peculiar regarding the explained meaning. By the word ‘adi ‘(etc.) in ‘puruṣadihother avataras such as Rama should be included. The words ‘tasya tasya ‘refer to avataras such as the puruṣavataras and the Lord of Vaikuṇṭha. Because Kṛṣṇabecomes manifest in connection with the material world, i.e., because He Himself comes to the material world in His form as Kṛṣṇa, He is said to be an avatara. One who descends from the transcendental world into the material world is called ‘avatara’. Full energies are also manifest in the Lord of Vaikuṇṭha, therefore the author says that Kṛṣṇadoes not depend on anyone. The words ‘tatha tatha bhavam ‘mean ‘attributes such as removing all suffering, and so on’. The words ‘tad-abhidhanat ‘mean ‘because attributes such as removing all suffering are mentioned in the scriptures’. The word’sah ‘refers to such attributes. The words ‘tad-adhisadi-bhavam ‘mean ‘attributes like being the Lord of Vaikuṇṭha’. By the word ‘adi ‘other avatar as such as the puruṣavataras should be included.

### Text 19

**Перевод:**

**Someone raises the following objection: “It is stated in the Bṛhad-āraṇyaka Upaniṣad (5.1.l): purnam adah purnam idam pūrnat purnam udacyate, purnasya purnam adaya purnam evavasisyate, ‘The Supreme Lord is complete, hence this universe is complete. Whatever emanates from the complete is manifest as complete. When the complete form of the complete disappears within the complete, only the original complete remains’. It is also said in the Maha-Varāha Purāṇa: sarve nityah sasvatas ca dehas tasya paratmanah, hanopadana-rahita naiva prakṛtijah kvacit, paramananda-sandoha jnāna-matras ca sarvatah, sarve sarva-gunaih purnah sarva-dosa-vivarjitah, ‘All the bodies of the Supreme Soul are eternal and become manifest again and again in this world. They are not accepted and rejected, and are never produced by material nature. They completely consist of an abundance of bliss and pure knowledge. They are full with all transcendental qualities and devoid of all defects’. From this it is understood that all forms of the Lord are complete in themselves, hence it is not possible to conclude that Kṛṣṇa’s own form is the only one that is not dependent on anyone.” This argument is not wise, for such a question would arise if there were differences in the Lord‘s svarūpa. However, such differences do not exist. The same Supreme Lord Kṛṣṇamanifests multiple forms within oneself like a vaidūrya gem. As stated in the Gopāla-tāpani Upaniṣad (1.19): eko vasi sarvagah kṛṣṇa idya eko ‘pi san bahudha yo ‘vabhati, “Kṛṣṇais the only all-pervasive and worshipable Supreme Lord. Although one, He appears as many.” Just like a learned scholar conversant with the six subsidiary Vedic treatises sometimes may seem to know everything when explaining all treatises, and sometimes may seem to know almost everything when explaining them partially or explaining only one or two treatises, similarly the same Kṛṣṇahas direct avataras and expanded avataras, depending on whether He displays more or less powers. The same thing is also said by the wise Rūpa Gosvāmi: ekatvam ca prthaktvam ca tathamsatvam utamsita, tasminn ekatra nayuktaṁ acintyananta-śaktitah, sakter vyaktis tathavyaktis taratamyasya karanam (Laghu-bhāgavatāmṛta, 1.5-90,93), “On account of His inconceivable potency, it is not inappropriate that in the same Supreme Lord there are features like oneness, division, being a partial avatara or being the source of all avataras. The cause of the gradations is the manifestation and non-manifestation of His power.” The source of all avataras is characterized by the manifestation of His multiple energies by His own will. A partial avatara of Himself is characterized by a minor display of power at all times.**

**Комментарий:**

Removing another doubt, the author now explains the spoken meaning with an illustration. The text starting with ‘pUrnam adah ‘is from the Bṛhad-āraṇyaka Upaniṣad (5.1.1). The word ‘adah ‘means ‘original form, ‘and ‘idam ‘means ‘manifest form’. Both are complete (рйгпат). From the complete original form emanates (udacyate) a complete manifest form. When that complete form of the complete disappears (adaya) within the complete, the complete original form remains (avasisyate). In the verse from the Maha-Varāha Purāṇa, the word ‘nityah ‘means ‘eternal, ‘and’sasvatah ‘means ‘becoming manifest in this world again and again’. To show that the previous ācāryas also agree with his conclusion, the author quotes the wise Srila Rūpa Gosvāmi. The word ‘tasmin ‘means ‘in the same Kṛṣṇa’. The rest is clear.

### Text 20

**Перевод:**

**By this argument is refuted the idea that being a form of the Lord of Vaikuṇṭha and other avataras, Kṛṣṇaalso manifests all transcendental qualities because Nārāyaṇa is also full of those qualities. The Nārāyaṇa Atharvasira Upaniṣad starts with the following words: atha puruso ha vai narayano ‘kamayata prajah srjeyeti, “The Supreme Person, Lord Nārāyaṇa, desired, ‘Let me create offspring’». Then, after describing that the creation of the universe was manifested from Garbhodakasayi Viṣṇu, it is stated: brahmanyo devaki-putrah, “He is the holy son of Devaki.” From this, some mistakenly conclude that here Kṛṣṇais said to be a form of Nārāyaṇa. This idea has also been refuted by the above argument. As for the view that Kṛṣṇais the hair of Nārāyaṇa, it has been completely removed by the said conclusion and also by the Śrimad-Bhāgavatam (10.14.14): narayano ‘ngam narabhQjalayanat, “Garbhodakasayi Viṣṇu, Who became manifest from Karanodakasayi Viṣṇu, is an expansion of You.” Rather, it is correct that therein the puruṣavataras are said to be Kṛṣṇa’s expansions, for the stated meaning would be contradictory if taken otherwise.**

**Комментарий:**

In order to say that other doubts have also been removed by the same meaning explained above, the author writes the present paragraph’. Puruṣavatarat ‘refers to Garbhodakasayi Viṣṇu. Similarly, the puruṣa mentioned in the Nārāyaṇa Atharvasira Upaniṣad is Kṛṣṇa, for it is so revealed subsequently. Therefore, statements that Brahma and others were born from Kṛṣṇashould be considered in the sense that He is non-different from Garbhodakasayi Viṣṇu, otherwise the meaning stated in the smṛti would be contradictory. Next, the author says that it is only out of hastiness that some conclude that Kṛṣṇais a form of Ksirodakasayi Viṣṇu. Some claim that the meaning of the verses of the Mahā-bhārata (1.189.31-32) is as follows: being requested by the demigods, Ksirodakasayi Viṣṇu extracted a white and a black hair (kesau)from His body, which became Balarāma and Kṛṣṇain order to remove the burden of the earth. On the basis of the stated conclusion, this idea is merely due to inattention. Rather, the meaning is as follows — the word ‘kesa ‘means ‘ray of light, ‘as understood from the following words of Kṛṣṇain the Mahā-bhārata (12.328.43): amsavo ye prakāśante mama te kesa-samjnitah, sarvajnah kesavam tasman mam ahur muni-sattamah, “The effulgent rays that emanate from Me are called ‘kesa’. Therefore, the best of sages who know everything call me Kesava». Therein it is also described that Nārada saw His multicoloured rays.  Therefore, in all instances only the word ‘kesa ‘is employed.  Similarly, since Kṛṣṇais the Supreme Lord Himself and Baladeva is His expansion, then Viṣṇu followed Them with His two expansions. It is said in the Śrimad-Bhāgavatam (3.2.15) that when the Supreme Lord Himself descends, all His pastimes expansions (vilāsa), quadruple expansions (vyuha), and direct expansions (svamsa) follow Him with Their parts and portions: sva-santa-rūpesv itaraih svarūpair abhyardyamanesv anukampitatma, paravareso mahad-amsa-yukto hy ajo ‘pi jato bhagavan yathagnih, “Being merciful toward His peaceful devotees afflicted by the demons, the Supreme Lord, Who is the Lord of both the liberated and the entangled souls, appears with His plenary portions just like fire, although He is unborn.” Moreover, upon being requested by the demigods, Ksirodakasayi Viṣṇu did not say that ‘I Myself will become Kṛṣṇa’. He rather said that the Supreme Person, God of gods, will appear. This is also mentioned in the quoted verse from the Śrimad-Bhāgavatam

(10.14.14), ‘narayano ‘ngam, etc., ‘which was spoken by Lord Brahma. “0 Kṛṣṇa, my father Garbhodakasayi Viṣṇu (narayanah), Who became manifest from Karanodakasayi Viṣṇu (narabhUjalayanat), is an expansion (angam) of You (tava)». This is the meaning. Some present a solution in the following way — “Let this be the established meaning: Lord Hari Himself entered that hair just like a thunderbolt in a handful of foam. Otherwise, the statement ‘kṛṣṇas tu bhagavan svayam ‘would be contradicted.” “If taken otherwise, ” i.e., “if the concluded meaning is not accepted, then there would be a contradiction in the meaning stated by the scriptures». If it is accepted that the Lord of Vaikuṇṭha descends as Kṛṣṇaand manifests Kṛṣṇa’s complete qualities, then this would contradict the statement in the Śrimad-Bhāgavatam (10.16.36) that His wife Laksmi-devi longs for the dust of Kṛṣṇa’s feet. If it is accepted that the three puruṣavataras manifest Kṛṣṇa’s complete qualities, then this would contradict the statement in the Brahma-samhita that They are His expansions. If it is accepted that Lord  Rama manifested Kṛṣṇa’s complete qualities, then this would contradict the description in the Padma Purāṇa (Uttara,272.166-7) that the great sages, residents of the Dandaka forest who had seen Rama, felt desire for Kṛṣṇa. This would also contradict the statement in the Brahma-samhita (5.39) that He appears as Rama and other avataras by a limited portion of Himself Similarly, only in this way the following descriptions would be consistent: Sankarsana’s considering Himself as the seat of Vāsudeva and showing great respect for Him; Baladeva considering Himself as Kṛṣṇa’s servant; Pradyumna and others considering Him as Their father and having much respect for Him, as described in the Śrimad-Bhāgavatam; Laksmana, Bharata, and Satrughna considering Rama as Their Lord, as described in the Ramayana; and Nara-Nārāyaṇa’s considering themselves subjects of the Lord of Svetadvipa and their devotion for Him, as described in the Narayaniya of the Mahā-bhārata. Therefore, it has been firmly concluded that the portion is manifest from the whole, and not the whole from the portion.

### Text 21

**Перевод:**

**In the Rama-tāpani Upaniṣad it is said that Matsya, Kūrma, and others are avataras of the husband of Sita: yo vai sri-ramacandrah sa bhagavan ye matsya-kurmadayoVataras tasmai vai namo namah, “Lord Ramacandra is the Supreme Lord, Whose avataras are Matsya, Kūrma, and so on. Obeisances unto Him.” This is quite appropriate, for He is a form of the Lord of Vaikuṇṭha. In the Ramayana (6.105.6) it is stated: trayanam api lokanam adi-karta svayam prabhuh, “O Rama, You are the Supreme Lord Himself, the original creator of the three worlds.” In the Śrimad-Bhāgavatam (9.11.20) it is stated: adhika-samya-vimukta-dhamnah, “No one is equal or superior to Lord Rama’s power.” Here, Lord Rama’s status is understood by the word ‘Himself (svayam), which is to be guessed to mean that He is the source of Matsya, Kūrma, and so on. If it is accepted that Rama is non-different from Kṛṣṇa, then this would contradict the statements of the Brahma-samhita that Rama is an expansion of Kṛṣṇa, and also the Padma Purāṇa description that the great sages of Dandaka, who had previously seen Rama, felt desire for Kṛṣṇa. When the Upaniṣads say ‘purnam adah pūrnam idam, etc., ‘the meaning is that all qualities exist eternally in the Lord‘s svarūpa, and not that all qualities are manifest. Therefore, it is proved that only Kṛṣṇais the original form of the Supreme Lord, since all energies are eternally manifest in Him.**

**Комментарий:**

Someone may argue, “Since in the Rama-tapanl Upaniṣad it is said that Matsya, Kūrma, and so on are avataras of the husband ofSita, and since in scriptures like the Ramayana He is expressed by the word’svayam, ‘why do you insist that all of Them are avataras of Kṛṣṇa?” The author replies to this here. The word ‘tasmin ‘means ‘in the Rama-tāpani Upaniṣad’. In the Padma Purāṇa it is described that the Lord ofVaikuṇṭha became the husband ofSita, and that Sesa, sudarsana-cakra, and His conch became Laksmana, Bharata, and Satrughna respectively. In the Viṣṇu-dharmottara Purāṇa it is said that the quadruple expansions, named Vāsudeva, Sankarsana, Pradyumna, and Aniruddha, became Rama, Laksmana, Bharata, and Satrughna. This is explained in the Laghu-bhāgavatāmṛta (1.3.79; 1.5.34). Similarly, it is certainly correct that Matsya, Кйгта, and others are avataras of Lord Rama. Someone may argue, “Since Kṛṣṇaand Rama are described as the origin of all avataras, then there must not be the slightest difference from one another. The answer is given — “If it is not accepted that Rama is at least slightly different than Kṛṣṇa, etc.” The affix ‘nan ‘(a) in ‘aviśeṣat ‘is used in the sense of insignificant.  The verses from the Padma Purāṇa (Uttara, 272.166-7) are as follows: pura maharsayah sarve daṇḍakaranya-vasinah, drstva ramath harith tatra bhoktum aicchan suvigraham, te sarve stritvam apannah samudbhūtas ca gokule, harim samprapya kamena tato mukta bhavarnavat, “Formerly, after seeing Rama, Who is Lord Hari Himself, the great sages, residents of the Dandaka forest, desired to enjoy His beautiful form. All of them became women in Gokula, and having approached Lord Hari with passionate desire, they became free from the ocean of birth and death. ”

### Text 22

**Перевод:**

**Complete energies like Primati Radharani, ‘qualities experienced by devotees like Śukadeva, such as unsurpassed mercy, the sweetness of His form, which astonishes even Himself, the sweetness of the sound of His flute, which bewilders all moving and non-moving beings and astonishes even knowers of the truth such as Lord Brahma, His being surrounded by companions who are full of exceeding love — all these are always manifest only in Lord Kṛṣṇaand nowhere else. In the Puruṣa-bodhint Upaniṣad it is stated: gokulakhye mathura-mandale vrndavana-madhye sahasra-dala-padma-madhye kalpa-taror mule’sta-dala-kesare govindo ‘pi syamah pitambaro dvi-bhujo mayura-puccha-siro venu-vetra-hasto nirguṇah saguno nirakarah sakaro nirihah sacesto virajate dve parsve candravali radhika ceti yasya amso laksml-durgadika śaktir iti, “In Vṛndāvana, which is situated near the area of Mathura called Gokula, at the foot of a desire tree in the middle of a thousand-petalled lotus, Govinda stands on a filament amidst eight petals. His two-handed dark complexioned body is dressed in yellow garments. He wears a peacock feather on His head and holds a flute and a cane in His hands. He is devoid of material qualities but full of transcendental qualities; devoid of a material body but endowed with a spiritual body; free from material activities but engaged in divine pastimes. At His two sides stand §ri Radhika and Śri Candravali, whose expansions are personified energies like Laksmi and Durgā.” Subsequently, it is said: tasyadya prakṛti radhika nitya nirguṇa sarvalaṅkāra-sobhita prasannasesa-lavanya-sundari, “Śri Radhika is His eternal original nature, devoid of any material quality.
