She is adorned with all ornaments, joyful, and more beautiful than all beauty.” In the Rg Veda Parisista it is stated: radhaya madhavo devo madhavenaiva radhika vibhrājante janesv a, “Surrounded by the inhabitants of Vraja in all directions, Madhava, the Supreme God, shines in the company of Radha, and Radha shines in the company of Madhava.”**

**Комментарий:**

Here the author shows the characteristics that are always manifest only in Kṛṣṇabut not in the Lord of Vaikuṇṭha and other avataras. He gives evidence of what was spoken by quoting the Puruṣa-bodhini Upaniṣad, which belongs to the Pippalāda branch of the Atharva Veda. The word ‘nirguṇah ‘means ‘untainted by the modes of material nature’. The word’saguṇah ‘means ‘endowed with the six transcendental qualities inherent in His svarūpa’. The word ‘nirakarah ‘means ‘devoid of a material form’. The word’sakarah ‘means ‘endowed with a human-like form inherent in His svarūpa’. The word ‘nirihah ‘means ‘free from material activities’. The word’sacestah ‘means that He performs eternal pastimes that are a manifestation of His own bliss, which are compared to waves emanating from the ocean. It should be understood that Srt Radha stands on His left side, for this is stated in the KṛṣṇaUpaniṣad: vamanga-sahita devt radha vrndavanesvari, “Goddess Radha, the Queen of Vṛndāvana, is situated on the left side of Lord Kṛṣṇa». Therefore, she is the most prominent among His beloved girlfriends. This is stated next. The word ‘yasyahy refers to Śri Radhika, whose expansions are Laksmi, who is the goddess of Vaikuṇṭha, and Durgā, who is the presiding deity of the mantra-raja. This is to be understood from the Bhakti-sandarbha (285). The words ‘nitya-nirguṇa \* mean ‘not even slightly tainted by the illusory energy’. Next, it is said that she is characterized by qualities inherent in her svarūpa. The plural in the word ‘vibhrājante ‘is peculiar to the Vedas. The word \*janesu ‘means ‘among the inhabitants ofVraja’.

### Text 23

**Перевод:**

**In the Brhad-gautamiya Tantra it is also said: sattvam tattvam paratvam ca tattva-trayam aham kila, tri-tattva-rupim sapi radhika mama vallabha, prakrteh para evaham sapi mac-chakti-rupim, “I am actually the three principles — cause (sattva), effect (tattva)y and that which is beyond both, and My beloved Radhika also exists in the form of these three principles. I am beyond the material energy, and she is My own energy.” devi kṛṣṇa-mayi prokta radhika para-devata, sarva-laksmi-mayi sarva-kantih sammohini para, “The goddess called Radhika, the superior energy, is Kṛṣṇa’s worshipper and is non-different from Him. All goddesses of fortune are her expansions and her desire to worship Kṛṣṇais like that of all of them combined. She is always pleasing Kṛṣṇa.” Here, by the words’she is the superior energy, non-different from Kṛṣṇa, ‘the following statement of the Śvetāśvatara Upaniṣad (6.8) is expressed: parasya śaktir vividhaiva sruyate svabhaviki jnāna-bala-kriya ca, “It is heard from the scriptures that the Supreme has multifarious intrinsic superior energies in the form of knowledge, power, and action.” In the Viṣṇu Purāṇa (1.19-77) it is also said: yatita-gocara vacam manasarh caviśeṣana, jnani-jnāna-paricchedya vande tam isvarim param, “I offer obeisances to the supreme goddess, who is beyond the senses and cannot be defined by speech and the mind, but who is perceived by the knowledge possessed by those who worship her.” In these verses, the one said to be non-different from the Supreme Lord and characterized by hlādini, sandhini, and samvit, that superior energy is the Goddess Radhika. Similarly, she is the love (prema) that consists of the essence of hlādini and samvit. This is clearly explained in the Govinda-bhāṣya (3-3.40-42) in the Kamady Adhikaraṇa.**

**Комментарий:**

Here the author corroborates the meaning of the śruti by quoting some verses from the Brhad-gautamlya Tantra, which were spoken by Kṛṣṇato Balarāma. “I am the three principles — cause (sattvam)y effect (tattvam), and that which is beyond both of them — and Radhika also exists in the form of these three». After this one and a half verse, there is the following one and a half verse: sattvikam mpam asthaya purno ‘ham brahma-cit-parahy brāhmaṇa prarthitah samyak sambhavami yuge yuge, taya sardham tvayā sardham nasaya devata-druham, “Being duly requested by Lord Brahma, resorting to a form made of pure goodness. I, the complete Supreme Brahman, appear in every age accompanied by Radha and You for the destruction of the enemies of the demigods. ” The conclusion here is, “Radha consists of the same substance as Me. ” The non-difference between cause and effect should be guessed by the principle that the pervaded is non-different from that which pervades. The verse starting with ‘devi kṛṣṇa-mayi ‘is also found in the Brhad-gautamlya Tantra in the description about the Radha-mantra. “Radhika is the superior energy” — this is the connection of the words. The word ‘devi ‘means that she is the main consort of God, Kṛṣṇa. In order to prevent the idea that this incurs any difference, it is said that she is non-different than Kṛṣṇa(kṛṣṇa-mayi), as confirmed by the verse starting with csattvam tattvam’. The words cpara-devata ‘mean ‘worshipper of Kṛṣṇa’. The words \* sarva-laksmi-mayi ‘mean that she is the original source of all Laksmis, as it is said in the Puruṣa-bodhinl Upaniṣad: yasya amso laksmt-durgadika śaktih, “She whose expansions are personified energies like Laksmi and Durgā.” The words csarva-kantih ‘mean’she whose desire to worship Kṛṣṇais like that of all Laksmis together’. Some say that her beauty is that of all of them collectively. The word’sammonihi ‘means’she who is always pleasing Kṛṣṇa’. The text (parasya śaktih, etc’. is from the Śvetāśvatara Upaniṣad (6.8). The Supreme Lord has (asya) a threefold (vividha) intrinsic (svabhaviki) energy (śaktih) constituted of samvit (jnāna), sandhini (bala), and hlada (kriya). Here, ‘kriya ‘is used in the sense of the verbal root ‘hlad ‘(to be happy). The verse starting with ‘yatita-gocard ‘is from the Viṣṇu Purāṇa (1.19.77) and was spoken by Prahlada. Radha is beyond the perception (atita-gocara) of speech (vacam) and the mind (manasam). She cannot be defined (aviśeṣana) by their qualities expressed by speech and the functions of the mind. Samvit is described in the Taittiriya Upaniṣad (2.4.1): yato vaco nivartante, “Failing to attain the Supreme, words return. ” Thus, samvit is demonstrated as non-different from the Supreme Lord. Then, is samvit devoid of attributes? In response, it is said that by the knowledge (jnāna) possessed by those who worship her (jnani), this supreme energy can be perceived (paricchedya) as having unlimited intrinsic qualities. “I offer obeisances (vande) to the supreme (param) goddess (isvarim), God’s life and soul, and His main consort. ” This is the meaning, as confirmed by the śruti: parasya śaktih, etc. The meaning of the rest of the paragraph is clear.

### Text 24

**Перевод:**

**Being so, as for the statement in the Skanda Purāṇa that after seeing the sweetness of the Lord‘s form, a certain dancing girl became Radha by dancing before Him on the Prabodhani Ekadasi, this is to be considered in the sense that she became similar to Radha, as in the Bṛhad-āraṇyaka Upaniṣad (4.4.6): brahmaiva san brahmapyeti, “Being similar to Brahman, one attains Brahman.”**

**Комментарий:**

In order to remove the doubts of dull-headed individuals, the author says, “Being so, ” i.e., “since it has been proved that Śri Radha is Maha-Laksmi, the primeval goddess of fortune, the essence of the Lord‘s intrinsic superior energy. ” Being similar to Brahman (brahmaiva san), a living entity who meditates on Brahman attains (apyeti) Brahman. According to the Visva-kośa (Avyaya, 63), the word ‘eva ‘is used in the sense of similarity and restriction. The liberated soul’s similarity with Brahman has been stated in the Muṇḍaka Upaniṣad (3.1.3): niranjanah parāmaṁ samyam upaiti, “The pure soul thus attains transcendental similarity with the Supreme Lord’. ‘Also in the Bhagavad-gita (14.2): idam jnānam apasritya mama sadharmyam agatah, sarge ‘pi nopajayante pralaye na vyathanti ca, “Taking shelter in this knowledge, the living entities attain a nature similar to Mine and thus they are neither born at the time of creation nor afflicted at the time of dissolution. ” The meaning is that meditating on Radha, that dancer became similar to Radha. Otherwise, the aforementioned śruti and smṛti would be baseless. This is the idea. As for the statement in the RSmayana (Uttara-kanda, chapter 17) that the sage Agastya told Lord Rama that there was a very beautiful maiden called Vedavati, born from Maharsi Kusadhvaja’s speech, who was engaged in austerities with the desire to have Lord Viṣṇu as her husband and was insulted by the wicked Ravana, which made her burn her own body and later became Sita in order to destroy him, that is to be understood in the Ramayana itself in the following way: Vedavati merely entered the body of Sita to become the cause of Ravana’s destruction, because Sita, being eternally Laksmi herself, Lord Rama’s life and soul, cannot be identified with Vedavati.

### Text 25

**Перевод:**

**In this way, because she is Maha-Laksmi herself, Śri Radha’s completeness is not contradicted. All Laksmis are indeed very dear to Lord Kṛṣṇa, yet because Radha is the chief of them, it is reasonable that she is Maha-Laksmi. As it is said in the Brahma-samhita: cintamani-Prakara-sadmasu kalpa-vrksa-laksavrtesu surabhir abhipalayantam, laksmi-sahasra-sata-sambhrama-sevyamanam govindam... (5-29), “I worship the primeval Lord Govinda, Who tends surabhi cows in abodes made of collections of transcendental gems, surrounded by millions of desire trees. He is always respectfully served by hundreds of thousands of Laksmis.” sriyah kantah kantah parama puruṣah (5-56), wThe goddesses of fortune are the consorts, and the lover is the Supreme Person.” The Śrimad-Bhāgavatam (10.33-14) also states: gopyo labdhvacyutam kantam sriya ekanta-vallabham, “The gopts, the goddesses of fortune, then obtained Acyuta as lover, their only beloved.” The Padma Purāṇa also corroborates: yatha radha priya visnos tasyah kundam priyam tatha, sarva-gopisu saivaika visnor atyanta-vallabha, “Just as Radha is dear to Kṛṣṇa, so is her pond dear to Him. Among all the gopts, she is exceedingly dear.” The Adi Purāṇa also says: trailokye prthivl dhanya tatra vrndavanam purl, tatrapi gopikah partha tatra radhabhidha mama, “The earth planet, where the town of Vṛndāvana is situated, is the most fortunate in the three worlds. О Partha, there live the cowherd girls, among whom Mine is called Radha.” The evidence from the Puruṣa-bodhini has also been shown: yasya amso laksml-durgadika śaktih, “She whose expansions are personified energies like Laksmi and Durgā.”**

**Комментарий:**

“In this way, ” i.e., “in the way as it has been described». The verse starting with c cintamani-prakara\* is from the Brahma-samhita (5\*29). He tends (palayantam) the cows (surabhih) by taking them to the forest, pasturing themy and bringing them to the cow pen. Sometimes He sports with the cowherd ladies (laksmi) in a solitary place. The verse starting with ‘g°py° labdhvacyutam ‘is from the Śrimad-Bhāgavatam (Ю.33.14). “The gopis; the very goddesses of fortune, ”— this is the connection of the words. Next, SriRadha’sprominence is mentioned by the verse ‘yatha radha, etc’. The verse ‘trailokye prthivV was spoken by Kṛṣṇato Arjuna. The word ‘dhanya ‘means ‘the most fortunate’. Someone may argue, “Evidence from the śruti, which is the basis of the evidence from the smṛti, should be given». In response, the Puruṣa-bodhini is quoted.

### Text 26

**Перевод:**

**Lord Kṛṣṇa’s companions ‘love for Him is incomparable and unsurpassed, which is understood by their acts for His sake, such as running to enter a lake blazing with Kaliya’s poison. From this is also understood how the Supreme Lord is tightly under their control, as spoken by Lord Brahma in the Śrimad-Bhāgavatam (10.14-35): esam ghosa-nivasinam uta bhavan kim deva rateti nas ceto visva-phalat phalarh tvad-aparam kutrapyayan muhyati, sad-vesad iva putanapi sakula tvam eva devapita yad-dhamartha-suhrt-priyatma-tanaya-pranasayas tvat-krte, “O Lord, what reward shall You give to the inhabitants of Vraja? Not finding any reward other than Your own self anywhere, since You are everything, our minds become bewildered at this thought. You gave Yourself even to Pūtana and her two brothers merely because she appeared like a wet nurse, so what of those whose very houses, wealth, friends, relatives, bodies, sons, lives, and hearts are dedicated to You?”**

**Комментарий:**

Here the author gives an example of how the companions of the Lord are filled with unsurpassed love for Him. The quoted verse is from the Srimad-Bhāgavatam (10.14.35) and was spoken by Lord Brahma. "O Supreme Lord (deva), son of Nanda Maharaja, what (kim) reward (phalam) shall You give (rata) to the inhabitants of Vraja (ghosa-nivasinam), whose houses and everything else have been given to You ? Not seeing (ayat) any reward beyond Yourself (tvad-aparam) anywhere (kutrapi), since You are everything (visva-phalat), our minds become bewildered (muhyati) at the thought of what could You give to become free from debt». This is the meaning. The Lord may argue, “7 may become free from debt by giving Myself to such devotees». In this case, Brahma says, “You gave Yourself (tvam) even to PUtana and her two brothers (sakula), Baka and Aghasura». The rest of the verse is clear.

### Text 27

**Перевод:**

**As for the sweetness of the sound of Kṛṣṇa’s flute, which bewilders all moving and non-moving beings and astonishes even those who know the truth, such as Lord Brahma, the Śrimad-Bhāgavatam (10.35.15) says the following: savanasas tad upadharya suresah sakra-sarva-paramesthi-purogah, kavaya anata-kandhara-cittah kasmalam yayur aniscita-tattvah, “Hearing the wide ranged pitch of Kṛṣṇa’s flute, the leading demigods headed by Indra, Siva, and Brahma, who are allknowing, lowered their necks and minds and became perplexed, not knowing the Lord‘s real nature.” Also in the Śrimad-Bhāgavatam (10.29.40): ka stry anga te kala-padamrta-venu-gita-sammohitarya-caritan na calet tri-lokyam, trailokya-saubhagam idarh ca niriksya rūpam yad go-dvija-druma-mrgah pulakany abibhran, “Dear Kṛṣṇa, who is the woman in the three worlds who, being bewildered by the songs of Your flute with its nectarean melodies, would not deviate from her own duties? Seeing this beautiful form of Yours, which is the happiness of the three worlds, the cows, birds, trees, and wild animals have their hairs standing on end.” In this and other verses, the sweetness of His form is also described, which also bewilders all moving and non-moving beings.**

**Комментарий:**

The verse starting with’savanasas tat ‘is from the Śrimad-Bhāgavatam (10.35.15) and was spoken by the damsels of Vraja. After hearing (upadharya) that (tat) variety of low, middle, and high pitched sounds (savanasah), even the all-knowing (kavayah) leading demigods (suresah) being unaware of the Lord‘s nature (aniscita-tattvah) just became bewildered (kasmalam yayuh). What were they like? Because of that sounding music, their necks (kandhara) and minds (cittah) lowered (anata). The verse ‘ka stri, etc’. is from the Śrimad-Bhāgavatam (10.29.40) and was spoken by the gopis to Kṛṣṇa. He may ask, “Why are you so attached to another man, Me?” They reply, “O dear (anga) Kṛṣṇa, who (ka) is the woman (stri) who, being bewildered (sammohita) by the songs of Your flute with its nectarean melodies, does not deviate (na calet) from her own duties (arya-caritat) ? Because even men bewildered by Your flute become perplexed». This is the idea. The meaning of the next quarter of the verse is clear. The word cyat ‘means ‘hence’. The verbal form ‘abibhran \* is used here instead of the regular plural form ‘abibharuh ‘(they bore). “The relinquishment of duty ensues by a mere sound indicating Your presence, so what to speak of directly experiencing You ?” This is the idea.

### Text 28

**Перевод:**

**In the Śrimad-Bhāgavatam (3.2.12) it is said: yan martya-lilaupayikam sva-yoga-maya-balam darsayata grhitam, vismapanam svasya ca saubhagarddheh param padam bhusana-bhusanangam, “Showing the power of His internal potency, Lord Kṛṣṇamanifested a body suitable for His pastimes in human form. That transcendental body, the ornament of its own ornaments, the supreme abode personified beyond the greatest beauty, is astonishing also to the Lord Himself.” Verses like this describe how the sweetness of the Lord‘s form is bewildering to everyone, even to Himself.**

**Комментарий:**

The verses quoted in this and the next paragraph are from the third book of the Śrimad-Bhāgavatam and were spoken by Uddhava. That figure, the Lord‘s body (yat), was manifested (grhitam) by the Lord in this world by showing (darsayata) the power of His yoga-maya, His superior energy (sva-yoga-balam), a form that was suitable (aupayikam) for His pastimes (lila) among the humans (marjjjfa) ‘J In other words, a form very charming due to its being two-handed, whfclt was proper for performing pastimes like a human. This is the meaning.

### Text 29

**Перевод:**

**In the Śrimad-Bhāgavatam (3.2.23) it is stated: aho bakl yam stana-kalakutam jighamsayapayayad apy asadhvi, lebhe gatim dhatry ucitam tato ‘nyam kam va dayalum saranam vrajema, “Oh, which other shelter shall we take besides the merciful Kṛṣṇa? Although the wicked Putana made Him drink poison from her breast with the intention to kill Him, she attained a destination befitting a wet-nurse.” Verses like this describe His unsurpassed mercy.**

**Комментарий:**

The interjection cahoy is used to express wonder due to Kṛṣṇa’s matchless and unsurpassed mercy. Bakx is another name of Putana. “With the desire to kill (jighamsaya) Him, she made Him drink (apayayat) poison (kalakutam), with which her breasts (stana) had been smeared. Although she was wicked (asadhvi), she attained (lebhe) a destination (gatim) bejfiting (ucitam) a wet-nurse like Yasodas friends named Ambika and Kilimba. Which (kam) other (anyam) shelter (saranam) shall we take (vrajema) besides (tatah) He who gave the supreme destination to such a person ?” The idea is that even among other avataras other than Kṛṣṇasuch mercy is not manifest.

### Text 30

**Перевод:**

**The various stages of love for God named prema and so on, which is the essence of hlādini and samvit, should be understood as prominent because they ensue after meditating on the Supreme Lord‘s svarūpa, which consists of bliss and knowledge. Therefore, Kṛṣṇais the original form of the Supreme Lord, since He manifests all transcendental attributes. When all His attributes are not manifest, He Himself is known as the Lord of Vaikuṇṭha and so on. Because there is a gradation of the manifested attributes in different avataras, all the different stages starting with the most complete are said to exist in Kṛṣṇa also.**

**Комментарий:**

Someone may argue, “Why is love, a mere modification of the mind, being praised in this way ? Knowledge is actually worthy of praise. In the Moksa-dharma (Mahā-bhārata, 12.196.12) knowledge is said to be the means to perceive the Supreme: mrgair mrganam grahanam paksinam paksibhir yatha, gajanam ca gajair evam jneyam jnanena grhyate, “Deer are captured by utilizing deer (as bait); birds are captured by utilizing birds; and elephants are captured by utilizing elephants. Similarly, the Supreme Soul (the object of knowledge) is apprehended by means of knowledge». The author responds with the words ‘premadinam, etc’. By the word ‘adi ‘(etc.) are included the different stages of love, such as pranaya (attachment), sneha (affection), raga (passion), mana (jealous anger), anuraga (intense attachment), and maha-bhava (ecstatic love), which are consecutively one superior to the preceding. Knowledge in the form of meditation on the meaning of the words ‘tat ‘and ‘tvam ‘is devoid of variety, just like an unblinking glance. On the other hand, love for God is variegated like a sidelong-glance. Remember that this has been previously explained. Bhakti is certainly not a modification of the mind. As stated in the Gopāla-tapanl Upaniṣad (2.97): manusi vijnāna-ghanananda-ghana sac-cid-anandaikarase bhakti-yoge tisthati, “My form is always present in unalloyed devotional service, which consists exclusively of consciousness, eternity, and bliss’. ‘Rather, bhakti is the essence of samvit and hlādini. This has been previously concluded. Then, starting with the word ‘tasmat ‘(therefore), the author gives the sum and substance of this topic. The meaning is clear. Next, the author says that the gradation in Lord Kṛṣṇas form is described. It is stated so, not out of dull faith in Kṛṣṇa’s form, but based on fact. According to Srila Rūpa Gosvāmi, Lord Hari has a threefold manifestation — most complete, more complete, and complete.

### Text 31

**Перевод:**

**This same Supreme Lord eternally performs pastimes in His own abode, which is full of varieties accomplished by His svarūpa-śakti, and He has companions who are similar to Him. In the Chāndogya Upaniṣad - (7.24.2) it is said: sa bhagavah kasmin pratisthita iti sve mahimni, “Gargi asked, ‘O holy one, on what does Bhuma (the Supreme Being) rest? ‘Yajnavalkya replied, ‘On His own greatness’». The Muṇḍaka Upaniṣad (2.2.7) states: divye pure hy esa samvyomny atma pratisthitah, “The Supreme Soul is situated in a divine city within the spiritual sky.” In the Rg Veda (1.154.6) it is said: tam vam vastuny usmasi gamadhyai yatra gavo bhuri-srnga ayasah, atraha tad-urugayasya vrsnah parāmaṁ padam avabhati bhuri, “We desire to attain the abodes of the Divine Couple, Radha and Kṛṣṇa, where auspicious cows with excellent horns dwell eternally. The Vedas say that the supreme abode of Lord Kṛṣṇa, Who is praised by the demigods and fulfils the desires of His devotees, appears as manifold.” To be situated in a city within His own greatness would not be possible without companions similar to Him. Nor would cows that bring about good luck and auspiciousness serve any purpose without a pastime. The mantra from the Rg Veda should be interpreted in this way: ‘tam ‘means ‘those, ‘‘vam ‘means ‘of the young couple Radha and Kṛṣṇa, ‘‘vastuni ‘means ‘the abodes, ‘‘gamadhyai ‘means ‘to attain, ‘‘usmasi ‘means ‘let us desire, ‘‘bhuri-srūgah ‘means ‘with excellent horns, ‘‘vrsnah ‘means ‘fulfilling the desires of His associates’. In the Rg Veda Parisista it is stated: radhaya madhavo devo madhavenaiva radhika vibhrājante janesv a, “Surrounded by the inhabitants of Vraja in all directions, Madhava, the Supreme God, shines in the company of Radha, and Radha shines in the company of Madhava.” Since by the use of the present tense here it is expressed that the Divine Couple are eternally accompanied by Their associates, it is thus indicated that Their pastimes are eternal. In the Puruṣa-bodhini, after the words ‘yasya amso laksml-durgadika śaktih, ‘the names of Kṛṣṇa’s associates, their standing around Him, and their service to Him are shown, starting on the left with Lalita in the front western side and moving clockwise. Thus, the stated topic is very clear.**

**Комментарий:**

In this way, it has been established that the Supreme Lord, the embodied form of eternity, knowledge, and bliss, is endowed with transcendental attributes and inconceivable powers. Now, the pastimes of His associates are to be described. They certainly do not enjoy pastimes that are like an ocean without waves or immutable like a stone or a pillar. If it were so, this would contradict the śruti statements about the Lord‘s abode and associates. Therefore, in order to establish that the Lord‘s abode, pastimes, and associates are all eternal, the author starts the present paragraph. The text starting with’sa bhagavah ‘is from the Chāndogya Upaniṣad (7.24.2). The text starting with ‘divye pure ‘is from the Muṇḍaka Upaniṣad (2.2.7). The text starting with ‘tam varh ‘is from the Rg Veda (1.154.6). The affix ‘as ‘in ‘ayasa, ‘as also seen in the word ‘viprasa ‘(brāhmaṇa), is peculiar to the Vedas. According to the Amara-kośa (1.3.27), the words ‘aya, ‘‘subhavaha, ‘and ‘vidhi ‘are all synonyms of auspiciousness and good luck. The word ‘bhUri ‘is used in the secondary sense and it means ‘excellence’. Because in these verses the Lord‘s pastimes and associates may not have been clearly understood, pointing out both, the author says, “To be situated, etc». He then provides a quotation from the Puruṣa-bodhinI Upaniṣad, in which both Kṛṣṇa’s associates and their pastimes are very clear. Similarly, the following passage from the Gopāla-tapanl Upaniṣad (1.10) should also be remembered in this connection: gopa-gopl-gavavitam, “One should meditate on Kṛṣṇasurrounded by gopas, gopis, and cows. ” The topic that was stated in the first sentence of this paragraph has been thus demonstrated by the śruti.

### Text 32

**Перевод:**

**This is the truth here: The Supreme Lord, Whose body is unlimited knowledge and bliss, has a transcendental city made by a manifestation of His cit potency that is not tainted by material nature. This unlimited city is called’samvyoma, ‘the spiritual sky situated in His own greatness, and it shines like a palace on a very extensive and large territory. Therein are seen abodes of large tracts of land that are suitable to meet the wills of the various manifestations of the Lord, His companions and servants. Those abodes have residential quarters with variegated walls, courtyards, palaces, and so on, beautified with incomparable gems like sapphires, rubies, and moon-stones. Their wells, ponds, lakes, and rivers are filled with nectar and have wonderful banks made of jewels. Therein the dust is full of camphor, and the trees, creepers, and bushes exhilarate at every moment. The sky has rows of beautiful airships and flocks of lovely birds and other beings. In those abodes, the liberated souls and the eternally liberated souls endowed with transcendental forms and qualities together with the goddess of fortune, who is revered by all, by various kinds of services, propitiate the Supreme Lord, Who is amused in multiple ways.**

**Комментарий:**

Reiterating the meaning explained above in another wayy the author writes the present paragraph. The Padma Purāṇa corroborates: vaikuntha-bhuvane nitye nivasanti mahojjvalah, avatarah sada tatra matsya-kurmadayo ‘khilahy “All the highly effulgent avataras of the Lord, such as Matsya and Kūrma, always reside in the eternal abode, Vaikuṇṭha.y\> The word ‘anukulayantV means cthey perform a pleasing service.3

### Text 33

**Перевод:**

**It is similarly stated in the Jitante-stotra (2.18-21): lokam vaikuntha-namanam divya-sadgunya-samyutam, avaiṣṇavanam aprapyam guna-traya-vivarjitam, nitya-siddhaih samakirnarh tvan-mayaih pancakalikaih, sabha-prasada-samyuktaṁ vanaiscopavanaih subham, vapi-kupa-tadagais ca vrksa-sandaih sumanditam, aprākṛtām surair vandyam ayutarka-sama-prabham, prakrsta-sattva-rasim tvam kada draksyami caksusa, krldantam ramāyā sardham lila-bhumisu kesavam, “When shall I see the planetary system called Vaikuṇṭha, which is endowed with six transcendental qualities, devoid of the three material modes, and cannot be attained by the non-Vaiṣṇavas? That abode is full of eternally perfect beings who are always absorbed in You and engaged in five devotional activities, О Lord. It is all auspicious with its forests and gardens full of halls and palaces. It is well adorned with ponds, wells, lakes, and multiple trees. That abode is transcendental, praised by the demigods and effulgent like thousands of suns. When shall I see You, Lord Kesava, Whose form consists of pure goodness, playing with the goddess of fortune in the arena of Your pastimes?**

**Комментарий:**

This verse was quoted by Rūpa Gosvāmi in the Laghu-bhāgavatāmṛta (1.4-70) and attributed to the Padma Purāṇa. Its exact reference could not be verified.

Here the author quotes the smṛti. Vaikuṇṭha-loka is free from the three modes of nature (guna-traya-vivarjitam) and is transcendental (aprākṛtām). From these qualities it is clear that it is not even slightly tainted by the illusory energy. Therefore, it is understood that all objects that exists there are a manifestation of the Supreme Lord‘s svarūpa-śakti. The words ‘tvan-mayaih ‘mean ‘absorbed in You’. The word ‘pancakalikaih ‘means that they are engaged in performing five kinds of activities peculiar to five periods of the day — abhigamana (going to the temple, cleansing the temple), upadana (offering articles to the deity), ijya (worship), adhyayana (studying and reciting the scriptures), and samādhi (meditation). The words ‘prakrsta-sattva-rasim ‘mean ‘the Supreme Person, Whose svarūpa is eternity, knowledge, and bliss; ‘‘prakrsta-sattva ‘means that His form is not mixed with the mode of passion and the mode of ignorance, nor with the material mode of goodness made by the illusory energy, for all these three modes are said to be in association with each other. His form is also not mere spiritual knowledge devoid of consciousness, for this would contradict the statements that the Supreme Lord is the Supreme Soul, Whose body is eternity, knowledge, and bliss. Therefore, those who know the truth say that the Supreme Lord‘s abode is the superior energy in the form of eternity, knowledge, and bliss, which is non-different from Him.

### Text 34

**Перевод:**

**On this topic, Vyāsadeva, the revered compiler of the Brahma-sūtra, speaks the following: antara bhuta-gramavat svatmanah (3.3.36), “For the Lord‘s liberated devotee, everything within the spiritual world appears as if similar to the material elements.”**

**Комментарий:**

Here the author declares that the great sage Vyāsadeva’s conclusion is also the same. The Muṇḍaka Upaniṣad (2.2.7) states: divye pure hy esa samvyomny atma pratisthitah, “The Supreme Soul is situated in a divine city within the spiritual sky. ” In this regard, someone may argue, “Is Lord Hari’s abode the sky itself or does it have a form pervasive like the sky with variegated walls, palaces, and so on ? If this question is asked, the answer is that it must be the sky itself, since it is expressed by the word ’sky’. The idea that it is a city is metaphorical. ” In response to this, an aphorism of the Brahma-sūtra is quoted here. Objects such as walls and palaces situated within (antara) the Lord‘s city, which is the spiritual sky made of Himself, appear to His liberated devotees as if similar to the material elements. As it is said in the Kaṭha Upaniṣad (1.2.23), yam evaisa vrnute tena labhyah, The Supreme Lord is attained only by one whom He chooses. ” To a living entity who has attained the Lord‘s mercy (svatmanah), everything appears as if made of earth, water, and so on (bhuta-gramavat). By the affix ‘vat ‘(similar), the idea that things there are material is refuted. In the Muṇḍaka Upaniṣad (2.2.11) it is also said that everything there is spiritual: brahmaivedam amrtam purastat pascac ca brahma daksinatas cottarena, adhas cordhvam prasrtam brahmaivedam visvam idam varistham, “The immortal Brahman is extended in the east, in the west, in the south, in the north, below, and above. The transcendental world is Brahman itself ” This should also be understood by the statement that the Lord is situated in His own greatness.

### Text 35

**Перевод:**

**This abode of the Supreme Lord is what is expressed by words such as ‘His greatness ‘and is manifest in the form of Vaikuṇṭha, Dvārakā, Mathura, and Gokula, each one superior to the preceding. Thus, there is a distinction between the identities of the Lord‘s forms in those respective abodes. This is the conclusion of the learned who have pondered over the agama scriptures.**

**Комментарий:**

Now the author explains that the spiritual abode has distinct positions that are manifest one above the other: above Vaikuṇṭha is Dvārakā; above Dvārakā is Mathura; and above Mathura is Gokula. In the agamas such as the Svayambhuvagama, Kṛṣṇa’s planet is described as being situated above Vaikuṇṭha, In scriptures like the Brahma-samhita, Brhad-vamana Purāṇa, and Hari-vamsa, Kṛṣṇa’s planet is described as independent and named Goloka or Gokula. It is also said to have three forms — Dvārakā, Mathura, and Goloka — according to the different expansions of the Lord, Who are manifest according to the different pastimes and associates. This is also confirmed in the Skanda Purāṇa: ya yatha bhuvi vartante puryo bhagavatah priyah, tas tatha santi vaikunthe tat-tal-lilartham adrtah, “Whatever cities exist on earth which are dear to the Supreme Lord, also exist in Vaikuṇṭha, are meant for those respective pastimes, and are praised by Lord Brahma and other demigods. "As for the description in the Uttara-khanda of the Padma Purāṇa, where, after enumerating the places of the ten avataras surrounding Maha-vaikuntha in the ten directions gradually starting from the east, among which Lord Kṛṣṇais said to be the ninth and His place is said to surround the upper region, this just confirms that Kṛṣṇa’s abode is the highest of all. Although Kṛṣṇa’s abode is the highest of all, its description as surrounding Vaikuṇṭha is understood as such because Vaikuṇṭha is the topic dealt with in the Padma Purāṇa. Otherwise, many scriptures like the Svayambhuvagama would be contradicted. The distinction between the different forms of the Lord should be seen in the Kṛṣṇa-sandarbha.

### Text 36

**Перевод:**

**The same abodes meant for those respective pastimes of the Lord are also manifest in the universe ruled by Brahma, as stated in the Skanda Purāṇa: ya yatha bhuvi vartante puryo bhagavatah priyah, tas tatha santi vaikunthe tat-tal-lilartham adrtah, “Whatever cities exist on earth which are dear to the Supreme Lord, also exist in Vaikuṇṭha, are meant for those respective pastimes, and are praised by Lord Brahma and other demigods.” These abodes do not appear divine only to those who do not have pure vision, like those who see the Supreme Lord as an ordinary human boy. Yet by seeing them as transcendental, one attains the higher planets, just as one who sees Kṛṣṇaas the Supreme Lord. This is corroborated by the Brahma-sūtra (3-3.53) and the Govinda-bhāṣya: na samanyad apy upalabdher mrtyuvan na hi lokapattih, “By an ordinary perception of the Supreme Lord one does not attain liberation, just as by mere death one does not become liberated. One attains only the heavenly planets.” Therefore, the Supreme Lord eternally performs pastimes with His associates in those abodes. It is thus proved that the Lord‘s pastimes manifested during the balya, pauganda, and kaisora ages are all eternal.**

**Комментарий:**

Those abodes in the spiritual sky that belong to Matsya, Кurmа, and other avataras also appear in the material world in a similar way. Therefore, those abodes are transcendental. This is what the author explains here. The word ‘adrtah ‘means ‘praised by Brahma and other demigods’. The word ‘asu ‘refers to the Supreme Lord‘s cities within the universe. The words ‘asamkrta-drsam ‘refer to those whose intelligence has not been purified by bhakti. They think that the Supreme Lord is like a human boy under the influence of the illusory energy. The words ‘tadrsa-bhagavad-drstya ‘mean ‘by seeing the Supreme Lord as an ordinary human boy, although He is visible before their eyes’. The quoted aphorism is from the Brahma-sūtra (3-3.53). Its meaning is expressed in the Muṇḍaka Upaniṣad (2.2.9): bhidyate hrdaya-granthis chidyante sarva-samsayah, ksiyante casya karmani tasmin drste paravare, “When the Supreme Soul, Who is both the cause and the effect, is seen, the knots in the heart are pierced, all doubts are removed, and all karma is destroyed». The Śrimad-Bhāgavatam (10.86.49) also says that by the vision of the Supreme Lord one attains liberation: etad-anto nrnam kleso yad bhavan aksi-gocarah, “When You become visible to one’s eyes, this marks the end of one’s suffering». Someone may argue, “This is not correct because although many people see the Lord at the time when He descends, there is no certainty that they will attain liberation». The author replies to this by quoting the Brahma-sūtra (3-3-53)-The word ‘api ‘in used for emphasis. The affix ‘at ‘in the word ’samanyat ‘is employed in the sense of the instrumental case according to Panini’s sūtra

7.1.39. Liberation does not ensue by an ordinary perception of the Supreme Lord as someone covered by the curtain of the illusory energy, just as by mere death (mrtyuvat) one does not attain liberation. “Then such an ordinary perception of the Lord is useless». The sūtra says that it is not useless (na hi). “Then what is the result?" The result is the attainment of the heavenly planets (lokapattih), as stated in the smṛti: samanya-darsanal loka muktir yogyatma-darsanat, “The vision of the Lord by an ordinary person leads to the heavenly planets, while His vision by a qualified soul leads to liberation.” When a qualified soul (yogyatma) sees the Supreme Lord‘s body as ‘cit ‘and the supreme object of love, then the result is liberation. This is the meaning.

### Text 37

**Перевод:**

**In this regard, some raise the following objection: “If there were no different places, times, and associates in the Lord‘s pastimes, only then they could be eternal, but this is not possible because there are different places and times, and also because by the previous and subsequent actions of an associate there would be a connection with several pastimes. If the previous pastime is eternal, then the connection of the associate connected with that pastime must also be eternal. If so, his connection with the subsequent pastime cannot happen. If we admit the connection with the subsequent pastime, then the previous one is not eternal. If the previous pastime is eternal, the associate connected with the subsequent one must be different. Therefore, your belief that the Lord‘s pastimes are eternal is a hallucination like a flower in the sky.”**

**Комментарий:**

“In this regard, ” i.e., regarding the eternity of the Lord‘s pastimes, some logicians who say that words, thoughts, and actions last for three moments, now bring up another objection. “The Supreme Lord and His associates are certainly not like a pillar or a stone, in one place and at one time. It is understood that pastimes like eating at home, tending the cows, and performing the rasa dance are made possible by different places, different times, and different associates. During previous pastimes in the balya andpauganda phases, and during subsequent pastimes in the kaisora phase, Kṛṣṇahas the same associates fondling Him, such as His father. In previous and subsequent pastimes like eating at home, tending the cows, and wrestling, the same friends are present. It must be supposed that in previous and subsequent pastimes in union and separation, the same girlfriends and servants are present. The wise should ponder over the pastimes of Lord Rama and other avataras in a similar way. ” Thus, being demonstrated that by the previous and subsequent activities of an associate in the pastimes there is difference in that associate, the opponent shows the flaw that results on this account. The words ‘tat-sambandhinah ‘refer to an associate who is part of the previous pastime, such as Kṛṣṇas father. “If it is so, ” i.e., “if it is admitted that a particular associate has an eternal connection with the previous pastime. ” The word ‘tasya’ refers to an associate who is part of the previous pastime, such as His father. “If it is admitted that an associate like His father has a connection with the subsequent pastime, then the previous pastime that happened in connection with him is not eternal. Or else, if it is admitted that the previous pastime that happened in connection with him is eternal, then in the subsequent pastime His father must be someone else. However, we do not have such experience. Similarly, by the appearance of more and more pastimes one after the other, the previous ones would also disappear one after the other, and consequently the pastimes are not eternal. ” This is the idea.

### Text 38

**Перевод:**

**In response, we say that you (the opponent) are ignorant of the inconceivable truth regarding the Supreme Lord, which is understood from the Upaniṣads. As stated in the Chāndogya Upaniṣad (7.23.1) ‘bhumaiva sukham bhuma tv eva jijnasitavyah, “Bhuma, the Supreme Lord, is happiness. He is to be inquired upon.” Referring to the Supreme Lord in this way, His characteristics are then mentioned: yatra nanyat pasyati nanyac chrnoti nanyad vijanati sa bhuma, “Upon realizing whom one sees, hears, and knows nothing else, He is the Supreme Lord.” Here, such idea is rejected, according to which one who has attained the Supreme Lord sees, hears, or knows anything but Him. After that, rejecting the idea of seeing anything awful, such as death, it is again stated that one who worships the Lord sees everything: na pasyo mrtyum pasyati na rogam nota duhkhitam, sarvam ha pasyah pasyati sarvam apnoti sarvasah, “A devotee who meditates on Bhuma, the Supreme Lord, never sees death, nor disease, nor suffering. Such a devotee sees everything. He attains everything thoroughly.” The Lord‘s pastimes in the balya, pauganda, and kaisora ages, which are full of manifold variegatedness, are here expressed by the word’sarva ‘(everything). According to one’s qualification, one fully sees those pastimes and attains them by taking part in them as an associate of His. Here, the manifoldness and variegatedness are very clear by the mutual distinction between the pastimes in the balya and pauganda ages and by the evident twofold aspects of the pastimes in the kaisora age as union and separation.**

**Комментарий:**

Here the author refutes the objection that was raised above. The text from the Chāndogya Upaniṣad was spoken by Sanat-kumara to Nārada. The Supreme Lord, Whose attribute is vastness (bhuma) and Whose form is happiness (sukham), should be enquired upon (jijnasitavyah). When (yatra) the Supreme Lord is being experienced, one who has realised Him does not see anything else but Him, just as one who has submerged in the ocean does not see anything but water. The word ‘tasya ‘refers to one who worships the Lord. The next quotations are from the same source in the same conversation between Sanat-kumara and Nārada. The word ‘pasyah ‘means ‘a devotee who meditates on the Supreme Lord’. The devotee does not see worldly features such as death, disease, and suffering, but having attained the Supreme Lord, one sees everything as His attribute. If the Supreme Lord, Bhūma, were devoid of attributes, the employment of the word ’sarva ‘would be meaningless. Therefore, His qualities and activities are understood by the word ’sarva’. The author explains this saying, “The Lord‘s pastimes, etc.” The words ’sphuta-dvaidhatvat ‘mean ‘by the evident twofold aspects’. The rule pertaining to the affix ‘dha ‘here is the one stated in the Astadhyay! (5.3.45): dvi-tryos ca dhamun, “After the words ‘dvi ‘and ‘tri, ‘the affix ‘dha ‘is optionally replaced by ‘dham. The affix ‘da ‘is seen at the end of the affix ‘dham ‘in the same meaning. This is how the word ‘dvaidhatvat ‘is formed.

### Text 39

**Перевод:**

**“Because of the mutual difference of time and place, an associate cannot be simultaneously present in many pastimes happening, which are different from each other, therefore how is it possible that one can see and take part in them in every respect? Or else, if this is not possible, then how can the pastimes that happened with their participation be eternal ? ” In case such a doubt is raised, the Chāndogya Upaniṣad (7.26.2) subsequently declares: sa ekadha bhavati dvidha bhavati tridha bhavati pancadha saptadha navadha caiva punas caikadāsa smrtah satam ca dāsa fflilrarh ca sahasrani ca vimsatih, “The liberated soul becomes one; he becomes two; he becomes three; he becomes five, seven, and nine; he becomes one hundred and eleven; he becomes twenty thousand.” It is here described that on the strength of mystic perfection, the liberated soul that wishes to take part in the Lord‘s pastimes assumes many forms. Because the devotee is present in those forms in several pastimes, he is one and the same person in all of those pastimes, hence it is demonstrated that he is not a different person. Therefore, there is nothing impossible here.**

**Комментарий:**

The Supreme Lord‘s pastimes have been demonstrated with regard to Him. Now the non-difference of the places, times, and associates, all of which are part of those pastimes, is demonstrated. The word ‘tasu ‘refers to those pastimes. “If this is not possible, ” i.e., “if due to the connection with a particular time and a particular place, an associate cannot be simultaneously present in many pastimes happening». The pastimes that happened with their participation,” i.e., “the pastimes that were accomplished in connection with a particular place, a particular time, and a particular associate». The author demonstrates the simultaneous presence of an associate in many pastimes by quoting the Chāndogya Upaniṣad (7.26.2). The word ’sah ‘refers to the liberated soul, a devotee of the Supreme Lord, Bhuma. The mystic perfection referred to is the power to make one’s will come true. The words ‘lila-prepsoh ‘mean ‘of one who is desirous of witnessing all pastimes’. The word ’sarvatra ‘means Kin all pastimes’. The words ‘tad-aviccheda-siddhih ‘mean ‘the demonstration of one’s presence in all pastimes’. It should be understood that although from the context it is clear that the quotation from the Chāndogya Upaniṣad refers to one who became liberated by performing sadhana, it must be also applied to the eternally liberated souls and all ranks of associates of the Lord, either because it is implied, or in view that how much more this is true in their case.

### Text 40

**Перевод:**

**The Supreme Lord‘s svarūpa distinguished by such transcendental pastimes is named ‘rasa/ The Taittiriya Upaniṣad (2.7) declares that one who attains this rasa has supreme bliss: raso vai sah, rasam hy evayam labdhvanandi bhavati, “The Supreme Lord is rasa personified. One who obtains this rasa becomes blissful.” Just as union with Him is rasa, separation from Him is also rasa, and hence a form of the Supreme Lord. Therefore, knowledge of rasa in separation is also God realization. This is the perfection of God realization at all times. The eternal pastimes are distinguished by both union and separation. In union there is external experience, and in separation there is experience only within the heart. Thus, even though there is a connection at all times, it is possible for Kṛṣṇato behave in various ways, such as when He promises, “See you again in the evening. Now give Me a hug.” This conclusion corroborates the meaning of the quoted śruti. The abode and the time in the Lord‘s pastimes, which are of the same nature as His, are present everywhere in them. This will be explained later. Logic has no access to something that is inconceivable and transcendental. Due to the Lord‘s exalted powers, this is not a fault, but rather an ornament.**

**Комментарий:**

The quoted texts are from the Taittiriya Upaniṣad (2.7). The Supreme Lord is the five kinds of rasas personified. The word ‘ayam ‘refers to one who worships Him. Someone may argue, “ When the Supreme Lord is engaged in tending the cows and so on, the devotees who stay at home do not see Him. ” Solving this doubt, the author says, “Just as union, etc». The words ‘taj-jnānam ‘refer to knowledge about separation. The word ‘tasya ‘means 1 of the Supreme Lord’. The words ‘tena tenay mean lby both union and separation’. It is thus possible for Kṛṣṇato behave in various ways, as He does with His devotees who stay at home when He is going to take the cows to graze and says, “See you in the evening when I come back. Now give Me a hug. ” The reason is that by union and separation there is increasing rasa. This is the idea.

### Text 41

**Перевод:**

**The logicians raise another objection — “Because there is action and parts in a pastime, it must be said to have a beginning and an end, without which its own nature would be lost. Therefore, how can it be eternal?”**

**Комментарий:**

The logicians again object, “Let the Supreme Lord‘s pastimes be manifold and connected with particular places, times, and associates. Yet they cannot be eternal, since they have a beginning and an end. ” The meaning is clear.

### Text 42

**Перевод:**

**In response, we say: Within the Supreme Lord there are eternally infinite forms, infinite prakāśa manifestations, infinite pastimes, and infinite individualities and manifestations of places of pastimes in unlimited material universes and Vaikuṇṭha planets and of associates who take part in them, therefore His pastimes cannot be non-eternal. Although the pastimes in connection with a particular form or manifestation of the Lord have a beginning and an end, when some particular parts of a pastime may have ended or not in a particular manifestation, they may have begun in another manifestation. Thus, the pastimes exist eternally within the Lord, for they have no interruption. A pastime may start sometimes with some differences and sometimes in the same way, depending on whether the peculiarities are the same or are different.**

**Комментарий:**

The author answers the above objection here. There are infinite forms within the Supreme Lord, as stated in the following śruti and smṛti: eko ‘pisan bahudhayo ‘vabhati(Gopāla-tāpani Upaniṣad, 1.19), “Although one, the Supreme Lord appears as many.” ekaneka-svarūpaya sthūla-sūksmatmane namah (Viṣṇu Purāṇa, 1.2.3), “Obeisances unto the Supreme Lord, Who has multiple forms and is the soul of both the subtle and the gross.” His pastimes are infinite, as stated in the Śrimad-Bhāgavatam (10.51.36): janma-karmabhidhanani santi me ‘nga sahasrasah, na sakyante ‘nusamkhyatum anantatvan mayapi hi, “Dear Mucukunda, I have thousands of births, activities, and names which cannot be counted even by Myself, for they are unlimited». There are infinite material universes, as stated in the smṛti such as the Viṣṇu Purāṇa (2.7.27): andanath tu sahasranam sahasrany ayutani ca, tadrsanam tatha tatra koti-koti-satani ca, “There are thousands, millions, and trillions of such material universes». There are infinite Vaikuṇṭha planets, as stated in the Rg Veda (l. 154.6): tad-urugayasya vrsnah parāmaṁ padam avabhati bhuri, “The supreme abode of Lord Kṛṣṇa, Who is praised by the demigods and fulfils the desires of His devotees, appears as manifold. ” There are infinite associates, as stated in the Chāndogya Upaniṣad (7.26.2): sa ekadha bhavati dvidha bhavati tridha bhavati pancadha saptadha navadha caiva punas caikadāsa smrtah satam ca dāsa caikam ca sahasrani ca vimsatih, “The liberated soul becomes one; he becomes two; he becomes three; he becomes five, seven, and nine; he becomes one hundred and eleven; he becomes twenty thousand. ” In this way, having established the infinite forms, names, pastimes, and associates of the Supreme Lord, the author corroborates that for His pastimes to be eternal, they must be uninterrupted, “Although the pastimes, etc». The words ‘ekatraikatra ‘mean ‘in a single manifestation’. The words ‘anyatranyatra ‘mean (in another manifestation’. The words ‘tad-avicchedaty mean ‘because the activities have no interruption.3 Sometimes (kvacit) He is sitting on the lap of His mother; sometimes He is sitting on a golden throne; sometimes He is eating fresh butter; sometimes He is laughing. Thus, the difference between the peculiarities of one manifestation and the other should be understood.

### Text 43

**Перевод:**

**Someone may argue, “Fine. Let the pastimes be uninterrupted. Yet how is a pastime said to be the same? Since it has a different beginning, it must be different.” In response to this we say the following: Although they are said to have different times, there is oneness in activities of a similar form. Just as there are others who have also ascertained the oneness of words based on the following understanding: dvir go-sabdo ‘yam uccarito na tu dvau go-śabdau, “When the word ‘cow ‘is uttered twice, the word ‘cow ‘is only one, and not two.”  It should also be understood that when it is said that someone has cooked twice, the act of cooking is only one, and not two. Similarly, the activity in a particular pastime is only one. The same is also stated in the Bṛhad-āraṇyaka Upaniṣad (3-8.4): yad gatam bhavac ca bhavisyac ca, “Past, present, and future are situated within the Supreme Lord.” In the Puruṣa-bodhini Upaniṣad (4) it is said: eko devo nitya-lilanurakto bhakta-vyapl bhakta-hrdy-antaratma, “The same Supreme Lord is engaged in eternal pastimes, pervades the devotees, and is also the Supreme Soul within the hearts of the devotees.” The Bhagavad-gita (4.9) also confirms: janma karma ca me divyam evam yo vetti tattvatah, tyaktva deham punar janma naiti mam eti so ‘rjuna, “One who factually knows My transcendental appearance and activities in this way does not, upon leaving the body, take birth again in this material world but attains Me, О Arjuna.” Such understanding, however, is very confidential and can be attained only by the Lord‘s mercy, as stated by Him in the Śrimad-Bhāgavatam (2.9.32): yavan aharh yatha-bhavo yad-rūpa-guna-karmakah, tathaiva tattva-vijnānam astu te mad-anugrahat, “By My mercy, may you have factual knowledge about Me as I am, with form, qualities, and activities.”**

**Комментарий:**

The oneness between the places, times, and associates, which are part of the pastimes, has been previously established. Now the oneness of the pastimes that have the same form is established, although they may be said to be different. In this connection, the opponent raises a doubt, “Yet how is, etc.” Then a couple of illustrations in conformity with the grammarians ‘view are stated. The word ‘others y refers to the Māyāvādis. On the aphorism ’śabda iti cet...’ (Brahma-sūtra, 1.3.28) they reflected upon such illustrations as having a figurative meaning. The text ‘yadgatam’ is from the Bṛhad-āraṇyaka Upaniṣad (3.8.4). Things related to the Supreme Lord which are characterized as His qualities and activities are eternal, for His permanence in all the three times is expressed by the words past, present, and future. The text ‘eko devah’ is from the Puruṣa-bodhini Upaniṣad, which belongs to the Pippalāda branch of the Atharva Veda. The verse ‘janma karma’ is from the Bhagavad-gita (4.9). The word ‘divyam’ means ‘transcendental, intrinsic to My nature, ‘for it is said that one who knows it attains liberation. “Such understanding, ” i.e., the comprehension that the Lord‘s activities are of His same transcendental nature. The verse ‘yavan aham’ is from the Śrimad-Bhāgavatam (2.9.32) and was spoken by the Supreme Lord to Brahma. The word ‘yavan’ means ‘Whose dimension’. Even if He is manifest as medium-sized. His dimension is characterized by its all-pervasiveness. The words ‘yatha-bhavah’ mean ‘of such existence’, ‘of such nature, ‘and ‘of such associates/ According to the Medini-kośa (Va, 20), the word ‘bhava’ means existence and nature. The rest is clear.

### Text 44

**Перевод:**

**Moreover, it is said in the Chāndogya Upaniṣad (7.26.2): na pasyo mrtyum pasyati na rogam nota duhkhitam, sarvam ha pasyah pasyati sarvam apnoti sarvasah, “ A devotee who meditates on Bhuma, the Supreme Lord, never sees death, nor disease, nor suffering. Such a devotee sees everything. He attains everything thoroughly.” Here, it is established that an associate of the Lord simultaneously sees all pastimes and takes part in all of them. Therefore, time is a form of the Lord in the pastimes, manifest in conformity with them as mere day and night, accomplished by the rising and setting of the sun, the moon, and the stars, which are also His forms. Therein time does not exist in the form of cyclic years, months, and so forth. The reason is that time being so, all the seasons are simultaneously manifest, and the rasas of all respective pastimes are accomplished. It is only in conformity to the Lord‘s pastimes that the parts of time become manifest and unmanifest in them. In this regard, the Brahma-samhita (5.56) says as follows: sa yatra ksirabdhih sarati surabhlbhyas ca sumahan nimesardhakhyo va vrajati na hi yatrapi samayah, bhaje sveta-dvipam tam aham iha golokam iti yam vidantas te santah ksiti-virala-carah katipaye, “I worship Goloka, also known as Sveta-dvipa, which only a few rare saintly persons on earth know, and where the great ocean of milk flows from Surabhi cows. Time known by its fractions of a moment does not pass there.” Similarly, the Lord‘s abode, which is another form of His, is also connected with all pastimes, as it is said by the wise Rūpa Gosvāmi in the Laghu-bhāgavatāmṛta (1.5-515) ‘- atah prabhoh priyanam ca dhamnas ca samayasya ca, avicintya-prabhavatvan natra kincit sudurghatam, “The Lord, His beloved devotees, His abode, and spiritual time — all of these have inconceivable power, on account of which nothing is impossible.” Therefore, the sun, the moon, and other celestial bodies in His abode are different than the material ones. Yet they are perceived by those taking part in the pastimes as being similar to the material ones.**

**Комментарий:**

It was previously said, “The abode and the time in the Lord‘s pastimes, which are of the same nature as His, are present everywhere in them. This will be explained later». The author explains this topic now. The

time situated in the eternal abode of the Lord is a form of His and is different from material time, for it is able to simultaneously and successively accomplish multiple tasks, just as the Lord. Material time may accomplish multiple tasks of a single person successively, but time as a form of the Lord accomplishes all simultaneously and is therefore different from material time. The words ‘tad-rūpa ‘mean ‘a form of His’. The words ‘tad-ista-tat-tal-lilanugunyavan ‘mean that time accomplishes the pastimes as desired by the Supreme Lord. “Time being so, ” i.e., “time being merely day and night». The parts of time become manifest only in a way favourable to the pastimes (tad-anugunataya). The verse ’sa yatra ‘is from the Brahma-samhita (5.56). Time does not pass because its parts, such as dawn, morning, midday, etc., are situated in the parts of the Lord‘s abode. Goloka is called ’sveta-dvipa’. It is a ‘dvipa ‘(planetary division or island) because it is very high, and it is very bright (sveta) because it is devoid of the contamination of the illusory energy. This is not the abode of Lord Aniruddha situated in the milk ocean, for the words ‘ihagolokam ‘(to this place called Goloka) are stated. The words ‘tad-atmakasya ‘mean ‘a form of the Supreme Lord’. The word ‘vrddhaih ‘(wise) refers to Śri Rupa Gosvāmi. The compound ‘lilasthaih ‘mean the devotees who are taking part in the manifest pastimes. The sun, the moon, and so on in the Lord‘s abode are similar to the well-known material sun, moon, and so on, but not material. This is the meaning.

### Text 45

**Перевод:**

**Therefore, in a single day, Nārada Muni saw pastimes to be performed in many days, as Śukadeva described in the verses starting with ‘narakam nihatam srutva tathodvaham ca yositam (Śrimad-Bhāgavatam, 10.69.l),y “Having heard that Lord Kṛṣṇa killed Narakasura and alone married thousands of ladies, Nārada desired to see that.” Nor can it be said that he saw those pastimes in many days, for in this case he would not have become astonished by seeing such a great display created by yoga-maya.**

**Комментарий:**

Corroborating what was previously mentioned, the author says, “Therefore, ” i.e., “on account of the assistance of spiritual time, which is a form of the Supreme Lord. ” Due to spiritual time manifest as dawn, morning, and so on, Nārada saw the pastimes of particular timings in a single day. This is the meaning.

### Text 46

**Перевод:**

**In the spiritual world there is no material sun and moon, and no material time. As stated in the Śvetāśvatara Upaniṣad (6.14): na tatra suryo bhati na candra-tarake nema vidyuto bhanti kuto ‘yam agnih, “In the spiritual world the sun does not shine, nor the moon, the stars, or lightning, what to speak of fire.” The Kaṭha Upaniṣad (1.2.25) corroborates: mrtyur yasyopasecanam ka ittha veda yatra sah, “Who really knows the place where He, for Whom death is the topping, lives?” The Śrimad-Bhāgavatam (2.2.17) affirms: na yatra kalo ‘nimisam parah prabhuh kuto nu deva jagatam ya isire, “In the spiritual world, time, the controller of the demigods, has no influence, what to speak of the demigods, who are the rulers of the material universes.” The Viṣṇu Purāṇa (4.1.84) also confirms: kala-muhurtadi-mayas ca kalo na yad-vibhuteh parinama-hetuh, “He by Whose power time, which consists of seconds, minutes, and so forth, does not cause any alteration.” Therefore, those who live in the spiritual world do not have any particular condition dependent on time.**

**Комментарий:**

Someone may argue, “It is not possible to accept that the time which is a form of the Lord and is different than material time accomplishes the pastimes in His eternal abode, for no evidence of this is seen. ” The author responds to this here. The word ‘tatra ‘means ‘in the eternal abode.\* The word ‘devah’ refers to the all-worshipable Supreme Soul. He is the benefactor of the living entities (bhuta-bhavanah) by His own will (kratubhih), that means He protects His devotees by various pastimes accomplished by time, which is a form of His. The meaning of the text ‘na tatra suryah ‘is clear. The text ‘mrtyur yasya ‘is from the Kaṭha Upaniṣad (1.2.25). All-devouring death is a part of time. “He for Whom (yasya) the universe is food, and death is a topping (upasecanam) such as yogurt». This is the meaning. The verse ‘na yatra kalahy is from the Srimad-Bhāgavatam (2.2.17). Time (kalah) is the supreme (parah) controller (prabhuh) of the demigods (animisam). Time has no influence in the spiritual world, so what to speak of the demigods who are controlled by it. This is the meaning. The verse ‘kala-muhurtadi’ is from the Viṣṇu Purāṇa (4.1.84). The word ‘tatratyanam ‘refers to those who live in the Lord‘s abode. They have no condition dependent on time, such as old age and death. According to the Amara-kośa (1.3.29), the word ‘avastha ‘means a particular condition dependent on time. Some say that material time also exists in the eternal abode but exerts no influence there, because this is the meaning understood from the above verse of the Śrimad-Bhāgavatam, and because no evidence to the contrary is found.

### Text 47

**Перевод:**

**In this way, Lord Hari’s phases such as childhood are eternal, and the pastimes connected with them are also eternal, which are all to be experienced by all His associates according to their qualification. This is so stated by a couple of aphorisms in the Brahma-sūtra (З-З-10-И)-. vyaptes ca samanjasam, “And because the Supreme Lord is all-pervading, meditation on His different features in childhood, youth, and so on, is appropriate.” sarvabhedad anyatreme, “Since the Lord‘s associates are also non-different, they can be simultaneously present elsewhere.” This is also similarly explained in the Govinda-bhāṣya.**

**Комментарий:**

Here the author concludes the present subject matter. “In this way, ” i.e., in the way previously spoken. In this regard, he quotes the conclusion of the great sage Vyāsadeva in two aphorisms. The meaning of the aphorism ‘vyaptes ca samanjasam ‘is as follows: In the Gopāla Upaniṣad and the Rama Upaniṣad it is heard that the Supreme Lord has attributes such as childhood and so on. Are they to be meditated upon or not ? In case of such a doubt, if it is assumed that they should not be meditated upon because those attributes imply a state of decrease and increase in the Supreme Lord‘s body, which contradicts the scriptural statements that He is immutable, the answer is that their meditation is appropriate because even while holding the attributes of childhood and so on, He is all-pervasive and devoid of decrease and increase. The meaning of the aphorism’sarvabhedad anyatreme ‘is as follows: Someone may argue, “Since the Lord‘s activities in childhood and so on have a beginning and an end, they are understood to be non-eternal, therefore what is the use in meditating on them?” The answer is that it must be admitted that Lord Hari, His devotees, and the parts of an activity that were present in a previous activity or time must be the same ones present in a subsequent activity or time. Why ? Because there is no difference in Lord Hari, nor in the devotees taking part in a previous and subsequent activity, nor between the parts of an activity happening in a previous and subsequent time, and thus, since all of them remain the same in previous and subsequent phases, they are all eternal, hence meditation of the Lord‘s features in different phases is appropriate. Particular details should be seen in the Govinda-bhāṣya on these aphorisms.

### Text 48

**Перевод:**

**The following is to be understood: the Lord‘s pastimes enacted by His svarūpa and cit potency are eternal, while those enacted by His svarūpa, material nature, and time are occasional, such as when the Lord creates the universe and when He kills demons. Otherwise, the scriptural descriptions of the material annihilation would be contradictory. Someone may argue, “In the annihilation of the material universe, all material elements perish, thus how can the pastimes visibly manifest in this world be eternal?” This argument does not apply, for even though the attribute of being perceivable within the material world disappears, its subject — the pastimes — does not vanish, just as when someone’s tuft of hair is cut off, it is said, “The one with the tuft of hair is finished.” Indeed, the position of those who know logic is as follows: saviśeṣane vidhi-nisedhau viśeṣanam upaśaiḥkramatah, “Affirmations or negations about a qualified subject change only its predicate.”**

**Комментарий:**

Here the author speaks about a certain peculiarity in the Lord‘s pastimes. The ‘enacted pastimes ‘referred to here should be understood as those in the eternal abode. The word ‘tena ‘means ‘by His svarūpa’. Next, the referred pastimes should be known as those performed in the material world. The word ‘kadacitki’ means ‘occasional’. “Otherwise,” i.e., “if is accepted that the pastimes like the creation of the universe are eternal. ” In the Viṣṇu-dharmottara Purāṇa (1.77.9-10) it is said: anantani tavoktani yany andani māyā purd, sarvani tani samhrtya sama-kdlam jagat-patih, prākṛtāu tisthati tada sa ratris tasya kirtita, “I have previously spoken to you that there are unlimited universes. After simultaneously destroying all of them, the Lord of the universe remains situated in His own nature. This is called His night. ” The words ‘tad-gocarayah ‘mean ‘beingsituated within the material world’. The words ‘tad-gocaratayah ‘mean ‘its attribute of being situated within the material world’. The words ‘tad-vyakteh ‘mean ‘of the subject in the form of the pastimes’. Just as a brāhmaṇa is not annihilated when his tuft of hair is cut off, similarly is the case here. The conclusion of the learned is the evidence on this matter, therefore the author quotes the principle ’saviśeṣane, etc’.

### Text 49

**Перевод:**

**In this way, twofold bhakti — that performed according to rules and regulations (sadhana) and that performed out of spontaneous love (ruci) — is the cause for accomplishing both the ultimate attainment of happiness and removal of distress. Such bhakti is produced by twofold knowledge, respectively having the Lord as its object in the aiśvarya mood and in the mādhurya mood, either towards His full manifestation as the Supreme Personality, Who is a reservoir of qualities such as opulence, sweetness, and magnanimity, Whose abodes and pastimes are eternal, and Who is designated by the words ’svayam bhagavan, ‘or towards His partial manifestations understood by words such as ‘vilāsa’. It is only by His will that different living entities engage in one or the other kind of bhakti. Among these two categories of devotees, those engaged in spontaneous loving devotional service are the best, as demonstrated by Vyāsadeva, the compiler of the Brahma-sūtra (З.З.29-31): chandata ubhayavirodhat, “By the Lord‘s will, it is not impossible that the devotees engage in either of these paths, for the scriptures concur with both of them.” gater arthavattvam ubhayathanyatha hi virodhah, “The goal can be attained by both paths of bhakti, for otherwise the scriptures would be contradictory.” upapannas tal-lakṣaṇārthopalabdher lokavat, “One endowed with love is superior, for by love it is possible to conquer the Supreme Lord, Who is attached to such devotees, just as in this world one who is expert in propitiating the king is praised for bringing him under control.” This is similarly described in the Govinda-bhāṣya. Thus, all that has been previously stated is very consistent.**

**Комментарий:**

**Thus ends the second chapter of the introduction to the Govinda-bhāṣya, named Siddhānta-ratnaṁ, whose topic is the ascertainment of the Supreme Lord‘s qualities, such as aiśvarya and mādhurya.**

With the words ‘tad evam, etc., ‘the author concludes the major topic under discussion. The meaning is clear. The word ‘tayoh ‘refers to the ultimate attainment of happiness and removal of distress. The words ‘tad-icchaya ‘mean ‘by the will of the Supreme Person’. The word ‘uttare ‘refers to the devotees in spontaneous love. The meaning of the aphorism ‘chandata ubhayavirodhat ‘is as follows: It is heard from the scriptures that both vidhi-bhakti and ruci-bhakti lead to the Supreme Lord. Out of the two, which will make one attain Him ? If it is assumed that on account of such uncertainty it is impossible to engage in either, Vyāsadeva says, ‘chandatah, etc’. The word ‘na ‘(not) is supplied from a preceding aphorism. By the Lord‘s wish (chandatah), which follows one’s association with a particular type of devotee, the living entities who associate with a devotee engage in either type of bhakti according to that devotee’s will. Therefore, engagement in devotional service is not impossible. Why? Because the scriptures concur to both types of bhakti. If it is accepted to be so, the next aphorism states that in order to attain the Lord, both vidhi-bhakti, which is manifested by His opulence, and ruci-bhakti, which is manifested by His sweetness, are fruitful. Otherwise, the scriptural statement propounding vidhi-bhakti and ruci-bhakti would be mutually contradictory. Who is the best? A devotee who performs vidhi-bhakti, or one who performs ruci-bhakti ? In case of such a doubt, if it is assumed that the former is the best because he is ornamented by rules and regulations, the next aphorism says that the latter is the best (upapannah). Why? Because the Supreme Lord, the son of Mother Yasoda, is the object of ruci-bhakti (artha), and He is characterized (tal-lakṣaṇā) by His attachment for such devotees. This is because He is attained (upalabdheh) by submission, just as in this world (lokavat) one who is expert in propitiating the king is praised for bringing him under control.

Thus, the second chapter, entitled ‘Kaumodaki, ‘has been explained in this commentary on the introduction to the Govinda-bhāṣya.

## Third Chapter – Sudarsana

### Text 1

**Перевод:**

**It should be understood that this Supreme Lord is the Master of all, the Supreme Person without equal or superior. He is endowed with superior energy, devoid of the six kinds of transformations such as birth, and is distinguished among the demigods. As stated in the Śvetāśvatara Upaniṣad (6.7-9) ‘- tarn isvaranam parāmaṁ mahesvaram tam devatanam parāmaṁ ca daivatam, patim patinam parāmaṁ parastad vidama devam bhuvanesam idyam, na tasya karyam karanam ca vidyate na tat-samas cabhyadhikas ca drsyate, parasya śaktir vividhaiva sruyate svabhaviki jnāna-bala-kriya ca, na tasya kascit patir asti loke na cesita naiva ca tasya lingam, sa karanam karanadhipadhipo na casya kascij janita na cadhipah, “May we know the Great Lord, the supreme of all lords, the Supreme Deity of the demigods, the Supreme Master above all masters, the Supreme God, the worshipable Master of the world. He has no material body and senses. No one is equal or superior to Him. It is heard from the scriptures that the Supreme has multifarious intrinsic superior energies in the form of knowledge, power, and action. No one in the world is His master or controller. He has no characteristic mark either. He is the ultimate cause, the ruler of those who rule over the senses. No one is His father or king.”**

**Комментарий:**

Obeisances unto Lord Kṛṣṇa, the destroyer of hurdles. Being pleased by knowledge and devotion, the Supreme Lord, Whose body is eternal bliss and knowledge, and Whose abode, associates, and pastimes are all eternal, gives everything to His devotee, up to Himself This has been demonstrated in the previous two chapters. This conclusion would be firmly established if those who are opposed to it and propound that God and all demigods are one would give up such ideas. Therefore, the third chapter begins to make them desist from this idea. The word ’sah ‘means ‘He Who was previously described’. The word ‘esah ‘indicates that He is within the range of one’s realization. The word ‘paravan ‘means ‘eternally endowed with His svarūpa-śakti known as superior (para)’. The six transformations are birth, material existence, growth, change, decline, and death. The words ‘devata-viśeṣah ‘mean that the Lord is worshipable by all divine beings that are different from Him. The words ‘karyam karanam ‘mean ‘a produced material body and senses’. A characteristic mark (lingam) is that by which inference is accomplished. The Lord has no such a characteristic mark, for He can be apprehended only through the scriptures. The word ‘janita ‘means ‘father, ‘as stated in Panini’s Astadhyayi (6.4.53): janita mantre, “In the Vedic mantras, the word ‘janita ‘is used.” Its formation is created from the word ‘janayitr ‘by the elision of ‘ni ‘before the affix ‘tr’. The rest is clear.

### Text 2

**Перевод:**

**This same Supreme Lord, the cause of all causes, is to be worshipped by all those who seek liberation, and no one else besides Him. As stated in the śruti: tam eva viditvati mrtyum eti nanyah pantha vidyate anaya (Puruṣa-sukta, 18), “Only by knowing the Supreme Lord can one transcend death. There is no other way to cross over it.” tam evaikam janatha atmanam anya vaco vimuncatha amrtasyaisa setuh (Muṇḍaka Upaniṣad, 2.2.5), “You should know the Supreme Soul, the only controller of all, the reservoir of immortality. Give up words about anyone else.” The sages also recollect this in the following way: isvaro bhagavan visnuh paramatma sanatanah, sasta caracarasyaiko yatinam parama gatih, dhyayate ‘rcayate yo ‘nyam visnu-lingam samasthitah, kalpa-koti-satenapi na gatis tasya vidyate, “God, the Supreme Lord Viṣṇu, is the eternal Supreme Soul. He is the only ruler of all moving and non-moving beings and the supreme goal for all ascetics. One who has adopted the characteristics of a Vaiṣṇava but worships and meditates on someone else does not attain the goal of life even in millions of days of Brahma.” tam ekam bahudhatmanam pradurbhutam adhoksajam, nanya-bhaktah kriyavanto yajante sarva-kamadam (Mahā-bhārata, 12.47.20), “Those who are exclusively devoted and engaged in His service worship only Lord Adhoksaja, Who is only one but manifests Himself in multiple forms and fulfils all desires.” tarn eva carcayan nityam bhaktya puruṣam avyayam, dhyayan stuvan namasyams ca yajamanas tam eva ca, anadi-nidhanam visnum sarva-loka-mahesvaram, lokadhyaksam stuvan nityam sarva-duhkhatigo bhavet, esa me sarva-dharmānam dharmo ‘dhikatamo matah (Mahā-bhārata, 13.135.5-6,8), “Always meditating on the eternal Supreme Person, Lord Viṣṇu, the great Master of all planets, the beginningless and immortal ruler of the world, worshipping with devotion, praising, bowing down to Him, offering sacrifices unto Him, and constantly chanting His glories, one surpasses all suffering. This is what I consider the highest of all religious principles.”**

**Комментарий:**

It was mentioned above that the Supreme Lord is distinguished among the demigods. Here, the author corroborates this distinction as His attribute of being the giver of liberation. “And no one else, ” refers to Rudra and so on. The text ‘tam eva ‘is from the Puruṣa-sukta (18) of the Yajur Veda. The word ‘tam ‘refers to Lord Viṣṇu, and ‘viditva ‘means ‘having known ‘and ‘having worshipped’. The text ‘tam evaikam ‘is from the Muṇḍaka Upaniṣad (2.2.5). “You should know (janatha) and meditate on Him (tam), the Supreme Soul, the only (ekam) controller of everything. Give up (vimuncatha) words (vacah) about demigods, who are different from Him (anyah), because He (esah) is the reservoir (setuh) of liberation (amrtasya)». The verses starting with ‘isvaro bhagavan ‘are from the Maha- bharata. “One who has adopted (samasthitah) the internal and external characteristics of a Vaiṣṇava (visnu-cihnam),” i.e., who has received Vaiṣṇava-diksa. The meaning is that if giving up Lord Viṣṇu, one worships demigods (anyam), then one never attains the goal of life (gatih),for that is a great offense. The words ‘nanya-bhaktah ‘mean ‘those who are exclusively devoted to Lord Viṣṇu’. They excel in Vaiṣṇava conduct (kriyavantah). The words ’sarva-duhkhatigah ‘mean that one is free from all suffering, up to the subtle body. The words ‘eva ‘(only) and ‘ekam ‘(only) in these texts corroborate that only Lord Viṣṇu is the giver of liberation and that only His worship is conducive to liberation.

### Text 3

**Перевод:**

**Lord Kṛṣṇa Himself gives this same instruction in the following words: mayy eva mana adhatsva mayi buddhim nivesaya, nivasisyasi mayy eva ata urdhvam na samsayah (Bhagavad-gita, 12.8), “Fix your mind upon Me only, direct your intelligence upon Me, and then you will live close to Me without any doubt.” daivi hy esa guna-mayi mama māyā duratyaya, mam eva ye prapadyante mayam etam taranti te (Bhagavad-gita, 7.14), “This divine energy of Mine, consisting of the three modes of material nature, is difficult to overcome. Only those who surrender unto Me cross beyond it.” catur-vidha mama jana bhakta eva hi te smrtah, tesam ekantinah sresthas te vai cananya-devatah, aham eva gatis tesam nirasih sarva-karmanam, ye tu sistas trayo bhaktah phala-kama hi te matah, sarve cyavana-dharmānah pratibuddhas tu moksa-bhak, brāhmaṇam sitikantham ca yas canya devatah smrtah, pratibuddha na sevante mam evaisyanti yat param, bhaktam prati viśeṣas te esa parthanuklrtitah (Mahā-bhārata, 12.328.30-32), “There are four kinds of devotees, out of which the ekantis, who are exclusively devoted to Me and have no other deity, are the best. They have no desire for the fruits of their prescribed duties, and I am their only goal. The remaining three kinds of devotees aspire for results, all of which have transient natures, but one who is enlightened attains liberation. The enlightened ones do not worship Brahma, Siva, and other demigods, but worship only Me as the Supreme. О Arjuna, this is the special characteristic of My devotee.” Therefore, only Viṣṇu, the Supreme Lord of all, should be meditated upon by those aspiring for liberation.**

**Комментарий:**
