Just as the śruti and the sages stated the above facts, so did the Supreme Lord too. The two verses starting with ‘mayy eva ‘are from the Bhagavad-gita. The words ‘mayy eva nivasisyasi ‘mean ‘you will come in close proximity to Me’. The verses starting with ‘catur-vidhah ‘are from the Nārayāṇiya section of the Mahā-bhārata. “The ekantis, those exclusively devoted to Me, ” i.e., “whose only goal in life is Me and who perform all their prescribed duties (sarva-karmanam) without personal desires (nirasih). In order to teach people, they perform their duties at a secondary time but perform devotional service to Me as the real aim. ” This is the idea here. The three remaining kinds of devotees are the afflicted, those who desire material gain, and the inquisitive. The compound ‘cyavana-dharmah ‘means ‘those whose results attained by worship are perishable’. The ‘enlightened ‘(pratibuddhah) is one who is exclusively devoted and knows the Lord‘s svarūpa. “Other demigods” refers to Ganesa and so on. The name ‘Partha ‘refers to Arjuna. The Supreme Lord of all (sarvesvarah) is Lord Viṣṇu only, as spoken by Lord Siva to Ghantakarna in the Hari-vamsa (3.80.30): mukti-pradata sarvesam visnur eva na samsayah, “Without a doubt, only Lord Viṣṇu is the giver of liberation for everyone. ” Someone may argue, “In some places, the smṛti also states that Lord Siva and other demigods also give liberation.” True. Yet they give liberation only from suffering other than the subtle body, while Lord Viṣṇu frees one even from the subtle body. Thus, everything is correct. Otherwise, Lord Siva’s statement would be contradicted. The illusory energy actually belongs only to Lord Viṣṇu. Being pleased with those who surrender unto Him, He removes the illusory energy for them. No one else has such power, for in the Bhagavad-glta He states that this divine energy belongs to Him: daivi hy esa, etc.

### Text 4

**Перевод:**

**As for others, like the demigods, they should not be disrespected, as stated in the Padma Purāṇa: harir eva sadaradhyah sarva-devesvaresvarah, itare brahma-rudradya navajneyah kadacana, “Only Lord Hari is the God of all demigods and is always to be worshipped, but others like Lord Brahma and Lord Siva should never be disrespected.”**

**Комментарий:**

Someone may argue, “If Lord Viṣṇu alone is the highest of all and the giver of liberation, then His worshippers can disregard the demigods, just as the associates of a king disregard the ministers and others». The author replies to this here. “Others ” refers to Lord Brahma, Lord Siva, and so on. The idea is that they should not be disregarded but rather respected as devotees of Lord Viṣṇu. The quoted verse is from the Padma Purāṇa.

### Text 5

**Перевод:**

**In this regard, some say the following: “It is not correct to say that the Supreme Lord of all, Viṣṇu, is distinguished among the demigods. In the Maha-narayana Upaniṣad (1.6) it is said: istapurtam bahudha jayamanam visvam bibharti bhuvanasya nabhih, tad evagnis tad vayus tat suryas tad u candramah, ‘Brahman, the Absolute Truth, protects manifold sacrifices and meritorious deeds being performed. That is the resting place of the world, and that alone maintains the universe. That alone is fire. That is the wind. That is the sun and the moon’. In the Yajur Veda it is stated: agnih sarva devatah, ‘Fire is all the demigods’. In the smṛti it is also understood the oneness of all divine beings, who are to be worshipped by all Vedic rituals: ye ‘py anya-devata-bhakta yajante sraddhayanvitah, te ‘pi mam eva kaunteya yajanty avidhi-purvakam, aham hi sarva-yajnānam bhokta ca prabhur eva ca (Bhagavad-gita, 9.23-24), ‘Those who are devotees of other divine beings and worship them with faith actually worship only Me, but against the prescribed rules, О Arjuna. I alone am the Lord and enjoyer of all sacrifices’. The supremacy of all divine beings is also understood from the śruti. Therefore, there is only one divine being that is worshipped by different ritualistic activities and holds different names. Thus, to say that only Lord Viṣṇu is the Supreme is mere rhetoric.”**

**Комментарий:**

It has been thus demonstrated that only Lord Viṣṇu has supreme powers and so on. In order to refute an opposite view, according to which God and all demigods are one, the author now repeats their doctrine. Brahman protects (bibharti) manifold (bahudha) sacrifices and meritorious deeds (istapūrtam) being performed (jayamanam). The word ‘ista ‘refers to sacrifices like the jyotistoma, and ‘apUrta ‘refers to meritorious deeds such as digging a well, making a garden, etc. Brahman maintains (bibharti) the whole universe (visvam) because it is the resting place (nabhih) of the world (bhuvanasya). What is the form of Brahman ? Expecting this question, the śruti says, “That alone is fire (agnih), etc. ” The meaning is clear. Brahman is understood by the view that all divine beings are one, since it is stated, “Fire is all demigods (sarva devatah).” In conclusion, it is first stated that through its energies, Brahman is all demigods, and subsequently fire is figuratively said to be all demigods, since it is the mouth of all of them — this is to be understood as the opponents ‘view. The quoted verses from the Bhagavad-gita are to be similarly interpreted as the previous quotes. Statements about the supremacy of all divine beings are to be seen in the respective sections in various śruti texts. “Therefore, the idea that only Viṣṇu is the Supreme has been refuted. ”

### Text 6

**Перевод:**

**It is not so, because it is seen in the scriptures that there are mutual differences between all demigods and Lord Viṣṇu, and because it is understood that He is superior to all of them. In the Puruṣa-sukta it is said: candrama manaso jatas caksoh suryo ajayata, srotrad vayus ca pranas ca mukhad agnir ajayata, “The moon was born from the mind of the Supreme Person; the sun was born from His eyes; the wind and the air were born from His ears; and fire was born from His mouth.” The Nārāyaṇa Upaniṣad declares: narayanad brahma jayate narayanad rudro astau vasavo jayante narayanad ekadāsa rudra jayante narayanad dvadāsaditya jayante, “From Nārāyaṇa was born Brahma; from Nārāyaṇa was born Siva; from Nārāyaṇa were born the prajapatis; from Nārāyaṇa was born Indra; from Nārāyaṇa were born the eight Vasus; from Nārāyaṇa were born the eleven Rudras; from Nārāyaṇa were born the twelve Adityas.” In the Viṣṇu-dharma Purāṇa (1.171.5-6) it is stated: brahma sambhus tathaivarkas candramas ca satakratuh, evam adyas tathaivanye yukta vaiṣṇava-tejasa, jagat-karyavasane tu viyujyante ca tejasa, vitejasas ca te sarve pancatvam upayanti ca, “Brahma, Siva, Surya, Candra, Indra, and other demigods are all endowed with the power of Lord Viṣṇu. However, at the end of their duties in the universe, they become devoid of that power. Being powerless, all of them perish.” The identity of case-relation between the Supreme Lord and all the demigods is figurative on account of their sustenance and functions being dependent on Him. Otherwise, many statements from the śruti would be contradictory, such as: tam isvaranam parāmaṁ mahesvaram tam devatanam parāmaṁ ca daivatam, patim patinam parāmaṁ parastad vidama devarh bhuvanesam idyam (Śvetāśvatara Upaniṣad, 6.7), “May we know the Great Lord, the supreme of all lords, the Supreme Deity of the demigods, the Supreme Master above all masters, the Supreme God, the worshipable Master of the world.” The different results stated in the smṛti would also be contradictory: devan deva-yajo yanti (Bhagavad-gita, 7.23), “Those who worship the demigods will go the demigods.” yanti deva-vrata devan pitrn yanti pitr-vratah, bhutani yanti bhutejya yanti mad-yajino ‘pi mam (Bhagavad-gita, 9.25), “Those devoted to the demigods will go the demigods; those devoted to the forefathers will go to the forefathers; those who worship ghosts will go to the ghosts; and those who worship Me will come to Me.” This being so, the supremacy of all demigods, as heard from the scriptures, must be either relative or for the sake of praise.**

**Комментарий:**

The text ‘candrama manasah ‘is from the Puruṣa-sukta. The word ‘caksoh ‘means ‘from the eye’. The text ‘narayanad brahma ‘is from the Nārāyaṇa Upaniṣad, which belongs to the Atharvanika branch. The verse ‘brahma sambhuh ‘is from the Viṣṇu-dharma. The word ‘pancatvam ‘means ‘death’. There are mutual differences between Lord Viṣṇu and the demigods, such as their being born in a particular place. Because the Supreme Lord is Nārāyaṇa, the source of all living entities, He is different from them, and hence His supremacy. “Otherwise,” i.e., “not having accepted that the identity in case-relation is because the sustenance and functions of the demigods are dependent on the all-powerful Lord; because they are pervaded by Him; and because they are not separated from Him, if it is accepted that there is prominent non-duality». The verses starting with ‘devan deva-yajah ‘are from the Bhagavad-gita. The word ‘apeksikam ‘means ‘in comparison to others who are lower than them’.

### Text 7

**Перевод:**

**Others speak as follows: “God and all demigods may not be one, but there is oneness amongst three of them — Brahma, Viṣṇu, and Siva, for evidence of this is seen in the smṛti: yo vai visnuh sa vai rudro yo rudrah sa pitamahah, eka murtis trayo deva rudra-visnu-pitamahah, varada loka-kartaro loka-nathah svayambhuvah, ardha-narisvaras te tu vratam tivram samasthitah (Hari-vamsa), ‘Viṣṇu is Siva, and Siva is Brahma. The three divinities — Brahma, Viṣṇu, and Siva — are actually one body. They are the self-manifest creators and masters of the world and its benefactors. These Lords have their feminine counterparts and are always engaged in severe religious vows’. In the Viṣṇu Purāṇa it is said: brahma narayanakhyo’sau kalpadau bhagavan yatha, prajah sasarja bhagavan brahma narayanatmakah (1.4.1-2), ‘In the beginning of creation, the Supreme Lord called Nārāyaṇa is Brahma. Lord Brahma, who is actually a form of Lord Nārāyaṇa, created all living entities’. tatah sa bhagavan visnu rudra-rupa-dharo ‘vyayah (6.3.16), ‘Then the imperishable Lord Viṣṇu takes the form of Rudra’. tatah kalagni-rudro’sau bhutva sarva-haro harih (6.3.24), ‘Then, becoming Kalagni Rudra, the same Lord Hari becomes the destroyer of everything’. sarga-sthity-anta-karanim brahma-visnu-sivatmikam, sa samjnam yati bhagavan eka eva janardanah (1.2.66), ‘The same Supreme Lord Janardana takes up the names Brahma, Viṣṇu, and Siva to perform the creation, maintenance, and destruction of the universe’. In the Śrimad-Bhāgavatam (12.10.20-22), Lord Siva also says the same thing to Markandeya: brāhmaṇah sadhavah santa nihsanga bhuta-vatsalah, ekanta-bhakta asmasu nirvairah sama-darsinah, saloka loka-palas tan vandanty arcanty upasate, aham ca bhagavan brahma svayam ca harir Isvarah, na te mayy acyute ‘je ca bhidam anv api caksate, ‘I, Lord Brahma, Lord Hari Himself, and the rulers of the universe accompanied by its inhabitants, praise, worship, and serve the saintly brāhmaṇas who are peaceful, free from worldly attachment, kind to all beings, exclusively devoted to us, free from enmity, and endowed with equal vision. They do not see the slightest difference between me, Lord Acyuta, and Lord Brahma’. Therefore, it must be admitted that these three divinities are one.”**

**Комментарий:**

As those who propound the doctrine that God and all demigods are one have been refuted, now a propounder that the three divinities are one raises an objection. The verses starting with ‘yo vai ‘are from the Hari-vamsa. They are called ’svayambhuvah ‘because they are said to be unborn. The verses starting with ‘brahma narayanakhyah ‘are from the Viṣṇu Purāṇa. The verses starting with ‘brāhmaṇah sadhavah ‘are from the Śrimad-Bhāgavatam (12.10.20-22). “I, Lord Brahma, and Lord Hari Himself praise (vandanti), worship (arcanti), and serve (upasate) those brāhmaṇas who do not see (caksate) the slightest (anu) difference (bhidam) between me (Siva), Viṣṇu, and Brahma». This is the meaning. “Therefore, ” i.e., “because of so many statements as quoted. ” The word ‘astheyam ‘means ‘it must be admitted’.

### Text 8

**Перевод:**

**It is not so, for the śruti statements are as follows: yo brāhmaṇam vidadhati pūrvam yo vai vedams ca prahinoti tasmai, tam ha devam atma-buddhi-prakāśam mumuksur vai saranam aham prapadye (Śvetāśvatara Upaniṣad, 6.18), “Desiring liberation, I surrender unto the Supreme Lord, Who reveals spiritual knowledge. In the beginning, it is He Who creates Brahma and imparts the Vedas to him.” narayanad brahma jayate narayanad rudro jayate (Nārāyaṇa Upaniṣad), “From Nārāyaṇa was born Brahma; from Nārāyaṇa was born Siva.” yam kamaye tam tam ugram krnomi tam brāhmaṇam tam rsim tam sumedham (Rg Veda, 10.125.5), “Whomever I wish, I make him a Rudra. I make him a Brahma, a sage, a wise man.” The Mahā-bhārata also confirms this, starting with the following verse: prahur aditya-varnam tam puruṣam tamasah param, brhantarh sarvagam devam isanam varadam prabhum (12.327.50), “The demigods and sages say that the Supreme Person has the complexion of the sun, is beyond darkness, is all-expanding, omnipresent, the Supreme God, the Master and Lord, the benefactor of all.” Afterwards, it is stated: tato brahma samabhavat sa tasyaiva prasadajah, krodhavistasya samjajne rudrah samhara-karakah, etau tau vibudha-sresthau prasada-krodhajau smrtau, tad-adarsita-panthanau srsti-samhara-karakau, nimitta-matram tav atra sarva-prani-pravartakau (12.328.15-17), “Brahma appeared from a lotus. He was born from Lord Nārāyaṇa’s grace. When Lord Nārāyaṇa was filled with anger, Rudra, the destroyer, was born. These two, the best among demigods, are said to have been born from Lord Nārāyaṇa’s grace and anger, and they perform the creation and destruction according to the directions given by Him. Both of them set in motion all living entities, yet both are merely instrumental causes.” sabrahmakah sarudras ca sendra deva maharsibhih, arcayanti sura-srestham devam narayanam harim (12.328.27), “The demigods accompanied by Brahma, Rudra, Indra, and the great sages worship Lord Nārāyaṇa, the best among divine beings, also known as Hari.” yad brahma rsayas caiva svayam pasupatis ca yat, havyam kavyam ca satatam vidhi-yuktaṁ prayunjate, krtsnam ca tasya devasya caranav upatisthati (12.331.48-49), “Whatever oblations Brahma, Siva himself, and the sages offer according to the prescribed rules, all go to the Supreme Lord‘s feet.” bhavisyatam vartatam ca bhutanam caiva bharata, sarvesam agranlr visnuh sevyah pujyas ca nityasah (12.328.28), “O descendant of Bharata, Lord Viṣṇu is to be always served and worshipped before everyone else who has ever existed, who exists, and who will exist.” Lord Siva also showed that Lord Hari alone is the Supreme Lord: svayam ca harir isvarah, “And Lord Hari, Who is God Himself.”**

**Комментарий:**

Here the author refutes the above objection. The word ‘prahinoti ‘means ‘He imparts through the mind’. The text ‘yam kamaye ‘is from the Rg Veda and was spoken by the Supreme Soul. “Whomever (yam) I wish (kamaye), I make (krnomi) that living entity a Rudra (ugram)». The rest is dear. The verses starting with ‘prahur aditya-varnam ‘are from the Narayaniya section of the Mahā-bhārata. In these verses, the supremacy of Lord Viṣṇu is very clear, and therein Brahma and Siva are also said to worship Him.

### Text 9

**Перевод:**

**This is the conclusion according to the Śrimad-Bhāgavatam (1.18.21): athapi yat-pada-nakhavasrstam jagad virincopahrtarhanambhah, sesam punaty anyatamo mukundat ко nama loke bhagavat-padarthah, “Moreover, who else in this world can befit the meaning of the word ‘bhagavan ‘besides Lord Mukunda? The water emanating from the nails of His toes are worshipped by Lord Brahma and purify the whole universe, including Lord Siva.” Statements that Lord Brahma and Lord Siva are non-different from Lord Viṣṇu are appropriate because their sustenance, functions, and so on are dependent upon Him. This is in conformity with the smṛti, such as: srjami tan-niyukto ‘ham haro harati tad-vasah, visvam puruṣa-rupena paripati tri-śakti-dhrk (Śrimad-Bhāgavatam, 2.6.32), “Being appointed by Him, I, Brahma, create the universe. Under His control, Siva destroys it. Holding three energies, the Supreme Lord Himself in the form of a puruṣavatara maintains it». brahma sambhus tathaivarkas candramas ca satakratuh, evam adyas tathaivanye yukta vaiṣṇava-tejasa, jagat-karyavasane tu viyujyante ca tejasa, vitejasas ca te sarve pancatvam upayanti ca (Viṣṇu-dharma Purāṇa, 1.171.5-6), “Brahma, Siva, Surya, Candra, Indra, and other demigods are all endowed with the power of Lord Viṣṇu. However, at the end of their duties in the universe, they become devoid of that power. Being powerless, all of them perish.” Therefore, it is proved that only Viṣṇu is the Supreme Lord of all.**

**Комментарий:**

Here the author states how this is an ancient conclusion, and not a modern one. The verse ‘atrapiti is from the Śrimad-Bhāgavatam (1.18.21). The words ’sesam jagat’ means ‘the whole universe including Lord Siva/ The meaning of the rest is clear. Then, what is the meaning of the sentences that describe non-difference between Lord Viṣṇu and the demigods? The author says, “Statements that, etc». The verse ’srjami tan-niyuktaḥ ‘is from the Śrimad-Bhāgavatam (2.6.32), and was spoken by Lord Brahma. The words ‘tan-niyuktaḥ ‘mean ‘being instigated by Lord Viṣṇu’.

### Text 10

**Перевод:**

**Some propound a solution in the following way: It is described in the Purāṇas that sometimes Lord Viṣṇu Himself accepts the form of Brahma and Rudra and performs the creation and destruction. From this perspective, the statements that both are non-different from Him also hold good. Yet the worship of Brahma and Siva is not prescribed for those who aspire for liberation, as it is the case with Lord Buddha, for various scriptures say so, such as: tam eva viditvati mrtyum eti nanyah pantha vidyate anaya (Puruṣa-sūkta, 18), “ Only by knowing the Supreme Lord can one transcend death. There is no other way to cross over it.” The Hari-hara-stotra of the Hari-vamsa (2.125.29-60) is also explained in a similar way.**

**Комментарий:**

Here the author presents another appropriate interpretation. “In the Purāṇas, ” i.e., in the Viṣṇu Purāṇa and so on. The words ‘tasya tasya‘ mumuksubhih, etc’. are as follows: When Lord Hari Himself becomes Brahma and Siva, the worship of His nature in that role does not lead to liberation. It is seen that those who worship Him in those roles are actually inimical towards Lord Viṣṇu and cause distress to the world and to the very same personalities they worship. On the other hand, those who directly worship Lord Viṣṇu also respect Lord Brahma and Lord Siva and please the whole world. In this regard, the Śrimad-Bhāgavatam (1.2.23) states: sattvam rajas tama iti prakrter gunas tairyuktaḥ parahpuruṣa eka ihasya dhatte, sthity-adaye hari-viranci-hareti samjnah sreyamsi tatra khalu sattva-tanor nrnam syuh, “Becoming endowed with the three modes of nature — goodness, passion, and ignorance — the same Supreme Person accepts the names Viranci, Hari, and Hara, and performs the creation, maintenance, and destruction of the universe. Out of them, men can receive liberation from Lord Hari, Whose body is pure goodness." This is wholeheartedly spoken by Ramacandra Kavirāja in the following words:

prahlada-dhruva-ravananuja-bali-vyasambarisadayo visnupāsanayaiva te ‘pi ca bhavadinam priya jajnire, ye ‘nye ravana-bana-paundraka-vrka-krauncandhakadya ami yad-bhrtya na ca tat-priya na ca hares tasmaj jagad-vairinah, sivo bhavatu vaiṣṇavah kim ajito ‘pi saiva svayam tatha samatayas tu va vidhi-haradi-murti-trayam, vilokya bhava-vedhasoh kim api bhakta-varga-krāmaṁ pranamya sirasapi tan vayam upendra-dāsan srtah,  “Merely by worshipping Lord Viṣṇu, devotees like Prahlada, Dhruva, Vibhisana, Bali, Vyasa, and Ambarisa became dear to Brahma, Siva, and other demigods. Others like Ravana, Bana, Paundraka, Vrka, Baka, and Andhaka are dear neither to the demigods whom they served, nor to Lord Hari, therefore they became hostile to the world. Siva may be considered a devotee of Viṣṇu, or Viṣṇu may be considered a devotee of Siva, or else Brahma, Viṣṇu, and Siva may be considered equal. Yet having seen the class of the devotees of Brahma and Siva, we bow down to Lord Viṣṇu’s servants and take their shelter». Thus, even from the view-point that Brahma and Siva are non-dijferent from Him, only the worship of Lord Viṣṇu in His own form gives liberation.

### Text 11

**Перевод:**

**Others say the following: “There may not be oneness between all three of them, but only between Hari and Hara, for in the Śvetāśvatara Upaniṣad the same subject expressed by names like Hara and Rudra is designated by words such as sahasra-sirsa (thousand-headed). In the smṛti it is also said: sivasya hrdayam visnur visnos ca hrdayam sivah, yatha siva-mayo visnur evam visnu-mayah sivah, yathantaram na pasyami tatha me svastir ayusi: ‘Viṣṇu is Siva’s heart, and Siva is Viṣṇu’s heart. Just as Viṣṇu exists in the form of Siva, so Siva exists in the form of Viṣṇu. Since I do not see any difference, let my life be prosperous’. Similarly, the oneness between both is ascertained from the Hari-hara-stotra. The Padma Purāṇa (4.25.15) also declares that one who sees difference between both incurs a great fault in the form of offense to the Holy Name: sivasya sri-visnor ya iha khalu namadi sakalam dhiya bhinnam pasyet sa khalu hari-namahita-karah, ‘One who sees difference between all names of Lord Viṣṇu and Lord Siva is actually adverse towards Lord Viṣṇu’s names’. ‘All names ‘(sakalam) include those mentioned in the Hari-hara-stotra, such as ‘holder of a discus ‘and ‘holder of a trident’. The meaning is that one who also sees these names as different commits offence. Therefore, there is no duality between Viṣṇu and Siva.” According to the above quoted śruti and smṛti, this view is not correct. The stated idea that both are one because the same personality expressed by names like Hara and Rudra is designated by words such as sahasra-sirsa is not correct, for in the Svetaivatara Upaniṣad Lord Viṣṇu alone is designated by names like Hara, Rudra, and so on.**

**Комментарий:**

In this way, as those who propound that the three divinities are one have been refuted, those who propound the oneness between Hari and Hara raise an objection here. The words ‘hara-hari-śabda-vacyasya ‘refer to the subject expressed by names like Hara and Rudra. The word ‘antaram ‘means ‘difference’. The Hari-hari-stotrafrom the Hari-vamsa (2.125.29-60) is well-known, in which the non-duality between Viṣṇu and Siva along with their companions is distinctly read. The verse ’sivasya sri-visnoh ‘is from the Padma Purāṇa (4.25.15) and was spoken by Sanat Kumara. The words ‘ata eva ‘mean ‘because of seeing difference while both are non-different’. The words’sahasra-sirsa ‘(thousand-headed) factually refer to Lord Viṣṇu, and this was witnessed by Arjuna and others. Lord Siva is known as ‘Tri-netra ‘(three-eyed), ‘Vrsabha-dhvaja ‘(whose flag has the emblem of a bull), and ‘Durgā-pati ‘(the husband of Durgā). As for the instances in which it is read that Siva has thousands of heads, that is to be interpreted in connection with Lord Viṣṇu as the Supreme Soul within him.

### Text 12

**Перевод:**

**In the Viṣṇu-sahasra-nama-stotra it is read that names like §arva, Siva, Sambhu, and Rudra are names of Lord Viṣṇu. The reason for their being semantically applied to Him is stated in the Brahmanda Purāṇa: rujam dravayate yasmad rudras tasmaj janardanah, isanad eva cesano maha-devo mahattvatah, pibanti ye nara nakam muktah samsara-sagarat, tad-adharo yato visnuh pinakiti tatah smrtah, sivah sukhatmakatvena sarva-samrodhanad dharah, krtty-atmakam idam vis varū yato vaste pravartayan, krttivasas tato devo virincas ca virecanat, brrhhanad brahma-namasav aiśvaryad indra ucyate, evam nana-vidhaih śabdair eka eva trivikramah, vedesu sapuranesu giyate purusottamah, “Because Lord Janardana drives away the pain of material existence, He is called ‘Rudra’. He is called ‘Isana ‘because of His command, and ‘Mahadeva ‘because of His greatness. Those who are free from the ocean of birth and death experience supreme happiness, whose basis is Lord Viṣṇu, hence He is called ‘Pinaki’. He is called ‘Siva ‘because He is bliss personified, and ‘Hara ‘because He is the destroyer of everything. Because while creating the universe He covers it with a shield of material elements, He is called ‘Krttivasa, ‘and His name is ‘Virinca ‘because He emits forth the elements. He is called ‘Brahma ‘because He is all expanding, and ‘Indra ‘because of His opulence. In this way, the Supreme Person, Trivikrama, is called by many varieties of names in the Vedas and Purāṇas.”**

**Комментарий:**

Here the author explains the reason for what was said above. It should be understood that the Viṣṇu-sahasra-nama-stotra mentioned here was spoken by Vaisampayana. The reasons by which the names Rudra, Pinaki, and Krttivasa are applied to Lord Siva are different when applied to Lord Viṣṇu. They are mostly applied to both according to their etymology as well as their conventional meaning. Names like Isana, Mahadeva, Siva, Hara, Brahma, and Indra are applied to Lord Siva and others according to their common etymology (yoga) but are applied to Lord Viṣṇu according to their meta-etymology (maha-yoga), just as words such as ‘king ‘can apply to the rulers of a province as well as to emperors, and thus they should be understood accordingly. The name ‘Rudra ‘means ‘one who removes (dravayati) the pain (rujam) of material existence’. The word ‘nakam ‘means ’supreme happiness, ‘and ‘pibanti ‘means ‘they experience’. The words ‘krtty-atmakam ‘mean ‘consisting of a shield, ‘‘vaste ‘means ‘He covers, ‘and ‘virecanat ‘means ‘because of manifesting the primordial cause of pradhana and the other elements, which is designated as tamah’.

### Text 13

**Перевод:**

**It is said in the Skanda Purāṇa that except for names like ‘Nārāyaṇa, ‘the Supreme Lord gives His other names to Siva and others: rte narayanadini namani purusottamah, pradad anyatra bhagavan rajevarte svakam puram, “Except for names like Nārāyaṇa, the Supreme Person gave His other names to other personalities, just as a king gives away towns, except for his own.” In the Brahma Purāṇa it is stated: caturmukhah satanando brāhmaṇah padmabhur iti, ugro bhasmadharo nagnah kapallti sivasya ca, viśeṣa-namani dadau svakiyany api kesavah, “Lord Brahma has names such as Caturmukha, Satananda, and Padmabhu, while Lord Siva has names like Ugra, Bhasmadhara, Nagna, and Kapall. Lord Kesava gave them some of His specific names.”**

**Комментарий:**

“Just as a king (rajeva) gives away (pradat) other towns (puram) to his ministers, except for his own (svakam) residence. ”

### Text 14

**Перевод:**

**The Śvetāśvatara Upaniṣad starts with the words, “The Brahmavadis speak as follows,” then the same Brahman is designated by those words. Otherwise, the statement of the following peculiarities would be contradictory: ya te rudra siva tanur aghorapapa-kasini (3-5), “O Lord, remover of distress, Your blissful body is peaceful, unalloyed, and effulgent.” mahan prabhur vai puruṣah sattvasyaisa pravartakah, sunirmalam imam santim isano jyotir avyayah (3.12), “The Great Lord is the Supreme Person, the imperishable intelligence that conducts the living entity. He is the possessor of completely pure peacefulness.” As for the fearsome form of Lord Siva wearing skulls (Kapali), that is to be given up by those who aspire for liberation, as stated in the Śrimad-Bhāgavatam (1.2.26): mumuksavo ghora-rupan hitva bhuta-patin atha, narayana-kalah santa bhajanti hy anasuyavah, “Giving up the terrific forms of Lord Siva and other demigods, those who aspire for liberation, are peaceful and free from envy, and worship only Lord Nārāyaṇa and His expansions.”**

**Комментарий:**

It was previously stated that in the Śvetāśvatara Upaniṣad Lord Viṣṇu alone is designated by names like Hara, Rudra, and so on. Here, the author explains this. Starting with the word ‘Brahman, ‘all those words express Brahman. This is the meaning. If an opposite interpretation is adopted, it would be unsubstantiated. Therefore, it is said, “Otherwise, etc». The meaning is that this would contradict the sentences that express Lord Viṣṇu’s attributes, such as having a gentle form, conducting the living entities, and possessing peacefulness. Someone may object, “These attributes can also be applied to Lord Siva». In response to this, the author says, “As for the fearsome form, etc». The quoted verse is from the Śrimad-Bhāgavatam (1.2.26). The meaning is clear.

### Text 15

**Перевод:**

**In verses like, “Viṣṇu is Siva’s heart, etc.,” it is simply expressed that there is no deceit between both, and not that their svarūpas are non-different, for the evidence of this is found in the scriptures quoted above. In verses like, “One who sees difference, etc.,” the view that Siva is an independent demigod is denied. Or else, he is a form of Lord Viṣṇu because his sustenance and functions are dependent upon Him, since Siva’s names and powers are given by Him. Thus, there is no flaw whatsoever. Others say that the meaning of ‘namadi sakalam, etc., ‘is that one who sees non-difference between all names of Lord Viṣṇu and Lord Siva is actually adverse towards Lord Viṣṇu’s names, for it has been previously stated, “The enlightened ones do not worship Brahma, Siva, and other demigods, but worship only Me as the Supreme,” and also because of the verse ‘yas tu narayanam devam, etc., ‘which will be explained later. Therefore, there is no oneness between the svarūpa of Viṣṇu and of Siva, and it is thus proved that only Viṣṇu is the Supreme.**

**Комментарий:**

The word ‘tayoh ‘refers to Hari and Hara. By a different explanation there is contradiction. It should be understood that ‘others ‘means the Sri-vaiṣṇavas. Subsequently to the verse under discussion, in the Padma Purāṇa (4.25.17) the words’siva-namaparadhah ‘mean ‘offense (aparadhah) to the Holy (siva) Name (nama)’. Or else, that verse can mean, “One who sees difference between the names and qualities of the auspicious (siva) Lord Viṣṇu and His svarūpa is actually adverse towards Lord Viṣṇu’s names, for His names and qualities are non-differentfrom His svarūpa.” Thus, there is no doubt at all. As for the statements found in some places in the Purāṇas about Siva’s supremacy and its reasons, they are to be overlooked because such Purāṇas belong to the mode of darkness and are contradicted by those in the mode of goodness. This conclusion is understood from the Matsya Purāṇa (53.67-69) and is also seen in the Vedānta-syamantaka (second chapter) and other treatises. As for similar statements made in the Mahā-bhārata, they will be refuted subsequently.

### Text 16

**Перевод:**

**Others speak as follows: “There is non-difference neither among the three divinities nor among two of them, but it must be accepted that the three divinities are similar, for it is ascertained that in the smṛti, three of them are the independent causes in the functions of creation, maintenance, and destruction: yatha sasarja devo’sau devarsi-pitr-danavan, manusya-tiryag-yaksadin bhu-vyoma-salilaukasah, yad gunam yat svabhavam ca yad rupam ca jagad dvija, sargadau srstavan brahma tan mamacaksva vistarat (Viṣṇu Purāṇa, 1.5.1-2), Maitreya said, ‘O twice-born one, in the beginning of creation Lord Brahma created the universe, its qualities, natures, and forms, as well as men, animals, yaksas, and other beings that live in the earth, in the sky, and in the water. Please tell me in detail about this’. caracarasya sarvasya rudrah saihhara-karakah, tatah kalagni-rudro’sau samharaty akhilam jagat, ‘Lord Rudra is the destroyer of all moving and non-moving beings. Therefore, this form, Kalagni Rudra, destroys the whole universe’. na hi palana-samarthyam rte sarvesvaram harim (Viṣṇu Purāṇa, 1.22.21), ‘Without the Supreme Lord Hari, the other living entities are certainly not able to maintain themselves’. Therefore, the conclusion that only Viṣṇu has supreme power is not correct.” According to the above quoted scriptures, this is not sound.**

**Комментарий:**

In order to refute the view that the three divinities are similar, the author first explains it. The verses starting with ‘yatha sasarja ‘are found in the Viṣṇu Purāṇa. The meaning is clear. The three functions (karya-traye) are creation, maintenance, and destruction. The author then refutes this view by saying that it is not sound (caturasram) in the light of the quoted scriptures.

### Text 17

**Перевод:**

**And also because similarity to the Supreme Lord is denied in the scriptures: na tat-samas cabhyadhikas ca drsyate (Śvetāśvatara Upaniṣad, 6.8), “No one is equal or superior to the Supreme Lord.” na tasya pratima asti yasya nama mahad yasah (Śvetāśvatara Upaniṣad, 4.19), “No one is similar to Him Whose name is very glorious.” na tvat-samas cabhyadhikah kuto ‘nyah (Bhagavad-gita, 11.43), “No one else is equal to You, let alone greater.” svayam tv asamyatisayas try-adhlsah (Śrimad-Bhāgavatam, 3.2.21), “Lord KṛṣṇaHimself is the Master of the three Viṣṇus. No one is equal or superior to Him.” nityam hi nasti jagati bhutarh sthavara-jangamam, rte tam ekam puruṣam vasudevam sanatanam (Mahā-bhārata, 12.326.31), “No moving or non-moving being in the universe is eternal except the only Supreme Person, the primeval Lord Vāsudeva.”**

**Комментарий:**

The first two texts quoted here are from the Śvetāśvatara Upaniṣad. The verse ‘na tvat-samah ‘is from the Bhagavad-gita (11.43) and was spoken by Arjuna. The verse’svayam tu ‘is from the Śrimad-Bhāgavatam

(3.2.21) and was spoken by Uddhava. The verse ‘nityam hi ‘is from the Nārayāṇiya section of the Mahā-bhārata. The meaning is that the whole universe led by Brahma, Siva, and other demigods is temporary, and among them, Lord Viṣṇu alone is eternal. As spoken by Lord Brahma in the Brahma Purāṇa: nahath sivo na canye ca tac-chakty-ekamsa-bhaginah, balah kridanakair yadvat krtdate’smabhir acyutah, “Neither I, nor Siva, nor anyone else share even a portion of Lord Viṣṇu’s energy. He plays with us like a child plays with toys. ”

### Text 18

**Перевод:**

**The Supreme Lord Hari actually performs the creation and destruction, while Brahma and Siva are merely instrumental causes under His control, as stated in the smṛti: nimittam param isasya visva-sarga-nirodhayoh, hiranyagarbhah sarvas ca kalasyaritpinas tava (Śrimad-Bhāgavatam, 10.71.8), “O Lord, in your form as invisible time You are the actual cause of the creation and destruction of the universe, while Brahma and Siva are merely Your instruments.” brāhmaṇam indram rudram ca yamam varunam eva ca, nigrhya harate yasmat tasmad dharir ihocyate (Padma Purāṇa, 6.226.86), “Having subdued Brahma, Indra, Siva, Yama, and Varuna, the Supreme Lord engages them in their respective functions, hence He is called ‘Hari’. ”**

**Комментарий:**

Here the author refutes the view that Brahma and Siva are independent in their respective functions. The verse ‘nimittam param ‘is from the Śrimad-Bhāgavatam (10.71.8) and was spoken by Uddhava. The Lord is known as ‘Hari ‘because He engages (harate) the demigods in their functions according to His will. The word ‘nigrhya ‘means ‘having subdued’. By the word ‘adi ‘(etc.) it is included the verse: nimitta-matram tav atra sarva-prani-pravartakau (Mahā-bhārata, 12.328.15-17), “Brahma and Siva set in motion all living entities, yet both are merely instrumental causes. ”

### Text 19

**Перевод:**

**In this regard, the venerable sage Parasara spoke the following words in the Viṣṇu Purāṇa (1.2.61-63): jusan rajo-gunarh tatra svayam visvesvaro harih, brahma bhutvatha jagato visrstau sampravartate, srstam ca paty anuyugam yavat-kalpa-vikalpana, sattva-bhug bhagavan visnur aprameya-parakramah, tamodreki ca kalpante rudra-rupi janardanah, maitreyakhila-bhutani bhaksayaty atibhisanah, “Entering the mode of passion, Lord Hari Himself, the Supreme Lord of the universe, becomes Brahma and works in the creation of the universe. Possessed of the mode of goodness, Lord Viṣṇu of immeasurable prowess protects the created universe throughout the ages until the end of Brahma’s day. О Maitreya, at the end of Brahma’s day, Lord Janardana assumes the very terrific form of Rudra, filled with the increasing mode of ignorance, and destroys all beings.”**

**Комментарий:**

“He becomes Brahma, ” i.e., entering (jusan) the mode of passion (rajo-gunam) through a very meritorious living entity, Lord Viṣṇu then enters that jiva and becomes Brahma. Similarly, it should also be interpreted that through a very meritorious living entity in the form of Rudra (rudra-rHpi), filled with the increasing mode of ignorance (tamodreki), Lord Viṣṇu enters that form and becomes Rudra. The word ‘bhaksayati ‘means ‘He destroys’.

### Text 20

**Перевод:**

**In the Viṣṇu Purāṇa (4.1.85-86), Lord Brahma spoke to King Raivata: yasya prasadad aham acyutasya bhutah praja-srsti-karo ‘ntakari, krodhac ca rudrah sthiti-hetu-bhūto yasmac ca madhye puruṣah parastat, mad-rupam asthaya srjaty ajo yah sthitau ca yo’sau puruṣa-svarQpi, rudra-svarupena ca yo ‘tti visvam dhatte tathananta-vapuh samastam, “It is by Lord Acyuta’s mercy that I have become the creator of the universe and all living entities. It is by His anger that Rudra becomes the destroyer. It is because of Him that above both of us, the puruṣavatara, Lord Viṣṇu, is the cause of the maintenance of the universe in the interim state. The same unborn Supreme Lord Who, assuming my form, creates the universe, Who in His own form as Viṣṇu maintains it, and in the form of Rudra destroys it, He Himself in the form of Ananta-sesa holds everything that exists.” In this way, since there is no one equal or superior to Him, it has been proved that Lord Viṣṇu has the supreme power. The fault of those who consider the three divinities as being equal is stated in the smṛti: yas tu narayanam devam brahma-rudradi-daivataih, samatvenabhijanati sa pasandi bhaved dhruvam, “One who considers Lord Brahma, Lord Siva, and other demigods as equal to Lord Nārāyaṇa is certainly a heretic.”**

**Комментарий:**

Here the author corroborates the above explained meaning by quoting the words of Lord Brahma. The word ‘yasya ‘means ‘of Viṣṇu, the Lord of all’. The meaning of the rest of the verses is clear. When a qualified living entity is not found, Lord Viṣṇu Himself sometimes becomes Brahma and Rudra. When a qualified jiva is available, then the Lord enters that soul and becomes Brahma and Rudra. This is the conclusion in the Viṣṇu Purāṇa. The first case is shown in the verses starting with: brahma narayanakhyo’sau kalpadau bhagavan yatha, prajah sasarja bhagavan brahma narayanatmakah (1.4.1), “In the beginning of creation, the Supreme Lord called Nārāyaṇa is Brahma. Lord Brahma, who is actually a form of Lord Nārāyaṇa, created all living entities. ” The second case is shown in the verses starting with:jusan rajo-gunarh tatra svayam visvesvaro harih, brahma bhUtvatha jagato visrstau sampravartate (1.2.61), “Entering the mode of passion, Lord Hari Himself, the Supreme Lord of the universe, becomes Brahma and works in the creation of the universe. ” This is the same conclusion presented by Srila Rūpa Gosvamt in the Laghu-bhāgavatāmṛta (1.2.16-33). According to the following verses from the Mahā-bhārata, Brahma and Siva are jivas, for it is described that they obtained their power on account of pious activities: yuga-koti-sahasrani visnum aradhya padmabhūh, punas trailokya-dhatrtvam praptavan iti susruma, “We heard that after worshipping Lord Viṣṇu for millions of yugas, Brahma then attained the status of creator of the three worlds». māyā srstah pura brahma mad-yajnam ayajat svayam, tatas tasya varan prito dadav aham anuttaman, mat-putratvam ca kalpadau lokadhyaksatvam eva ca (12.236.57-58), “Having been created by Me, Brahma himself first worshipped Me. Then, upon becoming pleased, I gave Him incomparable blessings and made him my son and master of the universe in the beginning of the kalpa». visvarūpo mahadevah sarvamedhe maha-kratau, juhava sarva-bhutani svayam atmanam atmana (12.8.36), “Lord Siva, who is known as Visvarūpa andMahadeva, once offered himself and all living entities as offering in a great universal sacrifice (sarvamedha). ” mahadevah sarvamedhe mahatma juhvatmanam deva-devo babhūva, visvaml lokan vyapya vistabhya kirtya virajate dyutiman krtti-vasa (12.20.12), “After offering himself as offering in a great universal sacrifice, the magnanimous Lord Siva became the lord of the demigods. Pervading and supporting the whole universe, resplendent, and dressed in a skin, he now shines by his glories». The author then states the fault in the opposite view, “The fault of those, etc." A heretic (pasandi) is one who is foreign to the Vedas. It is similarly stated in the Pancaratra: yo mohad visnum anyena hina-devena durmatih, sadharanam sakrd brūte so ‘ntyajo nantyajo ‘ntyajah, “A mere outcaste is not really an outcaste. A real outcaste is a fool who, out of ignorance, even once says that Lord Viṣṇu is equal to lower demigods. ” The word ‘hina ‘refers to those who are lower in comparison to Lord Viṣṇu.

### Text 21

**Перевод:**

**According to the narrative about the sage Upamanyu found in the Mahā-bhārata, Lord Kṛṣṇa worshipped Rudra by performing austerities in order to have a son with Jambavati. Therein it is also mentioned that Viṣṇu and all demigods emanate from Rudra’s limbs. As for the ignorant who imagine that on this account it is undeniable that Rudra is the supreme powerful Lord, that is very foolish, considering the above quoted scriptures. It is also seen in the smṛti that Rudra was actually defeated by Lord Kṛṣṇa in the fight against Bana, recited prayers to Him as His own origin, was bewildered by His appearance as Mohini, and was saved by Him from the sin of killing a brāhmaṇa and from the troubles created by Vrka and others.**

**Комментарий:**

Someone may argue, “It is not possible that even Rudra is controlled by Viṣṇu, for the evidence in the narrative about Upamanyu, which was spoken by Kṛṣṇa, substantiates that Rudra is the supreme powerful Lord. Also, in the Hari-vamsa (2.74.34), desiring to fight with Indra, Kṛṣṇa prayed to Rudra in the following words: aham brahma kapilo‘thapy anantah putrah sarve brāhmaṇas cativirah, tvattah sarve deva-deva prasuta evam sarvesah karanatma tvam tdyah, ‘O Lord of the demigods, I, Brahma, Kapila, Ananta, all the very powerful sons of Brahma — all of us were born from you. Thus, you are the Lord of all, the worshipable cause of everything’. Therefore, Rudra and Kṛṣṇa must be either non-different or similar. Yet Rudra alone has supreme power». The author refutes this view by calling it very foolish, and the reason for this is all the above quoted scriptures. Siva’s defeat in Kṛṣṇa’s fight against Bana, and other incidents as well, are mentioned in the Bhāgavatam and other scriptures. Vrka was the son ofSakuni. By the word ‘aditah ‘(etc.) it must be included the incident of Rudra’s chariot falling down in the fight against Tripura, Lord Kṛṣṇa in the form of a bull lifting it again and giving strength to Rudra, as learnt from the Hari-vamsa (3.133.70). Once Rudra cut off one of the heads of his father, Brahma. The skull stuck to Rudra’s hand and did not come off even after bathing in many places of pilgrimage. However, by Lord Viṣṇu’s mercy, that skull was broken into pieces, and Rudra became purified. This is well-known from the Matsya Purāṇa (chapter 183). It has been previously mentioned that the Mahā-bhārata states that Rudra’s powers are dependent on his pious deeds, which is a characteristic of his status as ajiva. His dependence on Lord Viṣṇu’s blessings to become the lord of all beings, and his not being freed from sin are also characteristics of his status, as we shall explain later.

### Text 22

**Перевод:**

**Therefore, it should be considered that Lord Kṛṣṇa is actually offering respects to Himself as Paramatma within Rudra’s heart while promulgating His devotee’s worship. His intention is to support Rudra’s worship among all those who have material desires, apart from His own devotees, as it is said in the Narayaniya section of the Mahā-bhārata by Lord Kṛṣṇa Himself: aham atma hi lokanam visvesam pandu-nandana, tasmad atmanam evagre rudram sampūjayamy aham (12.328.21), “I am the soul of all living entities, О son of Pandu. Therefore, I actually worship Myself within Rudra.” māyā krtam pramānam hi lokah samanuvartate, pramānani hi pujyani tatas tam pujayamy aham (12.328.23), “People follow the standards I set. Because these standards are honoured by them, therefore I worship Siva». na hi visnuh pranamati kasmaicid vibudhaya ca, ata atmanam eveti tato rudram bhajamy aham (12.328.26), “Viṣṇu certainly does not bow down to any demigod. Therefore, I worship Myself as Rudra.”**

**Комментарий:**

Someone may argue, “In the narrative about Upamanyu, it is understood that Viṣṇu worships Rudra. If Viṣṇu alone is the Supreme Lord of all, then how is that narrative coherent ?” The author responds to this here. Just as Lord Kṛṣṇa offers respects to His own devotees like Ugrasena for their prestige, the case of Siva should be seen similarly. The quoted verses were spoken by Lord Kṛṣṇato Arjuna. The word ‘atma ‘means ‘the Paramatma within, Who instigates the living entities’. The words ‘atmanam rudram ‘mean ‘Myself in the form of Rudra’. Someone may argue, “In between these three verses there is another verse in which Kṛṣṇastates that Rudra is non-different than Himself, yo’sau rudrah so‘ham asmi yo ‘ham asmi sivah parah, yatha rudras tathaham ca navayor antaram tatha, ‘Rudra is I, and I am Rudra, the Supreme. As Rudra is, so am I. There is no difference between both of us’. How is this to make sense?” You should understand that such non-difference is caused by love, as seen in the Śrimad-Bhāgavatam (l 1.26.34): devata bandhavah santah santa atmaham eva ca, “My saintly devotees are My deities, my relatives, My life and soul, My very Self ” If such non-difference is caused by love for His saintly devotees, how much more true is the case of Rudra, the crest jewel of devotees. One should be satisfied by this explanation. Otherwise, the statement that Brahma and Rudra are under the control of Lord Viṣṇu would be contradicted, as seen in previous and subsequent verses, such as: etau tau vibudha-sresthau prasada-krodhajau smrtau (12.328.17), “Brahma and Rudra, the best among demigods, are said to have been born from Lord Nārāyaṇa s grace and anger». tavantaratma mama ca ye canye dehi-samjnitah (12.339.4), “Lord Hari is the Supreme Soul and witness within you, me, and all embodied beings. ” We shall write about the verse ‘aham krtam ‘in the next paragraph. Lord Viṣṇu does not bow down (pranamati) to anyone, as stated in the Śvetāśvatara Upaniṣad (3.9): vrksa iva stabdho divi tisthaty ekah, “The only Lord is situated in the spiritual sky, immovable like a tree. ”

### Text 23

**Перевод:**

**The following is clear here: “I am the Supreme Soul within everyone, therefore I offer respects to My expansion in the form of Rudra, which is undistinguished from Me, just as iron is undistinguished from fire in a lump of hot iron. Otherwise, the standard set by Me that Rudra and other demigods should be revered would be contradicted. Therefore, I offer them respects. Since there is no one superior to Me, I do not worship anyone with the idea that this person is superior to Me. However, I do worship My own expansion.” In the Mahā-bhārata (12.339-4), Brahma told Rudra that Lord Viṣṇu is the Supreme Soul within both of them and everyone else: tavantaratma mama ca ye canye dehi-samjnitah, sarvesam saksi-bhuto’sau na grahyah kenacit kvacit, “Lord Hari is the Supreme Soul and witness within you, me, and all embodied beings. He can never be perceived by anyone.”**

**Комментарий:**

Here the author explains the meaning of the verses. By quoting a subsequent verse, he clarifies that Lord Viṣṇu is the Supreme Soul within Brahma, Rudra, and everyone else. This same verse also explains the following verse from the Śrimad-Bhāgavatam (8.7.31): na te giritrakhila-loka-pala-virinca-vaikuntha-surendra-gamyam, jyotih param yatra rajas tamas ca sattvam nayad brahma nirasta-bhedam, “O Siva, your supreme effulgence, where there are no modes of goodness, passion, and ignorance, and which is Brahman itself, cannot be conceived by the rulers of the universe, Indra, Brahma, and the Lord of Vaikuṇṭha». The meaning is that, “Within your heart, you have meditated on the supreme effulgence, Brahman devoid of duality, hence you are capable to remove the poison». Otherwise, the following statement of Siva in the Śrimad-Bhāgavatam (9.4.56) would be contradicted: vayam na tataprabhavama bhUmni, “Dear Durvasa, I, Brahma, and other demigods have no control over the Supreme Person, Lord Viṣṇu. "It is not incorrect to say that such effulgence cannot be conceived even for the Lord of Vaikuṇṭha, for in the Śrimad-Bhāgavatam (10.87.41) it is said: dyu-pataya eva te na yayur antam anantataya tvam api, “Since You are unlimited, neither the lords of the heavenly planets nor You Yourself know Your limits. ”

### Text 24

**Перевод:**

**Therefore, in order to support Rudra’s worship among those who have material desires, Lord Kṛṣṇa offered him respects as requested by His devotee Rudra. Thus, Rudra is not the Supreme powerful Lord.**

**Комментарий:**

Here the author concludes the present topic. In the Varāha Purāṇa (10.1-14) there is a narration that the demigods requested Lord Kṛṣṇa that they would receive worship, and He made them worshipable. Lord Varāha said: “O earth, having thus created the whole universe, the Supreme Lord, the benefactor of all living beings, stopped, and then the creation kept on expanding. As the creation expanded, performing great Soma sacrifices with faith and desiring to make themselves worshipable, all demigods accompanied by Indra became very satisfied. When they became thus satisfied in course of many thousands of years, the Supreme Lord became pleased and appeared before them. The Supreme God, with multiple arms, bellies, mouths, and eyes, then spoke in a voice as if tearing the peak of a great mountain, ‘О best of demigods, what boon should I give you ? Let Indra ask for a boon’. The demigods said, ‘O mighty Govinda! All glories to You. It is by the blessings given by You, the best of masters, that we are demigods. Yet even in the world of men, no one worships us. They worship only You, the primeval Lord. Candra, SUrya, the Vasus, the Sadhyas, the Visvedevas, the two Asvini-kumaras, the Maruts, the Usmapas — all these have taken Your shelter. Please make us worshipable in this world, О universal form’. Being thus addressed by the demigods, Lord Hari, the Master of the great yogis, said, ‘I will make all of you worshipable, ‘and disappeared. The demigods also left for their respective abodes, constantly praising the Lord. The Supreme Lord then assumed threefold natures. Becoming thus the creator of a threefold universe and resorting to goodness, passion, and ignorance, the Supreme Lord worshipped the demigods. By the mode of goodness, He recites the Vedas and offers sacrifices to the demigods. Part of His Self became the mode of passion, by which Lord Kesava worshipped with devotion the fierce form of Rudra as death personified carrying a trident, a form of His Self in the mode of passion. By the mode of ignorance, He became situated within the demons. Becoming thus the creator of a threefold universe, the Supreme Lord worshipped the demigods. Hence the world also became manifold. ” Also in the Varāha Purāṇa (73-40-45), being told by Brahma to create progeny, after greatly praising the Supreme Lord Viṣṇu, Rudra said, ‘“O Lord, I have been appointed by Brahma to create progeny. О benefactor of all beings, please give me threefold knowledge for this purpose’. Lord Viṣṇu said, ‘You will become omniscient, an eternal reservoir of knowledge without any doubt. You will be always the most worshipable among the demigods’. Being thus addressed,

Siva joyfully spoke again, ‘O Lord, please give me another boon — that I will be celebrated among all living entities. O Kesava, appearing in a human form, please worship me. Please carry me with Your body and accept a boon from me so that I may be the most worshipable among the demigods’. Lord Viṣṇu said, ‘When I assume a human form among My pastime avataras, I will certainly worship you. May you also give Me a boon. О lord of the demigods, husband of Uma, as you have asked Me to carry you, I shall become a cloud and carry you for a hundred years’. Having spoken in this way, Lord Hari Himself became a cloud and lifted Siva by its waters. He then addressed Siva in the following words, etc.” Similarly, other such narrations are to be seen in different places.

### Text 25

**Перевод:**

**In places where there are specific answers to general questions, it is concluded that Kṛṣṇa is the Supreme Lord. In places where both the questions and answers are specific, another meaning should be surmised. For instance, in the Mahā-bhārata (13.135.2-3), Yuddhisthira asks a general question about the Supreme Truth: kim ekam daivatam loke kim vapy ekam parayanam, stuvantah kam kam arcantah prapnuyur manavah subham, ко dharmah sarva-dharmānam bhavatah paramo matah, kim japan mucyate jantur janma-samsara-bandhanat, “Who is the only God? What is the ultimate goal in this world? Worshipping whom do men attain prosperity? Among all religious principles, which do you consider the best ? By uttering what can one become free from the bondage of repeated birth and death?” Then, Bhisma gave a specific answer starting with the following verse: jagat-prabhum deva-devam anantam purusottamam, stuvan nama-sahasrena puruṣah satatotthitah, “By glorifying the God of gods, the Lord of the universe, the unlimited Supreme Person, by His thousand names, one is saved forever.” Here, it is specifically answered that Lord Viṣṇu is the Supreme Truth and that by devotional service unto Him, one’s repeated birth and death come to an end. Similarly, it is seen in many other places in the Mahā-bhārata that by general questions and specific answers, it is concluded that Lord Viṣṇu is the Supreme. Due to the apprehension that this treatise may become too long, more instances are not mentioned here.**

**Комментарий:**

Those ascertaining the conclusion about the Supreme Truth accept the reasoning by means of general and specific questions and answers. Since this reasoning is found in favour of the argument about Lord Viṣṇu, here the author establishes that Viṣṇu alone is the Supreme Lord. The word ‘tat ‘refers to His supremacy. The meaning of the rest is clear. “In other places” refers to the Nārayāṇiya section. Similarly, in the Viṣṇu Purāṇa (1.1.5), Maitreya asked a general question: yan-mayam ca jagad brahman yatas caitac caracaram, linam astd yatha yatra layam esyati yatra ca, “O brāhmaṇa, what is this universe made of ? From where did all moving and non-moving beings come? Where was all this resting, and where will it merge?” Parasara Muni then gives a specific answer: visnoh sakasad udbhutam jagat tatraiva ca sthitam, sthiti-samyama-kartasau jagato’sya jagac ca yah (1.1.31), “The universe was born from Lord Viṣṇu and is resting in Him. He is the maintainer and destroyer of this universe. He is also the universe itself. ”A similar instance is also seen in the Gopāla-tāpani Upaniṣad.

### Text 26

**Перевод:**

**Since in the narrative about Upamanyu there are specific questions and answers, a different meaning should be surmised. This is also shown there. Otherwise, even the ocean would be the Supreme Lord because Lord Rama worshipped it.**

**Комментарий:**

In the narrative about Upamanyu found in the Moksa-dharma section of the Mahā-bhārata, Yuddhisthira asks Bhisma to speak about the glories of Siva, and Bhisma told him that the lotus-eyed Lord Kṛṣṇa will speak about it. Lord Kṛṣṇa then spoke to him in detail. Thus, here both the question and the answer are specific. “Otherwise, ” i.e., “if it is accepted that Rudra is superior to Kṛṣṇa because Rudra was worshipped by Him, then the ocean also must be superior to Rama. ” In the Ramayana (6.21.1-35), there is a narration about how Lord Rama worshipped the ocean with the desire that it would give way to Lanka. He later scolded the ocean for not giving way.

### Text 27

**Перевод:**

**Similarly, sometimes Lord Kṛṣṇa’s associates also worship other divine beings. That is merely a kind of pastime meant to proclaim that the demigods are worshipable, and it is certainly not in the scope of a philosophical conclusion.**

**Комментарий:**

“Similarly, ” i.e., “just as in the case of the Lord, so is the case with His associates. "Siva was worshipped in Ambikavana by Nanda Maharaja and the other cowherd men. Katyayani was worshipped by the gopis. Sūrya was worshipped by our Queen, Śri Radha. In all these instances, it is only Kṛṣṇa, the Supreme Soul within these demigods, Who is worshipped by His associates, who just follow His intention. Or else, those demigods are worshipped as devotees of Kṛṣṇa. Thus, one should not believe that any of those demigods is the Supreme.

### Text 28

**Перевод:**

**As for the interpretation of names like ‘Mahesa, ‘it should be understood to be as futile as the interpretation of names like ‘Mahendra’. The meaning of the Satarudriya spoken at the end of the Droṇa-parva of the Mahā-bhārata (173.79-106) actually refers to the Supreme Soul within Rudra’s heart, because it has no coherence otherwise. At the end of the war, there is also a description that when Rudra was fighting with Asvatthama, many Kesavas appeared from Rudra’s sparkles of light. That is also not incorrect, for being situated within Him and eager to help him, Lord Viṣṇu simply became manifest in this way. Therefore, Lord Viṣṇu alone is to be worshipped by all, while Rudra is His worshipper and is to be worshipped by the whole world. Thus, Lord Viṣṇu alone has supreme power.**

**Комментарий:**

The Saivas imagine that Rudra is the Supreme Lord on the basis of the interpretation of names like Mahesa, Mahadeva, and so on. Here, the author refutes this idea. According to the meaning of the verb root ‘id, ‘the word ‘indra ‘actually conveys one’s supreme power. That qualified by the word ‘mahat ‘(great) does not demonstrate superiority, since no such a thing exists. Everyone agrees that a living entity who performs one hundred Asvamedha sacrifices attains the position of Indra. Therefore, the explanation of the name ‘Mahesa ‘is as meaningless as the explanation of the name ‘Mahendra’. According to the above quoted scriptures, Rudra has been ascertained to be ajiva, therefore this explanation by interpreting such word meanings in the unrestricted sense cannot denote that he is the Supreme Lord, but it merely expresses his power and greatness in comparison with other demigods. This is the idea. Brahman alone is described as the Supreme powerful Lord, the cause of the creation, maintenance, and destruction of the universe. If it is accepted that Rudra is also the cause, then Brahman .would be twofold, which is a contradiction. Therefore, it is correct that the meaning explained in the Droṇa-parva actually refers to the Supreme Soul within Rudra’s heart. At the end of the war, there is also the following description: tasyasya-nasikabhyam ca sravanabhyam ca sarvasah, tebhyas caksi-sahasrebhyah pradurasan mahdrcisah, tatha tejo-maricibhyah sankha-cakra-gadadharah, pradurasan hrsikesah sataso ‘tha sahasrasah, tad atyadbhutam alokya bhūtam loka-bhayahkaram, draunir avyathito divyaih sara-varsair avakirat (Mahā-bhārata, 10.6.8-10), “Great flames emanated from Rudra’s mouths, nostrils, ears, and thousands of eyes. The hundreds and thousands of Kesavas, holding conchs, wheels, and maces, became manifest from those sparkles of fire. Beholding that amazing being, which terrified the world, Asvatthama remained unperturbed and covered him with a shower of celestial arrows. ” It should be understood that in this case the appearance of Lord Viṣṇu from Rudra’s limbs is just like Lord Narasimha’s appearance from a pillar, or Lord Varāha’s appearance from Brahma’s nostril. The author then concludes the present topic, “Therefore, etc. ” The meaning is clear.

### Text 29

**Перевод:**

**In this regard, there are some who think as follows: “The original element is named Sadasiva, Whose effects are Brahma, Viṣṇu, and Rudra. In the Kaivalya Upaniṣad (5-9) it is said: atyasrama-sthah sakalendriyani nirudhya bhaktya sva-gurum pranamya, hrt-pundarlkam virajam viśuddham vicintya madhye visadam visokam, acintyam avyaktam ananta-rupam sivam prasantam amrtam brahma yonim, tam adi-madhyanta-vihlnam ekam vibhum cid ānandam arupam adbhutam, uma-sahayam paramesvaram prabhum tri-locanam nUa-kantham prasantam, dhyatva munir gacchati bhuta-yonim samasta-saksim tamasah parastat, sa brahma sa sivah sendrah so ‘ksarah paramah svarat, sa eva visnuh sa pranah sa kalo ‘gnih sa candramah, sa eva sarvam yad bhutam yac ca bhavyam sanatanam, jnātvā tam mrtyum atyeti nanyah pantha vimuktaye, ‘After bowing down to one’s guru with devotion and restraining all senses, one situated in sannyasa, the praised asrama, should imagine a pure and spotless lotus within the heart. In the middle of that lotus, one should meditate on Lord Siva, who is pure, free from sorrow, inconceivable, unmanifest, of unlimited forms, peaceful, immortal, who is Brahman itself, the original cause, devoid of beginning, middle, and end, unique, all-pervading, consciousness, bliss, formless, wonderful, the companion of Uma, the Supreme Lord, the subdued master with three eyes and blue neck. A sage who meditates on Him goes to the original source of all beings, who is the witness of everything and is situated beyond darkness. He is Brahma, He is Siva, and Indra as well. He is the imperishable and independent Supreme Lord. He is Viṣṇu, the life air, time, fire, the moon. He is everything that existed and will exist eternally. Knowing Him as such, one crosses beyond death. There is no other way to attain liberation’. Afterwards, it is stated: trisu dhamasu yad bhogyam bhokta bhogas ca yad bhavet, tebhyo vilakṣaṇāh saksi cin-matro ‘ham sadasivah, ‘I am Sadasiva, the witness, and pure consciousness, different from whatever enjoyable things, enjoyers, and enjoyment that exist in the three worlds’. In the Mandūkya Upaniṣad (7) it is said: sivam advaitam caturtham manyante, ‘The sages consider Siva the non-dual fourth state’.**

**Комментарий:**

In order to refute the views of the Mahasaiva sect, the author here explains its tenet. The words ‘atyasrama-sthah ‘mean ‘one situated in the praised asrama, sannyasa, ‘i.e., a renounced person. According to the Visva-kośa (Avyaya, 24), the word ‘ati’ is used in the sense of praise. The sentence ‘hrt-pundarikam viśuddham vicintya ‘means ‘imagining a pure and spotless lotus within the heart, ‘i.e., ‘having made the heart pure by removing desire for sense objects’. In the middle of that lotus, one should meditate on Siva. This is the word order. What is Siva like ? He is pure (visadam), i.e., not even slightly tainted by the illusory energy. The meaning of the rest is clear. To express that He is the soul of everything through His own energy, maya, the text says, “He is Brahman. ” Although He is the soul of everything, He does not fall from His own nature. This is said, “He is imperishable (aksarah).” “He is everything (sarvam) that exists, starting with Brahma, everything that existed (bhūtam), and everything that will exist (bhavyam) in the next creation». “Knowing (jnātvā) Him (tarn) as eternal (sanatanam), etc.” This is the connection of the words. “Afterwards, ” i.e., subsequently in the same book. The word ‘dhamasu ‘means ‘in the three worlds’. The ‘enjoyers ‘(bhokta) are the multitude of living beings starting with Brahma. The word ‘tebhyah ‘means ‘from those enjoyable things, enjoyers, and enjoyment’. The word ‘anyatra ‘(elsewhere) refers to the Mandukya Upaniṣad. Sadasiva is immutable consciousness, the fourth state, turiya, which is different from the states of waking (visva), dreaming (taijasa), and deep sleep (prajna). This is the meaning.

### Text 30

**Перевод:**

**“Therefore, Sadasiva is the original cause, while Brahma and others are His effects, as stated in the Atharva-sikha Upaniṣad: brahma-visnu-rudrendras te sarve prasuyante, ‘Brahma, Viṣṇu, Rudra, and Indra are all born’. The creation of Brahma and so on becomes manifest from the pure Sadasiva in the form of knowledge and bliss by the union with His inconceivable energy, and liberation is possible through His worship. In the Śvetāśvatara Upaniṣad, the same Sadasiva is denoted by names like ‘Hara, ‘‘Rudra, ‘and so on.”**

**Комментарий:**

Here the Mahasaiva doctrine is concluded. The śruti is quoted as evidence that Brahma, Viṣṇu, and Rudra are effects of Sadasiva without any doubt. The word ‘tasmat ‘means ‘from Sadasiva’.

### Text 31

**Перевод:**

**In response, this is what we say: That Sadasiva is actually Viṣṇu, for being devoid of all defects, He is auspisciousness personified and the worshipable Deity of all. In the Viṣṇu Purāṇa (1.22.53) it is stated: evam-prakaram amalam nityam vyapakam aksayam, samasta-heya-rahitam visnv-akhyam parāmaṁ padam, “Such is the supreme position named Viṣṇu, unalloyed, eternal, all-pervading, undecaying, and devoid of all defects.” In the Mahā-bhārata (13.135.10) it is also said: pavitranam pavitram yo mangalanam ca mangalam, daivatam devatanam ca bhutanam yo ‘vyayah pita, “Lord Viṣṇu is the purest among the pure, the most auspicious among the auspicious. He is the worshipable Deity of all demigods and the imperishable father of all living entities.” When Goddess Laksmi appeared from the churning of the milk ocean, she also concluded that only Lord Viṣṇu is all auspiciousness personified.**

**Комментарий:**

Here the author refutes the above doctrine. The verse ‘evam-prakaram ‘is from the Viṣṇu Purāṇa (1.22.53). Before that, we find the following verses: nirvyaparam anakhyeyam vyapti-matram anaupamyam, atma-sambodha-visayam satta-matram alakṣaṇām, prasantam abhayath śuddhaṁ durvibhavyam asamsritam, visnor jnāna-mayasyoktam taj jnānam parāmaṁ padam (1.22.50-51), “Knowledge about Lord Viṣṇu, Who is knowledge personified, is said to be the supreme position, which is devoid of material activity, indescribable, universal, and incomparable. It reveals the self, it is pure existence, and it cannot be inferred. It is peaceful, fearless, pure, difficult to be apprehended, and it is not dependent on anything. ” The meaning is as follows: This knowledge is devoid of material activity (nirvyaparam) and it cannot be fully grasped by words (anakhyeyam). It reveals the self to oneself (atma-sambodha-visayam), it is merely known to exist (satta-matram), it cannot be inferred (alakṣaṇām), i.e., it is understood only through the scriptures. It is also stated there that Lord Viṣṇu’s svarūpa cannot be understood by Brahma, Rudra, and so on: yan na deva na munayo na caham na ca sankarah, jananti paramesasya tad visnoh parāmaṁ padam (1.9.56), “Neither the demigods, nor the sages, nor I, nor Śaṇkara know the supreme position of the Supreme Lord Viṣṇu». It is also said: saktayo yasya devasya brahma-visnu-sivatmikah, bhavanty abhūta-pūrvasya tad visnoh parāmaṁ padam (1.9.55), “That is the supreme position of Lord Viṣṇu, Who is unprecedented and Whose energies consist of Brahma, Viṣṇu, and Siva. ”Lord Viṣṇu is unprecedented (abhuta-pūrvasya) because He has no beginning. In this way, the misconception that Sadasiva’s svarūpa cannot be understood by Brahma and others has also been removed. The verse ‘pavitranam pavitram ‘is from the Mahā-bhārata (13.135.10). The word ‘pavitranam ‘means ‘among the pure, such as the Ganga, ‘and ‘pavitram ‘means ‘which yields the power of purification’. The word ‘mangalanam ‘means ‘among those who dispel obstacles, such as Ganesa, ‘and ‘mangalam ‘means ‘one who yields the power of dispelling obstacles’. Among all worshipable demigods (devanam), such as Indra, Lord Viṣṇu is the worshipable Deity (daivatam), i.e., He yields the power of being worship able. Laksmi Devi’s conclusion about the Lord is to be seen in the Śrimad-Bhāgavatam (8.8.17-23).

### Text 32

**Перевод:**

**In the Subala Upaniṣad (7) it is also said: antah-śarire nihito guhayam aja eko nityo yasya prthivi sariram, “The eternal unborn and unique Lord, Whose body is the earth, is situated within the core of the heart.” In the paragraph starting in this way, by His quality of being free from all sins it is then expressed that only Nārāyaṇa, Lord Viṣṇu, has exceeding auspiciousness: sa esa sarva-bhutantaratmapahata-papma divyo deva eko narayanah, “Only the divine Lord Nārāyaṇa, Who is the Supreme Soul within all living entities, is free from sin.” In the Nārāyaṇa Upaniṣad it is also declared: atha puruso ha vai narayano ‘kamayata prajah srjeyeti narayanad brahma jayate, “The Supreme Person, Nārāyaṇa, desired, ‘Let Me create progeny’. From Lord Nārāyaṇa Brahma was born.” Starting thus, it is subsequently stated that the creation starting with Brahma and Rudra is produced from Lord Viṣṇu, the son of Devaki, Who is called Nārāyaṇa. Because He is devoid of all defects, He is very auspicious, and by His worship one attains liberation. This is mentioned afterwards in the same text. Therein it is also said: nityo niskalanko nirakhyato nirvikalpo niranjanah suddho deva eko narayano na dvitiyo’sti kascit, ya evam veda sa visnur eva bhavati, “The Supreme Lord Nārāyaṇa is eternal, free from defects, expressed by the Vedas, free from duality, unalloyed, and pure. He is one without a second. One who knows Lord Viṣṇu in this way becomes similar to Him. ” In other places it is also stated: eko ha vai narayana asin na brahma ne£anah (Maha Upaniṣad, l.l), “Only Lord Nārāyaṇa existed, and not Brahma nor Siva.” eko ha vai narayana asin na brahma na ca sankarah, sa munir bhutva samacintayat tata ete vyajayanta, visve hiranyagarbhagni-yama-varuna-rudrendrah, “Only Lord Nārāyaṇa existed, and not Brahma nor Siva. Becoming contemplative, He meditated. From Him were born Hiranyagarbha, Agni, Yama, Varuna, Rudra, Indra, and all beings.”**

**Комментарий:**

Here the author gives some instances of śruti interpretation in the context of the meaning explained above. The word ‘guhayam ‘means ‘in the lotus of the heart’. The transcendental son of Devaki is Lord Nārāyaṇa, for it is so stated in the Nārāyaṇa Upaniṣad. The word ‘niskalankah ‘means ‘free from defects, ‘and ‘nirakhyatah ‘means ‘expressed by all the Vedas’. The word ‘nirvikalpah ‘means ‘devoid of dual nature, ‘as stated in the Brahma-sūtra (l.l.io): gati-samanyat, “Because this is the knowledge equally received from all Vedic scriptures». The word ‘niranjanah ‘means ‘devoid of the taint of material nature’. Therefore, He is pure (suddhah). There is no one else like Him, therefore He is one without a second, as stated in the Śvetāśvatara Upaniṣad (6.8): na tat-samas cabhyadhikas ca drsyate, “No one is equal or superior to the Supreme Lord. ” One who knows Lord Viṣṇu in this way and worships Him becomes similar to Him, being a part and parcel of Him, and free from the illusory energy. According to the Visva-kośa (Avyaya, 63), the word ‘eva ‘is used in the sense of similarity and restriction. It should be known that the word ‘anyatra ‘(in other places) refers to other scriptures like the Maha Upaniṣad and so forth.

### Text 33

**Перевод:**

**In the Taittiriya Arany aka (i0.ll.l-5) also, the word ‘narayana ‘is mentioned in the same case-relation as the word’siva:’sahasra-sirsam devam visvaksam visva-sambhuvam, visvam narayanam devam aksaram paramarh padam, visvatah parāmaṁ nityam visvam narayanam harim, visvam evedam puruṣas tad visvam upajlvati, patim visvasyatmesvaram sasvatam sivam acyutam, narayanam maha-jneyam visvatmanam parayanam, narayanah paro jyotir atma narayanah parah, narayanah param brahma tattvam narayanah parah, narayanah paro dhyata dhyanam narayanah parah, yac ca kincij jagaty asmin drsyate srayate ‘pi va, antar bahis ca tat sarvam vyapya narayanah sthitah, “One should meditate on the Supreme Lord, the supreme position, the imperishable Lord Nārāyaṇa, Who has thousands of heads and pervades the universe. He is the benefactor of the universe and the universe itself. Lord Nārāyaṇa, Hari, the Supreme Person, is eternally beyond the universe, and is Himself the universe. The universe rests on Him, the Supreme Person. He is the protector of the universe and controller of all living entities. Lord Acyuta Himself is the eternal Siva. Nārāyaṇa is the ultimate goal, the soul of the universe and the great object of knowledge. Nārāyaṇa is the Supreme Lord, the spiritual effulgence. The Supreme Lord Nārāyaṇa is the Supreme Soul. The Supreme Lord Nārāyaṇa is the Para-brahman. The Supreme Lord Nārāyaṇa is the Supreme Absolute Truth. The Supreme Lord Nārāyaṇa is the meditator. The Supreme Lord Nārāyaṇa is the meditation. Pervading everything that is seen or heard, within and without this universe, Lord Nārāyaṇa is situated in His own position.” Therefore, it is thus stated in the Narayaniya section of the Mahā-bhārata (12.335.83) — tattvam jijnasamananam hetubhih sarvato-mukhaih, tattvam eko maha-yogi harir narayanah prabhuh, “For those inquiring about the Truth by means of extensive reasoning, the Supreme Lord Nārāyaṇa, Hari, the greatest yogi, is the only Truth.”**

**Комментарий:**

Here the author says that because the words’siva ‘and ‘narayana ‘appear in the same case-relation, it must be accepted that Lord Viṣṇu Himself is Siva. “In the Taittiriya Āraṇyaka,” i.e., in the Maha-narayana

Upaniṣad (13-1-5), which is part of that text. He pervades the universe and is therefore called ‘visvaksam’. He brings about auspiciousness (sam) to the universe and is hence called ’sambhuvam’. By His own energy, He exists in the form of the universe. Similarly, below there will be repeated statements that corroborate this meaning. “The Supreme Person Himself is the universe. ” This is the prose order. In the sentence ‘tad visvam upajivati, ‘the word ‘visvam ‘(the universe) is the subject, and the object is the Supreme Person, upon whom the universe rests (upajivati). Therefore, He is the protector (patim) of the universe. He is the controller (tsvaram) of all living entities starting with Brahma. Among the objects of knowledge (jheyam), He is the great one (mahan) to be meditated by those aspiring for liberation. Lord Nārāyaṇa is both the meditator (dhyata), or worshipper, and the meditation. This statement about non-difference is in the sense that the pervaded is not separated from the pervasive, and not that they have the same svarūpa. The author clarifies this by quoting the text ‘yac ca kincit’. Whatever (yat kincit) is seen (drsyate) within this universe (jagati), such as kings, or whatever is heard about, such as Indra — Lord Nārāyaṇa pervades all that and remains situated in His own position. By such concept ofpervasive non-dualism, the object of meditation is non-different from the meditator and the meditation. This is the meaning. When two words that have different semantic causes are used in a single sense, there is identity of case-relation (samanadhikaranya). Yet the word ‘narayana ‘is used only for Lord Viṣṇu. This is stated by the verse quoted from the Mahā-bhārata (!2.335.83). Its meaning is clear.

### Text 34

**Перевод:**

**The word ‘narayana ‘is definitely a name of the husband of Goddess Laksmi on account of the employment of ‘natva’. As stated by the illustrious Panini (8.4.3): purva-padat samjnayam agah. Due to a particular cause in the preceding word, a subsequent ‘n ‘in a name changes into ‘n, ‘but not if there is a letter ‘g ‘intervening. This is the meaning of the aphorism. For instance, ‘Nārāyaṇa’. Why not after ‘g ‘? Because in such cases there is no ‘natva, ‘as in the word ‘rg-ayanam ‘(a treatise on the Rg Veda). Therefore, Lord Viṣṇu Himself is called Sadasiva. Otherwise, all the śruti and the smṛti would be contradictory. The words ‘paramatma ‘and ‘brahman, ‘which express Brahma-vidya in the Kaivalya Upaniṣad, would also be contradictory. These two words are certainly well-known for denoting Lord Viṣṇu. It has been previously mentioned that words such as ’siva ‘are used in connection with Lord Viṣṇu Himself.**

**Комментарий:**

In the Viṣṇu Purāṇa (1.4.6) it is said: apo nara itiprokta apo vai nara-sUnavah, ayanam tasya tah purvam tena narayanah smrtah, “The waters that emanated from the Supreme Lord (nara) in the beginning of creation are called ‘nara’. Because those waters became His resting place (ayana), He is called Nārāyaṇa». In the Narasimha Purāṇa (64.61) it is also stated: naraj jatani tattvani naraniti vidur budhah, tasya tany ayanam purvam tena narayanah smrtah, “The elements were born from the Supreme Person (nara) in the beginning of creation. Therefore, the wise know them as ‘nara’. Because those elements became His resting place (ayana), He is called Nārāyaṇa.” The word ‘narayana ‘is an exclusive designation of Lord Viṣṇu on account of His entering either the waters of the causal ocean or the elements produced from Him, the Supreme Person, thus having both as His resting place. This is also understood from Panini’s aphorism. This is what the author says in the present paragraph. “Otherwise, ” i.e., “if it is accepted that Sadasiva is someone else and the cause of even Viṣṇu.” The words ‘tatraiva ‘mean ‘in the Kaivalya Upaniṣad’. The word ‘tau ‘refers to the words ‘brahman ‘and ‘paramatma, ‘which are well-known in scriptures like the Bhagavad-glta.

### Text 35

**Перевод:**

**Someone may argue, “ How can words such as ‘uma-sahaya, ‘‘tri-locana, ‘and ‘nila-kantha ‘apply to Lord Viṣṇu, since there is no reason for this?” This argument is foolish, for these words have different meanings. For instance, ‘uma ‘means ‘glory’. According to the dictionary Visva-prakāśa (Ma, 34), the word ‘uma ‘is used in the sense of flax, the daughter of Himavan, turmeric, splendour, and glory. Lord Viṣṇu is accompanied (sahaya) by glory (uma) in the beginning of creation and is thus called ‘Uma-sahaya’. ‘Tri-netra ‘means ‘one who has knowledge (netra) about past, present, and future (tri)’. As stated in the Bhagavad-gita (7-26): vedaham samatitani vartamanani carjuna, bhavisyani ca bhutani mam tu veda na kascana, “O Arjuna, I know all beings that existed in the past, that exist at present, and that will exist.in the future. Yet nobody knows Me.” ‘M/e ‘is a kind of jewel (blue sapphire). Lord Viṣṇu is very fond of blue sapphires because they resemble His complexion. Therefore, ‘nila-kantha ‘means ‘one on whose neck there is a necklace of blue sapphires’. Or else, ‘nila ‘is a kind of divine treasure, in which case that means ‘one who wears nila around the neck’. The scriptures tend to teach spiritual knowledge through esoteric statements, therefore rejecting the apparent meaning of words is not a fault in this connection.**

**Комментарий:**

The word ‘atra ‘means ‘when interpreted in connection with Lord Viṣṇu’. “Since there is no reason, ” i.e., “because there is no semantic cause, such as being accompanied by Parvati.” This being a semantic cause (pravrtti-nimitta), one whose companion is glory (uma) is called ‘Uma-sahaya’.
