By the word ‘nila ‘in the sense of a kind of divine treasure, the other eight kinds of divine treasures are also implied. The idea is that Lord Viṣṇu is He by Whose blessings the devotees who have material desires obtain all treasures. Someone may argue, “How can the śruti impart instructions in this way?” In response, the author says, “The scriptures tend to teach, etc. ”

### Text 36

**Перевод:**

**On the other hand, these words would not apply to the Sadasiva of your doctrine, in which case reasons like being the husband of Parvati do not exist, for they exist only in the effect, Rudra.**

**Комментарий:**

After establishing his own view, the author here speaks about the lack of reasoning in the opponent’s view. The word ‘tatra ‘means ‘in Sadasiva, who is undifferentiated consciousness, non-dual, and the cause of everything’. Those words can apply only to the effect, Rudra (Kapali), the son of Brahma. This is the meaning.

### Text 37

**Перевод:**

**In the Śvetāśvatara Upaniṣad it is also taught that Lord Viṣṇu alone is the Para-brahman, the Supreme Absolute Truth, because from the very beginning in the sentence ‘brahma-vadino vadanti ‘up to the end, the word ‘brahman ‘is repeated. The word ‘bhagavan ‘is also repeated, such as: sarva-vyapi sa bhagavan (3.11), “The Supreme Lord is all-pervading.” evam sa devo bhagavan varenyah (5-4), “The Supreme Lord is the highest God.” This word is primarily used in connection with Lord Viṣṇu. The Purāṇa that has Lord Viṣṇu as its main topic is called ‘Bhāgavatam’. In the Viṣṇu Purāṇa (6.5.73-75), the etymology of the word ‘bhagavan ‘is explained in connection with Lord Viṣṇu alone: sambharteti tatha bharta bha-karo ‘rtha-dvayanvitah, neta gamayita srasta ga-kararthas tatha mune, aiśvaryasya samagrasya viryasya yasasah sriyah, jnāna-vairagyayos caiva sannam bhaga itingana, vasanti tatra bhutani bhutatmany akhilātmani, sa ca bhutesv asesesu va-kararthas tato ‘vyayah, “O sage, the syllable ‘bha ‘has two meanings — maintainer and protector. The syllable ‘ga ‘means leader, carrier, and creator. Full opulence, strength, fame, beauty, knowledge, and renunciation — all these six, either combined or individually, are expressed by the word ‘bhaga’. All living beings dwell within Him, the imperishable Supreme Soul of everyone, and He dwells within all of them. This is the meaning of the syllable ‘va.Then, the next verse concludes: evam esa maha-sabdo maitreya bhagavan iti, parama-brahma-bhutasya vasudevasya nanyagah, “O Maitreya, in this way this great word, ‘bhagavan, ‘pertains to Lord Vāsudeva, the Supreme Absolute Truth, and to no one else.” Therefore, this Upaniṣad is devoted to Lord Viṣṇu. If Sadasiva is also said to have attributes such as being called ‘Brahman, ‘then the disagreement is merely about names, and not about the named whatsoever.**

**Комментарий:**

It has been previously claimed that the sage Śvetāśvatara described this same Sadasiva in his Upaniṣad by uttering words such as Hara, Rudra, and Siva, but this is also not possible. This is what the author affirms here. In the Viṣṇu Purāṇa (6.5.73-75), the great sage Parasara explained the etymology of the word ‘bhagavan ‘in connection with Lord Viṣṇu only. “This Upaniṣad,” i.e., the Upaniṣad recited by the sage Śvetāśvatara in six chapters. The Mahasaiva may argue, “The Sadasiva described by me is the very Para-brahman, the Supreme Lord Himself. ” In response, the author says, “Then, the difference is merely in name, while the named is one and the same in the view of both of us». The idea is this: We hold the same internal characteristics as you do. As for external marks like bearing cakra, vertical tilaka, and tulasi, that is no difference at all. Someone may argue, “These things are not used because they are condemned in the Suta-sarūhita of the Skanda Purāṇa. On the other hand, ashes, horizontal tilaka, and so forth, are put on, for they are praised in the same text. "In reply, you should know that such criticism is done only out of envy, which is fruit of ignorance. Suta Gosvāmi is surrendered unto Vyāsadeva, and it is definitely not possible that he would speak such things in contradiction to his master, who praised the use of marks like conch, disc, and so on in the Padma Purāṇa a\*id other scriptures. Nor would he be laudable for his speech speaking in this way. Therefore, the Suta-saihhita is a concoction, as it is the case with texts like the Kasi-khanda of the Skanda Purāṇa, the Yoga-vasistha, the Astavakra-gita, and others. The wise say that the statements in those text which in part agree with the Vaiṣṇava views are meant to attract others.

### Text 38

**Перевод:**

**Someone may argue, “Viṣṇu is one of the three presiding deities of the modes of nature and is born, for it is stated in the Atharva-sikha Upaniṣad: brahma-visnu-rudrendras te sarve prasuyante, ‘Brahma, Viṣṇu, Rudra, and Indra are all born’. His origin is also seen in the Śrimad-Bhāgavatam, where it is described that the three gunavataras — Brahma, Viṣṇu, and Rudra — emanated from the puruṣavatara, Garbhodakasayi.” This is true. It is mentioned that Brahma and Rudra‘s birth and death are subject to their previous activities because they are jivas. On the other hand, having unfailing svarūpa-śakti and divine nature, Lord Viṣṇu’s regular manifestation of His appearance and activities as an avatara among the three divinities is meant for protecting the creation according to His own will, for the śruti refutes His birth and death in the following words: eko ‘pi san bahudha yo ‘vabhati (Gopāla-tāpani Upaniṣad, 1.19), “Although one, Kṛṣṇa appears as many.” ekam**

**Комментарий:**

santam bahudha drsyamanam, “Although one, the Supreme Lord is seen as many.” ajayamano bahudha vijayate (Puruṣa-sūkta), “The unborn Supreme Lord is born in many forms.” Therefore, in the (Śrimad-Bhāgavatam, 2.6.32) it is said that Lord Viṣṇu, in the form of a puruṣavatara, maintains the universe: srjami tan-niyukto ‘ham haro harati tad-vasah, visvam puruṣa-nipena paripati tri-śakti-dhrk, “Being appointed by Him, I, Brahma, create the universe. Under His control, Siva destroys it. Holding three energies, the Supreme Lord Himself in the form of a puruṣavatara maintains it.” It is not said that His appearance is subject to His previous karma.

As the Mahasaiva doctrine has been thus refuted, feeling very pained after hearing that Rudra is born and dies, with eyes red with anger, someone exclusively devoted to Kapali comes forward to argue with sophistry that Viṣṇu, the presiding deity of the mode of goodness and maintainer of the universe, is not eternal. “There should be no controversy on this matter, for you also agree that the three gunavataras emanate from Garbhodakasayi. ” Even though the Mahasaiva resorts to the Vedas on account of being a twice born, due to his contempt towards Lord Viṣṇu, he has not realized His real nature and thus babbles among his peers that Viṣṇu’s taking birth among forms such as a boar, His being killed by an arrow, and His dark complexion are signs of Viṣṇu’s inferiority, while Rudra is unborn, is not killed by any weapon, and has a fair complexion, which are signs of his superiority over Viṣṇu. The author replies to the objection saying that it is true that the gunavataras emanate from Garbhodakasayi. By the word ‘adi, ‘death is meant. This is understood from the above quoted smṛti: yuga-koti-sahasrani visnum aradhya padmabhuh, punas trailokya-dhatrtvam praptavan iti susruma, “We heard that after worshipping Lord Viṣṇu for millions of yugas, Brahma then attained the status of creator of the three worlds». brahma sambhus tathaivarkas candramas ca satakratuh, evam adyas tathaivanye yukta vaiṣṇava-tejasa, jagat-karyavasane tu viyujyante ca tejasa, vitejasas ca te sarve pancatvam upayanti ca (Viṣṇu-dharma Purāṇa, 1.171.5-6), “Brahma, Siva, Surya, Candra, Indra, and other demigods are all endowed with the power of Lord Viṣṇu. However, at the end of their duties in the universe, they become devoid of that power. Being powerless, all of them perish». The text ‘ajayamano bahudha ‘is from the Puruṣa-sukta. By the word ‘adi ‘in ‘karma-vasyotpatty-adi-nirasat ‘it is meant that Lord Viṣṇu has no death but simply disappearance, as described by Him: icchan muhurtan nasyeyam (Mahā-bhārata, 12.326.42), “Desiring so, I may disappear in a moment.” The word’svabhava ‘means ‘nature’. According to the Dhananjaya-kośa, the words’svabhava, ‘‘prakṛti, ‘and ’sila ‘are synonyms of ‘nature’. The words ’svecchavatara ‘mean that the Lord descends (avatara) from His abode into this material world according to His own (sva) will (iccha). In the Viṣṇu Purāṇa the sage Parasara said that Brahma, Viṣṇu, and Siva are energies of Para-brahman, but out of them, Viṣṇu is directly the Supreme: brahma-visnu-siva brahman pradhana brahma-saktayah (1.22.58), “O brāhmaṇa, Brahma, Viṣṇu, and Siva are the main energies of the Supreme.” sarva-śakti-mayo visnuh svarupam brāhmaṇah param (1.22.61), “Lord Viṣṇu is full with all energies. He is the supreme svarūpa of the Supreme Absolute Truth.” sa parah sarva-sakttnam brāhmaṇah samanantarah, murtam brahma maha-bhaga sarva-brahma-mayo harih (1.22.63), “Lord Hari is the superior among all energies and is situated beyond the impersonal Brahman. О virtuous one, He is the embodiment of Brahman and is the full-fledged Brahman. ” Among all (sarva) the energies (śaktinam) of the Supreme (brāhmaṇah), among Brahma, Viṣṇu, and Siva, Lord Viṣṇu is the Supreme (parah) because there is no difference (samanantarah) between Him and Brahman, and because He is the embodiment (mūrtam) of Brahman, a multitude of condensed knowledge and bliss. Brahma and Siva are not so. He is the full-fledged Brahman, i.e., He is the svarūpa of the complete Brahman, a direct expansion of the Supreme (svamsa). As for Brahma and Siva, both are separated expansions of the Supreme (vibhinnamia). This is the idea here.

### Text 39

**Перевод:**

**In the Subala Upaniṣad it is also said that the universe is born from the Supreme Person, Who is endowed with subtle energies expressed by the word ‘tamah, ‘and He Himself becomes manifest within it in His form as a puruṣavatara named Sahasrasirsa (thousand-headed), from Whom the creation starting with Brahma and Rudra is manifest: tad ahuh kim tad asit tasmai sa hovaca na sann asan na sad asad iti, tasmat tamah sanjayate tamaso bhutadir bhutader akasam akasad vayur vayor agnir agner apo ‘dbhyah prthivl tad andam samabhavat, tat samvatsaram usitva dvidhakarot, adhastad bhumir uparistad akasam madhye puruso divyah sahasra-sirsa puruṣah sarasraksah sahasra-pat, sahasra-bahur iti so ‘gre bhutanam mrtyum asrjat try-aksam tri-siraskam tri-padam khandaparasum tasya brahmabibhet, so ‘yam brāhmaṇam avivesa sa manasan saha putran asrjat te ha virajah sapta manasan asrjat, te ha prajapatayo brahmano’sya mukham asid bahfl rājanyah krtah, йгй tad asya yad vaisyah padbhyam sūdro ajayata, candrama manaso jatas caksoh suryo ajayata, srotrad vayus ca pranas ca hrdayat sarvam idam jayate, apanan nisada yaksa-raksasa-gandharvas casthibhyah parvata lomabhya ausadhi-vanaspatayo lalatat krodhajo rudro ‘jayata, tasyaitasya mahato bhutasya nisvasitam etad yad rg-vedah, “The disciples asked the master, ‘What is that which existed before creation? ‘The master replies to them, ‘That is neither gross (sat) nor subtle (asat). It is not gross and subtle either. From that was born material nature, tamah, from which was produced bhūtadi. Ether was produced from bhūtadi, air was produced from ether, fire was produced from air, water was produced from fire, earth was produced from water, and then the universal egg was produced. After dwelling in it for one year, the Supreme made it twofold — earth below, and sky above. The transcendental Supreme Person appeared in the middle as the purusdvatara with thousands of heads, thousands of eyes, thousands of feet, and thousands of arms. He first created death for the living beings. Brahma became afraid of death, that form of Rudra with three eyes, three heads, and three legs. Rudra then entered within Brahma. From his mind, Brahma simultaneously created sons, the four Kumaras, who became very effulgent. Brahma then produced seven sons from his mind. They are known as the prajapatis. The brāhmaṇas became the mouth of Lord Viṣṇu’s universal form; the ksatriyas, His arms; the vaisyas, His thighs; and the sudras were born from His feet. The moon was born from His mind. The sun was born from His eyes. The wind and life air were born from His ears. All that exists is born from His heart. The Nisadas, Yaksas, Raksasas, and Gardharvas were born from His anus; the mountains were born from His bones; the herbs and trees were born from His hairs; and Rudra was born from wrath out of Brahma’s forehead. The Vedas are the exhalation of this same great being.”**

**Комментарий:**

Because the presiding deity of the mode of goodness, Lord Viṣṇu, is the Para-brahman, He must have only appearance and disappearance, and not birth and death like Brahma and Rudra. Therefore, among the śruti statements it is not said that He is born with Brahma and Rudra. In order to give an illustration of the śruti expressing this idea, the author quotes the Subala Upaniṣad In this passage, the disciples are inquiring from the master. The subject of enquiry is stated in the sentence ‘kith tad asit, ‘i.e., ‘what is that imperishable essence (tat) that existed before creation V Вещ thus asked, the guru replies to the assembled disciples, ‘that essence which existed before creation is not sat, ‘i.e., it does not have a gross form made of earth, water, and fire’. It is also not asat, ‘i.e., it also does not have a subtle form made of pradhana, mahat, and so on’. Neither is it sat and asat, ‘i.e., it also does not have a form that is both gross and subtle, such as the sky and the air. Then what was that ? It should be understood that the said essence is the Supreme Brahman, Who is different from both gross and subtle and is endowed with energies such as tamah. Feeling compassion for the entangled souls who have the desire to enjoy in a material body and were absorbed within Himself, the Supreme Brahman glanced upon tamah, and then tamah (material energy) was produced from Him. Then, tamah became ready to manifest the entangled souls within pradhana. From tamah emanated the unembodied entangled souls, which are called ‘aksara’. From them emanated the avyakta with the manifested three modes of nature. From that was produced the threefold mahat, from which was produced the threefold ahahkara. The mind and the controlling deities of the senses emanated from the ahahkara in the mode of goodness. The ten senses emanated from the ahahkara in the mode of passion. From the ahankara in the mode of ignorance, called ‘bhutadi, ‘emanated the sense objects, from which were produced the five great elements starting with ether. It should be understood that this is explained according to the description of the universal devastation found in the same śruti later. This is described in the same way in the Govinda-bhāṣya with Sūkṣmā-tikā (2.3.12), which should be consulted. First (agre), the thousand-headed Lord Viṣṇu created death (mrtyum), Rudra (khandaparasu), the destroyer of the living beings (bhūtanam). Therefore, Rudra is Lord Viṣṇu’s son. The word ‘tasya ‘means ‘of Rudra’. That (sah) Rudra entered Brahma and was again born from him. Being commanded by Lord Viṣṇu, Brahma simultaneously (saha) created four sons from his mind — Sanat, Sanaka, Sanātana, and Sanandana — who became particularly effulgent. Brahma then created seven other sons from his mind, starting with Marici. It is similarly stated in the Viṣṇu Purāṇa (1.21.27-28): vaivasvate ca mahati varune vitate kratau, juhvanasya brahmano vai praja-sarga ihocyate, pūrvam yatra tu saptarsin utpannan sapta manasan, “During the great age of Vaivasvata Manu, when the sacrifice arranged by Varuna was performed, Brahma was the sacrificer. His creation of progeny will be now described. In the previous age, He produced seven sages from his mind.” These seven became prajapatis. The word ‘asya ‘in the sentence ‘brahmano’sya ‘refers to Lord Viṣṇu is the form of Hiranyagarbha. The same Rudra who had entered Brahma was later born from his forehead (lalatat).

### Text 40

**Перевод:**

**In the Satapatha Brahmana (6.1.3) it is heard: bhutanam patih samvatsare usasi reto’sincat, tat samvatsare kumaro ‘jayata so ‘rodit, tarn prajapatir abravit, kumara kim rodisi yac chramat tapaso ‘dhi jato’siti, so ‘bravid anapahata-papma va aham asmi nama me dhehi papmano ‘pahatya iti, tam prajapatir abravid rudro’siti, tasya tan-namakarod agnis tad-rupam abhavat, agnir vai rudro yad arodit tasmad rudrah, so ‘bravij jyayan va aham asmi dehy evam nameti, tam prajapatir abravit, bhavo’siti sarvo’slti isano’siti pasupatir aslti ugro’siti bhimo’siti mahadevo’slti, prajapatir devan asrjat, “After one year, the lord of creatures, Brahma, had intercourse with Usa. In course of another year, a boy was born and cried. Prajapati Brahma asked, ‘O boy, why are you crying, since you have been born after exertion and austerity? ‘The boy replies, ‘I am not free from sin. Please give me a name so that my sins may be removed’. Prajapati Brahma told him, ‘You are Rudra’. The boy said, ‘I am the eldest son. Give me more names’. Prajapati Brahma told him, ‘You are Bhava, you are Sarva, you are Isana, you are Pasupati, you are Ugra, you are Bhima, you are Mahadeva’. Prajapati Brahma then created the demigods.” te papmana samvita ajayanta virupaksaya brāhmaṇah putraya jyesthaya sresthayamoghaya karmadhipataye, “The demigods were born surrounded by sin, and in order to propitiate Brahma’s eldest son, named Virupaksa, the very praiseworthy giver of success and propounder of Vedic rituals, they became his followers.” so ‘bravid varam vrnisva, aham eva pasunam adhipatir asaniti tasmad rudrah pasunam adhipatir iti, “Brahma said, ‘Choose a boon’. Rudra replied, ‘May I become the master of the living beings’. Therefore, Rudra is the master of the living beings (Pasupati).”**

**Комментарий:**

It should be understood that sentences quoted here are found in the Satapatha Brahmana of the Kṛṣṇa Yajur Veda, in the third Brahmana of the sixth chapter, skipping some sentences here and there. Rudra had been born from Lord Viṣṇu, the Lord of all, and after entering within Brahma’s body, he remained there. Then, Brahma, the lord of creatures, discharged semen in the form of Rudra within the womb of Usa. Rudra said, “On account of not having received the name giving ceremony, I am not free from sin (anapahata-papma). Therefore, give me (dhehi) a name.” Desiring more names, he said, “I am your eldest son (jyayan), therefore give me (dehi) names so that my seniority may be well-known». This is the meaning. Thus, after having given birth to Rudra and named him, Brahma created other sons, “Prajapati Brahma then created the demigods». The demigods (te) were born (ajayanta) without names and were thus surrounded (samvitah) by sin (papmana). They were then given names, became free from sins, and became followers of Rudra. Thus, it is said, ‘virūpaksaya, etc’. The dative Case is used here according to Panini’s aphorism (2.3.14): kriyarthopapadasya ca karmani sthaninah, “The fourth case (dative) is used to express the sense of the object of a suppressed verb, which is connected with another word denoting action. ” The meaning is ‘in order to propitiate Rudra’. The word ‘jyesthaya ‘means ‘to the eldest; ‘‘sresthaya ‘means ‘to the very praiseworthy; ‘‘amoghaya ‘means ‘to the successful and giver of success; ‘and ‘karmadhipataye ‘means ‘to the propounder of Vedic rituals’. Brahma told Rudra, “Choose (vrntsva) a boon (varam). ” Rudra then chose a boon, “May I (aham) become the master (adhipatih) of the living entities (pasunam). ”

### Text 41

**Перевод:**

**In the above quoted śruti we find the reasons for the Supreme Lord to be the Supreme. Therein it is understood that Brahma and Rudra are born from Him, that Brahma was afraid of Rudra, and that Rudra was not free from sin. Such things are not heard of in connection with Lord Viṣṇu. Therefore, the Supreme Lord Viṣṇu’s manifestation among the demigods according to His own will for the purpose of universal affairs is called His ‘birth, ‘and it is similar to a king who happens to be among a group of thieves. His birth is indeed transcendental, and by knowing this, people can attain the cessation of birth, as stated by Lord Śri Kṛṣṇain the Bhagavad-gtta (4-9)" janma karma ca me divyam evam yo vetti tattvatah, tyaktva deham punar janma naiti mam eti so ‘rjuna, “One who factually knows My transcendental appearance and activities in this way does not, upon leaving the body, take birth again in this material world but attains Me, О Arjuna.” Therefore, the Viṣṇu avatara among the three divinities is directly the Supreme Lord of all. Thus, it has been proved that He is distinguished among the demigods.**

**Комментарий:**

**Thus ends the third chapter of the introduction to the Govinda-bhāṣya, named Siddhānta-ratnaṁ, whose topic is the ascertainment of Lord Viṣṇu’s supremacy.**

Having thus quoted the śruti, the author here gives its meaning. “In the above quoted śruti, ” i.e., in the Subala Upaniṣad and the Satapatha Brahmana. Brahma and Rudra are born from Him. It is not stated that Lord Viṣṇu was born, for He is Lord Viṣṇu by Himself. This is the idea. Brahma’s and Rudra’s birth, fear, and lack of a name, which causes them to attain sin, are all signs that they are jivas. Lord Viṣṇu has no such signs, for having on eternal body, being the Lord of all, and having names which are He Himself and destroy ignorance, those signs cannot possibly exist in Him even in dreams, as stated in the Viṣṇu Purāṇa: avikaraya suddhaya nityaya paramatmane, sadaika-rupa-mpaya visnave sarva-jisnave (1.2.1), "Obeisances unto the immutable and pure Lord Viṣṇu, the conqueror of all, Who is the eternal and all-pervading Supreme Soul and always manifests the very same form». pape gurūni guruni svalpe svalpani tad-vidah, prayascittani maitreya jaguh svayambhuvadayah, prayascittany asesani tapaḥ-karmatmakani vai, yani tesam asesanam kṛṣṇanusmaranam param, krte pape ‘nutapo vai yasya pumsah prajayate, prayascittam tad evasya hari-samsmaranam param (2.6.38-40), “O Maitreya, Svayambhuva Manu and others who know dharma have described minor atonements for minor sins and severe atonements for serious sins. Remembrance of Lord Kṛṣṇais superior to all kinds of atonements that consist of penances and ritualistic activities. Whenever one feels repentance for a sin committed, the best atonement is remembrance of Lord Hari Someone may argue, “If it is so, then where is the coherence in the statement of the Atharva-sikha Upaniṣad that Brahma, Viṣṇu, Rudra, and Indra are all born Г In response, the author says, “Therefore, the manifestation, etc. ” The meaning is that if desiring to know the affairs in the city, the king is found disguised as a thief among a group of thieves, he cannot be called a thief but must be certainly called their ruler. The meaning of the rest is clear.

Thus, the third chapter, entitled ‘Sudarśana, ‘has been explained in this commentary on the introduction to the Govinda-bhāṣya.

## Fourth Chapter — Tārkṣya

### Text 1

**Перевод:**

**In the context of what has been previously discussed, the monists argue as follows: “Two kinds of sentences are heard from the scriptures — those whose subject is saguṇa-brahma, and those whose subject is nirguṇa-brahma. Examples of sentences about saguṇa-brahma are: yah sarvajnah sarvavid yasya jnāna-mayam tapaḥ (Muṇḍaka Upaniṣad, 1.1.9), ‘The Supreme Lord is omniscient, has all opulences, and His reflection consists of knowledge’. ya atmapahata-papma vijaro vimrtyuh (Chāndogya Upaniṣad, 8.7.1), ‘The Supreme Soul is free from sins, free from old age, and free from death’. Examples of sentences about nirguṇa-brahma are: eko devah sarva-bhutesu gudhah sarva-vyapi sarva-bhutantar-atma karmadhyaksah sarva-bhutadhivasah saksi cetah kevalo nirguṇas ca, ‘The Supreme hidden within all elements is one without a second, the effulgent Supreme Soul of all living beings, all-pervading, the witness, the controller of all activities, the inner dweller in all beings, and is pure consciousness without any material quality’. satyam jnānam anantam brahma (Taittiriya 2.1), ‘The Supreme is truth, consciousness, and eternity’.**

**Комментарий:**

In the third chapter, the author refuted the Saiva doctrine and the doctrine that God and the demigods are one and thus corroborated that Para-brahman, the Supreme Lord of all, has a body constituted of consciousness and bliss, and has transcendental qualities and pastimes, which had been established in the first and second chapters. Unable to tolerate this, the monists, who support a philosophy similar to Buddhism and Sāṁkhya, raise an objection here. The author calls this chapter Tārkṣya (Garuda) because it swallows such snake-like objections. The word’sarvavit ‘means ‘one who possesses all opulences eternally/ and ‘tapaḥ ‘means ‘reflection’. The word ‘ekah ‘means ‘one without the possibility of a second’. The word ‘devah ‘means that the Supreme is an effulgent single element, consciousness. The Supreme is hidden (gūdhah) within all material elements starting with ether. According to the Halayudha-kośa (859), the word ‘bhUta ‘is used in the sense of four kinds of living entities, jive material elements starting with earth, the past tense, and celestial beings. ”The Supreme pervades all elements (sarva-vyapi) inside and outside. He is the Supreme Soul, the inner controller of the four kinds of living entities (sarva-bhutantar-atma), who are atomic consciousness, and by dint of maya, are separated parts of the Supreme, just like the sparks of fire. The Supreme Soul is the controller of all activities performed by the living entities (karmadhyaksah) and gives them the respective fruits. He is called ‘adhivasah ‘because He is situated within all beings. Because He is the direct witness of all their pious and impious activities, He is called ’saksi’. Because He imparts knowledge, He is called ‘cetah’. Essentially, He is pure (kevalah) consciousness because He has no material qualities. This is the meaning of the objection that has been raised. The Vaiṣṇava conclusion should be understood as follows: The word ‘ekah ‘means ‘the chief of all’. According to the Amara-kośa (3.3.16), the word ‘eka ‘means ‘chief, ‘‘other, ‘and ‘only’. The word ‘devah ‘means ‘worshipped by all, ‘and’sarva-bhUtesu gUdhah ‘means’situated within the hearts of all living entities’. The words’sarva-vyapi ‘refute the idea of separation. The words’sarva-bhūtantar-atma ‘refute the idea that the Supreme is indifferent like ether. The words ‘karmadhyaksah ‘express His mercy, i.e., He is the giver of the fruits of their work. He is the ultimate shelter of all living entities (sarva-bhūtadhivasah). Yet He is unaffected, hence called ’saksi, ‘He is merely situated and impartially observing. The word ‘cetah ‘means that He is of the nature of consciousness because He is not even slightly tainted by māyā (nirguṇah).

### Text 2

**Перевод:**

**“The purpose of the sentences about saguṇa-brahma is not to express qualities in Brahman, because they are merely a common description of qualities in general, and also because it is logical that those sentences have only one meaning so as not to contradict the sentences about nirguṇa-brahma. Therefore, only nirguṇa-brahma is indicated by all the Vedas. The meaning of those two kinds of sentences is not that Viṣṇu alone is the Supreme Lord of all.”**

**Комментарий:**

If someone argues that in this case, Brahman must be twofold, then the monist here replies that it is not so: “The words ‘guna-vidhane ‘mean ‘with the intention of expressing qualities’. The Vedas declare that qualities such as being free from sin, which are found in demigods, in great sages, in kings, and in very meritorious people, are also found in nirguṇa-brahma, so that this subject may enter one’s heart — that is all, for such qualities vanish. The sentences about saguṇa-brahma are not contradictory to the sentences about nirguṇa-brahma.” “How is there only one meaning?" If someone asks this question, the monist replies that only one nirguṇa-brahma, which is mere consciousness, is to be understood by all the Vedic sentences that express quality. The word ‘tesam ‘means ‘of those two kinds of sentences’.

### Text 3

**Перевод:**

**This idea is foolish, for a description of qualities is not possible without knowing them from another evidence. A description is an enumeration of something that is already known from another evidence. Those qualities are known neither through direct perception nor through inference but only from the Upaniṣads. Those qualities are also transcendental, for it is heard from the scriptures that they are inherent in the Supreme Lord: parasya śaktir vividhaiva sruyate svabhavikl jnāna-bala-kriya ca (Śvetāśvatara Upaniṣad, 6.8), “It is heard from the scriptures that the Supreme has multifarious intrinsic superior energies in the form of knowledge, power, and action.” Their eternity is also stated in the Śrimad-Bhāgavatam (1.16.29): ime canye ca bhagavan nitya yatra maha-gunah, prarthya mahattvam icchadbhir na viyanti sma karhicit, “O revered Dharma, these great qualities which are found in Lord Kṛṣṇaare eternal and never vanish. They are desirable to those who aspire for an exalted position.” Therefore, in the Chāndogya Upaniṣad (8.1.1) it is said that transcendental qualities should be sought after by those who desire liberation: tasmin yad antas tad anvestavyam, “Within the heart there is a small lotus-like palace, within which there is a small (dahara) interspace. Within it is that which should be sought after and inquired upon.”**

**Комментарий:**

Here the author refutes the aforementioned view. The words ‘tat-praptasya ‘mean ‘of what is known from another evidence’. If someone argues that by the argument that those qualities are found in demigods, great sages, and so on, it is proper to say that they are known through evidences like direct perception or inference, then the author says, “Those qualities are known, etc. ” If someone argues that the Upaniṣads declare that those qualities are imaginary, the author says, “They are transcendental». The verse ‘ime canye ‘is from the Srimad-Bhāgavatam (1.16.29) and was spoken by the earth personified to dharma personified. The word ‘bhagavan ‘means ‘O Dharma’. Those eternal great qualities (maha-gunah) which have been spoken, such as truthfulness, as well as those which have not been spoken, exist within (yatra) Lord Kṛṣṇa and never (na karhicit) vanish (vyanti), for they are eternal (nityah). The eight qualities that exist in the Supreme (dahara) as His inner nature, as described in the Chāndogya Upaniṣad, should be sought after. The idea is that if they were imaginary, they would not be sought after by those who aspire for liberation.

### Text 4

**Перевод:**

**There are some who speak as follows: “In the Bṛhad-āraṇyaka Upaniṣad (5-8.1) it is said: vacam dhenum upasita, ‘One should worship the cow-like words of the VedasSimilarly, sentences about saguṇa- brahma are meant for meditation. When the mental state has become purified by the practice of meditation, the subtle and attributeless Brahman will penetrate within one’s heart. Otherwise, since there is an injunction for meditation, qualities of meditation, and meaning of meditation, there would a fault called ‘vakya-bheda.  This statement is ill thought out, for in those sentences no word such as meditation is seen. If the meaning of those sentences is imagined to be so, although the word meditation is not there, then the same meaning would also be applied to sentences like ‘atmety evopaslta ‘(Bṛhad-āraṇyaka Upaniṣad, 1.4.7), “One should meditate only on the Supreme Spirit.”  Moreover, in his commentary on the Brahma-sūtra, Śaṇkaracarya has stated that attributes like bliss and the non-difference between God and the individual soul are also real, despite being the object of meditation: anandadayah pradhanasya (3-3.12), “The Supreme has transcendental qualities such as bliss.” vyatiharo visimsanti hitaravat (3-3-38), “There are interchanges of meditations because the scriptures distinguish between God and the jiva, as in other cases.” Similarly, why would also qualities like omniscience not be real?**

**Комментарий:**

Here the author refutes other arguments. “Just as the word ‘cow ‘is applied to speech, which is not a cow, similarly for the purpose of meditation it should be imagined that the attributeless Brahman has attributes. In this way the concept of the impersonal will enter one’s heart, just as one points out the moon through the branches of a tree.” “Otherwise”, i.e., “if such sentences were not meant for meditation». The word ‘tesu ‘means ‘in those sentences like yah sarvajnah sarvavit, etc’. If sentences that do not have the word meditation are taken as meant for meditation, then sentences that describe Brahman’s attributes such as being spirit would also be imaginary, being simply meant for meditation, and thus Brahman would not be spirit. This is the idea. You also cannot say that whatever is meditated is imagined, for in this way you would contradict your own statement. To express this, the author says, “Moreover, etc. ” The two quoted aphorisms are from the Brahma-sūtra. The meaning of the first one (3.3.12) is that Brahman (pradhanasya) has attributes like bliss and form that are to be meditated upon. Śaṇkaracarya commented that the meaning of the other aphorism (3.3.38) is that the non-difference between God and the jiva is proved by the mutual interchange between God as jiva, and jiva as God, as distinguished in sentences like: tvam va aham asmi bhagavo devate aham vai tvam (Varāha Upaniṣad, 2.34), “O revered God, I am You, and You are me. ” tat tvam asi (Chāndogya Upaniṣad, 6.8-16), “You are that.” “Just as in the monistic philosophy it is accepted that attributes like bliss and the nondifference between God and the individual soul are real, despite being the object of meditation, similarly attributes like being omniscient should also be accepted, so why are you hesitant?” This is the idea.

### Text 5

**Перевод:**

**Nor can it be said that, “Since both kinds of sentences are observed, there is the option that sometimes the topic is saguṇa-brahma and sometimes nirguṇa-brahma,” for the nature of Brahman being already established, there is no option. It certainly cannot be said that fire is sometimes hot and sometimes cold. Option exists in a performance yet to be accomplished, just as in the Atiratra sacrifice the sodasi vessel may or may not be utilized, or just as a sacrifice may be performed either before or after sunrise.**

**Комментарий:**

Here the author refutes the idea that Brahman has two forms. The word ’siddhe ‘means that Brahman’s supreme nature is already established.

The word ‘anusthane ‘means ‘in a performance," such as a sacrificial offering or fire sacrifice, and ’sadhye ‘means ‘which is to be accomplished’. The word ’sah ‘refers to the option. The author then gives two examples. The sodasi is a kind of sacrificial vessel. Atriratra is a kind of sacrificial ceremony. It is heard from the scriptures that the Atiratra sacrifice is twofold, with or without the employment of the sodasi: atiratre sodasinam grhnati, “In the Atiratra sacrifice one may utilize the sodasi». natiratre sodasinam grhnati, “In the Atiratra sacrifice one may not utilize the sodasi».  It is also prescribed in the scriptures that fire sacrifices are twofold according to the time they are performed, either before or after sunrise. When the sacrifice is forbidden before sunrise, it is performed after sunrise, and when the sacrifice is forbidden after sunrise, it is performed before sunrise.

### Text 6

**Перевод:**

**Some monists state that the sentences about saguṇa-brahma describe qualities pertaining to the relative world (vyāvahārika), while those about nirguṇa-brahma express the transcendental reality (pāramārthika) devoid of qualities. This is very foolish, because in those sentences there is no word that indicates an empirical state of existence; because if they expressed qualities that are false, they would be unauthoritative; and because by such reasoning, the use of the soda& would pertain to the empirical state and its non-use would pertain to the transcendental reality, which would contradict the principles enunciated in Purva-mimamsa. Besides that, this would imply the falsity of the existence described in the scriptures, such as’sad eva saumyedam agra asit ‘(Chāndogya Upaniṣad, 6.2.1), “Dear Svetaketu, in the beginning there was only existence (sat).” Consequentially, Brahman would be void. That idea is also opposed by the Taittiriya Upaniṣad (2.6), wherein those who propound non-existence are condemned and those who propound existence are praised: asann eva sa bhavaty asad brahmeti veda cet, asti brahmeti ced veda santam enam tato viduh, “One who thinks that Brahman is non-existent is wrong. Therefore, the wise know that if one thinks that Brahman exists, such a person is virtuous.”**

**Комментарий:**

Here the author addresses another theory, “Some monists, etc. ” The meaning is clear. “Since in your view all qualities are imaginary, they must be false. Therefore, the statements of the Vedas that express qualities are unauthoritative, just as sentences like ‘this is the son of a barren woman, ‘hence your philosophy is non-Vedic (nastika)». This topic will be dealt with in detail in the sixth chapter. "By applying the concept of different levels to the example of the sodasi, its use would be relative and its non-use would be transcendental which would contradict the principle enunciated in the Purva-mimamsa». The idea is that such a view is not accepted by Jaimini. In the sentence’sruta-sattvasya, etc.’ the śruti states that Brahman possesses existence as an attribute. The meaning of the rest is clear. These two flaws are difficult to be removed by one who propounds that qualities are false.

### Text 7

**Перевод:**

**Therefore, the sentences about saguṇa-brahma express transcendental qualities that are not known through other evidences, while the sentences about nirguṇa-brahma deny material qualities. This is the principle. In the Śrimad-Bhāgavatam (11.13-40) Lord Kṛṣṇa says: mam bhajanti gunah sarve nirguṇam nirapeksakam, suhrdam priyam atmanam samyasangadayo ‘gunah, “All qualities devoid of the modes of nature, such as equality and detachment, follow Me, the Supreme Soul, the well-wisher and dear of all, Who am free from all material qualities and desires.” In the Viṣṇu Purāṇa (1.9.44) it is said: sattvadayo na santlse yatra ca prākṛtā gunah, sa suddhah sarva-suddhebhyah puman adyah prasldatu, “The Supreme Original Person is the purest of the pure, and the modes of nature — goodness, passion, and ignorance — do not exist in Him. May He be pleased.” Also in the Viṣṇu Purāṇa (6.5.83) it is stated: sa sarva-bhuta-prakṛtir vikara-gunams ca dosams ca mune vyatltah, “O sage Maitreya, Lord Vāsudeva, the original cause of all elements, is beyond changeable qualities and faults.” By thus denying changeable qualities and then declaring that Lord Vāsudeva has unlimited auspicious qualities, the sage Parasara has accepted that the Lord has auspicious qualities that are intrinsic to His svarupa. In this way, the abovementioned principle is clear from the words of Parasara.**

**Комментарий:**

Here the author substantiates the stated meaning. The word ‘vidadhyuh ‘means ‘they express as the meaning’. The verse ‘mam bhajanti ‘is from the Śrimad-Bhāgavatam (l 1.13-40). The word ‘agunah ‘means that the transformations of the three modes of nature are not possible in those qualities, which are actually intrinsic to the Lord‘s svarūpa. The verse ’sattvadayah na santise ‘is from the Viṣṇu Purāṇa (1.9.44). By the statement that those qualities are not material, it is understood that they are transcendental. Those qualities should be known as purity and so forth. The word ‘asau ‘refers to the mentioned principle, which the author clarifies by quoting the words of Parasara Muni.

### Text 8

**Перевод:**

**Attributes like being a witness are also mentioned in sentences about nirguṇa-brahma. Otherwise, words such as ’saksi’ would not apply to the Supreme without a reason, and the first chapter of the Brahma-sūtra, entitled Samanvaya, would also be contradicted. Vyāsadeva, the revered compiler of the Brahma-sūtra, therein accepted qualities as semantic causes: antas tad-dharmopadesat (1.1.20), “The person within the sun is the Supreme Lord, for the mentioned qualities belong to Him only.” antaryamy-adhidaivadisu tad-dharma-vyapadesat (1.2.18), “The Supreme Soul is the inner controller among all demigods and material elements, for His attributes are mentioned in the scriptures.” adrsyatvadi-gunako dharmokteh (1.2.21), “Because of the mention of His attributes, the Supreme Lord is the one Who possesses the qualities of invisibility and so on.” sarva-dharmopapattes ca (2.1.37), “And because the Supreme Lord possesses all transcendental attributes.”**

**Комментарий:**

Someone may argue, “Since words that express absence of described in the Chāndogya Upaniṣad (l.6.6-7), ya eso ‘ntar aditye hiran-mayah puruṣah (...), “That person within the sun is perceived as having a golden form, etc. ” Why? Because of His attributes such as having a golden form, having lotus eyes, being free from all sins, and so on. The meaning of the aphorism ‘antaryamy-adhidaivadisu tad-dharma-vyapadesat (1.2.18) ‘is as follows: It should be understood that Lord Hari is the inner Soul described in sentences that include words like ‘adhidaiva ‘(presiding deity), such as the Bṛhad-āraṇyaka Upaniṣad (3.7.3), yah prthivyam tisthan prthivya antaro yam prthivt na veda (...), “He Who is situated on the earth and within the earth, but Whom the earth does not know, etc». Why ? Because He has attributes such as being immortal (amrtah), is the inner Soul (antaryami), and is the controller of all these changeable elements starting with earth. The meaning of the aphorism ‘adrsyatvadi-gunako dharmokteh (1.2.21) ‘is as follows: It should be understood that Lord Hari has attributes such as invisibility, as expressed in sentences of the Muṇḍaka Upaniṣad — atha parayaya tad aksaram adhigamyate, yat tad adresyam agrahyam (1.1.5), “This is the superior knowledge by which the imperishable, invisible, and imperceptible Supreme is comprehended.” divyo hy ammtah puruṣah (2.1.2), “The Supreme Person is transcendental and has no material form». Why ? Because His attributes are described: yah sarvajnah (Muṇḍaka Upaniṣad, 1.1.9), “The Supreme Lord is omniscient, etc. ”

### Text 9

**Перевод:**

**It has been previously stated that something that cannot be expressed by words cannot have a secondary meaning. If words such as ‘cin-matra ‘(mere consciousness) are rather interpreted as having a secondary meaning, then what is being indicated must be insentient. As for bhdga-tyaga-lakṣaṇā, that is not possible here due to the absence of contrary portions. Thus, this interpretation is insignificant. In this way, another foolish statement has also been driven far away, according to which the word ‘nirguṇa ‘was mentioned after the attributes such as being a witness in order to deny the existence of qualities. It has been previously stated that it is not possible that the śruti is simply giving a common description of qualities such as being a witness, which are known from the very śruti. Therefore, Lord Hari, Who is an ocean of unlimited transcendental qualities, is expressed by all the Vedas.**

**Комментарий:**

Someone may argue, "By giving up qualities like being a witness, mere consciousness should be taken as a secondary meaning». The author replies to this in the present paragraph. Just as in the sentence ’so‘yam devadattah ‘(This is that same Devadatta) it is seen that the secondary meaning is expressed by the words ‘pinda-matra ‘(the mere physical body), similarly the secondary meaning of words such as ’saksi’ (witness) are expressed by the words ‘cin-matra ‘(mere consciousness). “You have accepted that the pure Brahman is definitely not expressed by any word, so how is it that now you resort to a secondary meaning to define such Brahman ?” This is the idea. Someone may argue, “If you say that like the words ‘pinda-matra, ‘the words ‘cin-matra ‘have primary, secondary, and implied meanings, then I shall also take the words ‘cin-matra ‘in the secondary sense». The author demonstrates the undesirable consequences of this argument, “If words such as, etc.” Next, he says that a secondary meaning is not possible in connection with mere consciousness. The śruti repeats the description of qualities mentioned in the very śruti. A description is an enumeration of something that has been first spoken by someone and is then repeated by someone else. Therefore, Lord Hari is endowed with transcendental qualities and is expressed by the Vedas. This is the meaning here.

### Text 10

**Перевод:**

**The concept that the Supreme is mere consciousness devoid of attributes is certainly false, for the śruti and the smṛti state the following: na tat-samas cabhyadhikas ca drsyate (Śvetāśvatara Upaniṣad, 6.8), “No one is equal or superior to the Supreme Lord.” mattah parataram nanyat (Bhagavad-gita, 7.7), “There is nothing superior than Me.” The Supreme Lord is actually full of qualities and is the object of love, and not devoid of qualities, for such a state is worthless. Liberation is also attained through knowledge of His transcendental qualities, as declared by the śruti and the smṛti: yada pasyah pasyate rukma-varnam kartaram isarū puruṣam brahma-yonim, tada vidvan punya-pape vidhuya niranjanah parāmaṁ samyam upaiti (Muṇḍaka Upaniṣad, 3.1.3), “When one sees the Supreme Person of golden complexion, the Lord and creator, the source of the brahma-jyoti, then shaking off pious and sinful reactions, this wise seer whose heart is pure attains transcendental similarity with the Supreme Lord.” bhoktaram yajna-tapasam sarva-bhuta-mahesvaram, suhrdam sarva-bhutanam jnātvā mam santim rcchati (Bhagavad-gita, 5.29), “Knowing Me as the enjoyer of all sacrifices and austerities, the Supreme Lord of all beings, and the well-wisher of all living entities, one attains peace.”**

**Комментарий:**

If someone argues that on account of the inconceivable power of words, without semantic causes and without secondary meaning, words such as’saksi ‘reveal mere consciousness, then the author says, “The concept that, etc». This statement is false (alikam), for the above quoted sentences from the scriptures deny that there is anything that is mere consciousness, different from and superior to the Supreme Lord endowed with transcendental qualities. “Although the nirguṇa concept is demonstrated among your peers by this deceptive concocted philosophy, you will not attain the goal of life” — with this intent the author says, “The Supreme Lord is actually full of qualities, etc». “The Supreme Lord is the remover of distress, the giver of happiness, and the suitable object of love, and not something different from this. Therefore, your effort is useless. ” This is the idea. The author then shows that the Supreme Lord endowed with qualities (saguṇa) is actually the goal of life, “Liberation is also attained, etc». The text ‘yada pasyah ‘is from the Muṇḍaka Upaniṣad (3-1.3), and the verse ‘bhoktaram yajna-tapasam ‘is from the Bhagavad-gita (5.29).

### Text 11

**Перевод:**

**Someone may argue, “The scriptures say: yato vaco nivartante, aprapya manasa saha (Taittiriya Upaniṣad, 2.4-1), ‘Failing to attain the Supreme, words return accompanied by the mind’. yad vacanabhyuditam yena vag abhyudyate tad eva brahma tvam viddhi nedam yad idam upasate (Kena Upaniṣad, 1.4), ‘That which is not expressed by words but by which speech is manifest, that you should know as Brahman, and not this speech that the world worships’. yac chrotrena na srnoti yena srotram idam srutam tad eva brahma tvam viddhi nedam yad idam upasate (Kena Upaniṣad, 1.7), ‘That which is not heard by the ear but by which the ear hears, that you should know as Brahman, and not this ear that the world worships’. yato ‘prapya nivartante vacas ca manasa saha, aham canye ime devas tasmai bhagavate namah (Śrimad-Bhāgavatam, 3.6.40), ‘Failing to attain the Supreme, words return accompanied by the mind, as do the false-ego and all the controlling deities of the senses. Obeisances unto this Supreme Lord’. If the Vedas speak only about Viṣṇu, then what is the meaning of these sentences of the śruti and the smṛti ?” The actual meaning of these sentences is that the Supreme Lord cannot be fully expressed by words, as will be explained later. Words such as ’sarvajnah ‘(omniscient) apply to the Supreme Lord on grounds such as His omniscience, while words such as ‘nirguṇa ‘(devoid of attributes) apply to Him on grounds such as His being devoid of material attributes. Thus, all the Vedas have the Supreme Lord for their highest object. In this way, there is no contradiction in the statements of the scriptures, such as: sarve veda yat padam amananti (Kaṭha Upaniṣad, 1.2.15), “That spiritual nature which all the Vedas describe.”**

**Комментарий:**

Here someone raises an objection against the proposition that Lord Viṣṇu is expressed by all the Vedas. The text ‘yato vacah ‘is from the Taittiriya Upaniṣad (2.4.1). Speech (vacah) and the mind (manasa) return (nivartante) from the Supreme (yatah) without having reached (aprapya). The text ‘yad vacabhyuditam ‘is from the Kena Upaniṣad (1.4). The Supreme (yad) is not expressed (anabhyuditam) by words (vaca). The word ‘abhyudyate ‘means ‘is manifest’. The words ‘nedam ‘mean ‘these mind and senses are not the Supreme’. The words ‘na srnoti ‘mean ‘is not heard’. The verse ‘yato ‘prapya ‘is from the Śrimad-Bhāgavatam (3.6.40) and was spoken by Maitreya. “What is the meaning, ” i.e., “since in these sentences it is denied that the Supreme is expressed by words, they must have a secondary meaning, hence the idea that all the Vedas speak about Viṣṇu is flawed». The meaning of the rest is clear.

### Text 12

**Перевод:**

**In this regard, Vyāsadeva, the revered compiler of the Brahma-sūtra, has stated that the pure and complete Lord Viṣṇu is expressed by the Vedas: iksater naśabdam (1.1.5), “According to what is seen in the Upaniṣads, the Supreme is not inexpressible by words.” It was also commented therein (in the Govinda-bhāṣya) that the Supreme is not inexpressible but is actually expressible by words, since it is seen that the Supreme is described in the Vedas. As for the monists ‘explanation that the word ‘aśabdam ‘(inexpressible) refers to pradhana, that is not correct, for pradhana is also expressed by words, such as: ajam ekam lohita-sukla-kṛṣṇam bahvih prajah srjamanam sarupah (Śvetāśvatara Upaniṣad, 4.5), “Enjoying her, one unborn male follows an unborn female whose colour is red, white, and black, and produces many varieties of offspring of similar forms.”**

**Комментарий:**

Here the author states Vyāsadeva’s view on the above explained meaning. He then refutes another interpretation, “As for the monists ‘explanation, etc. ” The subject of the verb ‘vyacaste ‘(explains) is the monist. The word ‘tasya ‘refers to pradhana, which is expressed by the word ‘aja ‘(unborn) in the quoted mantra.

### Text 13

**Перевод:**

**Moreover, the scriptural section about knowledge (jnāna-kānda) directly speaks about the Supreme: tarn tv aupanisadam puruṣam prcchami (Bṛhad-āraṇyaka Upaniṣad, 3.9-26), “I am asking you about the Supreme Person taught in the Upaniṣads.” On the other hand, the scriptural section about rituals (karma-kanda) speaks about the Supreme indirectly, for it propounds ritualistic ceremonies, which are a limb of knowledge. As it is stated in the Bṛhad-āraṇyaka Upaniṣad (4.4.22): tam etam vedanuvacanena brāhmaṇa vividisanti yajnena danena tapāsanasakena, “The brāhmaṇas desire to know the Supreme Soul by means of studying the Vedas, performing sacrifices, giving charity, performing austerities, and fasting.” In the Bhagavad-gita (18.46) it is also declared: yatah pravrttir bhutanam yena sarvam idam tatam, sva-karmana tam abhyarcya siddhim vindati manavah, “The Supreme Lord is He from Whom all living beings appear and by Whom the whole universe is pervaded. Having worshipped Him by the performance of one’s own work, one attains perfection.”**

**Комментарий:**

Someone may argue, “It is possible to say that Viṣṇu is the subject described in the jnāna-kānda but not in the karma-kanda, wherein only Vedic rituals together with their limbs and mode of performance are propounded.” The author answers to this here. The text ‘tam tv aupanisadam ‘is from the Bṛhad-āraṇyaka Upaniṣad (3.9.26). What is taught in the Upaniṣads is called ‘aupanisada’. The affix ‘a ‘is characteristic in derivative nouns. The text ‘tarn etam ‘is also from the Bṛhad-āraṇyaka Upaniṣad (4.4.22). The word ‘tam ‘refers to the Supreme Soul. In this text, it is clear that activities like studying the Vedas and so on are limbs of the desire to know the Supreme. The verse ‘yatah pravrttih ‘is from the Bhagavad-gita (18.46). The ‘perfection ‘(siddhim) is knowledge about oneself and the Supreme Soul.

### Text 14

**Перевод:**

**Someone may argue, “It is well-known that the Vedas prescribe rituals that produce fruits like rain, sons, and the attainment of heaven.” It is not as you think, for such prescriptions are meant to arouse interest in this way. By seeing results such as rain from sacrifices like the Karin, one develops faith in the Vedas, and having interest in them, one properly deliberates on their meaning. Such a person thus knows the pitiful state of one who does not know the Supreme, as said in the Bṛhad-āraṇyaka Upaniṣad (3.8.10): yo va etad aksaram aviditva gargy asmal lokat praiti sa krpanah, “O Gargi, one who departs from this world without knowing the imperishable Supreme is a wretched.” He also understands that activities like studying the Vedas are limbs of knowledge, as stated in the Bṛhad-āraṇyaka Upaniṣad (4.4.22): tam etam vedanuvacanena brāhmaṇa vividisanti yajnena danena tapāsanasakena, “The brāhmaṇas desire to know the Supreme Soul by means of studying the Vedas, performing sacrifices, giving charity, performing austerities, and fasting.” Thus, he performs those Vedic rituals without selfish desires, and the Vedas then give him understanding about the Supreme. In this way, it is proved that all the Vedas have the Supreme Lord for their topic.**

**Комментарий:**

Here another objection is raised. “The Vedas prescribe rituals to yield fruits like rain by injunctions such as: kartrya yajeta vrsti-kamah, ‘One who desires rain should perform the Kariri sacrifice’. Yet the Vedas do not prescribe knowledge whose result is Viṣṇu. The jnāna-kānda is also only devoted to rituals, since it explains sacrifices and their purpose, performers, and deities. ” The author refutes this idea, "It is not as you think, etc». “In this way,” i.e., by results such as rain. The word ‘prarocana ‘means ‘interest’. The author then explains how interest is aroused, “By seeing results, etc. ” The text ‘yo va etat ‘is from the Bṛhad-āraṇyaka Upaniṣad (3.8.10). The word ‘aksaram ‘refers to the Supreme. The word ‘praiti ‘means ‘goes to the other world, ‘‘dies’. The word ‘tani ‘refers to the Vedic rituals, and ‘anutisthatah ‘means ‘of one performing’. The word ‘asau ‘refers to the Vedas.

### Text 15

**Перевод:**

**As it is stated in the Śrimad-Bhāgavatam (11.3.44,46): paroksa-vado vedo ‘yam balanam anusāsanam, karma-moksaya karmani vidhatte hy agadarh yatha, vedoktam eva kurvano nihsango ‘rpitam isvare, naiskarmyam labhate siddhim rocanartha phala-śrutih, “The Vedas make statements in a concealed manner and prescribe rituals for liberation from karma, just as a father persuades the children to take a medicine. Performing only the activities prescribed in the Vedas without attachment and offering them to God, one attains perfection in the form of freedom from karma. The scriptural descriptions of various fruits are meant to arouse interest in the Vedas.” Fruits such as the attainment of heaven are obtained only if desired, but not if they are not desired. In such case, one rather attains the purification of the mind, which is meant for advancement in spiritual knowledge.**

**Комментарий:**

Here the author gives evidence to the above mentioned principle.

The verses starting with ‘paroksa-vado vedah ‘are from the Srimad-Bhāgavatam (l 1.3.44,46). The Vedas prescribe ritualistic ceremonies for the purpose of liberation, and not for the sake of rain, sons, and the like, for they speak in a concealed manner (paroksa-vadah), just as if persuading (anusāsanam) children (balanam). The example given is that of a medicine. Just as a father administering a bitter medicine first allures the children with candy, gives the medicine, and then gives the candy. The fruit of taking the medicine is not to get the candy but to become free from disease. Similarly, alluring people with fruits like rain, the Vedas prescribe ritualistic activities with the intention to lead them to liberation from karma. Therefore, “performing only the activities prescribed in the Vedas (vedoktam eva kurvanah), ” but not activities forbidden by them. Someone may object, “The fruit for one who performs those rituals must be only fondness for rituals, and not freedom from karma». In this case, it is said, “without attachment (nihsangah)." The performance must be an offering (arpitam) to God (isvare), and not for the sake of the fruits. Someone may argue, “Since they are mentioned, there must be fruits." In this case, it is said, “they are meant to arouse interest in the Vedas (rocanartha), "just like showing a candy when a child is taking medicine.

### Text 16

**Перевод:**

**By this same argument the following idea is also refuted, as imagined by some followers of the new Mimāṁsā school: amnayasya kriyarthatvad anarthakyam atad-arthanam tasmad anityam ucyate (Mimāṁsā-sūtra, 1.2.1), ‘“Since the purpose of the Vedas is the performance of action, any statements for other purposes are useless. Therefore, the Vedas are said to be non-eternal’. tad-bhutanam kriyarthena samamnayo ‘rthasya tan-nimittatvat (ib., 1.1.25), ‘The enunciation of the words of a sentence is done together with a word expressing action, since it is based on the meaning of words that sentences have meaning’. As the statements in all the Vedas propound the performance of rituals as their topic, they do not have Viṣṇu as their topic. The karma-kanda of the Vedas directly describes ritualistic ceremonies, while the jnāna-kānda describes them through teachings about the performers and deities, which are limbs of the rituals.**

**Комментарий:**

“By this same argument, ” i.e., by the argument that all the Vedas describe Lord Viṣṇu as their topic. The word ‘idam ‘means ‘the idea that will be spoken now, according to ‘which all the Vedas propound rituals as their topic’. The word ‘navinah ‘refers to Prabhakara Miśra and other followers of new Mimāṁsā, and ‘vidhi-kande ‘means ‘in the Mimamsa-sutra’. The word ‘kriyam ‘means ritualistic ceremonies like fire sacrifices. “In the jnāna-kānda, the descriptions of the jiva are descriptions of the sacrificer who performs the rituals, and those of Viṣṇu are descriptions of the worshipable Deity of the rituals, thus both are described as limbs of the rituals. ” This is the meaning.

### Text 17

**Перевод:**

**“On the basis of the difference of topics, some say that the jnāna-kānda is not devoted to ritualistic ceremonies but to the Supreme, as the fruits of those are condemned in the Chāndogya Upaniṣad (8.1.6): tad yatheha karma-jito lokah ksiyata evam evamutra punya-jito lokah ksiyate, ‘Just as the world attained by work in this lifetime perishes, similarly the world attained by pious deeds in the next life perishes’. This is not true, for such sentences deal with the facts of life and are thus purposeless. It is seen that the only purposeful sentences are those that teach about activities to be performed or avoided, such as: svarga-kamo yajeta (Kṛṣṇa Yajur Veda, Maitrayani-samhita, 3-8.4), ‘One who desires heaven should perform sacrifices’. tasmad brāhmaṇah suram na pibet (ib., 2.4.2), ‘Therefore, a brāhmaṇa should not drink liquor’. Sentences about the facts of life serve no purpose, such as: sapta-dvipa vasundhara, ‘The earth has seven continents’. Therefore, all the Vedas are devoted to action.”**

**Комментарий:**

Here an argument that attacks the reasoning of the above conclusion is raised, “On the basis, etc.” The words ‘prakarana-bhedat ‘mean ‘on account of the different topics discussed in the karma-kanda and the jnāna-kānda’. Someone may argue against the Navya-mtmamsa, “If you say that because the jnāna-kānda describes the performer and the deities of the sacrifices as limbs of the rituals, therefore the jnāna-kānda must be in conformity with the karma-kanda, it is not so, for in the jnāna-kānda the fruits of rituals are condemned." The words ‘karma-jitah ‘mean ‘the middle and lower planets, ‘and ‘punya-jitah ‘means ‘the heavenly and higher planets’. In response, the Navya-mimamsa follower says that the jnāna-kānda is useless, since it describes the individual soul and Gody whose existences are already established from beginningless time. He then gives two examples to show that a scripture that deals with action is fruitful, while a scripture that deals with something that is already established is fruitless. The meaning is clear.

### Text 18

**Перевод:**

**This argument is a display of folly, ridiculous like covering the sun with the hand, for the scriptures state as follows: sarve veda yat padam amananti (Kaṭha Upaniṣad, 1.2.15)У “That spiritual nature which all the Vedas describe.” Moreover, nowhere is to be seen such a statement by Jaimini. As the view that the whole karma-kanda is devoted to action is supported by the aphorism ‘amnayasya kriyarthatvat, etc., ‘the aphorism ‘tad-bhutanam, etc’. corroborates the congruity of other sentences included in the karma-kanday such as: so ‘rodit (Satapatha Brahmana, 6.1.3)у “He cried.”**

**Комментарий:**

Here the author rebuts the above objection. By the word ‘adi ‘(etc.), other texts are also to be included, such as: vedais ca sarvair aham eva vedyah (Bhagavad-gita, 15.15), “I am to be known through all the Vedas». The meaning of the sentence ‘idrsa-jaiminy-ukty-adarsanat ‘is that no statement by Jaimini is found which may corroborate the idea expressed in the objection that has been raised. Someone may argue, “It is seen that Jaimini stated the aphorism ‘tad-bhutanam, etc’. (Mimamsa-sūtra, 1.1.25). ” The author replies, “As the view, etc. ”

### Text 19

**Перевод:**

**Moreover, the aphorism ‘amnayasya kriyarthatvat, etc’. is an objection (pūrva-paksa). Its meaning is as follows: Starting from the aphorism ‘codana-laksano dharmah ‘(Mimāṁsā-sūtra, 1.1.2), “Dharma is characterized by scriptural injunctions,” in the middle it is pointed out ‘autpattikas tu śabdasyarthena sambandhas tasya jnānam upadesah (ib., 1.1.5), ‘“There is an eternal connection between a word and its meaning, and knowledge of this is called upadesa,” and it is concluded ‘tad-bhutanam kriyarthena, etc’. (ib., 1.1.25), “The enunciation of the words, etc.” From this it is understood that the purpose of the Vedas is the performance of rituals. Therefore, since the purpose of the Vedas (amnayasya) is the performance of sacrifices and so on (kriyarthatvat), sentences that do not possibly amount to an injunction (atad-arthanam) because they lack a purpose dependent of men’s execution are purposeless (anarthakyam). They do not convey any meaning that gives accurate knowledge about dharma. Examples of such sentences are: so ‘rodid yad arodit tad rudrasya rudratvam (Kṛṣṇa Yajur Veda, Taittiriya-samhita, 1.5.1), “He cried, and because he cried, he became known as Rudra.” prajapatir atmano vapam ud akhidat (ib., 2.1.4), “Then Prajapati extracted his own guts.” deva vai deva-yajanam adhyavasaya diso na prājanan (ib., 6.1.5), “Having settled a place for the sacrifice, the demigods did not know the directions.” Therefore, sentences of this sort are called non-eternal. In other words, they are as if they were non-eternal, and not actually non-eternal, for the Vedas are not human compilations (apauruseya), and that idea would contradict the view that they are eternal, which is commonly accepted. Because this sort of sentence expresses crying, extraction of guts, unawareness of the direction, and so forth, its purpose is not the performance of ritualistic ceremonies.**

**Комментарий:**

It has been previously said that in the opinion of Jaimini, the karma-kanda is devoted to ritualistic ceremonies, but not the jnāna-kānda. Here, the author demonstrates this, “Moreover, etc.” It should be understood that the aphorisms quoted here are from Jaimini’s Mimamsa-sūtra. "The sentences whose purpose is not this (atad-arthanam), ” i.e., that are not meant for ritualistic ceremonies. This is the connection between the words. According to Jaimini, ‘autpattikas tu śabdasyarthena sambandhas tasya jnānam upadesah (ib., 1.1.5), ‘“There is a natural (eternal) connection between a word and its meaning. ” Thus, he accepted the eternity of the Vedas, of their meaning, and of the connection between the Vedas and their meaning, and therefore the raised objection contradicts what he accepts.

### Text 20

**Перевод:**

**The aphorism 4ad-bhutanam kriyarthena, etc.’ gives the Mimāṁsā conclusion. Its meaning is as follows: The enunciation (samamnayah) of words such as ’so‘rodit ‘(he cried), which exists (bhutanam) among words that have a particular meaning such as crying (rodana)y is done with words that express action, such as ‘yajeta ‘(one should perform a sacrifice). This is understood as the connection. Why? Because the meaning of a sentence is understood on account (tan-nimittatvat) of the meaning of its words (arthasya). Mere words just convey their respective meanings. Subsequently, the word meanings convey the meaning of a sentence with the assistance of akanksa (expectancy), yogyata (suitableness), and sannidhi (proximity). Therefore, on the strength of these three elements, sentences like ’so‘rodit’ should be somehow thought as being in congruity with the injunctions. The word meanings of these sentences have been accordingly inserted in ritualistic performances, therefore the same congruity should be effected by inserting other sentences. Nor has Jaimini rejected the authoritativeness of sentences in expressing their own meaning, because that would contradict his acceptance of the eternal connection between a word and its meaning, as he states in the aphorism ‘autpattikas tu śabdasyarthena sambandhah, ‘and also because all kinds of evidences are limited to what is apprehended within their own scope.**

**Комментарий:**

Now the author explains the aphorism ‘tad-bhutanam, etc’. The meaning is mostly clear. The line ‘autpattikas tu śabdasyarthena sambandhah ‘is part of another of Jaimini’s aphorisms (1.1.5). The meaning is that there is a natural (autpattika) connection (sambandhah) between a word of the Vedas and its meaning. The various kinds of pramānas starting with direct perception are limited to what is apprehended by them, such as pots and so on, otherwise their status as a particular kind of evidence would be lost. That which certainly produces valid knowledge is called pramāna (evidence).

### Text 21

**Перевод:**

**Nor is it possible to say that, “There is congruity between the jnāna-kānda and the karma-kanda,” because their topics are different; because what is actually heard would be given up and what it is not heard would be imagined; and also because the jnāna-kānda rather describes ritualistic ceremonies and their fruits as insignificant.**

**Комментарий:**

Now the author refutes the argument that the jnāna-kānda is in conformity with the karma-kanda because it gives descriptions about the performer and the deities of the sacrifices, which are limbs of the rituals. In this way, what is heard from the jnāna-kānda about the soul would be given up, and what is not heard there about ritualistic ceremonies would be imagined. The Muṇḍaka Upaniṣad (1.2.7) says the following about rituals: plava hy ete adṛḍhā yajna-rūpah, “Sacrifices are infirm boats to cross the ocean of material existence». The fruits of Vedic rituals in this life and also in heaven are condemned in the Chāndogya Upaniṣad (8.1.6): tadyatheha karma-jito lokah ksiyata evam evamutra punya-jito lokah ksiyate, “Just as the world attained by work in this lifetime perishes, similarly the world attained by pious deeds in the next life perishes». Thus, how can the jnāna-kānda be in accordance with the karma-kanda ? This is the meaning.

### Text 22

**Перевод:**

**Nor is the jnāna-kānda, which deals with the Supreme Spirit and is devoid of topics on activities to be performed or avoided, purposeless like sentences such as ’sapta-dvipa vasundhara, ‘“The earth has seven continents,” for by teaching about the existence of the soul and the Supreme Soul, they do have a purpose, like a sentence that informs about the existence of a treasure. Just as from sentences spoken by a reliable person, such as, ‘There is a treasure in your house, ‘one obtains joy as a result, similarly by knowing that the Supreme exists, Whose form is supreme bliss and of Whom I am a part and parcel, the result is supreme bliss, hence that is not purposeless. Therefore, the activities described in the jnāna-kānda are meant for this purpose. It is seen that in sentences with a particular intention, such as, ‘Your son was born, ‘or ‘This is not a snake, ‘the purpose is respectively to cause joy and to remove fear. Therefore, the statement about purposelessness should be understood in the case of topics about the facts of life, such as narrations that are not conducive for attaining the goal of life. Otherwise, prohibitions would also be purposeless, since they are not meant for the performance of action. Abstention is taught in the scriptures, such as ’suram na pibet, ‘‘One should not drink liquor’. This is not an action but merely indifference by abstaining from an action one is attached to.**

**Комментарий:**

It was previously stated by the opponent that scriptural sentences that deal with activities to be performed or avoided, as well as ritualistic ceremonies, are fruitful while those that deal with something that is already established are fruitless. Here, the author refutes this idea. The words ‘bhutatma-vastu-visayasya’ mean ‘that describe the individual soul and the Supreme, who are already established’. The word ‘anarthakyam ‘means fruitless’. The word ‘tasya’ refers to the jnāna-kānda, and ‘arthavattvat’ means (since they are fruitful’. The words ‘tat-sattvavagamat ‘mean from knowledge about the existence of the Supreme, ‘‘sah’ means ‘the result in the form of supreme bliss, ‘and ‘tad-artha pravrtti’ means ‘activities meant for attaining spiritual knowledge, such as hearing, reflecting, and meditating’. Here, the author then gives two illustrations of ordinary life, “Your son was born, etc». Someone may argue, “The aphorism ‘amnayasya kriyarthatvat ‘does speak about purposelessness, so how is that to be interpreted ?” In response, the author says, “Therefore, the statement, etc.” “Otherwise, ” i.e., “if it is accepted that all sentences other than those meant for ritualistic ceremonies are purposeless. ” The word ‘pratisedhakanam‘ refers to prohibitive sentences, such as’suram na pibet,’ “One should not drink liquor.

### Text 23

**Перевод:**

**Moreover, Jaimini accepted that Brahman has two forms — one form is the Vedic rituals to be performed, and the other is a form of knowledge and bliss to be attained by means of those rituals. As stated in the śruti: yajno vai visnuh, “Lord Viṣṇu is sacrifice personified (yajna)” brahmavid apnoti param, tad esabhyukta, satyam jnānam anantam brahma (Taittiriya Upaniṣad, 2.1), “One who knows Brahman attains the Supreme, about which the following Vedic hymn is uttered: Brahman is truth, consciousness, and eternity.” anando brahmeti vyajanat (ib., 3.6), “Bhrgu then realised that the Supreme is bliss personified.” Without first performing Vedic sacrifices, it is not possible to attain the Supreme — such is Jaimini’s conviction in the performance of sacrifices. It is a fact that Jaimini has faith in the Supreme, since he is a disciple of the venerable sage Badarayana, and also because Jaimini’s view is mentioned in the Badarayana’s Brahma-sūtra. Therefore, the conclusion is that the Supreme is expressed by the Vedas.**

**Комментарий:**

Here the author explains that Jaimini is a devotee of Lord Hari. It should be understood that the Supreme’s form as Vedic rituals is expressed by words, just as His form as the Vedas is expressed by words. The words ‘tad esa ‘mean ‘this Vedic hymn about the Supreme’. Without performing rituals such as sacrifices, it is not possible to attain the Supreme in the form of knowledge and bliss. The words ‘tatraiva ‘mean ‘in the performance of sacrifices and so on, ‘and ‘tad-bharah ‘means ‘Jaimini’s conviction’. The word ‘tasya ‘means ‘of Jaimini’. The view of Jaimini is mentioned by Vyāsadeva in his Brahma-sūtra.

### Text 24

**Перевод:**

**Someone may argue, “It is known from the śruti that the Supreme is both expressed and not expressed by words, such as: sarve veda yat padam amananti (Kaṭha Upaniṣad, 1.2.15), ‘That spiritual nature which all the Vedas describe’. yad vacanabhyuditam yena vag abhyudyate tad eva brahma (Kena Upaniṣad, 1.4), ‘That which is not expressed by words but by which speech is manifest, that you should know as Brahman’. God under the limiting adjunct (upadhi) of māyā is expressed by the Vedas, but in His pure aspect without attributes, He is only indirectly indicated by them. Nor is there any semantic cause such as name and class in His pure aspect, since it is said in the smṛti that He has no name or form.” It is not so, for it is stated in the smṛti that because He is pure, God is expressed only by the Vedas. In the Viṣṇu Purāṇa (1.9.44) it is said: sattvadayo na santlse yatra ca prākṛtā gunah, sa suddhah sarva-suddhebhyah puman adyah prasidatu, “The Supreme Original Person is the purest of the pure, and the modes of nature — goodness, passion, and ignorance — do not exist in Him. May He be pleased.” As for the idea that He is devoid of attributes, that is not correct, for it has been previously stated that according to the smṛti, He has transcendental qualities intrinsic to His svarūpa. Afterwards it will be clarified that God has no imagined names such as ‘dittha ‘ but has divine names and forms intrinsic to His svarūpa.**

**Комментарий:**

As those atheists devoted to Vedic rituals, such as Prabhakara Miśra, have been thus refuted, a shameless propounder that the Supreme is a single consciousness devoid of attributes here raises an objection once again, although he had been previously refuted. The word ‘пата ‘means ‘name ‘or ‘noun’. The pure Supreme Lord expressed by the Vedas is not devoid of attributes. “Previously” in this treatise. The word ‘tat ‘refers to name and form.

### Text 25

**Перевод:**

**Therefore, the same should be understood in the Upaniṣads: The Supreme Lord Himself becomes manifest in the form of the Vedas together with His names, which are intrinsic to His svarūpa and are the very Vedas. As it is stated in the śruti: namo vedadi-rupaya omkaraya namo namah (Rama-tāpani Upaniṣad, 4.13), “Obeisances unto Lord Rama, Who is the syllable Om, the Vedas, and other scriptures.” tasya va etasya mahato bhutasya nisvasitam etad yad rg-vedah (Bṛhad-āraṇyaka Upaniṣad, 2.4.10), “The Vedas are the exhalation of the Supreme Being.” In the smṛti it is also said: vedo narayanah saksat svayambhūr iti susruma (Śrimad-Bhāgavatam, 6.1.40), “We heard that the Vedas are directly the Self-manifest Lord Nārāyaṇa.” Hence the Supreme Soul’s name ‘aupanisada ‘(taught in the Upaniṣads).**

**Комментарий:**

Here the author starts to explain that the Supreme pure is expressed by the Vedas. “You must also admit the same in the Upaniṣads. ” If someone asksy “How?” the author says, “The Supreme Lord Himself etc.” “He becomes manifest” (gocari-bhavati), i.e., He is expressed by His names, which consist of the Vedas. The text cnamo vedadi ‘is from the Rama-tapani Upaniṣad (4.13). The text ‘tasya va‘ is from the Bṛhad-āraṇyaka Upaniṣad (2.4.Ю). Here, the Vedas are said to be the life air of the Supreme Lord. The verse ‘vedo narayanah ‘is from the Śrimad-Bhāgavatam (6.1.40). “Therefore, ” i.e., “since the Lord is expressed by the Vedas, which are His own Self.” The word ’samakhya ‘means that the word (aupanisaday is a derivative form of the word ‘upanisad.9

### Text 26

**Перевод:**

**The eternity of the Vedas is also declared by Vyāsadeva, the revered compiler of the Brahma-sūtra: ata eva ca nityatvam (l.3.29)\> “Therefore, the Vedas are eternal.” Nor can it be said that, “The statement that the Vedas — which consist of mere words — are eternal and a form of the Supreme Lord is just praise.” The reason is: since the Vedas are non-different from Him, their eternity and form as knowledge is possible, as understood on the strength of the śruti and the smṛti, according to which this is a manifestation of the Lord in the form of words, just like other manifestations.**

**Комментарий:**

Someone may argue, “As the Vedas are a collection of sounds, they must be subject to destruction in the third moment, hence they are noneternal.” The author answers to this here. The aphorism ‘ata eva ca nityatvam ‘is from the Brahma-sūtra (1.3.29). The Vedas are eternal, because they express eternal forms like that of Indra, and also because nobody recollects their having an author. The idea is that the Vedas appear and disappear, and the ignorant call this as production and destruction. The word ‘tasya ‘means ‘of the Vedas, ‘and ’sa ‘means ‘their being eternal and a form of the Lord’. The word ’stutih ‘means ‘praise’. Just as Lord Hari, Whose form is that of a divinity, appears informs of different species such as swan and fish, similarly the Vedas are His manifestation in the form of words. Being one with the Supreme Lord, the Vedas, despite being a collection of syllables, can also be eternal and have a spiritual form (cid-rupa), as it is understood on the strength of the scriptures, such as ‘namo vedadi-rupaya om-karaya namo namah ‘(Rama-tāpani Upaniṣad, 4.13).

### Text 27

**Перевод:**

**Therefore, it is seen that the Lord‘s names, which consist of the Vedas, are described as having a spiritual form, as giving liberation and as being fully beyond the range of the material senses, just like His svarūpa. In the Rg Veda (l .156.3) it is said: tam u stotarah purvyam yatha vida rtasya garbham janusa pipartana, asya jananto nama cid vivaktana mahas te visno sumatim bhajamahe, “O Lord Viṣṇu, we, who know things as they are, praise You, the beginningless Lord situated within the spiritual sky, which is like Your private residence. Knowing only a glimpse of Your self-luminous name, we propitiate You by our deeds in this birth.
