Merely by uttering it we attain knowledge about You.” Om tat sat, “You are Om, the Supreme eternal existence.” In the smṛti it is also stated: sakrd uccaritam yena harir ity aksara-dvayam, baddhah parikaras tena moksaya gamanam prati, “By uttering the two syllables “Ha-ri” even once, an entangled soul who serves the Lord becomes eligible for attaining liberation.” nama-karma-svarūpani na pariccheda-gocare, yasyakhila-pramānanam sa visnur garbhagas tava (Viṣṇu Purāṇa, 5.2.19)y “He Whose names, activities, and nature cannot be perceived or ascertained by any kind of evidence, that Lord Viṣṇu is within your womb.”**

**Комментарий:**

Here the author attempts to express the real attributes of the Vedas, such as having a spiritual form and so on. “Therefore, ” i.e., “as the Vedas are a form of the Supreme Lord. ” The words ‘tad-bhūtanam ‘mean ‘which consist of the Vedas’. The word ‘u ‘is an interjection used with the vocative case, ‘O Viṣṇu, ‘‘yatha vidah ‘means ‘we, knowers of things as they are, ‘‘pūrvyam ‘means ‘beginningless, ‘‘rtasya ‘means ‘of the Lord‘s abode, the spiritual sky, ‘and ‘garbham ‘means ‘like a private residence’. “We praise (stotarah) You (tam), Who are situated within it, and propitiate (pipartana) You by our deeds in this birth (janusa). We know (janantah) only a glimpse (a) of Your (te) name (nama), and by merely (vivaktana) uttering it, we obtain (bhajamahe) knowledge about You (sumatim).” What is His name like? It is of the nature of spiritual consciousness (cit), hence self-luminous (mahah). The text then indicates something about the name, “От tat sat.” It is similarly stated in the Bhagavad-gita (17.23): от tat sad iti nirdeso brāhmaṇas tri-vidhah smrtah, “The indications about the Supreme are said to be threefold — От tat sat. ” These are the three names of the Supreme Soul, as stated in the śruti: от ity etad brahmano nedistham nama, “The syllable ‘От ‘is the nearest name of the Supreme. ” tat tvam asi, (Chāndogya Upaniṣad, 6.8-16), “You are that (tat)». sad eva saumya (Chāndogya Upaniṣad, 6.2.1), “Dear Svetaketu, in the beginning there was only ’sat, ‘the Supreme Lord. ”Names like ‘Kṛṣṇa ‘are also implied here. The author gives an example of how the Lord‘s names give liberation, “sakrd uccaritam, etc. ” He then quotes a verse from the Viṣṇu Purāṇa (5.2.19) to illustrate how the Lord‘s names are completely beyond the range of the senses, “nama-karma-svarūpani, etc’. ‘Here, it is said that just as the Lord‘s svarūpa is completely beyond the range of any kind of evidence, so are His names and activities.

### Text 28

**Перевод:**

**This being so, the statement that the Supreme Lord has no name, as mentioned in some places, should be reconciled according to the following smrti: aprasiddhes tad-guṇānāṁ anamasau prakirtitah, “Because His qualities are not known, the Supreme Lord is said to have no name.” anama so ‘prasiddhatvad arupo bhūta-varjanat, “On account of His not being known, the Supreme Lord is said to have no name, and because He is free from the material elements, He is said to have no form.” His designation as unknown is due to His being distinct from anything material and hence not perceived, or else because He cannot be completely known.**

**Комментарий:**

Someone may argue, “In scriptures such as the Padma Purāṇa it is said: anama-mpa evayam bhagavan harir isvarah, akarteti ca yo vedaih smṛtibhis cabhidhiyate, ‘In the Vedas and the smṛti, the Supreme Lord, Hari, is said to have no name, no form, and no action’. How can such sentences be coherent?” The author responds here. "This being so,” i.e., “having concluded that the Lord‘s name and form are intrinsic to His svarūpa.” The verse ‘aprasiddhes tad-guṇānāṁ ‘is from the Vāsudevadhyatma. After this line, the following three lines should also be known: aprākṛtātvad rūpasyapy arūpo’sav udiryate, sambandhena pradhanasya harer nasty eva kartrta, akartaram atah prahuh puranam tam pura-vidah, “He is said to have no form because His form is not material. Because He has no connection with material nature, Lord Hari is said to perform no action. Therefore, those who know the ancient lore call Him the primeval non-doer.” The verse ‘anama sah ‘is from the Brahma Purāṇa. “His being distinct from anything material and hence not perceived, ” i.e., because He is perceived as similar to demigods, humans, and so on. In the Srimad-Bhāgavatam (10.2.36) it is stated: na nama-rupe guna-karma-janmabhir nirūpitavye tava tasya saksinah, mano-vacobhyam anumeya-vartmano deva kriyayam pratiyanty athapi hi. This verse should be interpreted in the following way: “O Lord (deva), the name and form (пата-гйре) of Yours (tava), Who is the witness (saksinah), along with Your qualities, activities, and birth cannot be really experienced (nimpitavye) even by the wise. Nevertheless (athapi), when there is pure devotional service (kriyayam), devotees factually (hi) experience (pratiyanti) them." Their experience is known by inference, therefore it is said, ‘mano-vacobhyam’. The compound ‘anumeya-vartmanah ‘means ‘of You, Lord, Whose path of love (vartma) is inferred (anumeya) by signs like a detached mind (manah) and a speech engaged in glorifying You’. If the Lord were absolutely devoid of name and form, the statements that describe His name and form would be contradictory. In the Śrimad-Bhāgavatam (8.3.8) it is said: na vidyateyasya ca janma karma va па пата-гйре guna-dosa eva va, tathapi lokapyaya-sambhavaya yah sva-mayaya tany anukalam rcchati. No doubt is left if we explain this verse in the following way: “The Supreme Lord is devoid of material birth (janma), activity (karma), name (nama), form (гйра), qualities (guna), and defects (dosa). Nevertheless, assuming the form of Brahma and Rudra, at the appropriate time He accepts those attributes by means of His own potency (sva-mayaya) in order to perform the creation (sambhavaya) and destruction (apyaya) of the world (loka). ” It should be understood that here it is not said that assuming the form of Viṣṇu He accepts birth and so on in order to maintain the world, for His birth and other attributes are free from the illusory energy.

### Text 29

**Перевод:**

**Similarly, the apparent meaning of Akrura’s words is also refuted: na yatra natha vidyante nama-jaty-adi-kalpanah, tad brahma parāmaṁ nityam avikari bhavan aja, na kalpanam rte ‘rthasya sarvasyadhigamo yatah, atah kṛṣṇacyutananta-visnu-namabhir idyase (Viṣṇu Purāṇa, 5-18.53-54), “O Lord, You are that Supreme eternal and immutable Brahman, in which concepts like name and class do not exist, O unborn one. Since without a concept there is no comprehension of any object, You are therefore worshipped with names like Kṛṣṇa, Acyuta, Ananta, and Viṣṇu.”**

**Комментарий:**

Just as the imagined meaning of sentences like ‘anama-rupa evayam ‘is refuted in view of verses from the smṛti, such as ‘aprasiddhes tad-guṇānāṁ,’similarly, by seeing its inapplicability, the imagined meaning of the above two verses from the Viṣṇu Purāṇa are also to be refuted.

### Text 30

**Перевод:**

**The word ‘concept ‘(kalpana) is meaningless in that verse, for the words ‘name, ‘‘class, ‘and so on suffice to convey the intended meaning. Akrura himself says in the previous verse: tad brahma parāmaṁ nityam, “You are that Supreme eternal Brahman.” Moreover, also in another verse, Akrūra accepted words such as ‘brahma, ‘‘aja, ‘and ’sat ‘as transcendental names: rūpaih param sad iti vacakam aksaram yat (Viṣṇu Purāṇa, 5.18.57), “The word ’sat ‘expresses Your transcendental form, which is imperishable.” A class characterized for being unborn is well-known from the scriptures, such as: ajam ekam lohita-sukla-kṛṣṇam bahvih prajah srjamanam sarupah (Śvetāśvatara Upaniṣad, 4.5), “Enjoying her, one unborn male follows an unborn female whose colour is red, white, and black, and produces many varieties of offspring of similar forms.” Thus, after denying the concept of name and so on, it is contradictory to again speak about the concept of names like Kṛṣṇa. Neither is the Lord glorified by a concept, nor can names like Kṛṣṇa, which are eternally fixed, be concepts, for concepts are inconstant.**

**Комментарий:**

Here the author explains that the primary meaning is not really applicable here. “The intended meaning, ” i.e., that the Lord has no name. The word ’svayam ‘(himself) refers to Akrura. Material nature, the living entity, and God are all unborn (aja). Their being unborn implies a class (jati), since this attribute is eternally inherent in manifold individuals. The word ‘tasya ‘means ‘of the Supreme Lord, Kṛṣṇa’. The words ‘idya-bhavah ‘mean ‘glorification, ‘and ‘tasyam ‘means ‘in a concept’. In a concept or imagination there is no constancy or certainty, for imagination is seen when men are given nicknames like ‘Devadatta ‘or when animals are given pet names like ‘Dittha ‘according to one’s will. The same cannot be said in the present topic, for the Lord is said to have thousands of names that are eternally fixed.

### Text 31

**Перевод:**

**Therefore, this is the meaning of Akrūra’s verse: In the Supreme there is no superimposition of names like Kṛṣṇa (nama) and species like demigods and humans (jati). By the word ‘adi ‘(etc.), activities are also included. However, those names, species, and activities are a manifestation of His svarūpa-śakti, as pointed out in the Śrimad-Bhāgavatam (10.37.22): visuddha-vijnāna-ghanam sva-samsthaya samapta-sarvartham amogha-vanchitam, sva-tejasa nitya-nirasta-maya-guna-pravaham bhagavantam imahi, “May we take shelter of the Supreme Lord, the embodiment of pure knowledge, Who fully possesses all opulences by His intrinsic energy, Whose will is never in vain, and by Whose supreme power the activity of the modes of the illusory energy is always dispelled.” “You (bhavan) are that (tat) Brahman.” This is the connection between the words of the verse. Because Kṛṣṇa is distinct from the material creation, Akrura says, “They do not exist (na vidyante).” Without attributing (kalpana) names and so on to visible things (sarvasyarthasya) such as men and animals, there would not be practical comprehension (adhigamah) of them. It is by such attributed names and so on that their comprehension is possible. “Therefore, You are distinct from them.” The meaning is that Lord Viṣṇu’s names are different from attributed names, are Viṣṇu Himself, and are non-different from Him, the all-pervading named. “You are glorified (idyase), You make Your glories manifest. By such names, Your greatness becomes revealed.” In the smṛti it is so stated that the Lord‘s names are of His same nature: nama cintamanih kṛṣṇas caitanya-rasa- vigrahah, pūrnah suddho nitya-mukto ‘bhinnatvan nama-naminoh, “The Holy Name is Kṛṣṇa Himself, a philosopher’s stone, a form of consciousness and rasa. It is complete, pure, and eternally free from the illusory energy, for there is no difference between the name and the named.” The names Kṛṣṇa, Acyuta, and Ananta are in the vocative case.**

**Комментарий:**

Having refuted the interpretation of the opponent, the author here gives the conclusive interpretation. “Therefore, ” i.e., “as the interpretation of the opponent has been refuted.” The word ‘kalpana ‘means’superimposition’. The verse ‘visuddha-vijfiana-ghanam ‘is from the Śrimad-Bhāgavatam (10.37.22) and was spoken by Nārada. “To Him by Whose supreme intrinsic (sva-samsthaya) energy He fully possesses (samapta) all (sarva) opulences (artham) in the form of names, qualities, and powers». Refuting the idea that His names and so on are illusory, it is said, “By His supreme power (sva-tejasa). ” “Of them ” (tasya), i.e., “of visible things such as humans and animals». The word ‘asau ‘refers to the comprehension. “Distinct from them ” means “different from any material creation». The word ‘tadrktvam ‘means that the Holy Name is Lord Viṣṇu Himself. The verse ‘nama cintamanih ‘is from the Padma Purāṇa. “O Kṛṣṇa, О Acyuta, О Ananta ” —these names are in the vocative case.

### Text 32

**Перевод:**

**Therefore, the pure and complete Lord Hari is expressed by the Vedas. The Vedas describe that supreme pure consciousness, the support of everything, by words such as ‘Kṛṣṇa, ‘and they describe sentient and insentient elements dependent on Him by words such as ‘jiva, ‘‘prakṛti, ‘and ‘kala’. Thus, it is proved that the Supreme Lord is known through all the Vedas. Words such as ‘anama ‘(nameless) are interpreted in a similar way in all places. One who claims that the meaning of such words is clear should be asked the following question: is knowledge about the Supreme acquired by such words or not? In the former case, those words are also names of the Supreme. In the latter case, the effort of the Vedas is useless. If the Supreme is not expressed by any word, a secondary meaning is not possible.**

**Комментарий:**

Here the author gives the sum and substance of this topic, “Therefore, etc. ” The meaning is clear. “In a similar way, ” i.e., the word ‘anama ‘(nameless) is interpreted in the sense that the Lord‘s qualities are not known, and ‘аruра ‘(formless) in the sense that His form is not material. “Such names” refers to names like anama. The ‘clear meaning ’suggested by the opponent is that the Supreme has no name and no form at all. The word ‘taih ‘means ‘by words such as anama’. Do they convey knowledge of the Supreme or not ? According to the first view (if they do), there would be knowledge about the Supreme, and according to the second view (if they do not), there would not be. “If you resort to a secondary meaning to explain words such as ‘anama, ‘then according to your philosophy that is also not possible». This has been previously explained.

### Text 33

**Перевод:**

**As for the following statements of the śruti, they simply deny that the Supreme Lord can be fully expressed, and not that He cannot be expressed at all: yato vaco nivartante, aprapya manasa saha (Taittiriya Upaniṣad, 2.4.l), “Failing to attain the Supreme, words return accompanied by the mind.” yad vacanabhyuditam yena vag abhyudyate tad eva brahma tvam viddhi (Kena Upaniṣad, 1.4), “That which is not expressed by words but by which speech is manifest, that you should know as Brahman.” Moreover, in that case, what is indicated by the ablative case in the word ‘yatah ‘(from where) would be irrelevant and other sentences in the śruti and the smṛti would be baseless, such as: sarve veda yat padam amananti (Kaṭha Upaniṣad, 1.2.15), “That spiritual nature which all the Vedas describe.” Therefore, at the end of those sentences it is also said that the Supreme is the object expressed by the words of the Vedas: ānandam brahmano vidvan (Taittiriya Upaniṣad, 2.4.1), “A wise person knows the blissfulness of the Supreme Lord.” tad eva brahma tvam viddhi (Kena Upaniṣad, 1.4), “That you should know as Brahman.”**

**Комментарий:**

By the śruti statements as quoted by fools in the sense that the Supreme has no name, it is not possible to get an understanding of the truth. This is what the author says here. In the sentence ‘The brāhmaṇa has returned from the river Gahga, ‘the idea is that he has seen a little of the Ganga. The words ‘tatra tatraiva ‘mean ‘at the end of those sentences’. The word ‘tasya ‘means ‘of the Supreme, ‘and ‘gocaratvam ‘means that His being an object of speech is stated.

### Text 34

**Перевод:**

**Similarly, in the Kena Upaniṣad (2.1-3) it is seen that the Supreme is both known and unknown: yadi manyase suvedeti daharam evapi nunam tvam vettha brahmano rupam, yad asya tvarh yad asya devesv atha mimarhsyam eva te, manye viditam, naham manye suvedeti no na vedeti veda ca, yo nas tad veda tad veda no na vedeti veda ca, yasyamatam matam tasya matam yasya na veda sah, avijnatam vijanatam vijnatam avijanatam, “If you think ‘I know Brahman well, ‘then what you know about Brahman’s form is indeed very small. That form of Brahman that you know is His form as the ruler among the demigods. Therefore, you should discuss about the form of Brahman.” Disciple: “I think that I know Brahman. I do not think that I know Brahman well, nor do I think that I do not know Brahman, but I know somehow. Among us, students, one who knows Brahman, knows in this way: ‘It is not that I know or do not know. I know somehow’. The Supreme is known to one who considers Him unknown. One who considers Him known does not know Him. He is unknown to those who have conceited knowledge, but He is known to those who are free from conceited knowledge.” Here, it is concluded that the Supreme is neither fully known nor fully unknown but is just known to some extent. The smṛti says the same thing: kartsnyena najo ‘py abhidhatum isa (Śrimad-Bhāgavatam, 12.4.39), “O Lord, even Brahma cannot fully describe Your pastimes.” aprasiddher avacyam tad vacyam sarvagamoktitah, atarkyam tarkyam ajneyam jneyam evam param smrtam, “On account of being unknown, the Supreme is said to be indescribable, inconceivable, and unknowable, yet through the statements of all the scriptures He is describable, conceivable, and knowable.” ‘Unknown ‘means indescribable, inconceivable, and unknowable in full extent.**

**Комментарий:**

Here the author corroborates the stated principle that the teachings about the Supreme Lord are given according to the capacity of the scriptures but not completely. "In a similar way,” i.e., "in the way as we have explained». Seeing that the disciple was feeling proud of his knowledge, the master said, “O disciple, if (yadi) you (tvam) think (manyase) ‘I (aham) know (suveda) Brahman well, ‘then your knowledge about Brahman is very small (daharam). ” Deriding that idea, the master says, "О disciple, that form (rupam) of Brahman (asya) that you know (vettha) is His form situated as the ruler among the demigods (devesu). ”In case the disciple says, “I do not know, ” then the master says, "You should discuss about that form with the other students. "Perceiving the master’s intention, the disciple says, “O master, I (aham) think (manye) that now I know Brahman (tat)». How ? “I do not think that I know (vedmi) Brahman well, nor do I think that I do not know Brahman, but I know somehow. ” Thus, having shown the way he knows the Supreme, he speaks about the others, “Among us (nah), students, one (yah) who knows Brahman, knows in this way. ” He then explains the way, “It is not that I know or do not know. I know somehow. ” This is the meaning. He again confirms this: “The Supreme is unknown to those who have conceited knowledge (vijanatam), but He is known to those who are free from conceited knowledge (avijanatam)». The author then clarifies the explained meaning by quoting the smṛti. The verse ‘kartsnyena najah ‘is from the Śrimad-Bhāgavatam (12.4.39). The words ‘ajo ‘pi ‘mean ‘even Lord Brahma’. The verse ‘aprasiddher avacyam ‘is from the Garuda Purāṇa. The word ‘aprasiddheh’should be applied to the words ‘avacyam, ‘‘atarkyam, ‘and ‘ajneyam’. The word ‘param ‘means Brahman, the Supreme.

### Text 35

**Перевод:**

**Someone may argue, “Why cannot the Supreme be fully described or known?” You should understand that it is due to His being unlimited, as stated in the Śrimad-Bhāgavatam (Ю.51.З6): janma-karmabhidhanani santi me ‘nga sahasrasah, na sakyante ‘nusarhkhyatum anantatvan mayapi hi, “Dear Mucukunda, I have thousands of births, activities, and names which cannot be counted even by Myself, since they are unlimited.” dyupataya eva te na yayur antam anantataya tvam api yad-antaranda-nicaya nanu savaranah, kha iva rajamsi vanti vayasa saha yac chrutayas tvayi hi phalanty atan-nirasanena bhavan-nidhanah (ib., 10.87.41), “O Lord, because You are unlimited, even the demigods do not know Your full extension, nor do You. Multitudes of universes with multiple layers rotate within You according to the wheel of time, just as dust moves simultaneously in the sky. Therefore, the Vedas, which merge in You at the time of devastation, are fruitful by eliminating material concepts and having You as their final destination.” That about which full comprehension is not possible is said to be unknown in practical life. It is said: na tad idrg iti jneyam na vacyam na ca tarkyate, pasyanto ‘pi na pasyanti mero rūpam vipascitah, “The Supreme should not be known, described, or thought as only this much. Even while beholding the Mount Meru, the wise do not see its full form.” Therefore, the Para-brahman is expressed by the Vedas. Being different from the material manifestation consisting of the living entities and inert matter, being meditated upon and being known, the Supreme removes ignorance and gives His own nature, supreme bliss. This is the conclusion of all those who know the truth.**

**Комментарий:**

Here someone asks the reason for which the Supreme cannot be fully described. The verse ‘janma-karmabhidhanani ‘is from the Śrimad-Bhāgavatam (10.51.36) and was spoken by Lord Kṛṣṇato Mucukunda. The verse ‘dyupataya eva ‘is also from the Srimad-Bhāgavatam (10.87.41) and was spoken by the personified Vedas to Lord Kṛṣṇa. “O (nanu) Supreme Lord, even demigods (dyupatayah) like Brahma do not know (na yayuh) Your full extension (antam). You also do not know it.” Why? They say, “This is because You are unlimited (anantataya), since Your svarūpa, qualities, and powers have no limit. Nor does this mean that Your omniscience is lost in this way. A scholar is certainly not ignorant because he does not know a flower in the sky. ” This is the idea. They describe His unlimitedness, “Multitudes (nicayah) of universes (anda) with multiple layers (savaranah) rotate (vanti) within You (yad-antara) according to the wheel of time (vayasa)». They give an illustration, “Just as dust (rajamsi) in the sky (khe), which moves simultaneously (saha), and not in succession. Because Your are so, therefore (yat), the Vedas are fruitful (phalanti) in You (tvayi), they become fruitful by making You their object according to their capacity». Then they say that the Vedas are not able to describe the Lord fully, “Having briefly described You as the possessor of transcendental attributes, the Vedas engage in describing You in detail by eliminating material concepts (atan-nirasanena), because You are their final destination (bhavan-nidhanah). ” “Just as by removing dirt and stones from a jewel mine one obtains jewels, similarly by removing things that are not spiritual, such as material nature, one attains the Supreme. Thus, since we, who remove those unlimited things that are not spiritual, merge within You at the time of universal devastation, how can we reach Yourfull extension ? ” This is the meaning. The Supreme (tat) should not be known as having limited svarūpa, qualities, and powers but should be rather known as having unlimited svarūpa, qualities, and powers. Similarly, even one who knows the Supreme is said to not know the Supreme, just as one who has seen Mount Meru is said to have not seen it, since it cannot be fully seen. It is similarly stated in the Śrimad-Bhāgavatam (1.18.23): nabhah patanty atma-samam patatrinas tatha samam visnu-gatim vipascitah, “Just as birds fly in the sky according to their capacity, similarly even the wise describe Lord Viṣṇu’s pastimes only to the extent of their understanding. ” “All those who know the truth ” refers to sages like Parasara.

### Text 36

**Перевод:**

**The same thing is spoken by the lotus-eyed Supreme Lord in the Bhagavad-gita (15-15-20): vedais ca sarvair aham eva vedyo vedanta-krd vedavid eva caham, “I am to be known through all the Vedas. I am the knower and the very compiler of Vedānta.” dvav imau puruṣau loke ksaras caksara eva ca, ksarah sarvani bhutani kutastho ‘ksara ucyate, “There are two kinds of existent beings, and these are known in the Vedas as the entangled souls and the liberated souls. All entangled souls are called perishable because of their material bodies. The liberated souls are immutable and therefore called imperishable.” uttamah puruṣas tv anyah paramatmety udahrtah, yo loka-trayam avisya bibharty avyaya isvarah, “The Supreme Person, however, is different from them and is called the Supersoul. Having entered the three worlds, this unfailing Supreme Controller supports all beings.” yasmat ksaram atito ‘ham aksarad api cottamah, ato’smi loke vede ca prathitah purosottamah, “Because I am situated beyond the entangled souls and even higher than the liberated souls, I am known in the Vedas and the smṛti as the Supreme Person.” yo mam evam asammudho janati purusottamam, sa sarvavid bhajati mam sarva-bhavena bharata, “One who, without any doubt, knows Me in this way as the Supreme Person knows everything. О descendent of Bharata, such a person worships Me by all means.” iti guhyatamam śāstram idam uktam mayanagha, etad buddhva buddhiman syat krta-krtyas ca bharata, “O sinless Arjuna, I have thus spoken the most confidential scripture. О descendent of Bharata, knowing this, one knows the visible and the invisible.” Similar statements are also seen in other places.**

**Комментарий:**

Thus ends the fourth chapter of the introduction to the Govinda-bhāṣya, named Siddhānta-ratnaṁ, whose topic is the ascertainment of Lord Viṣṇu’s knowability through all the Vedas.

Having realised the Supreme Lord, all sages like Parasara reached this same conclusion, therefore the author says, “The same thing, etc’. ‘Lord Kṛṣṇa says, “In the form of Badarayana, I establish the conclusion of the meaning of the Vedas (vedanta-krt) through the Brahma-sūtra in four chapters. I know both the accent as well as the vocabulary of the Vedas (vedavit).” What is the meaning of the Vedas? He says, “There are two kinds of existent beings, etc. ” The word ‘loke ‘means ‘in the Vedas, ‘as per the following etymological explanation: lokyate tattvam anena, “That by which the truth is seen. ” What are those two ? The group of entangled souls in various stages of life is called perishable (ksarah) because their material bodies are perishable. They are indicated as one group on account of their connection with a common attribute — their association with insentient matter. The group of liberated souls situated in only one stage is called imperishable (aksarah) because they have no such connection. They are indicated as one group on account of their connection with a common attribute — their disconnection with insentient matter. He clarifies, “All living beings (sarvani bhutani),from Brahma to a blade of grass, are called (ucyate) perishable». The liberated living entities eternally situated in the same stage (kutasthah) are called imperishable and should be understood as a single group by the same reasoning stated above. Lord Kṛṣṇa then states the purpose for which He described these two kinds of beings, “The Supreme (uttamah) Person (puruṣah), however (tu), is different (anyah) from these perishable and imperishable beings and should not be imagined to be one of them». This is the idea. He mentions the attributes that make Him the Supreme, “Having entered, etc.” It cannot be said that the maintenance and protection of the universe are done by the entangled living entities, for this is not possible. Nor can it be said that they are done by the liberated souls, for the Brahma-stitra (4-4.17) denies that: jagad-vyapara-varjam, “The liberated souls cannot engage in affairs like the creation, maintenance, and destruction of the universe. ” The Supreme Lord is unfailing (avyayah) because He never fails to support the universe. If Arjuna asks, “How should I know this Supreme Person ? ” He says, “I Myself am that Supreme Person ” — “Because I am situated, etc. “ The word ‘loke ‘means ‘in the smṛti, ‘as per the following etymological definition: lokyate vedartho ‘nena, “That by which the meaning of the Vedas is known is called smṛti. ” The word ‘prathitah ‘means ‘known’. Examples are seen in the smṛti, such as the Viṣṇu-sahasra-nama (4): jagat-prabhum deva-devam anantam purusottamam, stuvan nama-sahasrena puruṣah satatotthitah, “By glorifying the unlimited Supreme Person, the Lord of the universe and God of the demigods by reciting His thousand names, one becomes eternally exalted.” The Lord is also known as Purusottama in the śruti: esa samprasado’smac charirat samutthaya param jyotir upasampadya svena rūpenabhinispadyate sa uttamah puruṣah (Chāndogya Upaniṣad, 8.12.3), “Becoming thus liberated, the individual soul raises out from the body and becomes manifest in one’s original spiritual nature after attaining the supreme light, which is the Supreme Lord.” To elucidate the intended meaning, the Lord states the result for one who knows Him as Purusottama, “One who (yah), without any doubt (asammudhah) about My supremacy, knows (janati) Me (mam) in the way described by Me (evam), and not just as if ‘Purusottama ‘were a mere name, such as Mahavrksa — such a person knows all the Vedas (sarvavit) by understanding the meaning of these three verses and worships (bhajati) Me wholeheartedly. Although one may know the Vedas, if he is doubtful about this truth, he is ignorant, and even if worshipping Me with sixteen items, He is a non-devotee. ” This is the idea. With the intention to convey that this is not to be revealed to those who are not qualified, He says, “I have thus spoken, etc.” The word ‘buddhiman ‘means ‘one who knows the invisible, ‘and the words ‘krta-krtyah ‘mean ‘one who knows the visible’.

Thus, the fourth chapter, entitled ‘Tārkṣya, ‘has been explained in this commentary on the introduction to the Govinda-bhāṣya.

## Fifth Chapter — Vāmana

### Text 1

**Перевод:**

**Regarding the nature of the Supreme, the monists speak as follows: “The illusory material manifestation is caused by the ignorance of the individual soul, which is itself the Supreme Brahman. This material manifestation is to be dispelled by mere knowledge of nonduality. Therefore, in order to destroy this primary cause of evil, one should enquire into the non-duality of the individual soul and the Supreme Soul in this life. This is the very purport of the scriptures.”**

**Комментарий:**

While explaining meanings that are dear to him, Acarya Baladeva Vidyabhūsana defeated the monist here and there, just as one who is eating something sweet throws stones at an approaching dog. Now the same monist has returned with full strength and should be refuted as if by smashing his face. The fifth chapter named ‘Vāmana ‘and the sixth chapter named ‘Trivikrama ‘are introduced for this purpose. This chapter is called ‘Vāmana ‘because it is full of reasoning and guise. The next chapter is called ‘Trivikrama ‘because it presents extensive proof from the śruti. The word ‘atra ‘(in this regard) means that it has been thoroughly described that the import of the scriptures is that when He is known and meditated upon, the Supreme Person — Whose body is bliss and knowledge, Who has eternal abodes and pastimes, Who is the source of all avataras, and Who is the controller of māyā and everything different from Himself— removes all distress and gives all desirable objects, up to Himself. Unable to tolerate this interpretation, the monists present their own conclusion. “Just as the noncognizance of a rope gives rise to the perception of a snake, this illusory material world is created due to the ignorance about Brahman. By mere knowledge of the rope, illusory creations like a snake vanish». This is the meaning. “This primary cause of evil” i.e., the ignorance of oneself as Brahman. “One should desire to know the non-duality of the individual soul and the Supreme Soul by sentences like ‘tat tvam asi ‘(you are that)». Someone may argue, “Then what is the meaning of sentences like ‘dva suparna V” The monists reply, “This is the very purport, etc. ”

### Text 2

**Перевод:**

**This is not true, for non-duality is unsubstantiated. For instance, is the non-duality between the jiva and Brahman different from Brahman, or is it Brahman itself? The first proposition is not valid, because in this way non-duality would be lost, and by teaching that anything different from Brahman is false, the scriptural teachings would be untrue. Thus, duality would be real, because duality and non-duality being mutually contradictory, the negation of one is invariably accompanied by the assertion of the other. The second proposition is also not valid, for being self-luminous, the Supreme Spirit is eternally perfect, and hence the scriptures would be a futile means to something already accomplished.**

**Комментарий:**

Here the author refutes the above claim. Since anything different from Brahman is false, if non-duality is different from Brahman, it is also false. Therefore, the scriptures would be teaching something false by sentences like ‘tat tvam asi ‘(you are that). Because their nature is that of existence and non-existence, duality and non-duality are mutually contradictory, just like light and darkness. If non-duality is different from Brahman, and thus false, its opposite, duality, would be supported. If nonduality is Brahman itself, then being self-luminous, Brahman is eternally perfect, and thus the scriptures that lead to Brahman would be useless, being a means to an objective already accomplished. This is the idea.

### Text 3

**Перевод:**

**Someone may argue, “Although Brahman is eternally perfect by its self-luminous nature, since it cannot be apprehended on account of its characteristic identity as non-dual and so on, the scriptures do not have the fault of being a means to something already accomplished. In your view, although there is non-duality between a quality and its possessor, even when an object is perceived by touch, its quality of colour is not apprehended. The case here is similar.” This is a mistaken idea, for Brahman being devoid of attributes, there cannot be any attribute that has not been apprehended. Therefore, the illustration of an object that possesses attributes is also refuted, for although the colour is non-different from the pot, it is considered as being different from it, and not as being the pot itself.**

**Комментарий:**

Here the monist again objects. The word ‘tattvena ‘means ‘by its self-luminous nature, ‘‘tasya ‘means ‘of Brahman, ‘and the next ‘tasya ‘means ‘of the scriptures’. “ The scriptures do not have the fault of being a means to something already accomplished.” The word ‘tava ‘means ‘of yours, Dvaitavadi’s’. “Between a quality and its possessor” such as a pot and its colour. “Just as in your view, even though an earthen pot is perceived by the skin, the eyes are not useless when engaged to perceive its colour, which is non-different from the earthen pot. Similarly, although by its self-luminous nature Brahman is eternally perfect, the scriptures are not useless for teaching that its characteristics as non-dual, eternal, pure, wise, and liberated exist in the soul, which is Brahman itself. ” This is the meaning. The author refutes this idea by calling it mistaken. “Therefore, ” i.e., since the object of the illustration, the soul, is devoid of attributes, while the illustration given is something that possesses attributes.

### Text 4

**Перевод:**

**Someone may argue, “If it is a fault that the scriptures say that there is non-duality, then we ask: how come they remove the covering of ignorance? Once this covering is removed, non-duality becomes established by itself.” This argument is not sound. Ignorance certainly does not cover anything, because being self-luminous, one’s spiritual nature is eternally established, and moreover, it is devoid of attributes.**

**Комментарий:**

Having been refuted with his unsuitable illustration, the monist again objects in a different way here. The fault mentioned here: being a means to something already accomplished. “Having covered the soul’s nonduality and so on, indescribable darkness, which is a synonym of illusion (maya) and ignorance (avidya), creates the apparent manifestation of God, the individual soul, and the universe. The scriptures remove this covering of ignorance. Since the removal of this covering did not exist before, the scriptures are not a means to something already accomplished». This is the meaning. Then, how is non-duality to be attained? The monistsays, “Once this covering, etc. ” The author then refutes this idea. The word ‘caturasram ‘means ‘quadrilateral. According to the Sridhara-kośa, the word ‘asra ‘is used in the sense of angle and hair. “This is not a sound response. ” The word ‘kasyacit ‘(of anything) means that ignorance covers neither one’s spiritual nature nor its attributes. The word ‘svarūpasya’ is connected to both the preceding and subsequent word in the sentence.

### Text 5

**Перевод:**

**For instance, a covering is invariably accompanied by what is covered. There is no such object that is covered by ignorance. Does ignorance cover Brahman’s svarūpa or its attributes ? The first case is not possible, since Brahma’s svarūpa is eternally established as being luminous. The second case is also not possible, for you do not accept the existence of attributes. Moreover, as a major term does not exist, the existence of the middle term of the syllogism, the covering, is also not possible. Therefore, as the scriptures are meant to remove a covering that does not exist, they are not free from fault.**

**Комментарий:**

Having clarified the meaning spoken above, here the author gives an illustration. Wherever there is a covering in the form of darkness and the like, there is certainly an object being covered, such as land, which thus invariably accompanies the covering. The word ‘tat ‘refers to what is being covered. Affirming that ignorance does not cover anything, the author asks a question. In the next sentence, the word ‘tat ‘refers to the cover of ignorance. In case one says that ignorance covers Brahman’s svarūpa, this is not possible. The word ‘tasya ‘means ‘of Brahman’s svarūpa’. If someone says that ignorance covers the attributes inherent in Brahman, such as qualities and powers, this is also not possible. Why ? Because the monistic philosophy does not accept that Brahman has any attribute. The author then illustrates the demonstrated meaning. On account of its greater scope, as in the case of fire, what is being covered — Brahman’s svarūpa or its attributes — is the major term. On account of its minor scope, as in the case of smoke, the covering — ignorance — is the middle term. “As the proposition that either Brahman’s svarūpa or attributes are covered has been refuted, either of which is the major term in this syllogism, they are not being covered at all, so ignorance is accepted by you as the covering of what ? What is that which you claim that the scriptures remove and are therefore a means to something not yet accomplished ?” This is the meaning.

### Text 6

**Перевод:**

**In this way, if the scriptures are a means to something already accomplished, they are unintelligible and unauthoritative. The scriptures are actually meant to teach spiritual knowledge to one who is inquisitive about the soul. If they instruct the perfect soul, they are inadmissible, since the soul is already perfect. Authoritativeness (pramāna) is characterized by imparting the meaning of something unknown. If the scriptures lack such characteristic, they are unauthoritative.**

**Комментарий:**

“In this way, ” i.e., in the way explained above that by refuting the idea that ignorance is a covering, what is being covered has no factual existence, and thus the scriptures, which had been said to be a means to something not yet accomplished by removing that covering, again stand as a means to something already accomplished and must consequentially be inadmissible and unauthoritative. This is the meaning. The author then explains their inadmissibility, “The scriptures are meant to teach knowledge about the unknown nature of the soul to one who is inquisitive about the soul. ” The word ‘tasya ‘means ‘of the scriptures’. “Being eternally self-luminous, the soul of such an inquisitive person is already perfect and is Brahman itself, hence by teaching this, the scriptures are inadmissible». This is the meaning. How are the scriptures unauthoritative ? “Authority is a valid means of knowledge (pramāna) that imparts the meaning of something which is unknown. The soul is certainly eternally known, as stated in the Brhad-aranydka Upaniṣad (3-4.1): у at saksad aparoksad brahma, ‘Brahman is directly and perceptibly the self within all ‘Therefore, as the scriptures teach about the soul, which is already known, they are inadmissible. “ This is the meaning. Some say that just like by striking the dead, what was already finished is finished, or just as a forgotten necklace is found, although it was already in one’s possession, the scriptures are not a means to something already accomplished. This is a hasty argument, because in the first example, one who engages in such an act must be insane, and in the second example, by finding an object different than oneself, such as a necklace, the concept of non-duality is baffled. Therefore, this argument is insignificant.

### Text 7

**Перевод:**

**Thus, since ignorance does not exist, the scriptures also cannot possibly have a topic and purpose. The topic is certainly unknown, for otherwise there would be the fault of over-application (atiprasanga). The purpose is liberation, which is the cessation of ignorance. As it is said: avidyastamayo moksah sa samsara udahrtah, “Ignorance is called samsara, and the disappearance of ignorance is liberation.” The cessation of ignorance is also not possible if ignorance does not exist. As for the idea that the disappearance of ignorance is the aspired soul itself, that is incorrect, for the soul being already perfect, then perfection cannot be accomplished by the cessation of ignorance. Nor can it be said that only the known soul is the cessation of ignorance, and not just the soul, for you do not accept that the soul can be an object of knowledge.**

**Комментарий:**

Having refuted the idea that ignorance is a covering, the author now refutes the concept that the topic and purpose of the scriptures exist because of ignorance. “Thus, ” i.e., according to the stated reasoning, since ignorance cannot be a covering, the scriptures cannot possibly have a topic and purpose. How can this be an impossibility? The author says, “Because the topic is certainly unknown». “Otherwise, ” i.e., if even what is known is the object of the scriptures, then they would be required even to perceive a visible earthen pot. If ignorance cannot be a covering, then it cannot be said that the covering of ignorance disappears. If there is no cover, its removal is out of the question. Someone may object, “The disappearance of ignorance is the soul itself, for by its disappearance the purpose is accomplished. ” In response, the author says, “This is incorrect». The meaning is clear. “The soul cannot be an object of knowledge, ” as confirmed by the evidence of the śruti: yato vaco nivartante (Taittiriya Upaniṣad, 2.4.1), “Failing to attain Brahman, words return. ”

### Text 8

**Перевод:**

**Believing that there is flaw in the three aforementioned propositions, and imagining that ignorance is of a fourth variety, some accept that its destruction is then of a fifth variety. This view is also refuted because ignorance itself does not exist.**

**Комментарий:**

Disappearance of ignorance is liberation, disappearance of ignorance is the soul itself, and disappearance of ignorance is only the known soul — these three propositions are defective. Now, in a different way, the author refutes the concept that liberation is the cessation of ignorance, as established by the monist. The words ‘tan-nasasya ‘mean ‘the destruction of ignorance’. The view presented here is also refuted because ignorance cannot possibly exist as a covering.

### Text 9

**Перевод:**

**The following is accepted by some monists: “Ignorance has no existence, for its existence would imply the loss of non-duality. It is also not non-existent, for this would contradict its clear perception. It is also not existent and non-existent, for this is self-contradictory. Therefore, a fourth variety of proposition is to be accepted — ignorance is indescribable. Its destruction also cannot be similarly indescribable, since ignorance and the destruction of ignorance must be unavoidably different. Hence, a fifth variety of proposition must be accepted. As it is said: na san nasan na sad-asan nanirvacyas ca tat-ksayah, yaksanurūpo balir ity acaryah pratyaplpadan, ‘The preceptors have explained that the destruction of ignorance is neither existent, nor non-existent, nor existent and non-existent, nor indescribable. The oblation is always proportional to the sacrifice’. This is also not wise, for according to the above explained way, ignorance is an impossibility, and hence the fifth proposition imagined on the basis of the existence of ignorance is similar to a flower in the sky.**

**Комментарий:**

Here the author explains the meaning of what was stated above. The word ‘paraih ‘means ‘by some monists’. “If ignorance does not exist, there would not be such perception that ‘I am ignorant’. Its existence and non-existence in the same place are also not possible because they are contradictory, just like cold and heat. Therefore, a fourth variety of proposition must be accepted, according to which ignorance is different from existence, non-existence, and existence and non-existence». This is the meaning. The destruction of ignorance also cannot be of the same sort as the fourth proposition. Why ? “Since ignorance and the destruction, etc. ” Their difference is unavoidable, just as with an earthen pot and the destruction of an earthen pot. “Therefore, the destruction of ignorance must be according to a fifth variety of proposition. ” This is the meaning. The monist then quotes the opinion of the preceptors on the matter. The words ‘tat-ksayah ‘mean ‘the destruction of ignorance’. “It cannot be existent, for this is accepted as implied. The idea is that if the destruction of ignorance has any attribute, this would undermine the concept of absence of attributes. It is not non-existent, because this would contradict the cognition that 7 have a spiritual nature that is pure, wise, and liberated’. It is also not existent and non-existent, because this is contradictory. It is also not indescribable, because ignorance being such, its destruction must be unavoidably something different from that. Therefore, the destruction of ignorance is different from existence, non-existence, existence and nonexistence, and indescribability. ” The author then refutes this view by calling it unwise (apesalam), i.e., incorrect. According to the Visva-kośa (La, 93), the word ‘pesala ‘is used in the sense of beautiful and clever. “According to the above explained way” that as a major term does not exist, the existence of a middle term — the covering — in the syllogism, is also not possible. The word ‘tena’ means ‘by ignorance’, and ‘tasyah’ means ‘of the fifth variety.9

### Text 10

**Перевод:**

**Moreover, in this fifth variety of proposition, is the disappearance of ignorance different from the soul, or is it the very nature of the soul ? Also, in the first case, is that an effect of ignorance or not? The first case is definitively not possible, for ignorance would remain existing even then. Without a material cause, there is certainly no effect. The last case is also not possible, for something different from the soul must be unavoidably inferred as either ignorance or its effect. The second case is not possible either, because of the same fault already shown. Thus, the disappearance of ignorance being impossible in either way, it has no definite nature, and hence the fifth variety of proposition has been again driven far away.**

**Комментарий:**

Here the author refutes the fifth variety of proposition in a different way. “In the first case,” i.e., if the disappearance of ignorance is different from the soul, is that an effect of ignorance or not ? In the first case, the proposition that the disappearance of ignorance is different from the soul and is an effect of ignorance is not valid. “Even then, ” i.e., ignorance would remain existing even during its disappearance. The author clarifies the matter, “ Without a material cause, etc. ” The cessation of ignorance is brought about by a mental state identified with Brahman. Such mental state is the immediate cause (upadana) of the cessation of ignorance. The cause, the mental state, partially follows its effect, the cessation of ignorance. Therefore, since ignorance remains partially existing, such proposition cannot be liberation. “In the last case,” i.e., if the disappearance of ignorance is different from the soul and is not an effect of ignorance. This also cannot be said to be so. Why ? “Because something different from the soul must be unavoidably inferred as either ignorance or its effect, for this is accepted by you. Thus, the cessation of ignorance cannot be liberation». In the second case, the first option is that in the fifth variety of proposition, the disappearance of ignorance is the very nature of the soul. This also cannot be said to be so. Why ? Because of the same fault already shown, i.e., because the soul being already perfect, it cannot be accomplished by the cessation of such ignorance. Thus, the disappearance of ignorance being impossible in either way, i.e., since it is not possible to describe the cessation of ignorance either as different from the soul or as the nature of the soul, this fifth variety of proposition has been driven far away.

### Text 11

**Перевод:**

**Moreover, things like earthen pots are existent, and things like a rabbit’s horns are non-existent. Only things like earthen pots are existent or non-existent according to time, place, and circumstance. Therefore, since only these three varieties of propositions are perceived, a fourth variety does not exist at all. Since a fourth variety of proposition does not exist, a fifth one is impossible, just like an eighth flavour.**

**Комментарий:**

Since no object other than the existent or non-existent is known, it is not possible to say that ignorance is such. This is stated by the author here. The fourth variety of proposition is that ignorance is something different from the existent and non-existent. The fifth proposition is that the cessation of ignorance is different from something that is different from the existent and non-existent. The author gives an illustration. As there are only six flavours,\[16\] the seventh one is non-existent, and therefore the idea of an eighth one is driven far away. Similarly, so is the case here.

### Text 12

**Перевод:**

**In this way, as neither the topic nor the purpose exist, a person entitled to the scriptures also cannot exist, and in the absence of all these, any possible relation is also refuted. A relation is possible only among existent things. As ignorance does not exist, there can be neither a topic nor a purpose in connection with it, hence no relation exists here, and the effort in deliberating upon the scriptures is useless. Therefore, the statement that the scriptures remove such covering of ignorance and are thus free from fault is mere rhetoric.**

**Комментарий:**

Thus ends the fifth chapter of the introduction to the Govinda-bhāṣya, named Siddhānta-ratnaṁ, whose topic is the refutation of the monistic philosophy.

A person endowed with qualities like equanimity is entitled to know from the scriptures about what is unknown and attain the purpose —-cessation of ignorance. According to the way explained above, since ignorance does not exist, a person entitled to the scriptures also cannot exist in your non-dualistic philosophy. This is what the author says here. The words ‘tad-abhavat ‘mean’ since the topic, the purpose, and the person entitled to them do not exist’. The author then demonstrates the nonexistence of a relation. The relation between the scriptures and a qualified soul is that of instructor and instructed, what is to be read and the reader. The relation between the scriptures, the soul, and liberation is that the scriptures lead the soul to attain liberation. These unfolded relations are only due to the existence of ignorance. If ignorance does not exist, these relations are also not possible, for the relation would be between whom and what? Deliberation is the ascertainment of facts about a doubtful object. Doubt is certainly created by ignorance, in the absence of which there is no effort of deliberation. This is the meaning.

Thus, the fifth chapter, entitled ‘Vāmana, ‘has been explained in this commentary on the introduction to the Govinda-bhāṣya.

## Sixth Chapter — Trivikrama

### Text 1

**Перевод:**

**Regarding the topic discussed above, some monists speak as follows: “If the meaning of the scriptures is something unknown and able to yield fruit, the ultimate truth must be non-difference, for this is the intended meaning. The fruit is the very nature of the unlimited and blissful Brahman. Non-difference from Brahman is comprehended only through the scriptures. Brahman being non-dual, all the various kinds of differences as knower, knowledge, and knowable are imaginary, hence false. The śruti states the following: brahmavid brahmaiva bhavati (Muṇḍaka Upaniṣad, 3.2.9)\> ‘One who knows Brahman becomes Brahman’. brahmaiva san brahmapy eti (Bṛhad-āraṇyaka Upaniṣad, 4.4.6), ‘Being Brahman, one attains Brahman’. sad eva saumyedam agra asid ekam evadvitiyam (Chāndogya Upaniṣad, 6.2.1), ‘Dear Svetaketu, before creation there was only’sat, ‘the Supreme, which is only one without a second’. neha nānāsti kincana (Bṛhad-āraṇyaka Upaniṣad, 4.4.19)у ‘Within Brahman there is no difference whatsoever’. yatra hi dvaitam iva bhavati tad itara itaram pasyati yatra tv asya sarvam atmaivabhut tatra kena kam pasyet kena kam vijaniyat (Bṛhad-āraṇyaka Upaniṣad, 4.5.15)\> ‘When there is duality, so to say, then one sees another as different. When everything becomes spirit for him, whom would he see and through what? Whom would he know, and through what? ‘vacarambhanam vikaro namadheyam mrttikety eva satyam (Chāndogya Upaniṣad, 6.1.4), ‘What is called a transformation of clay is a mere word, for clay alone is real’. ito ‘nyad artam (Bṛhad-āraṇyaka Upaniṣad, 3.4.2), ‘Anything different from spirit is perishable’. enam brahma-lokam na vidanty anrtena hi pratyudhah (Chāndogya Upaniṣad, 8.3.2), ‘Daily all living beings go to the abode of Brahman, but they do not know this, being covered by ignorance’.**

**Комментарий:**

Here the monist has returned with different approaches and has to be refuted. This sixth chapter, entitled ‘Trivikrama, ‘begins for this purpose. First, the author explains their view. The fruit for a soul aspiring for liberation is to attain such Brahman, whose non-duality can be comprehended only through the scriptures, not through direct perception or inference. All differences are imagined due to ignorance about such Brahman. The opponent gradually gives evidence to what was stated. “Before creation (agre), the only existence (sat) was Brahman. ” This is the meaning. As stated in the Kaṭha Upaniṣad (2.3.13), astity evopalabdhavyah, " ‘It exists ‘— this is how the Supreme is to be known». In the Viṣṇu Purāṇa (1.2.11), it is also said: apaksaya-vinasabhyam parinamarddhi-janmabhih, varjitah sakyate vakturh yah sadasttti kevalam, “The Supreme Lord is free from birth, growth, transformation, decay, and annihilation. It is possible to state that He simply exists eternally in His own transcendental nature». This world is imagined due to ignorance about Brahman but disappears by knowledge about it. There it is said that Brahman is one without a second, i.e., it is devoid of internal difference (svagata-bheda), difference among objects belonging to the same class (sajatiya-bheda), and difference among objects belonging to different classes (vijatiya-bheda). Within Brahman (iha) there are no imagined differences such as knower, knowledge, and knowable. “When there is duality, ” i.e., at the time of worldly life. “When everything becomes spirit, ” i.e., at the time of liberation. For that living entity (asya) everything becomes spirit, Brahman. In other words, one has become Brahman. What is called a transformation (vikarah), such as an earthen pot, is a mere word (vacarambhanam), hence that is false. Only the clay (mrttika) is real (satyam). Similarly, because the world is a mere word, it is false. Only Brahman is real. This is the meaning. Anything different (anyat) from Brahman (atah) is perishable (artam), i.e., false. Being covered (pratyudhah) by ignorance (anrtena), all living beings go to that abode, Brahman itself (brahma-lokam), but they do not know this, for due to ignorance, that appears to them as the material world. This is the meaning.

### Text 2

**Перевод:**

**“It is similarly said by Lord Kṛṣṇain the Śrimad-Bhāgavatam (11.10.31 -33): gunah srjanti karmani guno ‘nusrjate gunan, jivas tu guna-sarhyukto bhunkte karma-phalany asau, yavat syad guna-vaisamyam tavan nanatvam atmanah, nanatvam atmano yavat paratantryam tadaiva hi, yavad asyasvatantratvam tavad isvarato bhayam, ‘The senses create material activities, and ignorance sets the senses into motion. The living entity endowed with senses enjoys the results of work. For as long as there is illusory diversity created by the modes of nature, there is a plurality of souls. For as long as there is a plurality of souls, there is dependence. For as long as there is dependence, there is fear of God’.**

**Комментарий:**

Having made such a conclusion from the śruti, here the monist makes the same conclusion from the smṛti. The senses (gunah) create (srjanti) material activities (karmani), and not the soul. According to the Medini-kośa (Na, 10), the word ‘guna ‘is used in the sense of bow-string, a secondary element, sense objects like colour, cook, abandonment, qualities like heroism, political expedients like conciliation, modes of nature such as goodness, repetition, and rope. Someone may argue, “The soul acts with the senses. ” Lord Kṛṣṇasays, “Covering the soul with a shadow, ignorance (gunah) acts like the consciousness and sets the senses into motion». Thus, the soul is not the doer. Then, He says that the soul’s quality of being an enjoyer is not factual but only due to ignorance: “The living entity (jivah) endowed with a group of senses that are a false creation of ignorance (guna-sarhyuktaḥ) enjoys (bhuhkte) the results of work in the form of happiness and distress, and not the pure soul. ” Therefore, the enjoyer is actually the modes of nature. As for the idea that there are multiple souls, that is also an illusory creation of ignorance, as stated in the next verse. For as long as (yavat) there is diversity (vaisamyam) in the form of false-ego, senses, and mind created by the illusory modes of nature, which are created by ignorance, there will be a plurality of souls divided by false-ego, mind, and so forth, just like the sky is divided by earthen pots. When all divisions disappear through knowledge about the real nature of the soul, then oneness is established. This is the idea. Someone may argue, “If the souls are one, then how do they become dependent and how are the maintainers of the universe said to be afraid of You ?” Lord Kṛṣṇa says, “As long as there is plurality, etc». The meaning is clear.

### Text 3

**Перевод:**

**“The secret here is this: The soul is mere consciousness, without a second, and devoid of attributes like being a doer and enjoyer. Due to ignorance about the soul, one imagines the illusion in the form of the modes of nature and a multitude of activities created by the diversity of the modes. Thus, the soul imagines the sense of ‘Г as one and the sense of ‘you ‘as many. Here, the sense of ‘I\* is the soul in respective individual forms. The sense of ‘you ‘is threefold — the souls; inert elements, which differ from the soul; and God, Who is a special kind of soul. In the Nrsimha-tapani Upaniṣad (Uttara, 9) it is said: jivesav abhasena karoti māyā cavidya ca svayam eva bhavati, ‘Ignorance (avidya) itself becomes may a and creates God and the jiva as reflections’. Due to contact with the modes of nature, qualities like being a doer and enjoyer are imposed on the soul as the sense of ‘I’. When that ignorance is destroyed, its effect, maya, vanishes. By the disappearance of maya, plurality also vanishes. There being no plurality of souls, God, dependence on Him, fear of Him, and so on are all driven far away. Therefore, the nature of the soul is mere consciousness without a second.**

**Комментарий:**

Here the monist explains the meaning and intention of the verse ‘gunah srjanti ‘quoted above. The word ‘avidyaya ‘means ‘due to ignorance about the soul’. Ignorance is the doer and creates God and the individual soul as reflections. It creates the individual soul as a reflection of Brahman on the mind, and it creates God as a reflection of Brahman on maya. Someone may argue, “In the Śrimad-Bhāgavatam (11.11.3) Lord Kṛṣṇa says that ignorance is a function of maya: vidyavidye mama tanU viddhy uddhava sartrinam, bandha-moksa-kari adye mayaya me vinirmite, ‘O Uddhava, you should know that knowledge and ignorance are My energies, created by Me through the agency of maya. Knowledge and ignorance are the respective causes of liberation and bondage for the embodied beings since beginningless time’. So, how is it that ignorance creates māyā ?" The monist replies by quoting the Nrsimha-tāpani Upaniṣad (Uttara, 9). The concept of difference between māyā and avidya should be thus taken in a secondary sense. This is the idea. By knowledge about the real nature of the soul, ignorance disappears, then illusion (maya) disappears, and then the plurality of souls disappears. The words ‘tad-abhavat ‘mean ‘there being no plurality of souls’. In this connection it should be understood that ignorance about the soul comes forth like the idea that 7 am not the tenth one, ‘and knowledge about the soul comes forth like the idea that 7 am the tenth one’.

### Text 4

**Перевод:**

**“Subsequently, in the Śrimad-Bhāgavatam (11.28.4-7) it is also said: kim bhadram kirn abhadram va dvaitasyavastunah kiyat, vacoditaih tad anrtam manasa dhyatam eva ca, chaya-pratyahvayabhasa hy asanto ‘py artha-karinah, evam dehadayo bhava yacchanty amrtyuto bhayam, atmaiva tad idam visvam srjyate srjati prabhuh, trayate trati visvatma hriyate haratisvarah, tasman na hy atmano ‘nyasmad anyo bhavo nirupitah, nirupiteyam tri-vidha nirmula bhatir ātmani, idam guna-mayam viddhi tri-vidham mayaya krtam, ‘Within this unreal world of duality, what is auspicious or inauspicious, and to what extent? Whatever is perceived by the senses and the mind is false. Although shadow, echo, and reflection are false, they convey meaning. Similarly, things such as the body produce fear until their dissolution. The soul itself is the created universe and the controlling lord who creates it; the soul is the protector and the protected, the destroyer and the destroyed. Therefore, those who know the truth describe no existing thing other than the soul, which is different than anything else. They have also described the perception of the three modes of nature in the soul as baseless. You should know that this world made of the three modes is created by maya’.**

**Комментарий:**

“Subsequently,” i.e., in chapter 28. Within the material world (dvaitasya) what (kim) is auspicious (bhadram) or inauspicious (abhadram), and to what extent (kiyat) ? The meaning is that it is just like a bunch of flowers in the air, because it is false (avastunah) like a rope mistaken for a serpent. The falsity is explained, “Whatever is perceived, etc. ” The external senses are implied. What is apprehended by speech and other senses, and what is thought by the mind, that is false, for in a later stage that does not remain as it appeared. Someone may argue, “As the body, mind, and senses are false, how come they.are causes of fear?” The next verse replies with an illustration. The word ‘chaya ‘means ’shadow, ‘‘pratyahvayah ‘means ‘echo, ‘‘abhasah ‘means ‘a reflection like that of silver on a shell’. Although these are false, yet they convey meaning. Similarly, things such as the body produce fear until their dissolution. The word ‘mrtyuh ‘means ‘dissolution’. For as long as they are not dissolved by knowledge of the truth, they will produce fear, i.e., repeated birth and death. Someone may argue, “In the śruti it is said: yato va imani bhūtani jayante (Taittiriya Upaniṣad, 3.1), ‘You should know Him from Whom all living beings are born’. Since duality is so proved, how can it be false ? ” The next two verses answer this. The prose order is as follows: The soul (atma) itself (eva) is the universe (visvam), just as a shell itself is silver. Being under the influence of ignorance about the self the soul itself is the object and subject of creation, protection, and destruction. This is the meaning. Someone may argue, “It is contradictory to say that the subject itself is the object». In response, it is said, “Therefore, etc. ” Besides the soul (atmanah) no other existing thing (bhavah) is described (nirūpitah) by those who know the truth, but the soul itself is described as the subject — the creator of the universe, and the object — the universe, as stated in the Taittiriya Upaniṣad (3.1): tad atmanam svayam akuruta, “The soul created the universe by itself\>y Another objection is raised, “Then there would be no transformation». It is said, “which is different from anything else, ” i.e., which is untainted by any kind of transformation. Then why is the soul said to be the object ? It is said, “The perception (bhatih) of the three modes of nature is baseless (nirmula)». When a shell is mistaken for silver, this misapprehension has no basis other than ignorance about the shell. This is what is said in the next line, “This is created (krtam) by maya, which is created by ignorance. ”Nor is it contradictory to say that something baseless is based on ignorance, for when there is knowledge, such illusory manifestation disappears.

### Text 5

**Перевод:**

**“In the Śrimad-Bhāgavatam (11.28.8-9) it is further said: etad vidvan mad-uditarh jnāna-vijnāna-naipunam, na nindati na ca stauti loke carati suryavat, pratyaksenanumanena nigamenatma-samvida, ady-antavad asaj jnātvā nihsango vicared iha, ‘One who knows the conclusion of all theoretical and practical knowledge, as spoken by Me, neither blames nor praises anyone. He moves in the world impartial like the sun. Knowing by direct perception, by inference, by the scriptures, and by self-knowledge that everything that has a beginning and an end is false, one should act in this world without attachment’.**

**Комментарий:**

Here the means to remove this meaningless illusion is stated. The words ‘mad-uditam ‘mean ‘in the way spoken by Me, ‘and ‘naipunam ‘means ‘the conclusion’. Another means is stated, “He should be impartial like the sun (sūryavat).” The means to comprehend the conclusion of all theoretical and practical knowledge is stated: Knowing (jnātvā) by direct perception (pratyaksena) that things like earthen pots and cloth have a beginning and an end (ady-antavat), knowing by inference (anumanena) that visible things like earth have a beginning and an end, knowing by the Vedas (nigamena) that invisible things like ether have a beginning and an end, and knowing by self-knowledge (atma-samvida) that everything different from consciousness has a beginning and an end and is therefore false (asat), one should then act (vicaret) in this world (iha) without attachment (nihsahgah).

### Text 6

**Перевод:**

**“Similarly, in the Viṣṇu Purāṇa (1.4.38-41), the four Kumaras speak as follows: paramarthas tvam evaiko nanyo’sti jagatah pate, tavaisa mahima yena vyaptam etac caracaram, yad etad drsyate murtam etaj jnānatmanas tava, bhranti-jnanena pasyanti jagad-rūpam ayoginah, jnāna-svarūpam akhilam jagad etad abuddhayah, artha-svarūparh pasyanto bhramyante moha-samplave, ye tu jnāna-vidah suddha-cetasas te ‘khilam jagat, jnānatmakarh prapasyanti tvad-rupam paramesvara, ‘0 Lord of the universe, there is nothing besides You only, the highest truth. Such is Your greatness, by which You pervade all moving and non- moving beings. Whatever is seen is a form of Yours, Who are knowledge personified. Those who have no knowledge about You see the form of the universe through mistaken knowledge. The ignorant see this whole universe as matter, although it consists of consciousness, and thus they are tossed about in a flood of delusion. On the other hand, those conversant with knowledge and whose mind is pure see the whole universe as a form of Yours made of consciousness, О Supreme Lord’.**

**Комментарий:**

Just as spoken by Lord Kṛṣṇa in the eleventh book of the Śrimad-Bhāgavatam, it is similarly stated by Sanandana and the other Kumaras in the first part of the Viṣṇu Purāṇa, which corroborates the explained meaning. “You (tvam) alone are the highest truth (paramarthah). Besides You, everything is false. ” This is the meaning. “It is due to ignorance about Your svarūpa that this universe is imagined to exist within You. ” Therefore it is said, “those who are devoid of jnana-yoga (ayoginah), devoid of knowledge about You as the non-dual Brahman, etc. ” The meaning of the rest is clear.

### Text 7

**Перевод:**

**“In the Bhagavad-gita, Lord Kṛṣṇa also says: ksetrajnam capi mam viddhi sarva-ksetresu bharata (13-3), ‘0 Arjuna, you should know that I am also the knower of the field within all bodies’. na tad asti vina yat syan māyā bhutam caracaram (10.39), ‘Except for Me, no moving or non-moving being exists’.**

**Комментарий:**

“Being divided by the mind or being reflected on it, I myself am the jiva. By knowledge about My svarūpa, when the mind is dissolved, just as the dreaming state is dissolved by awakening, the individual soul is no other than Myself ” This is the meaning. “Except for Me, this moving and nonmoving universe has no existence. It is due to ignorance about Me that those who do not know this imagine that the universe exists within Me, just as due to ignorance about the shell, one imagines silver within it. ”

### Text 8

**Перевод:**

**“Falsity is actually the suppression of knowledge about Brahman, which is its locus. When a locus such as a shell is factually perceived, imagined things like silver, which are only due to a mistake, are suppressed. This mistake is mere ignorance (avidya), which causes manifold projections that cover the real nature of Brahman, and which is beginningless and indescribable on account of being different than existent and non-existent. This avidya it referred to in the scriptures by words such as ‘tamah ‘(darkness), ajnana ‘(ignorance), and ‘māyā ‘(illusion). For instance: na sad asm no’sad iait tadanim, tama asit tamasa gudham agre praketam (Rg Veda, 10.129.1.3), ‘There was neither existence nor non-existence then. In the beginning there was darkness, and Brahman was concealed by darkness’. indro mayabhih puru-rupa iyate (Bṛhad-āraṇyaka Upaniṣad, 2.5.19), The Supreme Soul manifests Himself in multiple forms by dint of the work of avidya’. mayam tu prakṛtim vidyan mayinam tu mahesvaram (Svetaivatara Upaniṣad, 4.10), ‘One should know that material nature is māyā (illusion) and the Supreme Lord is the illusionist’.**

**Комментарий:**

It has been previously said that duality is false. Now the characteristic of falsity is described. It should be understood that falsity is a perishable element. The words ‘dosa-vasat ‘mean ‘on account of ignorance about Brahman, which is accompanied by illusion and misapprehension’. The word ‘tasmin ‘means ‘in the locus where illusion happens, such as in a shell’. What is the fault in the present context? It is said to be avidya itself endowed with the power ofcovering and projecting. The words ’saiva ‘refers to avidya. The monist then gives evidence from the scriptures that avidya is different from existent and non-existent. The word ‘tadanim ‘means ‘during the time of dissolution, ‘and ‘praketam ‘means ‘Brahman, ‘the resting place of everything, which was resorted to by darkness (tamasa). The text ‘indro mayabhih ‘is from the Bṛhad-āraṇyaka Upaniṣad (2.5.19). The Supreme Soul (indrah) appears (iyate) in the form of innumerable jivas (puru-rūpah) with multiple minds, which are the functions of avidya (mayabhih). The text ‘mayam tu ‘is from the Śvetāśvatara Upaniṣad (4.10).

### Text 9

**Перевод:**

**“Avidya again disappears by knowledge about the oneness of the soul with Brahman, as stated in the śruti: na punar mrtyave tad ekam pasyati, ‘One who sees Brahman as only one does not meet avidya again’. na pasyo mrtyum pasyati (Chāndogya Upaniṣad, 7.26.2), ‘One who sees oneness in Bhiuna, the Supreme, never sees avidya’. tam eva viditva atimrtyum eti (Puruṣa-sūkta, 18), ‘Only by knowing the Supreme one transcends avidya’. tatra ко mohah kah soka ekatvam anupasyatah (Isa Upaniṣad, 7), ‘What is delusion and what is sorrow for one who sees oneness in all beings? ‘In these passages, the word ‘mrtyu ‘(death) denotes avidya, as employed in the Mahā-bhārata (5-42.4): pramadam vai mrtyum aham bravimi sadapramadad amrtatvam bravimi, ‘I refer to death as ignorance. I call immortality as ‘amrtatva ‘because it is always devoid of ignorance’.**

**Комментарий:**

The word ‘iyam ‘refers to avidya. One who sees (pasyati) Brahman (tat) as only one (ekam) does not meet avidya (mrtyave) again (punar). This is the meaning. The word ‘pasyah ‘means ‘a learned person who sees oneness’. The word ‘atimrtyum ‘means ‘liberation surpassing avidya’. The verse ‘pramadam vai ‘is from the Mahā-bhārata (5-42.4) and was spoken by Sanatsujata. The word ‘pramadam ‘means ‘ignorance, ‘and ‘apramadat ‘means ‘by being devoid of ignorance, ‘i.e., by knowledge of oneness. The words ‘tasyam tasya ‘in this paragraph mean that in this verse the word ‘mrtyu ‘is used in the sense of avidya.

### Text 10

**Перевод:**

**“The svarūpa of Brahman is actually mere consciousness devoid of attributes, for all attributes are denied by the śruti and the smṛti: satyam jnānam anantam (Taittiriya Upaniṣad, 2.1), ‘The Supreme is truth, consciousness, and eternity’. niskalam niskriyam santam niravadyam niranjanam (Śvetāśvatara Upaniṣad, 6.19), ‘The peaceful Supreme has no parts, no actions, no defects, and no blemishes’. neha nānāsti kincana (Bṛhad-āraṇyaka Upaniṣad, 4.4.19), ‘Within the Supreme there is no difference whatsoever’. athata adeso neti neti, na hy etasmad iti nety anyat param (Bṛhad-āraṇyaka Upaniṣad, 2.3.6), ‘Now, the instruction about Brahman is that it is not so, it is not so. Anything other than Brahman does not really exist, does not exist. Brahman is different than the material manifestation’. pratyastamita-bhedam yat satta-matram agocaram, vacasam atma-samvedyam taj-jnānam brahma-samjnitam (Viṣṇu Purāṇa, 6.7-53), ‘That which is mere existence, which is beyond words and in which all differences disappear, is called Brahman. Knowledge about it is realised only by the soul’. sasvat prasantam abhayam pratibodha-matram śuddhaṁ samarh sad-asatah param atma-tattvam (Śrimad-Bhāgavatam, 2.7.47), ‘The nature of the soul is eternal, peaceful, and fearless. It is knowledge devoid of attributes, pure, impartial, and beyond cause and effect’.**

**Комментарий:**

Expecting that someone may ask what the svarūpa of Brahman is like, the monist describes it here. Brahman’s svarupa is truth (satyam), knowledge (jnānam), and eternity (anantam). Within Brahman (iha) there is no manifold difference (nana) in the form of knower, knowable, and knowledge whatsoever (kincana). The text ‘athatah ‘is from the Bṛhad-āraṇyaka Upaniṣad (2.3.6). “Now (atha), ” i.e., after the description of form and formless. Because there is nothing superior (param) to knowledge about Brahman, therefore the instruction (adesah) is that it is not so, it is not so (neti neti). The meaning is that Brahman alone is to be known as being indicated by the words ‘not so, not so’. The repetition is meant to deny that Brahman is either a mundane sentient or insentient being, which are both different than Brahman. In order to give this instruction, the śruti says that anything different (anyat) than Brahman (etasmat) actually does not exist, hence it is said 4t is not so’. By the first ‘neti ‘it is meant that no mundane sentient or insentient being actually exists, both of which are different than Brahman, and by the second ‘neti ‘the same thing is corroborated. Someone may argue, “Brahman must also not exist, just like the material manifestation». In response, it is said that it is not so. Brahman is mere existence, the end of all illusion, different (param) than the visible material manifestation. The verse ‘pratyastamita-bhedam ‘is from the Viṣṇu Purāṇa (6.7.53). The meaning is clear. The verse ’sasvat prasantam ‘is from the Śrimad-Bhāgavatam (2.7.47). The word ’sasvat ‘means ‘eternal, ‘and ‘pratibodha-matram\* means that Brahman’s nature is consciousness without attributes.

### Text 11

**Перевод:**

**“Brahman must be considered the very same soul of one who understands and worships it, as stated in the Bṛhad-āraṇyaka Upaniṣad (1.4.7): atmety evopasita, ‘One should meditate only on the atma’. The same is also accepted by Vyāsadeva, the compiler of the scriptures: atmeti tupagacchanti grahayanti ca (Brahma-sūtra, 4-1.3), ‘Those who know the truth know and teach that Brahman is one’s own soul’.**

**Комментарий:**

The word ‘tat ‘refers to Brahman, that is pure consciousness devoid of attributes. The text ‘atmety eva ‘is from the Bṛhad-āraṇyaka Upaniṣad (1.4.7). The meaning is that one should worship with the idea that ‘I am that’. The aphorism ‘atmeti tu ‘is from the Brahma-sūtra (4.1.3). The meaning is that those who know the truth (tattvajnah) know (upagacchanti) and teach (grahayanti) their disciples that Brahman is one’s own soul.

### Text 12

**Перевод:**

**“Nor can it be said that, ‘If words such as satyam (truth) express only knowledge about nirguṇa-brahma, then they must be all synonyms, and the simultaneous employment of synonyms in a sentence is tautology, hence words such as satyam denote specific qualities’. This idea is mistaken, for it contradicts the śruti statements about nirguṇa-brahma.**

**Комментарий:**

The Dvaitavadis explain that passages of the śruti such as ’satyam jnānam anantam ‘are about the Supreme endowed with attributes. Here, the monist rejects this idea. The word ‘tesam ‘ means ‘of words such as satyam, etc’. Words that express the same meaning are synonyms (paryaya). Unnecessary repetition of words is called tautology (paunaruktya), as in the sentence ‘bring ghata, kalasa, and kumbha’. The connection between the words here is ‘iti na vacyam, ‘‘it should not be said so’. Why? Because if it is accepted that words such as ’satyam ‘denote specific qualities, this would contradict the following sentence: saksi cetah kevalo nirguṇas ca, “The Supreme is the witnessing pure consciousness without any material quality. ” This is what is said here: According to ācāryas like Bharata, words are of three kinds — directly expressive (vacaka), indirectly indicative (laksanika), and implicit (vyanjaka). They have respectively three functions — direct denotation, indirect indication, and implicit suggestion, by which three meanings are understood — primary meaning, secondary meaning, and insinuation. On account of their clear perception, it is accepted and known by all that there are innumerable varieties of implicit suggestions and insinuations, and this is also accepted by us monists.
