Without semantic causes such as generic ones (jati), by indirect indication words such as ’satyam ‘express consciousness devoid of attributes. This must be accepted on the basis of the well-known śruti statements about nirguṇa-brahma.

### Text 13

**Перевод:**

**“Therefore, such words gradually convey distinction from what is false, insentient, and limited. Distinction is neither an existent nor a non-existent attribute but is of the very same nature as Brahman, which is distinct from everything else. Just as the distinction of white from blue and other colours is white itself, and not other attribute. This being so, words such as ’satyam ‘express the same object without synonymy. Thus, there is no fault here whatsoever.**

**Комментарий:**

Having noted the tautology in the abovementioned objection, in order to establish that Brahman is consciousness without attributes, the monist now proceeds with a view called ‘Apohavada, ‘ which is accepted by the non-believers (nastika). The meaning is that the monist has rejected the abovementioned objection. The ablative case is used here in the sense of object, there being an ellipsis of agerundive (lyab-lope karmani pancamt). Just as a cow is different from anything other than a cow, the svarūpa of Brahman is discriminated as different from what is false. The word ‘tesam ‘means ‘of words such as satyam’.

### Text 14

**Перевод:**

**“As for the statement about duality understood in passages like ‘dva suparna sayuja sakhāyā, ‘ that is not actually within the scope of the intended meaning of the scriptures, since this is well-known and duality yields no fruit, but it is within the empirical scope, which is false. The material manifestation, which consists of the jivas and inert matter, is indeed a false imposition on Brahman, just like silver on a shell. Hence, material difference is also false.**

**Комментарий:**

Someone may argue, “The difference between the living entity and God is also mentioned in the scriptures. What is its meaning?” The monist replies here. Even simple people who plough the land know very well the difference between oneself and others, and the scriptures are not required for this. Even when those who propound dualism and have attained Vaikuṇṭha by worshipping God, they remain His worshippers there, and there is no end to their dependence, just like those who serve a king, even though they are rich. Therefore, this is no advantageous fruit. This is the idea. Someone may argue, “Why cannot difference be the goal of life ?” This is because just as due to ignorance about the shell, it appears to be silver, similarly it is only due to ignorance about Brahman that the material manifestation is imagined to exist in it. Thus, the material manifestation is as false as silver. Therefore, the difference that exists in the material manifestation is also false.

### Text 15

**Перевод:**

**“The sage Parasara also makes the same conclusion in the Viṣṇu Purāṇa (2.12.38-42): jyotlmsi visnur bhuvanani visnur vanani visnur vidiso disas ca, sarit-samudras ca sa eva sarvam yad asti yan nasti ca vipra-varya, ‘Lord Viṣṇu is the luminaries, Lord Viṣṇu is the planets, Lord Viṣṇu is the forests, the directions, the rivers, the oceans, and everything that exists and does not exist, О best of brāhmaṇas’. jnāna-svarupo bhagavan yato’sav asesa-murtir na tu vastu-bhutah, tato hi sailabdhi-dharadi-bhedan janihi vijnāna-vijrmbhitani, ‘As the Supreme Lord‘s form is consciousness, all material elements have no substance. You should know that differences like rocks, oceans, and mountains are imagined on His form of consciousness due to mistaken cognizance’. yada tu śuddhaṁ nija-mpi sarvam karma-ksaye jnānam apasta-dosam, tada hi sankalpa-taroh phalani bhavanti no vastusu vastu-bhedah, ‘When all material activities have ended and one’s knowledge assumes its original form and becomes pure, free from the impurities of difference, then the fruits of the tree of thoughts in the form of difference no more exist among objects’. vastv asti kim kutracid adi-madhya-paryanta-hinam satataika-rupam, yac canyathatvam dvija yati bhuyo na tat tatha tatra kuto hi tattvam, ‘O brāhmaṇa, can a material object that has eternally one form and is devoid of beginning, middle, and end ever exist somewhere? Whatever is otherwise has no such characteristics and undergoes repeated transformation, so how can it be real? ‘mahl ghatatvam ghatatah kapalika kapalika-curna-rajas tato ‘nuh, janaih sva-karma-stimitatma-niscayair alaksyate bruhi kim atra vastu, ‘Clay becomes an earthen pot, which becomes a potsherd, which becomes small pieces and then dust, which becomes atoms. Then, tell me, how can people whose spiritual knowledge has become stagnated due to their material activities see the truth about Brahman in this world? ‘**

**Комментарий:**

The great sage Parasara also spoke the same meaning. It should be understood that in the first verse quoted here there is identity of case-relation in the sense of negation (badhayam samanadhikaranya) of one of the terms, as in the sentence’sthanuh puruṣah, ‘‘thepost is a man’. Since the Supreme Lord‘s form is consciousness (jnāna-svarūpa), therefore all visible forms (asesa-murtih) such as rocks (saila), oceans (abdhi), and mountains (dhara), as well as demigods, humans, and so on are false (na vastu-bhutah). The reason is that besides the Supreme Lord‘s form of consciousness, all forms are false. You should know (janihi) that differences (bhedan) like rocks and oceans are imagined (vijrmbhitani) on His form of consciousness due to ignorance of it, i.e., due to mistaken cognizance (vijnāna). The use of the neuter gender in the word ‘vijrmbhitani ‘is poetic licence taken by the sages (arsa). Someone may ask when this illusion will disappear. The next verse gives the answer: When (yada) one’s consciousness assumes its original form and becomes correct and pure. When does it become so ? When all material activities disappear (karma-ksaye) by knowledge about the truth obtained from the instructions of an authentic spiritual master and the impurity of difference has disappeared, then the fruits (phalani) of the tree of thoughts (sankalpa-taroh) in the form of difference (vastu-bhedah) no more exist among objects (vastusu). The meaning is that differences like rocks, oceans, and so on will disappear just as silver disappears by knowledge about the shell. The next verse says that rocks and so on are not factual differences, since they do not really exist. “O Maitreya (dvija), can a material object (vastu) that has eternally one form (satataika-rūpam) devoid (hinam) of beginning (adi), middle (madhya), and end (paryanta) ever exist somewhere (kutracit) ? No, it cannot». This is the meaning. As for objects like rocks, whose nature is otherwise (anyathatvam), i.e., which undergo transformation again and again (bhūyah), they do not have the mentioned characteristics. According to the Srldhara-kośa, the word ‘bhūyah ‘is used in the sense of again and again. “How (kutah) there can be reality (tattvam) in such objects (tatra)? There is not. ” This is the meaning. In the next verse, a clear explanation is given as to their being otherwise. Clay (maht) is used by a potter and becomes an earthen pot (ghatatvam). From a broken earthen pot comes a potsherd (kapalika). That becomes small pieces, which turn into dust (rajas), which becomes atoms (anuh). This happens again and again, and therefore things like rocks have a changeable nature. “Tell me (bruhi) how can people (janaih) whose spiritual knowledge (atma-niscayaih) has become stagnated (stimita) due to their material activities (sva-karma) see (alaksyate) the truth about Brahman (vastu) in this world (atra) made of things like rocks, which are repeatedly changing? They cannot. ” This is the meaning.

### Text 16

**Перевод:**

**“It is further said therein (2.12.43-44): tasman na vijnānam rte’sti kincit kvacit kadacid dvija vastu jatam, vijnānam ekam nija-karma-bheda-vibhinna-cittair bahudhabhyupetam, ‘Therefore, except for consciousness, no created thing ever exists anywhere, О brāhmaṇa. Although consciousness is only one, it appears manifold by contact with different minds according to their different kinds of activities’. jnānam viśuddham vimalam visokam asesa-lobhadi-nirasta-sangam, evam sadaikam paramah paresah sa vasudevo na yato ‘nyad asti, ‘Consciousness is pure, unalloyed, free from grief, devoid of any contact with bad qualities like greed, and eternally immutable. In this way, the soul endowed with consciousness is the venerable Vāsudeva, the Supreme Lord. There is nothing different from consciousness’.**

**Комментарий:**

It has been previously stated that the only real substance is Brahman, which consists of consciousness; everything else that is imagined due to ignorance about Brahman has no reality. In order to corroborate this again, the monist quotes the above two verses. Except for consciousness (vijnānam), no created (jatam) thing (vastu), such as rocks, exist. Someone may argue, “How to deny that created things are clearly perceived?” In response, it is said, “Consciousness is only one without a second’. ‘Just as a rope is apprehended as a snake by those who are not aware of its real form, similarly those who are not aware of the spiritual nature apprehend it as things like rocks and oceans. Thus, they are as false as the perception of a snake. This is the meaning. The next verse explains the spiritual nature. The word ‘pure ‘(viśuddham) means ‘devoid of attributes, ‘‘unalloyed ‘(vitnalam) means ‘free from any connection with a body, ‘and free from grief (visokam) means ‘devoid of any connection with internal and external senses’. Hence, it is devoid of any bad qualities. “In the way explained (evam) by me, consciousness (jnānam) is eternally immutable (ekarasam). The very same spirit soul endowed with consciousness is the venerable (paramah) Vāsudeva. There is nothing different from consciousness (yatah). Everything else is false. ”

### Text 17

**Перевод:**

**“Moreover (2.12.45-47): sad-bhava evam bhavato mayokto jnānam yatha satyam asatyam anyat, etat tu yat samvyavahara-bhutam tatrapi coktam bhuvanasritam te, ‘The real import of the scriptures have been thus explained by me to you: consciousness is real knowledge, and everything else is false. I have also spoken to you about the empirical state that belongs to this world’. yajnah pasur vahnir asesa rtvik somah surah svarga-mayas ca kamah, ity-adi-karmasrita-marga-drstam bhūr-adi-bhogas ca phalani tesam, ‘The sacrifice, the animal, the fire, all the priests, the Soma, the demigods, and the desire for heaven — all these are seen in the path of Vedic rituals, and their fruits are enjoyment on earth and in heaven’. yac caitad bhuvana-gatam māyā tavoktam sarvatra vrajati hi karma-vasya ekah, jnātvāitad dhruvam acalam sadaika-rupam tat kuryad visati hi yena vasudevam, ‘One is dependent on Vedic rituals to go anywhere within this material universe that I have described to you. Knowing this, one should adopt a means by which one merges within Vāsudeva, Who is eternal, is complete, and has only one form eternally. \***

**Комментарий:**

“In the way stated above (evam), the real import (sad-bhavah) of the śruti has been explained by me (maya) to you (bhavatah). ” What is it like ? “It is consciousness, the nature of the self Anything else (anyat), such as rocks, oceans, and mountains, is false (asatyam). In this description (tatra) about real and false, whatever was spoken by me to you (te) that belongs to this world (bhuvanasritam) pertains to the empirical state (samvyavahara-bhutam) and is just like a multitude of things seen in a dream. They are not transcendental. ” This is the meaning. In the next verse, he demonstrates that. The word ‘tesam ‘means ‘of Vedic rituals’. In the next verse, he says that the results of Vedic rituals are temporary. The word ‘etat\* refers to the enjoyment, enjoyable things, and the places attained. One goes (vrajati) to any of these places (sarvatra) now and then only on the basis of the Vedic rituals performed. Knowing (jnātvā) that such sort of fruits of Vedic rituals is temporary, one should adopt a means (kuryat) by which one merges (visati) within Vāsudeva, Who is eternal (dhruvam) and complete (acalam).

### Text 18

**Перевод:**

**“Therefore, the śruti condemns one who accepts difference and states that he will attain fear: atha yo ‘nyam devatam upaste, anyo’sav anyo ‘ham asmiti na sa veda yatha pasur evam sa devanam (Bṛhad-āraṇyaka Upaniṣad, 1.4.Ю), ‘One who worships the Supreme Soul as different and thinks ‘He is different, and I am different,’such a person does not know the truth. Thus, he is ignorant like an animal for the demigods’. yad eveha tad amutra yad amutra tad anv iha, mrtyoh sa mrtyum apnotiya iha naneva pasyati (Kaṭha Upaniṣad, 2.1.10), ‘As is the jiva, so is God. As is God, so is thejiva. One who sees both as if they were different simply attains death after death in this world’. yada hy evaisa etasminn udaram antaram kurute, atha tasya bhayam bhavati (Taittiriya Upaniṣad, 2.7), ‘When one thinks that there is even a small difference within Brahman, then one becomes fearful’. Therefore, only nondifference between the individual soul and the Supreme Soul is real (pāramārthika). It is thus proved that difference is only relative (vyavahdrika).**

**Комментарий:**

“Therefore, ” i.e., “since difference is false». The text ‘atha yo’nyam’ is from the Bṛhad-āraṇyaka Upaniṣad (1.4.10). One who worships (upaste) the Supreme Soul (devatam) as different (anyam) than oneself is ignorant like (yatha) an animal (pasuh). This is the meaning. The text ‘yad eveha ‘is from the Kaṭha Upaniṣad (2.1.10). Only one consciousness is to be known in the svarūpa of the jiva (iha) and in the svarūpa of God (amutra). One who sees (pasyati) difference (naneva) between both attains (apnoti) continuous birth and death (mrtyor mrtyum). The text ‘yada hi’ is from the Taittiriya Upaniṣad (2.7). When (yada) one thinks (kurute) that there is a small (udaram) difference (antaram) within the blissful Brahman (etasmin), then (tada) one’s fear (bhayam) of repeated birth and death arises. According to the Halayudha-kośa (871), the word ‘antara’ is used in the sense of interval, external, connection, difference, and occasion. Difference exists only in the relative platform, as stated in the Viṣṇu Purāṇa (2.12.45): etat tu yat samvyavahara-bhutam tatrapi coktam bhuvanasritam te, “Whatever was spoken by me to you that belongs to this world, that pertains to the empirical state. ”

### Text 19

**Перевод:**

**“In the śruti it is also said: tvam va aham asmi bhagavo devate aham vai tvam asi bhagavo devate (Varāha Upaniṣad, 2.34), ‘0 revered God, You are I, and I am You, О Lord’. tad yo ‘ham so’sau yo’sau so ‘ham (Aitareya Āraṇyaka, 2.2.4), ‘What I am, He is, and what He is, I am’. tat tvam asi (Chāndogya Upaniṣad, 6.8-16), ‘You are that’. From this and other passages it is to be understood that after rejecting the portions that contain contradictory qualities by exclusive and non-exclusive implication (bhaga-tyaga-laksatta), the svarūpa of the jiva and God is only one pure consciousness, which is a synonym of ’samvid ‘and ‘anubhūti, ‘as this is in conformity with the statements about nirguṇa-brahma. Sentences about nirguṇa-brahma are more important because they deal with the svarūpa of Brahman.**

**Комментарий:**

“If those of dull intellect have become satisfied neither with the proposition of the self-evident scriptural knowledge nor with the proposition of reasoning, then I shall now establish non-dualism by indirect indication. ” — With this in mind, the monist speaks the present paragraph. The text ‘tad yo ‘ham ‘is from the Aitareya Āraṇyaka (2.2.4). The term ‘bhaga-lakṣaṇāya ‘means ‘by exclusive and non-exclusive implication without rejecting the original meaning’. “Having rejected the portions containing contradictory qualities, such as being all-pervasive and atomic, the controller and the controlled, etc. ” Sentences about saguṇa-brahma are less important because they deal with qualities. This is the meaning.

### Text 20

**Перевод:**

**“As for the Supreme’s form, which the scriptures describe in some places, that is indeed only imaginary, as stated in the Rama-tāpani Upaniṣad (1.7): cin-mayasyadvitiyasya niskalasyasaririnah, upasakanam karyartham brahmano rupa-kalpana, ‘Brahman is mere consciousness without any division, without parts, and without body. The imagination of its form is just for the sake of a particular purpose for the worshippers’. The purpose for the worshippers is to gradually fix the mind in the spirit by the maxim of showing the Arundhati.**

**Комментарий:**

Someone may argue, “In the scriptures we hear that Brahman has form, such as’sat-pundarika-nayanam meghabham vaidyutambaram, ‘ so why do you say that it has no attributes ?” Here, the monist answers to this. The word ‘kvacit ‘means ‘in some places in the śruti and the smṛti’. The text ‘cin-mayasya ‘is found, in the Rama-tāpani Upaniṣad (1.7). Brahman is mere consciousness (cin-mayasya), impossible to be divided (advitiyasya), devoid of parts (niskalasya), and devoid of connection with a body, senses, and life air (asaririnah). The purpose of an imaginary form is to engage the mind in Brahman, which is mere consciousness. This is the meaning.

### Text 21

**Перевод:**

**“In this way, although the scriptures, the preceptors, and the practices taught are actually false, one becomes Brahman by them, just as one experiences factual pleasure when dreaming about union with a woman, and pain when dreaming about one’s head being cut off, although all this is false. So, in the Śrimad-Bhāgavatam (11.28.5) it is said: chaya-pratyahvayabhasa hy asanto ‘py artha-karinah, ‘Although shadow, echo, and reflection are false, they convey meaning’. Therefore, only the attributeless and non-dual Brahman in the form of pure consciousness is true. Anything else is something false and imagined to exist on it.”**

**Комментарий:**

Someone may argue, “If according to the theory that Brahman is non-dual consciousness everything different from it is false, since it is just imagined, how can the scriptures, the masters, and the practices taught by them, which are also all false, be the cause of liberation in the form of becoming Brahman?” Here, the monist responds to this. Although the scriptures, the masters, and the practices are false like silver perceived in a shell, one attains real liberation by them. An illustration is given herein: although false, dreams like embracing a woman are known to produce factual pleasure. So is the case here, as it was spoken by Lord Kṛṣṇain the Śrimad-Bhāgavatam (11.28.5), which has been previously explained. The monist then gives the sum and substance of the whole objection (pūrva-paksa) raised above, “Therefore, etc. ”

### Text 22

**Перевод:**

**In this regard, we say the following: There is certainly no fruit in non-difference. The desired fruits are the removal of ignorance and attainment of bliss. The first does not exist in non-difference, as we have previously explained. The second also does not exist there, for in that case Brahman’s nature as being devoid of attributes would be lost. Nor can it be said that the attainment of bliss is the very svarūpa of Brahman and not an attribute, for in such case the means would be useless and unnecessary. Nor does the quoted śruti give evidence that one becomes Brahman, for therein the word ‘eva ‘is used in the sense of similarity. The instruction in the Amara-kośa (3-3.10) is that the words ‘va, ‘‘vd, ‘‘yatha, ‘‘tatha, ‘‘eva, ‘and ‘evam ‘are used in the sense of similarity. Therefore, ‘brahmaiva ‘means’similar to Brahman’. Hence, it is coherent that one enters into Brahman after becoming similar to Brahman, and not otherwise. The śruti also says: niranjanah paramarh samyam upaiti (Muṇḍaka Upaniṣad, 3-1-3), “The pure soul thus attains transcendental similarity with the Supreme Lord.” yathodakaṁ suddhe śuddhaṁ asiktam tadrg eva bhavati, evam muner vijanata atma bhavati gautama (Kaṭha Upaniṣad, 2.1.15), “Just as pure water poured into pure water becomes alike to it, similarly the soul of a wise person who realises Brahman becomes alike to it, О Gautama». The smṛti also confirms: idam jfianam apasritya mama sadharmyam agatah, sarge ‘pi nopajayante pralaye na vyathanti ca (Bhagavad-gtta, 14.2): “Taking shelter in this knowledge, the living entities attain a nature similar to Mine and thus they are neither born at the time of creation nor afflicted at the time of dissolution.” In the Brahma-sūtra (4-4.21) it is also stated: bhoga-matra-samya-lingac ca, “The jiva is not equal to God because there are also indications that the similarity is only in enjoyment.”**

**Комментарий:**

“In this regard, ” i.e., “regarding this long objection, we say the following as its response and rebuttal». Both of these will be accomplished keeping in mind the above stated objections. “In non-difference, ” i.e., in the view that Brahman is non-dual pure consciousness. “Previously,” i.e., in the previous chapter, entitled ‘Vāmana’. The idea here is that by becoming blissful, Brahman’s nature as being devoid of attributes would be lost. The word ‘avdptih ‘means ‘the attainment of bliss’. The quoted śruti is: brahmavid brahmaiva bhavati (Muṇḍaka Upaniṣad, 3-2.9), ‘One who knows Brahman becomes Brahman’. The sentence about words in the sense of similarity is from the Amara-kośa (3-3-10). As the word ‘eva ‘is used in the sense of similarity, the idea that one becomes Brahman is thus removed, and it is therefore explained that one enters into Brahman by becoming similar to it. “Otherwise, ” i.e., “if it is accepted that one becomes Brahman. ” The text ‘niranjanah parāmaṁ ‘is from the Muṇḍaka Upaniṣad (3.1.3). The word ‘niranjanah ‘means ‘pure, free from maya’. The text ‘yathodakaṁ suddhe ‘is from the Kaṭha Upaniṣad (2.1.15). The word ‘tadrk ‘means ‘alike to it’. “The soul of a wise person (muneh) who meditates on Brahman and has realised Brahman (vijanatah) thus becomes similar to Brahman, just as water poured into water». This is the meaning. Similarity should be understood here as sharing the quality of freedom from maya. The verse ‘idam jnānam ‘is from the Bhagavad-gita (14.2). Liberation is said to be freedom from birth at the time of creation and freedom from affliction at the time of dissolution. The quoted aphorism is from the Brahma-sūtra (4.4.21). The word ‘na ‘(not) follows from a previous aphorism. The meaning of the sentence ‘parāmaṁ samyam upaiti ‘is not that the jiva and God have the same svarūpa and capacity. Why? Because in the sentence’so’snute sarvan kaman ‘ it is indicated that the liberated soul has similarity with God only in enjoyment.

### Text 23

**Перевод:**

**Nor can it be said that, “Non-difference between the jiva and Brahman, which is not known in this world, becomes known through the scriptures, and hence non-duality is their intended purport.” This idea is mistaken, for there are six indications that are decisive as to the purport of the scriptures being their difference, as will be ascertained in the next paragraphs, and also because their non-difference has already been previously refuted starting with the question ‘is non-duality different from Brahman or is it Brahman itself? ‘Therefore, such unknown non-difference is as unreal as the horns of a man.**

**Комментарий:**

It had been said in the middle of the first paragraph (6.1) that nondifference from Brahman, which is unknown in this world, is known only from the scriptures. This view is refuted herein. Someone may argue, “The monists have also concluded non-duality by six indications. ” In response, the author says that non-difference has already been previously refuted in the fifth chapter. The word ‘tathatvam ‘means’such unknown nondifference’.

### Text 24

**Перевод:**

**Similarly, in the sentence ’sad eva saumyedam agra asid ekam evadvitiyam, ‘ the intended meaning is that Brahman endowed with śakti is the efficient and material cause of the universe, which is expressed by the word ‘idam, ‘because it had been declared that by this single knowledge all knowledge is known, and also because this is understood from the words used there. Moreover, this sentence is no evidence of mere non-difference, for if just by the word ‘ekam ‘(one) non-difference is proved, then the other words would be useless. Nor can it be said that, “All the three words are meaningful because they refute three kinds of difference — internal difference (svagata-bheda), difference among objects belonging to the same class (sajattya-bheda), and difference among objects belonging to different classes (vijatiya-bheda)” This is a wrong conception, for non-difference has already been refuted. Therefore, other ideas are also driven far away, such as the idea that the word ‘eva ‘(only) means that anything different than Brahman has no existence, and the word ‘advitiya ‘(without a second) means that such non-existence (abhava) is unreality and has the same nature as its substratum, Brahman. Moreover, even by this idea your view is not proved, for by accepting non-existence (abhava), the existence of a principle second to Brahman is admitted. Even if it is unreality, anything second to Brahman must be based on it. According to others, this idea is mistaken because if non-existence has the same nature as its substratum, then Brahman must also be unreality, and also because nobody has experience that if there is no pot on the floor, the substratum (the floor) must be on the floor. Therefore, this idea is insignificant.**

**Комментарий:**

In the Chāndogya Upaniṣad (6.1.3) it had been said: yenasrutam srutam bhavaty amatam matam avijnatam vijnatam, “The instruction by which the unheard becomes heard, the unthinkable becomes thinkable, and the unknown becomes known.” Because by this single knowledge all knowledge becomes known, the Supreme is the material cause (upadana). It is further said: tad aiksata bahu syarh prajayeyeti tat tejo’srjata (ib., 6.2.3), “The Supreme Lord thought, ‘Let Me become many. Let Me create the living beings’. He then created fire’. ‘From the used words, such as’srjati ‘(creates), the Supreme is also understood as the efficient cause. “This sentence,” i.e., the sentence’sad eva saumya’. The words ‘tat-siddhau ‘mean ‘in proving non-difference’. “The other words, ” i.e., the words ‘eva ‘and ‘advitiyam ‘would be useless. Sajatiya-bheda is difference from things that belong to the same class, such as from the individual souls. Vijatiya-bheda is difference from things that belong to different classes, such as from material nature. Svagata-bheda is known as internal difference, such as from one’s own qualities. It cannot be said that the three words (ekam, eva, advitiyam) convey meanings that deny all these three kinds of difference, for non-difference has already been refuted in the fifth chapter, entitled ‘Vāmana’. “Therefore,” i.e., “since non-difference has already been refuted. ” The non-existence of anything different than Brahman refers to unreality like a flower in the sky. “The same nature as its substratum, ” i.e., “the same nature as Brahman». Thus, by all these means, only non-duality is established. Yet, since non-duality has already been previously refuted, these means are all useless. This is the meaning. By the word ‘advitiya ‘it is imagined that Brahman is the shelter of the non-existence of anything different from itself, and it is also imagined that anything non-existent is unreality. “Even by this idea, your view that Brahman is non-dual consciousness without attributes is not proved.” “Of a principle second to Brahman, ” i.e., “of non-existence, which is different than Brahman’. ‘Since it is perceived that the substratum of non-existence is actually existence, the flaw is thus presented by their own monistic philosophy. The author then points out the flaw according to the Nyāya philosophy. If there is no pot on the floor, there is no perception that there is a floor on the floor. If Brahman is of the nature of non-existence, then it is void. This is the flaw in the monist’s argument as pointed out by others, such as the logicians — the followers of Nyāya. It should be understood that according to the author’s view, although Brahman is the substratum of non-existence, Brahman possesses non-existence on the strength of ‘viśeṣa’. “This idea, ” i.e., the idea that the word ‘advitiya ‘has these two meanings.

### Text 25

**Перевод:**

**The śruti ‘neha nānāsti kincana ‘ denies the difference perceived among the quadruple expansions of the Supreme. Although there is non-difference, They are perceived as different. This is stated by the śruti: eko ‘pi san bahudha yo ‘vabhati (Gopāla-tāpani Upaniṣad, 1.19), “Although one, Kṛṣṇa appears as many.” ekam san tam bahudha drsyamanam, “Although one, the Supreme Lord is seen as many.” Or else, this is a denial of difference between the Lord and His attributes. The śruti also says: yathodakaṁ durge vṣṛṭam parvatesu vidhavati, evam dharmān prthak paśyaṁs tan evanuvidhavati (Kaṭha Upaniṣad, 2.1.14), “Just as the rain water on the hills runs down into impassable places, similarly one who sees the Lord‘s attributes as different from Him falls down.” Nor is it denied that there are manifold knowers, known, and knowledge, for the śruti does propound the three of them.**

**Комментарий:**

Here the author explains the meaning of the śruti ‘neha nānāsti kincana’. The quadruple expansions of the Supreme are Vāsudeva, Sankarsana, Pradyumna, and Aniruddha. The meaning of this sentence is that either the perception of difference among Them, or between Them and Their attributes is denied here. The author then points out the flaw in the meaning of this sentence according to others ‘opinion, “Nor is it denied, etc.” The word ‘tasya ‘refers to the existence of knower, known, and knowledge. The idea is that the śruti would be insane if it denies the same thing that it propounds in sentences like: tam atmastham ye‘nupasyanti dhirah (Kaṭha Upaniṣad, 2.5.13), “The wise see the Supreme Soul situated within oneself. ” etaj jneyam nityam evatma-samstham (Śvetāśvatara Upaniṣad, 1.12), “The eternal Supreme Soul within oneself should be known. ”

### Text 26

**Перевод:**

**In śruti passages like ‘yatra hi dvaitam ‘ difference is not denied, for it is eternally existent. Moreover, according to the śruti, knowledge about the difference between God and the living entity is the cause of liberation: prthag atmanam preritaram ca matva justas tatas tenamrtatvam eti (Śvetāśvatara Upaniṣad, 1.6), “Considering oneself and the Supreme Ruler as different, one then becomes pleased and thus attains immortality.” justamyadapasyaty anyam Isam asyamahimanam iti vita-sokah (ib., 4.7% “When one sees that the blissful Supreme Lord is different than oneself and understands His greatness, one then becomes free from sorrow.”**

**Комментарий:**

In the first paragraph of this chapter, four passages from the śruti were quoted, starting with ‘yatra hi dvaitam, ‘on the basis of which the monist assumes that difference is denied as an imagination. Here, the author refutes this idea and then quotes the Śvetāśvatara Upaniṣad as to the reason why difference is real. Considering (matva) oneself (atmanam) and the Supreme Ruler (preritaram) as different (prthak), understanding the difference between both as the ruled and the ruler, the atomic and the all-pervading, the servant and the master, then (tatah) the worshipper becomes pleased (justah) by this difference (tena) and attains immortality (amrtatvam). The meaning of the text ‘justam yada ‘is clear. The words ‘taj-jhanasya ‘mean ‘of the knowledge about difference’. The purport here is that on account of eternal attributes like being atomic and being all-pervading, the difference between the individual soul and God is real, and knowledge about this is the cause of liberation. If difference were unreal, then the śruti would not declare it as a cause of liberation. The śruti certainly does not state that cognizance of silver in a shell is a cause of liberation. Someone may argue, “In this case, cognizance of the shell must be a cause of liberation, since that is real. ” It is not so, for the scriptures make no such statement either. In the Mahā-bhārata (12.306.74) it is declared by Yajnavalkya how knowledge about difference is a cause of liberation: yada tu pasyate ‘nyo ‘ham anya esa iti dvija, tada sa kevali-bhūtah sad-vimsam anupasyati, “O brāhmaṇa, when one sees that ‘I am someone, and God is someone else, ‘then being pure, one sees the twenty-sixth element, the Supreme Soul. ”

### Text 27

**Перевод:**

**Rather, the meaning of the quoted śruti is as follows: Since its sustenance is dependent on Brahman, this universe is said to consist of Brahman, just like speech and the other senses are said to consist of life air, since their sustenance and functions depend on the life air. As stated in the Chāndogya Upaniṣad (5.1.15): na vai vaco na caksumsi na srotrani na manamsity acaksate prana ity evacaksate prano hy evaitani sarvani bhavati, “The wise do not call the senses as speech, eyes, ears, or mind. They call them ‘prana’. Prana actually becomes all the senses. ”**

**Комментарий:**

Having refuted the above raised objection, the author now explains the meaning of the śruti statements according to his own view and gives evidence of it. Speech and the other senses are not designated by the word ’speech ‘and so on but rather by the word ‘prana,’since their functions are dependent on prana. Just as the senses are a form of prana, the universe is a form of Brahman.

### Text 28

**Перевод:**

**The universe is also said to consist of Brahman due to its resting on Brahman or on account of its being pervaded by it, as stated in the smṛti: anyas ca paramo rajams tathanyah panca-vimsakah, tat-sthatvad anupasyanti hy eka eveti sadhavah (Mahā-bhārata, 12.306.75), “O King, the Supreme Lord is one, and the twenty-fifth element, the jiva, is someone else. Because the Supreme Lord is situated within the jiva, the sages see both as one.” sarvam samapnosi tato’si sarvah (Bhagavad-gita, 11.40), “You pervade everything, hence You are everything.”**

**Комментарий:**

Here the author explains two other meanings for the quoted śruti. The verse ‘anyas ca ‘is from the Moksa-dharma section of the Mahā-bhārata (12.306.75) and was spoken by Yajnavalkya to Visvavasu, the king of the Gandharvas. The word ‘paramah ‘means ‘the Supreme Lord Hari, ‘and ‘panca-vimsakah, ‘means ‘the twenty-fifth element, the jiva’. The words ‘tat-sthatvat ‘mean ‘on account ofthejiva’s resting on the Supreme Lord, ‘and ‘ekah ‘means ‘non-different from the Supreme Lord’. The verse ’sarvam samapnosi ‘is from the Bhagavad-gita (11.40) and was spoken by Arjuna. The word ’samapnosi ‘means ‘You pervade’. “Hence (tatah) You are everything (sarvam), everything is said to consist of You». This is the meaning.

### Text 29

**Перевод:**

**Similarly, in the worldly stage (yatra), due to ignorance of the truth the ignorant soul is as if independent (dvaitam iva). One (itarah) then sees (pasyati) oneself as different (itaram), i.e., being as if independent, one understands oneself as independent. However, when due to the mercy of the scriptures and the preceptors one’s ignorance is destroyed, and by constant meditation, one realises the Supreme and becomes His companion by the mercy of His svarūpa-śakti, then whom (kam) would one see and through what (kena)7. One would actually see only the Supreme Lord by divine eyes. This is the meaning.**

**Комментарий:**

Having explained the above three meanings, the author here substantiates the meaning of the sentence ‘yatra hi’. The words ‘tattvajnānat ‘mean ‘by not understanding that the universe consists of Brahman because its sustenance is dependent on it, either because it rests on it or because it is pervaded by it’. “When, ” i.e., “at the stage of knowledge, or at the stage of liberation». One whose ignorance has been destroyed is one whose conception of oneself as independent has been destroyed and to whom the above three attributes have become manifest. By constant meditation and by the mercy of hlādini and samvit, which are the essence of the Supreme Lord‘s svarūpa-śakti and which act according to His will in conformity with the mood of the devotee, such a devotee attains a body fit to serve the Lord. Then, lacking material body and senses, through which material eyes would one see others such as relatives ? One would not see anything by any such means. This is the meaning. Rather, one would see the Supreme Lord by divine eyes.

### Text 30

**Перевод:**

**The śruti also states the same thing: yam evaisa vrnute tena labhyas tasyaisa atma vivrnute tanum svam (Kaṭha Upaniṣad, 1.2.23), “The Supreme Lord is attained only by one whom He chooses. He makes one’s original transcendental body become manifest.” prayujyamane mayi tam śuddhaṁ bhagavatim tanum, arabdha-karma-nirvano nyapatat panca-bhautikah (Srimad-Bhdgavatam, 1.6.28), “As I was being endowed with a pure, divine body, my body made of five material elements was destroyed and the prarabdha-karma connected to it was extinguished.” adatte hari-hastena hari-drstyaiva pasyati, gacchec ca hari-padena muktasyaisa sthitir bhavet, “One then receives things with Lord Hari’s hands, sees with Lord Hari’s eyes, and moves with Lord Hari’s feet. This is the position of a liberated soul.” Otherwise, the word ’sarva ‘(everything) would be restricted.**

**Комментарий:**

Here the author quotes the Kaṭha Upaniṣad to illustrate that one realises the Supreme Lord‘s body and then attains a divine body by His will. The Supreme Soul (esa) is attained (labhyah) only (eva) by a living entity (tena) that desires liberation and engages in sadhana, whom (yam) He chooses (vrnute). How is He attained ? He reveals His own body and gives one’s (tasya) original (svam) transcendental body (tanum) according to His will. The author then quotes two verses from the smṛti. The verse ‘prayujyamane mayi ‘is from the Śrimad-Bhāgavatam (1.6.28) and was spoken by Nārada Muni. “As I (mayi) was being endowed (prayujyamane) or carried towards a divine (bhagavatim) body (tanum), etc.” In the next verse it is said that one receives things with Lord Hari’s hands, etc., because everything in enacted by the essence of Lord Hari’s svarūpa-śakti, which is engaged according to His will. It is also said in the Śrimad-Bhāgavatam (3.9.11): tvam bhakti-yoga-paribhavita-hrt-saroja asse sruteksita-patho nanu natha pumsam, yad-yad-dhiya ta urugaya vibhavayanti tat-tad-vapuh pranayase sad-anugrahaya, "O Lord, You are situated within the lotus heart of one who engages in devotional service. The path towards You is actually perceived through the ears of men. By whatever way they meditate upon You, that same form You display to them in order to show Your mercy towards the devotees, О glorious Lord. ” “Otherwise, ” i.e., “if it is accepted that at the stage of liberation one becomes non-different than Brahman devoid of attributes». In this case, the meaning of the word’sarvam ‘(everything) in the sentence ‘yatra tv asya sarvam atmaivabhūt ‘would be restricted, for if that were the intended meaning, it would suffice to say that ‘one then becomes spirit’. This is the idea.

### Text 31

**Перевод:**

**The meaning of the sentence ‘vacarambhanam vikarah ‘ is that the effect, the universe, is non-different from the cause, Brahman. Vyāsadeva, the revered compiler of the Brahma-sūtra, similarly states: tad-anyatvam arambhana-śabdadibhyah (2.1.14),“ The non-difference between the Supreme Lord and the universe is understood from the passage starting with ‘vacarambhanam.” ‘In this aphorism it is also demonstrated that the universe is real like Brahman. Otherwise, there would be no difference between real and unreal.**

**Комментарий:**

The universe is non-different (anyatvam) from Brahman (tat) endowed with energies like pradhana and the jiva. Wherefrom is this understood ?From sentences like ‘vacarambhanam vikarah, etc’. The words ‘tatra ca ‘mean ‘also in the quoted aphorism’. “Otherwise, ” i.e., “if it is accepted that the universe is false like silver perceived in a shell». In this case, there would be no difference between the real, Brahman, and the unreal, the universe.

### Text 32

**Перевод:**

**The meaning of the sentence ‘ito ‘nyad artam ‘ is that the other — the universe — is distressed, as understood from the Bhagavad-gita (7.16): arto jijnasur artharthi jnani ca, “One who is distressed (artah), one who is inquisitive, one who desires wealth, and one who is wise worship Me.” This whole universe is distressed, as stated in the Śrimad-Bhāgavatam (10.14.22): tasmad idam jagad a&esam asat-svarūpam svapnabham asta-dhisanam puru-duhkha-duhkham, “Therefore, this whole universe has a mutable nature like a dream, is devoid of knowledge, and is replete with great distress.” The meaning of the sentence ‘enam brahma-lokam ‘ is that an unpleasant and untrue statement that is harsh, deceitful, and opposite to spiritual engagement is called ‘anrtam ‘(false). Because the living entities are covered (pratyūdhah) by such falsehood, they do not know (vidanti) the supreme abode (brahma-lokam). The word ‘rtam ‘actually means a truthful statement, as explained by Lord Kṛṣṇain the Śrimad-Bhāgavatam (11.19.37): rtam ca sunrta van!, “Truthfulness means truthful speech.” Speech different than that is untruthful (anrtam).**

**Комментарий:**

The verse ‘tasmad idam ‘is from the Śrimad-Bhāgavatam (10.14.22) and was spoken by Lord Brahma. The words ‘asat-svarūpam ‘mean ‘whose nature is mutable, ‘or ‘dependent’. As stated in the smṛti: sattvarh svatantryam uddistam, “The word ’sattvam ‘is explained as independence.” The word ‘pratyudhah ‘means ‘covered ‘or ’seized’. According to the Amara-kośa (1.5.19), the word ’sūnrta ‘is used in the sense of real and dear. The verse ‘rtam ca sūnrta ‘is from the Śrimad-Bhāgavatam (11.19-37).

### Text 33

**Перевод:**

**Similarly, it cannot be said that the lotus eyed Lord Kṛṣṇa teaches monism in the verse ‘gunah srjanti, ‘ for by establishing that even His own identity as the Supreme Lord is false, His being an instructor would not be possible, and this would also contradict His own statements in previous and subsequent verses. It is read in this same scripture that on account of aversion towards the Supreme Lord as the highest truth, the jiva transmigrates in the material world, while by turning towards Him one’s transmigration stops.**

**Комментарий:**

Having refuted the objection raised by the opponent while explaining the śruti, the author then presented his own view. Now, presenting his own view while explaining the smṛti, he points the flaws in the opponent’s objection. “In this same scripture, ” i.e., in the eleventh book of the Śrimad-Bhāgavatam.

### Text 34

**Перевод:**

**Therefore, the meaning is as follows: Lord Kṛṣṇa says “Observing the world as transient, performing detached work with the intention of instructing people and purifying the heart, properly observing rules and regulations, having known the three elements — God, the jiva, and material nature — by serving a preceptor who knows Me, after due discrimination by spiritual knowledge acquired by serving a preceptor, one whose mind is concentrated on My form gives up the cycle of birth and death. I have previously taught My view in this way, starting with the verse ‘mayoditesv avahitah ‘(Śrimad-Bhāgavatam, 11.10.1)” Then, in order to clarify this conclusion, other opposing philosophical views are refuted in twenty verses.**

**Комментарий:**

Here the author provides the meaning of the quoted verse according to the Vaiṣṇava conclusion. The idea here is that while properly observing rules and regulations, a devotee’s intentness is not on them but only on bhakti. The three elements are God, the jiva, and material nature. Time and karma are implied. To have discriminated between these elements means to have distinguished God and oneself from them. The compound ‘pranihita-mad-rūpah ‘means ‘one who is concentrated on My eternal form’. The words ‘tad-upasattih ‘mean’ service unto a preceptor’. Opposing views are then refuted by Lord Kṛṣṇa in order to clarify and give perfect knowledge about the philosophical conclusion taught by Him.

### Text 35

**Перевод:**

**Among those verses, by seventeen verses starting with ‘athaisam karma-kartfnam ‘(Śrimad-Bhāgavatam, 11.10.14), the Lord says “Having Myself explained the philosophy of those who became dull by performing Vedic rituals, I pointed out the flaw in it. Then, resorting to the Sāṁkhya philosophy by one verse starting with ‘gunah srjanti karmani’ (ib., ll.10.31), I refute part of that view according to which the jivas are themselves doers and enjoyers. According to Sāṁkhya, there are manifold independent puruṣas. Resorting to the philosophy that everything is mere non-dual consciousness, I refute that view with one and a half verse starting with ‘yavat syad guna-vaisamyam ‘(ib., 11.10.32)” Then, by the next half verse starting with ‘ya etat samupasiran, ‘the Lord Himself refutes these three philosophical views as spurious.**

**Комментарий:**

Among those twenty verses, through seventeen verses starting with ‘athaisam karma-kartfnam ‘the Supreme Lord Himself brings up the philosophical view of those who became dull by performing Vedic rituals and then by His own reasoning He refutes it. Part of the view accepted by the dull followers of Karma-mimamsa is then condemned by the Supreme Lord by resorting to the Sathkhya philosophy. This is the connection between the words of the sentence. The verse ‘gunah srjanti karmani ‘is in accordance with the Sāṁkhya philosophy, and its meaning is as follows: The word ‘gunah ‘refers to the three modes of nature — goodness, passion, and ignorance. According to the Medini-kośa (Na, 20), the word ‘guna ‘is used in the sense of bow-string, secondary, sense objects like colour, cook, abandonment, qualities like heroism, political expedients like conciliation, modes of nature such as goodness, repetition, and rope. The modes of nature with their combined functions create (srjanti) material activities (karmani), and not the soul. Someone may argue, “It is experienced that the senses perform activities». In response it is said that the false-ego (gunah) creates (anusrjate) the senses (indriyani). Thus, the doership of material activities that pertain to the senses created by the false-ego, which is a byproduct of the modes of nature, actually belongs to the modes of nature. This is the meaning. Someone may argue, “The state of being a doer and enjoyer belongs only to the soul itself, since both of these have only one basis». According to the philosophical conclusion of the followers of Sāṁkhya, the living entity (jivah) endowed (samyuktaḥ) with material senses (indriyaih) and not in its pure state, enjoys (bhuhkte) the fruits of its activities (karma-phalani), and thus even the state of being an enjoyer belongs only to the modes of nature, since it is caused by them. Haifa verse starting with ‘yavat syad guna-vaisamyam ‘(ib., 11.10.32) should be considered as previously explained. By this conclusion accepted by those who propound non-dual consciousness without attributes, the Sāṁkhya view that there are manifold independent puruṣas has been condemned and refuted by the Supreme Lord. This is the connection between the words. Then, the Lord Himself points out the flaws in these three philosophical views. Those who have accepted (samupastran) the views of the dull followers of Karma-mimdmsa, Sāṁkhya, and Advaita are tied with sorrow (sucarpitah) caused by these philosophies, since they are manifested due to misconceptions, and thus they plunge (muhyanti) in the pool of repeated birth and death. This is the meaning.

### Text 36

**Перевод:**

**In this way, the concocted secret according to the view that at jivas are one, as described in the third paragraph of this chapter, has also been refuted. This is also corroborated by the Kaṭha Upaniṣad (2.2.13) nityo nityanam cetanas cetananam eko bahunam yo vidadhati kaman, “There is one eternal being among many eternal beings, one sentient being among many sentient beings, Who fulfils their desires.” This being so, the interpretation of the śruti ‘jlvesav abhasena ‘ conforms with the imagined concept of God and the jiva as per the Nyāya doctrine, and not what is established by the śruti, for in the śruti it is taught that the jiva is a minute consciousness endowed with qualities like eternity and knowledge, while God’s svarūpa has the form of bliss and all-pervading consciousness endowed with qualities like eternity and knowledge.**

**Комментарий:**

“In this way, ” i.e., “these three philosophical views, which are manifested due to misconceptions, having been thus refuted by the Lord.” The meaning is that the concocted secret in the view that all jivas are one, according to the monists ‘interpretation of two and a half verses starting with ‘gunah srjanti, ‘has also been refuted. That is also refuted by the Kaṭha Upaniṣad ‘nityo nityanam, etc. 9 God is one (ekah) eternal (nityah) sentient being (cetanah) among many (bahunam) eternal (nityanam) sentient beings (cetananam), Who fulfils (vidadhati) their desires (kaman). The word ‘tau ‘refers to God and the jiva. In the Nyāya doctrine it is accepted that God and the jiva are insentient, all-pervading like the sky, and are the substratum of knowledge. This concoction of the logicians bewildered by the Lord‘s illusory energy is contradicted by the śruti: avidyayam antare vartamanah svayam dhirah panditam manyamanah, dandramyamanah pariyanti mudha andhenaiva niyamana yathandhah (Kaṭha Upaniṣad, 1.2.5), “Revolving in ignorance but considering themselves learned, fools move around in circles, just as blind men led by the blind». The author then demonstrates the view of the śruti as regards to God and the jiva, “For in the śruti it is taught, etc. ”

### Text 37

**Перевод:**

**Similarly, it is not correct to assume that in the verses starting with ‘kim bhadram ‘ the Supreme Lord says that this dualistic material manifestation, which is made of His own energy, is false, for this would contradict other statements in the śruti, such as: ya eko varno bahudha śakti-yogad varnan anekan nihitartho dadhati (Śvetāśvatara Upaniṣad, 4.1), “The Supreme Lord is one unique category. Having a particular purpose, He creates manifold varieties of categories by engaging His energy.” Moreover, the Lord Himself accepts the view that there are twenty-six elements, and before and after the instruction given in the quoted verses, it is explained that the universe is a form of His. As per the opponents ‘view, the Lord also could not possibly be an instructor at all.**

**Комментарий:**

The word ‘asau ‘refers to the Supreme Lord with lotus eyes. The quoted text from the Śvetāśvatara Upaniṣad states that God creates the universe through His own energy. If the universe were false, the meaning of this śruti would be contradictory. The word ’svayam ‘means ‘by the Lord Himself’. According to the view that there are twenty-six elements, God and the jiva are different, and thus duality is real’. Before the instruction given in the quoted verses, ‘i.e., the verses starting with ‘kim bhadram’. The word ‘tasya ‘means ‘of the dualistic material manifestation’. If the monists ‘view is accepted, then the Supreme Lord being false, He could not be an instructor at all. This is the main fault in their philosophy.

### Text 38

**Перевод:**

**This had been explained before in verses like: visvam ekatmakam pasyan prakrtya purusena ca (Śrimad-Bhāgavatam, 11.28.1), “ Observing that this universe, which is effected by the Supreme Person and material nature, is non-different from Him and His energy, etc.” It is also explained subsequently in verses like: jnānam viveko nigamas tapas ca pratyaksam aitihyam athanumanam, ady-antayor asya yad eva kevalam kalas ca hetus ca tad eva madhye, yatha hiranyam sukrtam purastat pascac ca sarvasya hiran-mayasya, tad eva madhye vyavaharyamanam nanapadesair aham asya tadvat (ib., 11.28.18), “Discrimination is knowledge, and its means is the Vedas, penance, personal experience, descending instruction, and logic. That which exists alone in the beginning, in the middle, and in the end is the Supreme Lord, the cause of everything. Just as gold well manufactured in the form of bracelets and so on is merely gold before and after the destruction of all such golden ornaments, and in the middle it is conventionally dealt with by various names such as ‘bracelet,’ similarly, in the beginning, middle, and end, this universe is I Myself endowed with My energies, Who am thus designated in such-and-such way.”**

**Комментарий:**

Observing (pasyan) this universe (visvam), which is effected by material nature (prakrtya) and the Supreme Person (purusena), as non-different (ekatmakam) from God endowed with His energies, and knowing the principle taught in the text ‘ya eko varnah’, one should neither praise nor reproach the nature of others, such as peaceful, aggressive or foolish deeds. This is the connection with the words of the previous line of the verse. The meaning is that knowing this world as a creation of God, being a mixture of Him and material nature, one should give up praise and reproach. Discrimination (vivekah) is knowledge (jnānam). This is the order of the words. The means of knowledge is then stated: The word ‘nigamah ‘means ‘the Vedas/ Penance (tapaḥ) is to adopt some bodily affliction as described in the Vedas. The word ‘pratyaksam ‘means ‘personal experience’. The word ‘aitihyam9 means ‘instruction descending from the disciplic succession. The word ‘anumanam9 means ‘logic/ The result is then stated: That (tat) which (yat) exists alone (kevalam) in the beginning (adi), in the middle (madhye), and in the end (anta). What is that ? That which reveals (kalayati) is called ‘kalah, ‘the Supreme Lord. The word ‘hetuh ‘means ‘the cause’. The material cause (upadana) is characterized by material nature and the living entities. Kala and karma are the instrumental causes of the universal transformations. The result is knowledge, the discrimination that this universe is non-different from God endowed with His energies, which is attained by means of the Vedas and so on. Lord Kṛṣṇa then gives an illustration: “Just as gold (hiranyam) manufactured (sukrtam) in the form of bracelets and so on is merely gold before (purastat) and after (pascat) the destruction of all (sarvasya) such golden (hiran-mayasya) ornaments, and in the middle (madhye) it is conventionally dealt with (vyavaharyamanam) by various names (nanapadesaih) such as ‘bracelet’ similarly (tadvat), in the beginning, middle, and end, this universe is I (aham) Myself endowed with My energies, Who am thus designated in such-and-such way." Here, both previously and subsequently, it is said that the universe is non-different from God endowed with His energies.

### Text 39

**Перевод:**

**Moreover, two other views will also be refuted by Lord Kṛṣṇa Himself at the end of His instruction on the truth: one according to which the universe is Brahman itself when perplexed, thus duality is mixed together like the grains in a pot of cooked rice, and hence its eternity is not contradictory; and the other according to which being an imposition, the universe is unreal like silver in a shell. In the Śrimad-Bhāgavatam (11.28.36-37), He says: etavan atma-sammoho yad vikalpas tu kevale, atman rte svam atmanam avalambo na yasya hi, yan namakrtibhir grahyam panca-varnam abadhitam, vyarthenapy artha-vado ‘yam dvayam pandita-maninam, “As Brahman is devoid of attributes, the illusion that the spirit is a material manifestation is a mere mistake. Without the Supreme Lord, Whose powers are extraordinary, the jiva cannot be the support of this universe. Duality in the form of the five Material elements and apprehended by names and forms is not false. The statement of a non-dual meaning through a fallacious inference is made by those who merely judge themselves learned.” The word ‘vikalpah ‘means ‘mistake’.**

**Комментарий:**

Here the author says that the monist interpretation given by you (the opponent) on the verses starting with ‘kim bhadram ‘is not intended by Lord Kṛṣṇa, for that is refuted by Him at the end. Resorting to the apparent meaning of śruti statements like’sa eva maya-parimohitatma sariram asthaya karoti sarvam, ‘ monists describe that just like a fisherman who is actually the perplexed son of a king, the jiva is actually Brahman itself when perplexed. With half a verse starting with ‘etavan atma-sammohah, ‘Lord Kṛṣṇapoints out the fault in this interpretation Brahman being immutable consciousness devoid of attributes (kevale), the idea that ‘I am a material manifestation ‘is said to be a mere mistake (vikalpah). The meaning is that this is the ignorance of one who speaks so. It is not possible to say that there is any mistake in such Brahman. Resorting to the apparent meaning of śruti statements like ‘ajam ekam, ‘ the followers of Sāṁkhya philosophy describe that just as by being moistened and soft, the grains in a pot of cooked rice become mutually connected, similarly, being connected with material nature, multiple jivas become doers and enjoyers. Lord Kṛṣṇa points out the fault in this view with half a verse starting with ‘atman rte’. Without (rte) the Supreme Lord (atmanam), Who has extraordinary (svam) powers such as turning all wishes true, the jiva (ātmani) cannot be the support (avalambah) of the transformations in the form of the material manifestation (yasya). The jivas are said to be the support of the universe on account of its being made by material nature, which becomes conscious by the reflection of the jivas. However, the jivas have no reflection, since they have no form. Nor can they have energy and omniscience, for this is not heard from the scriptures. Thus, the jivas cannot be the support of the universe. Resorting to the apparent meaning of smṛti statements such as ‘rajjvam aher bhoga-bhavabhavau yatha, ‘certain monists imagine that just as silver is imposed on a shell by those who are not aware of the nature of the shell, similarly the universe is imposed on Brahman by those who are not aware of the nature of Brahman, hence the universe is false just as silver. Lord Kṛṣṇa points out the fault in this by the next verse starting with ‘yan namakrtibhih’. The duality in the form of the five categories (panca-varnam) of material elements — earth, water, fire, air, and ether — apprehended by names and forms is made of the energy of the real Supreme Lord. This is not false, since it is proved by evidences. By the fallacious inference (vyarthena) that the universe is false because it is an imposition on Brahman, just like silver on a shell, those who propound such meaning merely judge themselves learned (pandita-maninam), while actually they are not.

### Text 40

**Перевод:**

**Therefore, the meaning here is this: It had been previously taught by Lord Kṛṣṇa that devotional service to Him is the cause to attain Him. To encourage one to give up praise and reproach of others, which are unfavourable to devotional service, starting with the words ‘kim bhadram ‘the Lord teaches how the universe is changeable and dependent. The word ‘avastunah ‘means ‘changeable’. In the Viṣṇu Purāṇa it is said: yat tu kalantarenapi nanya-samjnam upaiti vai, parinamadi-sambhutam tad vastu nrpa tac ca kim (2.13.№0)\> “O King, what is that thing which in the course of time does not change its designation nor has transformation, form, and name?” anasi paramarthas ca prajnair abhyupagamyate, tat tu nasi na sandeho nasi-dravyopapaditam (2.14.24), “That is the imperishable and spiritual truth which is attained by the wise. Without a doubt, what is produced by changeable material elements is changeable.” This duality is called ‘anrta’. An unpleasant and untrue statement that is harsh, deceitful, and opposite to spiritual engagement is called ‘anrta ‘(false). So is that which is perceived by the mind and expressed by words and thus understood as real. This is stated by Lord Kṛṣṇa in the words ‘vacoditam tad anrtam, etc’. The apparent meaning that ‘dvaitam ‘(duality) is false is not possible here. As stated in the Viṣṇu Purāṇa (1.4.19): yat kincin manasa grahyam yad grahyam caksur-adibhih, buddhya ca yat paricchinnam tad rūpam akhilam tava, “Whatever is apprehended by the mind, perceived by the senses, and ascertained by the intelligence, that is all a form of Yours.” Therefore, praise of the material manifestation is meaningless. Dependence is also duality. With the words ‘chaya-pratyahvayabhasah, etc’. the Lord says that any result from that is not spiritual. Rather, it consequentially produces bondage. The word ‘asantah ‘means ‘dependent’. As stated in the smṛti: sattvam svatantryam udistam tac ca krsne na capare, asvatantryat tad anyesam asattvam viddhi bharata, “The word’sattvam ‘is explained as independence, and it applies only to Lord Kṛṣṇaand no one else. О descendent of Bharata, you should know that everything else is called ‘asattvam,’since they are dependent.” Therefore, reproach of the material manifestation is meaningless.**

**Комментарий:**

In this way, having refuted the objection that has been raised, now the author provides the meaning of those verses according to his own view. The words ‘tat-pratikulayoh ‘mean ‘unfavourable to bhakti’. The word ‘tasya ‘means ‘of the universe’. The author then explains that according to the verse of the Viṣṇu Purāṇa (2.13.IOO) ‘yat tu kalantarenapi, etc., ‘the word ‘avastu ‘means ‘changeable’. The word ‘anasi ‘means ‘devoid of change, form, and name’. Material activities and the universe created by them are all changeable (nasi). That which is perceived by the mind and expressed by words as such is ‘anrta, ‘i.e., devoid of truthful and pleasant statements. The apparent meaning that ‘dvaitam ‘(duality) is false is not possible here. In this connection, author quotes a verse from the Viṣṇu Purāṇa (1.4.19) that opposes the idea that duality is false, as spoken by the earth personified to Lord Varāha. Therefore, since the material manifestation is changeable, its praise is meaningless. Having thus demonstrated the changeable nature of duality, the author states its dependence. It is described in the verse ‘chaya-pratyahvayabhasah ‘that shadow, echo, and reflection are dependent, as demonstrated by direct perception. By quoting a verse from the Mahā-bhārata, the author then explains that the word ‘asantah ‘is used in this verse in the sense of ‘dependence’. The words ‘tad-bhinnanam ‘refer to everything that is different from Kṛṣṇa, both sentient and insentient.

### Text 41

**Перевод:**

**By one and a half verse starting with ‘atmaiva tad idam, ‘Lord Kṛṣṇa adds that the dependence of the material manifestation is due to its being made of His (God’s) energy. The Supreme Lord (prabhuh) is capable on account of His union with various energies. Then, does He undergo transformation? No. The Lord says, “anyasmat, etc.” Even upon assuming the state of creator and created, by the greatness of His inconceivable svarūpa, the Lord does not undergo transformation whatsoever. This is the meaning of the sentence that there is no state other than Him (anyasmad any ah). This is indeed our view. The threefold (tri-vidha) modes of nature or threefold states — adhyatma, adhibhūta, and adhidaiva — that appear (bhatih) in the living entity (ātmani) are described (nirupita) by the ignorant as being merely caused by their activities. This idea is baseless (nirmula) and there is no evidence of it at all. Lord Kṛṣṇa then clarifies that the universe is made of His (God’s) energy, ‘idam guna-mayam’. By the next verse, it is dear that by praising and reproaching others, one’s inclination towards devotional service is lost. By avoiding both praise and reproach and by the increasing glare of bhakti, one becomes like the sun (suryavat). In the next verse, the Lord says, “The creation and destruction of this universe is dependent (asat) on Me. Understanding this by direct perception, inference, and the scriptures, one should act without attachment to it.” The meaning is that such a person never loses the inclination towards devotional service. Similarly, in the verse ‘amulam etat, ‘ the affix V is used in the sense that this world is not praiseworthy, just as in the sentence ‘akesa vanita ‘(a woman whose hair is bad). Aversion towards God is actually the root cause of the mind and so on, and that is not praiseworthy. In this way, the subsequent collection of verses should also be interpreted as a description of the svarūpa of God and of the jiva, and as dealing with liberation caused by surrender unto the Supreme Lord.**

**Комментарий:**

The word ‘tasya ‘means ‘of the material manifestation’. Regarding the statement ‘on account of His union with various energies, ‘one should follow the śruti ‘ya eko varno bahudha śakti-yogat’. “By the greatness of His inconceivable svarūpa, ” i.e., by the strength of His svarūpa, which has powers of omniscience and fulfilling all desires. The meaning is that the Lord does not undergo transformation, because creation is accomplished by His mere will. “By the ignorant, ” i.e., by the dull atheistic followers of Karma-mimamsa’. They think that by their own activities, the jivas create this universe made of fourteen planetary systems, which is their place of enjoyment. There is no evidence that the material manifestation is merely caused by the activities of the jivas. The wise say that according to the scriptures, the universe is caused by God along with His energies. This is stated by quoting the verse ‘idath guna-mayam’. The word ‘a ‘in ‘аmulam‘ in the referred verse is described by Vanamali as expressing Lord Viṣṇu (a), Who is the root cause (mulam) of this universe (asya). A similar interpretation of subsequent verses should be examined in the text called ‘Manorama’.  Due to the apprehension that this book may become too long, that is not written here.

### Text 42

**Перевод:**

**Someone may object: “Because the words ‘maya-māyā ‘(made of illusion), ‘maya-krta ‘(made by illusion), and ‘asat ‘(false) are used to describe it, this universe is unreal, as if produced by a mirage.” This idea is mistaken, for it contradicts scriptural statements such as follows: kavir manisi paribhuh svayambhur yathatathyato ‘rthan vyadadhac chasvatibhyah samabhyah (Isa Upaniṣad, 8), “The omniscient, wise, all-pervading, and independent Supreme Lord truly creates the material elements since time immemorial.” brahma satyam tapaḥ satyam satyam caiva prajapatih, satyad bhūtani jatani satyam bhuta-mayam jagat (Mahā-bhārata, 14-35.24), “The Supreme Lord is real, reflection on Him is real, Prajapati is real, the universe composed of material elements is real, and the living creatures are born from the real.” tad etad aksayam nityam jagan muni-varakhilam, avirbhava-tirobhava-janma-nasa-vikalpavat (Viṣṇu Purāṇa, 1.22.60), “O best of sages, this whole universe is eternal and imperishable, and it has alternate appearances and disappearances, births and deaths.” Even something produced by a mirage is not false, because bringing the impression from another place, it exhibits only an object that is real, and because such object remains existing somewhere even now, as it was then displayed.**

**Комментарий:**

Here the opponent raises an objection. The word ‘kuhakam ‘means ‘mirage’. The text ‘kavir manisi ‘is from the Isa Upaniṣad (8). The word ‘yathatathyatah ‘means ‘truly’. According to the Halayudha-kośa (144), the words ‘rta, ‘‘satya, ‘‘samicina, ‘‘samyak, ‘‘tathya, ‘and ‘yatha-tatha ‘are all synonyms of ‘true’. The verse ‘brahma satyam ‘is from the Mahā-bhārata (14.35.24). Here, ‘brahma ‘means ‘the Supreme Lord, ‘and ‘tapaḥ ‘means ‘reflection on Him’. As stated in the Taittiriya Upaniṣad (2.6.1): so ‘kamayata bahu syam prajayeya, sa tapo ‘tapyata tapas taptva idam sarvam asrjata, “The Supreme Soul desired, ‘Let Me become many’. He then reflected, and having reflected, He created the whole universe». The verse ‘tad etad aksayam ‘is from the Viṣṇu Purāṇa (1.22.60). In that same chapter, it had been previously stated that the Supreme has two forms — perishable and imperishable. These perishable and imperishable natures are present in all beings. The imperishable is the Para-brahman, and the perishable is this universe. Starting with the verse ‘ekadesa-sthitasyagneh, ‘after mentioning the forms of Brahma, Viṣṇu, and Siva in this universe, which is a form of the Lord‘s energy, the verse ‘tad etad aksayam ‘is spoken. The universe consisting of God, the jivas, and material nature is eternal and also imperishable. Its portion in the form of God is eternal, immutable. Its portion in the form of the jivas and material nature is imperishable, devoid of absolute destruction. This is stated in the next half of the verse. The portion consisting of God appears and disappears, while the portion consisting of the jivas has an alternation of birth and death. Birth and death are the appearance and destruction of their positions. The words ’satyasyaiva ‘mean that an illusionist displays only real objects, such as fruits. Someone may argue, “Since such objects are displayed only for a moment, they must be considered unreal». In response, the author says, “Because such object remains existing, etc. ” The meaning is that an orchard of pomegranates displayed by a mirage in the desert remains existing even now.

### Text 43

**Перевод:**

**The universe is real, since it is made of the energy of the Supreme Lord, Who is real. The reality of God, the jivas, and material nature is clear from the aja-mantra and a collection of several other statements from the Viṣṇu Purāṇa. The jivas and material nature are energies of God, as stated in the Bhagavad-gita (7-4-5): bhumir apo‘nalo vayuh kham mano buddhir eva ca, ahankara itiyam me bhinna prakṛtir astadha, apareyam itas tv anyam prakṛtim viddhi me param, jiva-bhutam maha-baho yayedam dharyate jagat, “Earth, water, fire, air, ether, pradhana, mahat-tattva, and false-ego — these are My eightfold separated material energies. They are my inferior energy. О mighty-armed Arjuna, you should know that other than these, there is another energy of Mine, the superior energy in the form of the living entities, by which this universe is accepted for enjoyment.” The universe is an effect of these energies, as stated in the śruti: ya eko varno bahudha śakti-yogad varnan anekan nihitartho dadhati (Śvetāśvatara Upaniṣad, 4.1), “The Supreme Lord is one unique category. Having a particular purpose, He creates manifold varieties of categories by engaging His energy.” The smṛti also confirms: mama yonir mahad brahma tasmin garbham dadhamy aham, sambhavah sarva-bhutanam tato bhavati bharata (Bhagavad-gita, 14.3), “Material nature, the cause of the material manifestation, is My womb, in which I place the embryo. О descendent of Bharata, all living beings have their birth from that.” ekadesa-sthitasyagner jyotsna vistarinl yatha, parasya brāhmaṇah śaktis tathedam akhilam jagat (Viṣṇu Purāṇa, 1.22.56), “Just as its light spreads in all directions, although fire is situated in one place, similarly this whole universe is the energy of the Supreme Lord.” This refutes the following idea: “The universe is false because its creation and destruction would not be possible otherwise. If the universe were real like Brahman, its creation and destruction would not be possible at all.” Birth and death are indicatory signs of non-eternity. Reality is a factor common both to eternity and non-eternity. Therefore, this universe is real and noneternal. As stated by the Supreme Lord Himself in the Bhagavad-gita (9.33)" anityam asukham lokam, “This world is temporary and miserable.” False is something that is invalidated in the past, present, and future, such as a flower in the sky.**

**Комментарий:**

It has been previously said that the universe is real and imperishable. Here, the author teaches that both of these attributes are due to the universe being made of the energy of the Supreme Lord, Who is real The ‘aja-mantra ‘is the text from the Śvetāśvatara Upaniṣad (4.5) starting with ‘ajam ekam lohita-sukla-kṛṣṇam’. The universe is real because it is unborn. The author gives evidence that the universe is made of the Lord‘s energy by quoting two verses from the Bhagavad-gita (7.4-5). “Material nature consists of twenty-four elements, out of which eight elements starting with earth should be known as My eightfold material energies. “The five sense objects — smell, taste, colour, touch, and sound — which are the causes of earth, water, fire, air, and ether, are included among these. The eleven senses, which are effects of the false-ego, are included in it. The mahat-tattva is expressed by the word ‘buddhi’. Pradhana, which is conceivable by the mind, is expressed by the word ‘manah’. Thus, twenty-four elements are demonstrated. This energy is called inferior (apara) on account of its being insentient and enjoyed. “Other (anyam) than these (itah), you should know (viddhi) that I have another energy in the form of the jivas (jiva-bhutam), which is superior (param) on account of being sentient and an enjoyer. This (idam) universe (jagat) is accepted (dharyate) by this energy (yaya) for enjoying couches, foodstuffs, and so forth, by means of their material activities. The verse ‘mama yonir mahad brahma ‘is also from the Bhagavad-gita (14.3). “J am the creator of millions of universes, and My (mama) womb (yonih), the place that holds the embryos, is pradhana (brahma), which is the cause (mahat) of the material manifestation and where I place the embryos in the form of the atomic souls’. ‘The rest is clear. Having declared that the Supreme has two forms — perishable and imperishable — and having explained that the imperishable form is the Para-brahman, and the perishable form is all movable and non-movable beings starting with Brahma, the author then quotes the Viṣṇu Purāṇa (1.22.56) to say that these are also made of the Supreme Lord‘s energy. Just as (yatha) the burning fire (agneh) of a lamp is situated in a single place (ekadesa-sthita) and its light (jyotsna), which is different from fire, spreads (vistarini), i.e., its illuminating power spreads, similarly this universe is the spread energy of the Supreme Lord, Who resides in Vaikuṇṭha. This removes the doubt that “How can the perishable be a form of the imperishable, being different from it?” In the next verses, the illustration of light clarifies that the gradation among the jivas starting with Brahma is admitted: tatrapy asanna-duratvad bahutva-svalpata yatha, jyotsna-bhedo’sti tac-chaktes tadvan maitreya vidyate (Viṣṇu Purāṇa, 1.22.57), “O Maitreya, just as there is difference in the light of fire, which is abundant or scanty according to its proximity or distance, similarly there is difference among the Lord‘s energies. ” Just as the light of a nearby fire is abundant, and the light of fire situated far away is scanty, similarly the Lord‘s energy in the form of moving and non-moving beings starting with Brahma also displays more or less power according to the gradation of their state of ignorance. This is the meaning. It will be similarly said later: tayatirohitatvac ca śaktih ksetrajna-samjnita, sarva-bhutesu bhupala taratamyena vartate (ib., 6.7.63), "O King, on account of being enveloped by ignorance, the energy called ksetrajna (the jiva) exists in various gradations among all created beings.” It should be understood that although mentioned among Brahma and Siva, who are effects of His energy, Lord Viṣṇu is distinguished from them, just like a king in the middle of thieves. The meaning of the last sentences of this paragraph is clear.

### Text 44

**Перевод:**

**The verse ‘paramarthas tvam evaiko nanyo’sti ‘ denies the idea that the universe is eternally independent, as concluded by those who speak that the universe is never different than what it is now (na kadacid anidrsam jagat). Yet the universe, which is also a form of the Supreme, is not denied, for by accepting the Supreme as real, the universe is also accepted as real. Otherwise, the statement that He is the Lord of the universe would be contradictory. The Vedas demonstrate that the universe is real and dependent on the Supreme. Otherwise, by rejecting His greatness, His praise found in the scriptures would be contradictory. “This universe (etat) is Yours (tava), Who is consciousness personified (jnānatmanah)y i.e., it is connected with You, since it is made of Your energy. Those who are devoid of jnāna-yoga (ayoginah) by a mistaken perception (bhranti-jnanena) see (pasyanti) this world in the form of demigods, humans, and so on as eternal and independent, and not as connected with You.” In the next verse (jnāna-svarūpam akhilam), it is said that they do not attain liberation from repeated birth and death. The compound ‘jnāna-svarupam ‘means this universe as such, whose maintainer (svarūpa) is the Supreme Himself (jnāna), i.e., this universe, which is dependent on Him. The ignorant (abuddhayah), some dull followers of Karma-mimamsa who are attached to the words of the Vedas, see (pasyantah) this world in the form of fruitive results (artha-svarūpam), independent, and eternal. Those conversant with knowledge (jnāna-vidah) are those who know the scriptures. The compound ‘jnānatmakam ‘means the universe, whose instigator (atma) is the Supreme (jnāna). The same thing is stated by the words ‘tvad-rupam, ‘‘whose form emanates from You’. The word ’svarūpa ‘is actually explained in the sense of ‘maintainer ‘in the scriptures, such as in the conversation between the senses.**

**Комментарий:**

Here the author explains some statements of the Viṣṇu Purāṇa. Some dull followers of Karma-mimamsa declare that the universe is never different (anidrsam), i.e., dependent and non-eternal, but rather it has the same visible form as now, i.e., it is independent and eternal. The universe, which is a form of the Supreme, is eternal, which is understood by accepting the Supreme as eternal. “Otherwise, ” i.e., “if the visible universe were not dependent on the Supreme and were thus eternal and independent.” According to the śruti, the universe is dependent on the Supreme, i.e., it is made by the Supreme’s energy, and is thus real. “Otherwise,” i.e., “if the movable and non-movable universe were devoid of God and thus not pervaded by Him. ” Those devoid of yoga see this visible (drsyate) form of the world (murtam) as independent and eternal. The nature (svarūpa) of the universe is that of consciousness (jnana) and so on. That which by creation and maintenance displays (rūpayati) the universe within the Supreme Self (sva) is called ’svarūpa’. This is the etymological explanation of the word ’svarūpa ‘in the sense of ‘maintainer’. Hence, in the next verse it is said ‘tad-rupam, ‘‘whose form emanates from You’. The fruitive results (artha-svarūpam) in this world are the happiness from experiencing women, sons, tasty foodstuffs, elephants, horses, and so on; in the upper world, they are the great happiness from experiencing celestial women, drinking nectar, and so on. They conclude that because the universe is the cause of all these things but has no cause itself, it is independent and eternal. According to the Visva-kośa (Tha, 7), the word ‘artha ‘is used in the sense of manner, subject, wealth, cause, thing, meaning of words, prevention, and purpose.
