As for the sentient portion, being devoid of transformation, it has always the same form, and although distinct from bodies as demigod or human, due to its different kinds of work, which are the cause of those bodies, it is considered by people with different minds (vibhinna-cittaih) as having multiple forms (bahudha) such as demigod, human, and so on. This is stated in the next line starting with ‘vijnānam ekam’. The verse ‘jnānaih viśuddham ’says that this is created by material activities, which are caused by the aversion towards the Supreme Lord, and not in consequence of one’s svarūpa. The word ‘viśuddham ‘means ‘free from material activities, ‘‘vimalam ‘means ‘free from contact with material nature, ‘‘visokam ‘means ‘free from the suffering caused by that ‘and ‘free from negative qualities like greediness’. “In the way spoken by me (evam), the soul is always immutable (sadaikam).” Thus, the pure nature of the individual soul situated within the material universe has been described. Then, Parasara Muni describes God, by Whose worship one attains liberation. The Supreme Lord (paresah) is eternally accompanied by Laksmi (paramah) and manifests pure consciousness, as stated in the Śrimad-Bhāgavatam (4.3.23): sattvam viśuddham vasudeva-sabditam yad iyate tatra puman apavrtah, “The Supreme Person, Vāsudeva, Who is devoid of all coverings, is manifest in pure consciousness, which is therefore named ‘vasudeva/ The universe is non-different (na anyad asti) from this Supreme powerful Lord (yatah). Therefore, He is different from the jiva.**

**Комментарий:**

Here the author explains the verse ‘tasman na’. The meaning is clear. The original form of the jiva should be understood by the qualities ‘viśuddham ‘(pure), etc. Both God and the jiva are expressed by the word ‘asti’. Here, first the jiva is described as being expressed by ‘asti’. Then, the author describes God, “by Whose worship, etc." Since the living entities are not eternally accompanied by Laksmi and do not eternally manifest pure consciousness, God, Who is endowed with such characteristics, is different from them. This is the meaning.

### Text 82

**Перевод:**

**The sage Parasara then concludes his instruction with the verses starting with ’sad-bhava evam’. “I (maya) have explained to you (tava) the power and nature (bhavah) of the Supreme (sat) in this way (evam), i.e., as being eternally accompanied by Laksmi and manifesting pure consciousness.” The word ‘jnānam ‘means ‘the sentient portion, ‘and ‘anyat ‘means ‘the insentient portion’. The former is real (satyam) and is expressed by the word ‘asti ‘because it has always the same form. The latter is unreal (asatyam) and is expressed by the words ‘nasti ‘because changing every moment, it is subject to destruction, as explained (uktam) to you (te). Someone may argue, “For what purpose is the insentient portion of the Supreme mentioned?” Therein it is said ‘etat tu yat’. In the description of the Viṣṇu-tattva (tatrapi), that which is the insentient portion (yad etat) situated in this world, named rocks, mountains, oceans, and so on are dealt with as being truly useful to attain the ultimate goal, and not as being false like silver in a shell. The verse ‘yajnah pasuh ‘clarifies that the forms as demigod, human, and so on are due to material activities. The verse ‘yac caitat’ states that the insentient portion is factually dealt with. Being subject to the result of its activities (karma-vasyah), the jiva goes (vrajati) through many forms of life (sarvatra). Knowing (jnātvā) this (etat) and becoming detached, one should perform (kuryat) that activity (tat) in this world, by which (yena) one attains (visati) Lord Vāsudeva. When it is said that one enters the town, it is understood that one reaches the town. The cause of approaching the Supreme Lord is bhakti, as declared in the Bhagavad-gita (18.55): bhaktya mam abhijanati yavan yas casmi tattvatah, tato mam tattvato jnātvā visate tad-anantaram, “It is only by devotion that one truly knows Me as I am. Having knowing Me in fact, afterwards one then approaches Me.”**

**Комментарий:**

The word’sat9 is used to express the Supreme, as stated in the Bhagavad-gita (17-4): om tat sad iti nirdeso brāhmaṇas tri-vidhah smrtah, “Om, tat, sat — these are said to be threefold designations of the Supreme.99 Power and nature are expressed by the word ‘bhavah.9 According to the Medini-kośa (Va, 20), the word ‘bhava9 means existence and nature. The rest is clear.

### Text 83

**Перевод:**

**Resorting to identity of case-relation in the sense of negation (badhaydm samanadhikaranya) of one of the terms, the monists imagine that in these verses Brahman is described as real and only one, devoid of any attribute, and that anything other than this is unreal. This reasoning is not sound, because there would be contradiction between the previous and subsequent statements, and because it is not possible to ascertain who observes such negation.**

**Комментарий:**

Here the author points out another flaw in the Māyāvādi’s interpretation. The meaning is as follows: By taking to identity of case-relation in the sense of negation (badhayam samanadhikaranya) of one of the terms, the monists declare that (l) according to the verse ‘jyotimsi visnuh ‘Brahman is a single element; (2) the verse ‘jnāna-svarupo bhagavan’says that the universe is a superimposition manifest on it due to ignorance; (3) the verse ‘yada tu śuddhaṁ ‘corroborates this; (4) the two verses starting with ‘vastv asti kim ‘demonstrate its falsity even at the time of perception; (5) the line ‘tasman na vijnānam ‘concludes that Brahman is a single element, as declared previously; (6) the line ‘vijnānam ekam ‘clarifies that material activities are the root cause of the concept of difference; (7) the verse ‘jnānam viśuddham ‘clarifies the svarūpa of Brahman; and (8) according to the verse ’sad-bhava evam ‘Brahman alone is the ultimate truth, and anything other than this is false and its reality is only empirical. This is the secret of the scriptures, therefore Brahman is only one undivided consciousness. Then, having explained that due to indescribable ignorance, the universe consisting of rocks, oceans, and so on, is superimposed on Brahman, the verse ‘yac caitat ‘explains that when all material designations (upadhi) disappear by the non-dual consciousness that ‘I am Brahman, ‘then one’s very self enters into Brahman (Vāsudeva), just as when a pot is destroyed, the space within it merges with the unlimited space. According to the monists, this is what the sage Parasara spoke in these verses. However, this interpretation is foolish. The author then explains the reason. Having described the extensive nature of the material universe, Parasara then briefly describes another form of it, which had not been mentioned: samksepah srūyatam punah, yad ambu vaiṣṇavah kayas tato vipra vasundhara, padmakara samudbhuta parvatabdhy-adi-samyuta (Viṣṇu Purāṇa, 2.12.36-37), “Hear again the summary. О brāhmaṇa, the water from the Garbhodaka ocean is an embodied form of Lord Viṣṇu, and from that is produced the earth planet, whose form is like a lotus, along with its mountains, oceans, and so on. ” Then the verse ‘jyotimsi visnuh ‘is spoken. It is said that there are two portions in the material universe, which is Lord Viṣṇu’s body: the sentient and the insentient. The sentient portion is expressed by the word ‘asti ‘because it has always one form. The insentient portion is defined as being expressed by the words ‘nasti ‘because it is changeable and subject to destruction. When the sentient portion enters the insentient portion, worldly life results, and when it approaches Lord Viṣṇu, liberation results. The peculiarity in all this that had been previously described is then stated: ‘na yato ‘nyad asti, ‘"Apart from Whom there is nothing else». In this way, the following reiteration by Maitreya is also consistent: visnv-adharam yatha caitat trailokyam samavasthitam, paramarthas ca me prokto yatha jnānam pradhanatah (Viṣṇu Purāṇa, 2.13.2), “As you have spoken to me, Lord Viṣṇu is the basis upon which the three worlds are situated. He is the ultimate reality, and knowledge about Him is the highest of all Therefore, the perishable nature of the material manifestation was described by the words ‘nasti9 and ‘asatyam,3 as this is similarly understood from the two verses starting with ‘vastv asti kim’. According to those who know the truth, the words (nasti9 and ‘asatyam9 are spoken in order to instigate indifference towards this world. If identity of case-relation in the sense of negation (badhayam samanadhikaranya) of one of the terms is accepted, then who is the one who observes the negation ? If the observer is the jiva, then it would not be possible to be the observer of its own negation. Since the jiva is to be negated and is hence the object of the observation, if the observer is the svarūpa of Brahman, then there would be difference.

### Text 84

**Перевод:**

**In this same scripture, the venerable Parasara had previously stated that God, the living entity, material nature, and time are eternal. For instance: avikaraya suddhaya nityaya paramatmane, sadaika-rupa-rupaya visnave sarva-jisnave (l.2.l)y “Obeisances unto the immutable and pure Lord Viṣṇu, the conqueror of all, Who is the eternal and all-pervading Supreme Soul and manifests always the very same form.” pradhanam puruṣam capi pravisyatmecchaya harih, ksobhayam asa samprapte sarga-kale vyayavyayau (1.2.29)у “At the time of creation, after entering the pradhana and the living entity, according to His own will Lord Hari stirred up both the changeable and the immutable.” avyaktam karanam yat tat pradhanam rsi-sattamaih, procyate prakṛtih suksma nityam sad-asad-atmakam (1.2.19), “The subtle material nature, which is called by the best of sages aspradhana, avyakta, and karana, is eternally endowed with cause and effect.” anadir bhagavan kalo nanto ’sya dvija vidyate, avyucchinnas tatas tv ete sarga-sthity-anta-samyamah (1.2.26), “O best of brāhmaṇas, the Supreme Lord is time itself, devoid of both a beginning and an end. From Him creation, maintenance, and destruction happen continuously.” They are not destroyed just because one thinks so, for this would contradict the statements that they are eternal.**

**Комментарий:**

The author writes this section with the intention to convey that the imagination that there is only one consciousness devoid of attributes being described in the same Viṣṇu Purāṇa is only due to great foolishness, for it contradicts what the preceptor Parasara had in mind. The verses quoted here are all from the first part of the Viṣṇu Purāṇa. The word ‘puruṣam ‘means ‘the living entity, ‘and ‘vyayavyayau ‘means ‘mutable and immutable beings’. The jiva is eternal because it is devoid of transformation. Time is eternal because it is devoid of beginning (anadih) and end (nantah).

### Text 85

**Перевод:**

**The difference between these four elements is repeated in the Viṣṇu Purāṇa: pradhana-puruṣa-vyakta-kalanam parāmaṁ hi yat, pasyanti surayah śuddhaṁ tad visnoh parāmaṁ padam (1.2.16), “The demigods contemplate Lord Viṣṇu’s supreme pure position, which is superior to the pradhana, the living entities, the manifested universe, and time.” visnoh svarapat parato hi te ‘nye rūpe pradhanam puruṣas ca vipra, tasyaiva te ‘nyena dhrte viyukte rūpena yat tad dvija kala-samjnam (1.2.24), “O brāhmaṇa, from Lord Viṣṇu’s supreme svarūpa two forms emanate: the pradhana and the living entity. These two are held together at the time of creation and separated at the time of destruction by another form of His named time.” na santi yatra sarvese nama-jaty-adi-kalpanah, satta-matratmake jneye jnānatmany atmanah pare, sa brahma tat param dhama paramatma sa cesvarah, sa visnuh sarvam evedam yato navartate yatih, prakṛtir ya māyā khyata vyaktavyakta-svarupini, puruṣas capy ubhav etau liyete paramātmani (6.4.37-39), “Imagined names and classes do not exist in the Lord of all, Who is to be known as pure existence, knowledge personified, and superior to the individual soul. He is Brahman, the supreme abode, the Supreme Soul, and the Supreme God. He is Viṣṇu; He is everything. An ascetic who attains Him never returns to this world. Both material nature, which is called māyā and whose form is manifest and unmanifest, as well as the living entity become absorbed in the Supreme Soul.”**

**Комментарий:**

The word ‘parāmaṁ ‘means ‘the supreme’. The meaning is that the Lord is much different from the other elements. The word ‘anya,’stated twice in the verse ‘visnoh svarūpat, ‘clearly conveys the meaning of difference. Imagined names and classes do not exist in Him, i.e., His names, classes, and pastimes are all real. The Lord is superior (pare) to the jiva (atmanah). It should be understood that the first two verses quoted here are from the first part of the Viṣṇu Purāṇa, and the other three verses are from its sixth part.

### Text 86

**Перевод:**

**The conversation between Bharata and Rahugana found in another place in the Viṣṇu Purāṇa is also explained in the same way. Therein, it is clearly said that the realization of the pure Supreme is the highest truth, and not the non-difference between the Supreme and the jiva.**

**Комментарий:**

Here the author explains the conversation between Bharata and Rahugana found in the second part of the Viṣṇu Purāṇa. “In this way, ” i.e., by demonstrating the changeable with the words ‘nasti ‘and ‘asatya, ‘by demonstrating the eternity of God, the living entity, material nature, and time, and by repeating the difference between them, which is seen in this same scripture. "Is also explained, ” i.e., is also ascertained as dealing with difference and devotion to the Supreme Lord. The author then affirms this, “Therein, it is clearly, etc." Bharata’s words are as follows: paramatmatmanoryogah paramartha itisyate, mithyaitad anya-dravyam hi naity anya-dravyatam yatah (2.14.27), “If it is supposed that the union between the individual soul and the Supreme Soul is the highest truth, this is false, for one element does not turn into another element. “ This is what is being stated here: in the verse ‘yo bhavanyan nimittam va ‘(2.13-75), upon being questioned by King Rahugana, Bharata teaches that despite the concept of ‘I, ‘one should not believe oneself as being independent. Then, being asked about the highest good, Bharata mentions higher and lower things like wealth and sons, up to becoming one with God, and after explaining that these are not the highest truth, he declares that the highest truth is the realization the pure Supreme: tasmac chreyamsy asesani nrpaitani na samsayah, paramarthas tu bhupala samksepac chrūyatarh mama, eko vyapi samah suddho nirguṇah prakrteh parah, janma-vrddhy-adi-rahita atma sarva-gato ‘vyayah, para-jnāna-mayo’sadbhir nama-jaty-adibhir vibhuh, na yogavan na yukto ‘bhun naiva parthiva yoksyati, tasyatma-para-dehesu sato ‘py eka-mayam hi yat, vijnānarn paramartho’sau dvaitino ‘tattva-darsinah, venu-randhra-vibhedena bhedah sadjadi-samjnitah, abheda-vyapino vayos tatha tasya mahatmanah, ekatvam rūpa-bhedas ca bahya-karma-pravrttijah, devadi-bhedam adhyaste nasty evavarano hi sah (2.14.28-33), “Therefore, О king, without doubt all these are good things. Now, briefly hear from me about the highest truth, О king. There is one independent, all-pervading Supreme Soul, Who is impartial, pure, devoid of material attributes, superior to material nature, omnipresent, unlimited, and devoid of birth, growth, and so on. О king, He is the highest consciousness, the Supreme, Who is not connected with unreal things like names and classes, was never connected with them, and will never be. Although situated in Himself as well as in the bodies of other souls, He is one. This realization is the highest truth. Those who see duality do not see the truth. The same air pervades everything without any difference, yet there is difference, which is named as seven musical notes according to the different holes in a flute. Similarly, the Supreme Soul is the same in different bodies. Different forms are produced due to the material activities performed by the individual souls, who are indifferent to Him, but without being covered, He remains situated in all different bodies, either of demigods, humans, and so forth». The meaning of these verses is as follows: There is one independent (ekah) Soul (atma) constituted of consciousness and bliss, Who pervades everything inside and outside (vyapi). He consists of consciousness superior to that of the jivas (para-jnāna-mayah). By the statement that He is devoid of unreal things (asadbhih) like names and classes (nama-jaty-adibhih), it is meant that He is endowed with real names and classes that exist in His svarūpa at all times. This Supreme Soul is essentially one (eka-mayam), although situated in Himself as well as in different bodies (atma-para-dehesu), just as the sun-god is situated in his own eyes as well as in the eyes of others. This realization (vijttanam) is the highest truth (paramarthah). As for those who, observing the various bodily designations, think that there is difference (dvaitinah) in the Supreme Soul, they do not see the truth (atattva-darsinah). Using an illustration, Parasara then demonstrates how the Supreme Soul is non- different while manifest in multiple forms: just as the air (vayoh) coming from all the holes (randhra) of a flute (venu) is the same, so (tatha) is the nature of the Supreme Soul (mahatmanah). This is the connection between the words. The different forms (rūpa-bhedah), the various kinds of bodies such as demigods and humans, is produced by the activities performed (karma-pravrttijah) by the living entities who are indifferent (bahya) towards the Supreme Soul (sah), Who, becoming situated within, rules (adhyaste) the different kinds of bodies (devadi-bhedam) as the inner Soul. Because He is devoid of the stain of those various bodily designations, He has no (nasti) covering at all (avaranah). Here, the independent, all-pervading Soul constituted of consciousness and bliss is God. The jiva is pervaded by Him. The conclusion is that realization of the pure Supreme Lord is the highest truth for the living entity.

### Text 87

**Перевод:**

**The conversation between Rbhu and Nidagha, which is quoted by the monists to support the meaning explained by them, is also explained in the same way. The pure svarūpa of the jivas, which is devoid of hunger and so on, is dependent on the all-pervading Supreme Lord, and their residing, going, coming, and so forth, are also dependent on Him. This is the pervasive non-duality described therein, and nothing else. The conclusion should also be interpreted in the same way. The negation of the idea of difference within Lord Viṣṇu is stronger here.**

**Комментарий:**

Removing the doubt that pure monism is corroborated in the conversation between Rbhu and Nidagha found in the Viṣṇu Purāṇa, the author writes the present paragraph. There it is said: ity ukte mauninarh bhūyas cintayanam mahipatim, pratyuvacatha vipro’sav advaitantargatam katham, rbhur namabhavat putro brāhmaṇah paramesthinah, tasya sisyo nidagho ‘bhUt pulastya-tanayah pura, pradad asesath vijnānam sa tasmai paraya muda, avapta-tattva-jnānasya na tasyadvaita-vāsanam, sa rbhus tarkayam asa nidaghasya naresvara (2.15.1,3-5), “That being said, King RahUgana became silent and very pensive. The brāhmaṇa Bharata then told him a narration about nonduality: ‘The chief living entity, Brahma, had a son named Rbhu. In ancient times, Nidagha, a son of the sage Pulastya, became Rbhu’s disciple. With great joy, Rbhu imparted complete knowledge to him. Although Nidagha had received knowledge of truth, Rbhu suspected that he had no inclination towards non-dualism, О King’. The meaning is that Rbhu suspected that Nidagha had no inclination towards non-dualism, “He has knowledge of the nature and qualities of God and the jiva, but he does not know that being pervaded and controlled by God, the jiva is non-different from Him. ” Then, after a thousand celestial years passed, Rbhu visits Nidagha’s house. After feeding him, Nidagha asks whether he was satisfied, where he resided, and so on: api te parama tustir utpanna tustir eva va, api te manasam svastham aharena krtam dvija, kva nivaso bhavan vipra kva va ganturh samudyatah, agamyate ca bhavatayatas tac ca dvijocyatam (2.15.17-18), “O brāhmaṇa, has your hunger been appeased? Are you fully satisfied? Is your heart content with the food you have taken, О brāhmaṇa? Where do you reside ? Are you about to go somewhere else ? Please tell me wherefrom you have come, О brāhmaṇa.” Rbhu removes the doubts regarding satisfaction and so on by a description of the nature of the pure soul, according to which hunger and thirst belong to the embodied living entities and are not attributes of the soul: ksud yasya tasya bhunkte ‘nne trptir brāhmaṇa jayate, na me ksud abhavat trptim kasmad brāhmaṇa prcchasi (2.15.19), “O brāhmaṇa, one who feels hunger is the one who feels satisfaction upon eating food. I feel no hunger, so why do you ask about feeling satisfied ?” He then removes the doubt about residence and so on: kva nivasas tavety uktam kva gantasi ca yat tvayā, kutas cagamyate tatra trtlye ‘pi nibodha me, atma sarva-gato vyapi akasavad ayam yatah, kutah kutra kva gantasity etad apy arthavat katham, so ‘ham na ganta naganta naikadesa-niketanah, na canye ca na ca tvam ca nanye ‘nye naham apy aham (2.15.23-25), “You have asked me where I reside, and where I am going, and wherefrom I came, so hear me. As the Supreme Soul is all-pervading like ether, He is omnipresent. Thus, how can such questions about coming, going, and residing be meaningful ? I, the soul, do not go, do not come, and do not reside in a particular place. What is conventionally named ‘I, ‘‘you, ‘and ‘others ‘is not actually I, you, and others. ” The word ‘atma ‘here refers to Lord Vāsudeva, Whose form is consciousness and bliss. Pervading everything (vyapi) inside and outside, He is situated everywhere (sarva-gatah). Yet, like ether, He is not tainted by contact with the pervaded things. "Thus, since I am pervaded by the Supreme Lord and dependent on Him, I am non-different from Him, so your individual questions about my going, coming and residing make no sense». He explains this in the verse ’so ‘ham ganta’. “Beingpervaded by Him, He is my soul. I am not separated from Him. ” Nor am I the only one like this, but rather all others are also such. Someone may argue, “Since it is perceived that there is independence and different identities in the activities performed by you, me, and others, why do you say something that contradicts direct perception ?”It is said ‘na ca tvam’. “Among these, the ‘you ‘perceived as independent is not the real you. The ‘others ‘perceived as different are not the real others. The 7 ‘perceived by you as independent is not the real ‘I’. Rather, all of us are pervaded by God, and hence we are not something different. God perceived in all actions should be defined as one and the same. You should adopt this concept of pervasive non-dualism. ” The idea here is as follows: Although the living entities do go, come, and so on, these activities are possible because they are pervaded by the Supreme Soul, just like the going and coming of the army depend on the going and coming of the king and are called royal activities. Although Lord Vāsudeva’s going and coming are not possible, as He is all-pervading, nevertheless they do happen by dint of His inconceivable potency. Thus, the activities of the living entities become one with His activities. Rbhu concludes in the following way: evam ekam idam brahman na bhedi sakalam jagat, vasudevabhidheyasya svarūpam paramatmanah (2.15.35), “O brāhmaṇa, in this way, this whole universe is one without duality. It is a form of the Supreme Soul named Vāsudeva. ”It should be understood that the word ’svarūpam ‘(form) is used here because the universe is pervaded and supported by Him. Corroborating what was stated in his narration of the talk between Rbhu and Nidagha, Bharata tells Rahūgana: tatha tvam api dharmajna tulyatma-ripu-bandhavah, bhava sarva-gatam janann atmanam avanipate, sita-niladi-bhedena yathaikam drsyate nabhah, bhranta-drstibhir atmapi tathaikah sa prthak prthak, ekah samastam yad ihasti kincit tad acyuto nasti param tato ‘nyat, so ‘ham sa ca tvam sa ca sarvam etad atma-svarūpam tyaja bheda-moham (2.16.21-23), “O knower of dharma, knowing that the Supreme Soul is everywhere, you should also have an equipoised mind towards friends and enemies, О king. Just as the same sky is seen as white, blue, and so on, similarly the same Supreme Soul is seen as different by those whose vision is erroneous. Everything that exists in this world is the one Lord, Acyuta, besides Whom nothing else exists. I, you, and everything that exists is a form of the Supreme Soul. Therefore, give up the delusion of duality." The meaning of these verses is this: The word ‘atmanam ‘refers to the Supreme Lord, here expressed by the names Vāsudeva and Acyuta. He is only one. The concept of difference is a mistake due to the perception that He assumes the form of demigods, humans, and so on, which are products of the three modes of nature. Such concept of difference is unwise, just like the difference seen on the same sky due to a superimposition of attributes that belong to the clouds, such as white and blue colours. The Supreme Soul is indeed the embodiment of consciousness and bliss. The same Acyuta is everything. Because everything is pervaded and maintained by Him, there is nothing apart from Him. “Give up the delusion of duality, i.e., the foolishness of independence and self-conceit». This is the instruction given on the concept of pervasive non-dualism.

### Text 88

**Перевод:**

**Similarly, it should also not be expected that a single consciousness devoid of attributes is intended by words such as ‘atma-bhava, ‘which are subsequently seen in the conversation between Kesidhvaja and Khandikya, for therein duality between the meditated and the meditator, the attained and the cause of attainment, is clear.**

**Комментарий:**

Some claim that monism is also seen in the topic discussed between Khandikya and Kesidhvaja. Here, the author refutes this idea. The following narration is found in the sixth part of the Viṣṇu Purāṇa: atma-bhavam nayaty enam tad brahma dhyayinam типе, vikaryam atmanah śaktya loham akarsako yatha (6.7.30), “O sage, the Supreme Soul brings towards His spiritual nature one who meditates upon Him without personal motivations, just as a magnet attracts iron by its own power». The words ‘atma-bhavam ‘mean ‘towards His spiritual nature, ‘or ‘towards existence within Himself, ‘and not oneness with Himself. An object attracted by a magnet is certainly not seen to assume the nature of the magnet. This is also concluded from subsequent verses: vijnānath prapakam prapye pare brahmani parthiva, prapaniyas tathaivatma praksinasesa-bhavanah, ksetrajnah karani jhanam karanam tasya vai dvija, nispadyam mukti-karyam vai krta-krtyo nivartate (6.7.92-93), “O king, knowledge leads to the attainment of the Supreme Absolute Truth, and the one who attains is the individual soul free from the three kinds of consciousness. 0 brāhmaṇa, the living entity is the one who utilizes the means; knowledge is its means; and liberation is the result to be attained. One who has attained this objective refrains from material activities. “ By meditating on the Supreme Soul, a living entity thus becomes free from the three kinds of consciousness — brahma-bhavana, karma-bhavana, and a mixture of both — hence it becomes ready to attain the Supreme. By the statement of something that is attained and the cause of attainment mentioned here, difference must be certainly expressed. It is further said: tad-bhava-bhavam apanno yad asau paramatmana, bhavaty abhedi bhedas ca tasyajnāna-krto bhavet, vibheda-janake ‘jnane nasam atyantikam gate, atmano brahmano bhedam asantam kah karisyati (6.7.94-95), “Then, in contact with the Supreme Soul, one attains the purity of His nature and becomes devoid of the difference created by material bodies caused by ignorance about Him. When the ignorance that causes different material bodies is totally destroyed, and such difference between the individual soul and the Supreme Soul no more exists, what would create this difference again ?” The words ‘tad-bhava ‘mean ‘the nature of the Supreme Soul, ‘and not oneness, for otherwise the second word ‘bhava ‘would have no meaning and there would be contradiction between the previous and subsequent verses starting with ‘vijfianam prapakam’. The meaning here is that by meditating on the nature of the Supreme Soul, which is devoid of the three kinds of consciousness, one becomes pure (bhavam apannah). Then, one becomes devoid of vijatlya-bheda (abhedi) created by various material bodies like demigods and humans, whose root cause is ignorance in the form of material activities. This is explained in the verse ‘vibheda-janake’. When ignorance (ajnane) in the form of material activities, which causes the vijatiya-bheda created by various material bodies (bheda-janake), is totally destroyed (atyantikam nasam gate) by meditating on the nature of the Supreme Soul, and such difference (bhedam) between the individual soul (atmanah) and the Supreme Soul (brāhmaṇah) no more exists (asantam), since its cause no more exists, what (kah) would create (karisyati) this difference again ? Nothing would. This is the meaning. Yet, sajatiya-bheda remains existing even then.

### Text 89

**Перевод:**

**It has been further argued that difference is not the intended meaning of the scriptures, since śruti statements like ‘atha yo ‘nyam ‘ condemn one who accepts difference and declare that he will attain fear. This idea is not very intelligent, for it has already been previously said that difference is actually the intended meaning of the scriptures. In the text ‘atha yo ‘nyam, ‘one who has become dull due to Vedic rituals performed out of material desires is being condemned, and not a desireless devotee of the Supreme Lord, who knows that the Lord is different from oneself, because such devotee is praised in the text ‘jussam yada’.  The text ‘yad eveha ‘ speaks about one who sees difference among the expansions of the Supreme Lord, and the text ‘yada hy evaisa ‘ says that one who splits away from devotion to the Supreme Lord obtains the fear of mundane existence.**

**Комментарий:**

In this way, having dispelled the doubts regarding the Viṣṇu Purāṇa, here t/ze author dispels other doubts. Before the text ‘atha yo ‘nyam, ‘it is stated in the Bṛhad-āraṇyaka Upaniṣad (1.4.10): tad dhaitat pasyann rsir vamadevah pratipede ‘ham manur abhavam sūryas ca, “Realizing the Supreme, the sage Vamadeva declared, 7 became Manu and the sun-god’». Having reflected on the Supreme as the cause of one’s sustenance, activities, and so on, and having taught that the universe headed by Manu and others is a form of the Supreme, as their activities and subsistence are dependent of Him, in order to consolidate this consciousness, one devoid of such knowledge and who became dull by performing Vedic rituals is condemned by the words ‘atha yo ‘nyam’. One who worships other demigods, who are neither one’s original source nor pervade one’s self, considering them as benefactors merely because they are limbs in Vedic rituals, such a person does not know the truth. He is like an animal. Just as a worldly animal’s life is appropriated by people and it is always troubled by serving them, similarly such a person becomes a servant for the demigods. Thus, he is devoid of understanding of the goal of life. It is heard from that śruti that the demigods actually desire such service and hence obstruct knowledge of the Supreme. As for the Supreme Lord, being full of unlimited bliss, He is completely free from desires, and being the well-wisher of everyone, He is everyone’s benefactor without any motive. He gives one realization of His knowledge and svarūpa. His devotee is also fully satisfied with devotion for Him and wants only this, and nothing else, and therefore he is praiseworthy. The compound ’sakama-sevakatvat ‘is a tatpuruṣa karmadharaya. The same is also the case in the compound ‘niskama-tad-bhaktah’. In the text ‘yad eveha, ‘it is denied that there is difference among the Supreme Lord‘s avataras in the same way as the known difference among the living entities. It is not denied that there is difference between the Supreme Lord and the living entities, for difference is demonstrated by the indications of such meaning. In the text ‘yada hy evaisa, ‘falling down from the devotion to the Supreme Lord is expressed by the word ‘antara, ‘and devotion is expressed by the word ‘pratistha ‘in a subsequent sentence. In the Śrimad-Bhāgavatam (2.1.5), Śukadeva Gosvāmi declares that devotion to the Supreme Lord is the cause of perpetual fearlessness, and that upon discontinuing such devotion, one obtains fear of mundane existence: tasmat sarvatmana rājan bhagavan harir isvarah, srotavyah kirtitavyas ca smartavyas cecchatabhayam, “Therefore, О king, one who desires fearlessness should wholeheartedly hear about the Supreme Lord, Hari, glorify Him, and remember Him. ”

### Text 90

**Перевод:**

**The text ‘tvam va aham ‘ is also explained in this manner. One should also not believe that the text ‘upasakanam karyartham ‘ depicts Brahman as being devoid of attributes, for the Supreme Lord‘s attributes are mentioned subsequently by the words ‘ramo brāhmaṇandaika-vigrahah ‘(Rama-tāpani Upaniṣad, 2.3), “Lord Rama, Whose unique form is spiritual bliss personified, etc.” This is also confirmed by the Puruṣa-sUkta (l8): vedaham etam puruṣam mahantam aditya-varnam tamasah parastat, “I know this Supreme Person, Whose complexion is like the sun and Who is beyond darkness.” Therefore, the text ‘upasakanam karyartham’ should be interpreted as referring to a material form.**

**Комментарий:**

“In this manner, ” i.e., that which is brought into existence and is pervaded is not different than the cause of its existence and its pervader, as taught in the conversation between the senses and in other passages as well. The author then reconciles the sentence from the Rama-tāpani Upaniṣad. The meaning is that the Supreme has transcendental attributes, for His form is mentioned there. The form of the Supreme Lord is also clearly stated in the Puruṣa-sukta. Hence, the form mentioned in the sentence ‘upasakanam karyartham ‘ is material, the universal form.

### Text 91

**Перевод:**

**“Difference is a by-product of avidya because it does not exist in the state of liberation.” These are hasty words, for difference after liberation has been declared in sentences like ‘niranjanah parāmaṁ samyam’. This is also corroborated by sentences like ‘karma-ksaye yati sa tattvato ‘nyah, ‘ and ’so ’smite sarvan kaman’. Therefore, the difference between God and the living entity is real.**

**Комментарий:**

Despite having heard the previously quoted sentences about difference at the time of liberation, turning deaf ears to them, the monist objects again here. His claim is that there are no statements in the scriptures that teach about difference in the state of liberation. The text ‘karma-ksaye yati ‘is from the Śvetāśvatara Upaniṣad (6.4). When the results of all material activities are destroyed (karma-ksaye), the jiva (sah) attains (yati) the Supreme. What is the jiva like? Even then, it actually (tattvatah) remains different (anyah) from the Supreme.

### Text 92

**Перевод:**

**Hence, the smṛti says as follows: yathesvarasya jivasya satyo bhedo viniscayat, evam eva hi me vacam satyam kartum iharhasi, “Just as the difference between God and the jiva is true, since it is so ascertained, it behoves you to make my words prove similarly true.” yathesvaras ca jivas ca satya-bhedau parasparam, tena satyena mam devas trayantu saha-kesavah, “Just as God is true and different from the jiva, the jiva is true and different from God. May the demigods accompanied by Lord Kesava protect me by this truth.”**

**Комментарий:**

Thus ends the sixth chapter of the introduction to the Govinda-bhāṣya, named Siddhānta-ratnaṁ, whose topic is the refutation of the monistic philosophy in a different way.

Here the author quotes two verses to illustrate the mentioned reality of difference.

Thus, the sixth chapter, entitled ‘Trivikrama, ‘has been explained in this commentary on the introduction to the Govinda-bhāṣya

## Seventh Chapter – Nandaka

### Text 1

**Перевод:**

**Regarding the nature of Brahman, the monists state, “In texts like ‘ekam evadvitiyam, ‘and’satyam jnānam anantam, ‘no attributes such as potencies, qualities, and pastimes are ascertained, and therefore it is understood that the Supreme Absolute Truth is jnāna (consciousness) devoid of the three kinds of difference. If the word ‘jnāna ‘is expressed in the bhava sense, then only this Truth’s reality and indivisibility without the three kinds of difference would be possible, as understood by words such as ‘eva ‘(only) and ‘eka ‘(only one). Otherwise, if ‘jnāna ‘is expressed as a grammatical case-relation (karaka), there would be division according to knower, knowable, and the means of knowledge. Being thus divided, the Supreme would be fragmented. In this way, if ‘jnāna ‘is expressed as an agent, being a doer and hence mutable, it would be false. If it is expressed as an instrument, being inanimate like an axe, it would also be false. Therefore, ‘jnāna ‘is expressed only in the bhava sense. Moreover, this Truth named jnāna and devoid of attributes is a synonym of the words ’samvit, ‘‘anubhūti, ‘and ‘jnapti’. Anything other than that is false, as stated by the Sruti such as ‘nānāsti kincana ‘and the smṛti such as ‘jnāna-svarupo bhagavan’. ”**

**Комментарий:**

The philosophical conclusion of the monist has been refuted by the previous chapter. Now, this chapter, entitled ‘Nandaka, ‘has begun to dispel some of his crooked arguments. The monist proposes the following: “From sentences like ‘ekam evadvitiyam ‘and’satyam jnānam anantam ‘it is understood that Brahman is mere undivided jnāna, for in the word ‘jnāna, ‘the affix ‘lyut’ is used in the bhava sense. This sentence has greater importance because it deals with the svarūpa of Brahman. Sentences like ‘yah sarvajnah ‘ have less importance because they deal with qualities. Therefore, on the strength of sentences that deal with its svarūpa, it must be accepted that the Supreme Absolute Truth is mere jnāna». This is what the Māyāvādi repeats here in this paragraph. By the words ‘śakty-adi ‘(potencies, etc.), qualities and pastimes are also to be included. The word’santatvam ‘means ‘fragmented’. The word ‘jnāna ‘means either ‘that which knows, ‘or ‘that by which it is known’. The word ‘jnāna ‘is used in the sense of subject, to which the affix ‘lyut ‘(ana) is applied, since the verbal root ‘jna ‘belongs to the class of verbal roots starting with ‘nand’. In Panini’s Astadhyayi (3-3.113), it is said: krtya-lyuto bahulam, “The affixes ‘krtya ‘and ‘lyut ‘are employed with multiple denotations.” By either applying or not the word ‘bahulam ‘(multiple denotations) in this aphorism, we have one or two rules, and thus the affix ‘lyut ‘is also used in the sense of object. This affix is also used in the word ‘jnāna ‘in the sense of the instrumental case, as stated in the aphorism ‘karanadhikaranayos ca ‘(ib., 3.3.117): “The affix ‘lyut ‘is also used in the sense of the instrumental and locative cases». “It would be false, ” i.e., what is mutable and inanimate is false. The idea here is this: “Anything other than the attributeless Brahman is false because it is an imagination. On the basis of sentences like ‘neha nānāsti kincana, ‘this a strong interpretation. ”

### Text 2

**Перевод:**

**In response, we say that this argument is no wiser than the previous ones. Even if the Supreme Truth is taken as a state of being (bhava), by the gale-grhita-nyaya, its potency must be definitely accepted, for its existence is unavoidable, as the perception of effects like the universe would not be possible otherwise. Nor is postulation (arthapatti) the only evidence of this, for there are statements from the śruti and the smṛti, such as: ya eko varno bahudha śakti-yogad varnan anekan nihitartho dadhati (Śvetāśvatara Upaniṣad, 4.1), “The Supreme Lord is one unique category. Having a particular purpose, He creates manifold varieties of categories by engaging His potency.” Someone may argue, “The Supreme may have potency. Yet it is false, since it is just imagination.” One should not fall into this error, because in sentences like ‘parasya śaktih ‘and ’saktayah sarva-bhavanam, ‘ the scriptures declare that the Supreme has inherent powers, and because it is not defined who is the one imagining the existence of potency. Nor is the Supreme fragmented as per the above reasoning, for His pervading everything inside and outside is understood from the śruti, such as ‘yac ca kincij jagat sarvam’. It is also said: tilesu tailam dadhiniva sarpih (Śvetāśvatara Upaniṣad, 1.15), “Oil exists in sesame just as ghee exists in yogurt.” This illustration demonstrates how such pervasiveness acts in everything. Nor can it be said that, “If the word ‘jnāna ‘expresses a subject and other case-relations, then Brahman would be mutable and hence unreal,” for the śruti proves that by dint of His inconceivable svarūpa-śakti, the Supreme Lord, jnāna personified, is a subject and so forth, and nevertheless the power of His svarūpa remains unfailing.**

**Комментарий:**

Here the author refutes the objection that was raised above. The words ‘bhava-rupasya ‘mean ‘whose meaning as light is established in the word ‘jnāna, ‘which consists of the affix ‘lyut ‘(ana) applied to the verbal root ‘jna ‘in the bhava sense. Since fatness is unexplained in the case of one who does not eat during the day, for argument’s sake, one assumes that such a person must eat at night. Similarly, since this visible effect in the form of the universe is unexplained without the Supreme ’spotency, for argument’s sake, one assumes that the Supreme must have potency. Therefore, just as it must be swallowed that a fat person who does not eat during the day must be eating at night, also in this case it must be swallowed that the Supreme, the cause of the universe and everything, has potency. If someone argues that potency is imagined as existing in Brahman, then the one who is imagining should be named. If Brahman is the one imagining, then there would be difference. If either the jiva or māyā is the one imagining, then either would be its own substratum. "As per the above reasoning," i.e., according to the reasoning that by expressing subject, object, and instrument, jnāna would be divided. The word ‘pravibhagah ‘means ‘division’. The text ‘tilesu tailam ‘is from the Śvetāśvatara Upaniṣad (1.15). The word ‘tam ‘refers to the pervasiveness. The meaning here is this: it is not true that if jnāna expresses a subject, it would be mutable, and if it expresses an instrument, it would be inanimate, and for this reason it would be unreal and false. The śruti demonstrates that the Supreme is the immutable creator of the universe. For instance: sa visva-krd visva-vid atma-yonir niskalam niskriyam santam niravadyam niranjanam (Śvetāśvatara Upaniṣad, 6.19), "The creator and knower of the universe is the source of all living entities. That peaceful Supreme has no parts, no actions, no defects, and no blemishes. ”

### Text 3

**Перевод:**

**Someone may argue, “To be a knower actually means to be a doer of the action of knowing, which is mutable, insentient, and unreal, for this is understood on account of the non-difference between the action and the sense of ‘Г when one thinks ‘I am the knower’. This sense of T is not the spirit, for this is understood on account of the non-difference between the ‘I ‘and the body when one thinks ‘I am fat, ‘and so on. This is also corroborated by the observation that the presence of the spirit is felt during deep sleep or swoon, although there is no perception of the false ego. Therefore, the quality of being a knower is similar to the false ego and the material body, and is a superimposition on the pure spirit.” This argument is foolish. To be a knower means to be the substratum of the quality of knowledge. Knowledge is eternal because it is a natural attribute of the eternal Supreme. The śruti also confirms this by sentences like ‘parasya śaktir vividhaiva srūyate ‘and ‘avinasi va are ayam atmanucchitti-dharma, ‘(Bṛhad-āraṇyaka Upaniṣad, 4.5-14), “The individual soul is imperishable and has indestructible attributes.” Without any contradiction, the Supreme Lord, Whose form is knowledge, is Himself the substratum of knowledge, just as the sun, whose form is light, is itself the source of light. Thus, even when expressing a state of being, the word ‘jnāna ‘is not contradictory. Similarly, there is also no contradiction in the sentence ‘vijnataram are kena vijanxyat, ‘(Bṛhad-āraṇyaka Upaniṣad, 2.4.14), “O Maitreyi, by what means can one know the knower, the Supreme Lord ? ” Therefore, the Supreme is endowed with energies in the form of knowledge, power, and action rather than being mere consciousness, a synonym of ‘anubhuti ‘and ’samvit’.**

**Комментарий:**

Here the monist raises an objection. The quality of being a knower is itself insentient, for this is understood by the non-difference between the 7 ‘and the insentient false ego when one thinks 7 know’. The false ego is certainly insentient matter, since its immediate cause is the mahat-tattva. Therefore, without any doubt, the false ego is not spirit. The monist then gives another reason for this fact, “The presence of the spirit is felt during deep sleep or swoon, although there is no perception of the false ego». As stated in the Śrimad-Bhāgavatam (11.3.39): sanneyad indriya-gane ‘hami caprasupte kutastha asayam rte tad-anusmṛtir nah, “ When all the external senses and the false ego vanish during deep sleep, the immutable soul is still present, and therefore we recollect the self even without the subtle body. ” If the false ego were the soul, then there would be no such feeling in its absence. The monist concludes, “Therefore, etc. ” The author refutes this argument by calling it foolish. To be a knower does not really mean to be a doer of the action of knowing in the sense that the soul combined with the mind produces knowledge, but it rather means to be the substratum of knowledge and qualities. The mind just expresses knowledge. Someone may object, “Why not accept that knowledge is produced?” The author replies, “Knowledge is eternal, etc. ” In the text ‘parasya śaktih ‘it is said that the

Supreme Soul has eternal knowledge, and the same is said about the jiva in the text ‘avinasi are’. “O Maitreyi (are), the jiva (atma) is by nature imperishable (avinasi)». This is also true regarding its qualities. The soul’s attributes (dharma) are indestructible (anucchitti). This means that they are all imperishable, since the soul and its attributes exist as a whole. Otherwise, there would be tautology. It should be understood that the soul is consciousness and is the substratum of consciousness, just as in sentences like ‘there is existence, ‘the substratum of existence is existence itself. The word ‘vijnataram ‘refers to God. By the word ‘adi ‘(etc.), other śruti sentences that teach that the jiva is a knower should also be included, such as: karta boddha vijnānatma puruṣah (Praśna Upaniṣad, 4.9), “The conscious individual soul is a knower and a doer. ”

### Text 4

**Перевод:**

**Let consciousness be considered either as being luminous to its own substratum by its own existence in the present state, or as illuminating objects to itself by its own existence. In both cases, the monist will face the conclusion that Brahman is endowed with potency. You (the opponent) have established that since it illuminates objects, consciousness is self-luminous. If consciousness is devoid of a nature that illuminates objects, then being unable to illuminate itself and be perceived by any other means of cognizance, it would be worthless.**

**Комментарий:**

Here the author explains that even according to the monists ‘view, Brahman, Whose form is consciousness, has attributes. “Substratum,” i.e., the conscious being itself. “Or consciousness illuminates other objects, such as earthen pots, by its own existence. ”In both of the propositions presented here, i.e., whether consciousness is luminous to its own substratum by its own existence, or whether it illuminates other objects to itself, the existence of potency poses itself to one who considers that there is only one absolute consciousness (tan-matra-vadinah). With the intent of informing the opponent that he cannot deny this, the author says, “If consciousness is devoid, etc. ” The word ‘tasyah ‘refers to consciousness. The word ‘tvayā ‘refers to the monist. If consciousness is devoid of a nature that illuminates objects, then being unable to illuminate itself, it would be inanimate and worthless like a flower in the sky. This is the meaning.

### Text 5

**Перевод:**

**Someone may argue, “Consciousness is of the nature of knowledge and does not have any known attribute.” It is not so, for you yourself have accepted that attributes like eternity and self-luminosity are proved by the given evidences. Nor is your intended conclusion proved by the explanation that consciousness is the abhava of noneternity, unconsciousness, and so forth, for even if consciousness had such attributes, this would baffle your conclusion. By saying that the counterpart of non-eternity and so on does not exist in consciousness apart from its svarūpa, if attributes in the form of abhava (non- existence) are also not accepted, then by denying those attributes, nothing would have been said at all.**

**Комментарий:**

The opponent says, “Consciousness is luminous to its own substratum, illuminates other objects, and does not have any known attribute. ” The attributes of consciousness have been proved by the given evidences, such as ‘nityo nityanam ‘and’satyam jnānam anantam, ‘as previously stated. Based on the view that the meaning of a word excludes any meaning different from it, the conclusion intended by the monist is that there is only one undivided consciousness because attributes like eternity are of the nature of the non-existence of non-eternity and so on. “Such attributes, ” i.e., of the nature of non-existence. If by denying non-eternity, etc., the non-existence of non-eternity is not established, then such statement would be useless.

### Text 6

**Перевод:**

**Moreover, is such consciousness proved or not? In the first case, it would have an attribute. In the second case, it would be worthless like a flower in the sky. If it is argued that consciousness is the very proof, then it must be stated to whom this is a proof and of what. If it is a proof of nothing and to no one, then it is no proof at all. Proof is something relative, like the quality of being a son: it belongs to a person in relation to someone. If it is argued that the proof belongs to consciousness, then being understood that there is difference between consciousness and proof, it is concluded that proof is a potency of consciousness, and not merely its nature. In this way, inherent attributes like being eternal, being a knower, and so on, do exist in Brahman even if its svarūpa is mere consciousness.**

**Комментарий:**

Here the author demonstrates in a different way that consciousness has attributes. He asks the monist, “Is such consciousness proved or not?” In the first case, it would have an attribute, as it would be proved. In the second case, it would be like a flower in the sky, since it would have no essential nature. The monist again objects that the proof is consciousness itself. His idea is this, “You cannot ask about the proof of proof, for this would lead to regressus ad infinitum». The author then asks, “You must tell me: is such consciousness the proof of what and to whom ? Showing what the monist may say, the author refutes the idea that such consciousness can be proved. The meaning is that such consciousness is certainly neither proof of any object nor proof to anyone seeking such substantiation.

### Text 7

**Перевод:**

**It had been previously argued that because the soul is ascertained as present during deep sleep without the sense of ‘I, ‘and because of the perceptions according to bodily differences, such as ‘I am fat, ‘the sense of ‘I ‘is not the soul, just as the body is not. This is incorrect, for the sense of ‘I ‘exists due to the pure soul. Thus, the sense of ‘I ‘cannot be inert matter and an object of perception for something other than the self. The reason is that the sense of ‘Г is perceived by the self and is luminous to oneself. That which is manifest to itself is manifest as the ‘I’. That which is not manifest as the ‘I ‘is not manifest to itself, as for instance, an earthen pot and the like. Therefore, proving itself by its own existence, the soul is the very sense of ‘I, ‘and not inert matter. “The knower proved by the perception that ‘I know ‘is not the soul,” — One who says this makes a statement like ‘My mother is barren, ‘in which the word ‘barren ‘has no meaning. Pure knowledge and happiness are manifest to this knower, the ‘I’. Although the svarūpa of the ‘I ‘is consciousness itself, its being the knower of the feeling that ‘I know ‘and ‘I am happy ‘is not contradictory, just as the sun and other celestial bodies are essentially light and are also illuminators. This has been previously mentioned.**

**Комментарий:**

Here the author dispels the doubt that has been previously raised. The words ‘tad-virahitatma-pratipadanat ‘mean ‘because the soul is ascertained as present during deep sleep without the false ego’. The word ‘tasya ‘means ‘of the sense of I’. It should be understood that according to the monists ‘definition, ‘asmad-artha ‘(the ‘I ‘) is the soul, and ‘yusmad-artha ‘(the ‘you ‘) is what is not the soul. The author clarifies that since the 7 ‘is the soul, it is self-luminous, “That which is manifest, etc. ” He then mocks those who say that the 7 ‘is inert matter, “One who says, etc». The words ‘yusmad-artham ‘mean ‘what is not the soul’. The word ‘prasūh ‘means ‘mother’. He then says that if the 7 ‘were not the soul, similar to things like pots, then it would not have attributes like being a knower and being happy. Someone may argue, “From śruti passages like ’satyam jnānam anantam ‘and ‘vijnānam yajnam tanute ‘it is heard that the svarūpa of the soul is consciousness itself, so by what reason would it be a knower?” With the intention to convey the idea that this is so on the strength of ‘viśeṣa, ‘as seen in sentences like ‘there is existence, ‘the author replies, “Although the svarūpa, etc. ”

### Text 8

**Перевод:**

**Someone may argue, “The sense of ‘I ‘is superimposed on the spirit, which is mere consciousness.” It is not so, for there is no one to superimpose. Neither pure consciousness devoid of false ego, nor the false ego, which is inert matter, can be the one superimposing. Nor is the reflection of consciousness manifest on the ‘I, ‘for neither of them has form. Nor should one think that the quality of being a knower is produced in the ‘I ‘due to contact with pure consciousness, just like heat is produced in a lump of iron due to contact with fire, as the ‘I ‘cannot acquire attributes the way fire acquires heat. Someone may argue, “The false ego interwoven with itself manifests consciousness and then becomes a knower.” It is not so, for consciousness is itself the possessor of attributes like the false ego and thus cannot possibly be an attribute. Hence, the self-luminous consciousness cannot be displayed by the false ego, which is inert matter. As it is said: santangara ivadityam ahankaro jadatmakah, svayam-jyotisam atmanam na vyanaktiti yuktimat, “Just as extinguished charcoal does not reveal the sun, it is not logical to think that the inanimate false ego reveals the self-luminous soul.” Consciousness cannot manifest from the false ego, since the manifestation of the latter is dependent on the former.**

**Комментарий:**

Although already defeated, here the monist returns once again and is defeated in a different way. In a superimposition, things such as a shell are the basis, and consciousness along with the mind are known to be the ones superimposing. In the case of pure consciousness being the basis, then who is superimposing the false ego on it? If it is argued that consciousness itself is the one superimposing, just as in the analogy of the fisherman who is actually a king’s son, we disagree, for consciousness has no false ego like the king’s son has. If it is argued that the false ego is the one superimposing, that is also not possible, for it is inert matter like a pot. Someone may argue, “Even though the false ego is matter, by the reflection of consciousness on it, the false ego becomes endowed with consciousness, just as in the analogy of the hot iron, and thus it is possible that the false ego becomes the one superimposing. ” Intending to say that this proposition is as ludicrous as feeding on imaginary sweets, the author replies, “Nor is the reflection, etc. ” The meaning is clear. Someone may argue, “Well, there may not be such a reflection of consciousness on the ‘I, ‘but its connection with the 7 ‘cannot be excluded. Therefore, it is by the connection with consciousness that the false ego becomes endowed with consciousness». It is not so. Why ? Because you do not agree that to be a knower is an attribute of consciousness in the same way as heat is a natural attribute of fire. Someone may argue, “To be a knower is not actually an attribute of consciousness, but for argument’s sake, let it be so: when the false ego indicates consciousness, which has entered it, let it be a knower. ” This is not true. Consciousness is indeed the locus of every thing different from itself, and it is the possessor of attributes. By calling it an attribute of the false ego, you are propounding an erroneous conclusion. This is the idea here. Everyone agrees that subordination is an attribute. Moreover, to be known does not mean to be indicated, but rather to be revealed. This is not possible in the case of consciousness. Therefore, the author says, “The self-luminous, etc». The meaning is that just as an extinguished charcoal does not illuminate the sun, it is logical that the false ego, which is inert matter, does not make the pure undivided soul become manifest. The manifestation of the false ego is dependent on consciousness. On the other hand, consciousness is self-manifest. An earthen pot and the sun are respective examples of this.

### Text 9

**Перевод:**

**Someone may argue, “Consciousness is revealed by the false ego, and the false ego is revealed by consciousness, just as the sun’s rays are revealed by the palm of the hand, which in turn is revealed by the sun’s rays.” It is not so, for the sun’s rays are not revealed by the palm of the hand. The span of the sun’s rays obstructed by the palm of the hand are just perceived more clearly on account of their own abundance. Thus, as the cause is merely the abundance of sun’s rays, the palm of the hand does not really reveal them. Therefore, since the individual soul proves itself as the knower in the sense of ‘I, ‘the soul is not mere consciousness, for such is the thought of one upon waking up: sukham aham asvapsam na kincid avedisam, “I slept happily and was not aware of anything.” Even during deep sleep, the sense of ‘I, ‘happiness and the quality of being a knower are understood. Their perception is not very clear due to the covering of the mode of ignorance at that time. The concept that the sense of ‘I ‘is not the soul, just as the body is not, is also refuted in a similar way. During deep sleep, the one asleep is understood neither as the body nor as being devoid of the sense of ‘I’.**

**Комментарий:**

Being embarrassed, here the monist returns with an obtuse sophism, “ The sun’s rays are seen by being revealed by the palm of the hand, and the palm of the hand is revealed by the sun’s rays. Similarly, consciousness, which has entered the false ego, is revealed by the false ego, which is revealed by consciousness». It is not so. Why ? Because the sun’s rays are not revealed by the palm of the hand. The word ‘tasya ‘refers to the sun s rays. The idea here is that the palm of the hand is predominantly made of earth, and the sun s rays are predominantly made of fire, so how can one say that they are revealed by it ? Then, how is such perception possible ? Due to misapprehension. Therefore, the author says, “The span of the sun’s rays, etc.” The word ‘asau ‘refers to the sun’s rays. He then concludes, “Therefore, ” i.e., “due to the multiple reasons stated here’. ‘From the feeling that ‘I have slept happily, ’so to say, both the sense of feeling happy and the sense of ‘V are clear. From the feeling that 7 was not aware of anything ‘it is clearly concluded that one is ignorant of the material false ego and the sense organs, which are its effects, all of which are different than the sense of feeling happy and the sense of ‘I’. The reason is that during deep sleep, the false ego and its effects vanish. From the sense of feeling happy, the sense of ‘1, ‘and the feeling of unawareness, it is also clear that the soul is a knower. The words ‘tatrapi ‘mean ‘when the jiva is in deep sleep’. Someone may argue, “During deep sleep, the consciousness itself is the self, but during the wakingstate, the feeling of being happy and so on is perceived on account of the connection with the mind». In reply, the author says, “Their perception is not very clear because of the covering of being overcome by sleep due to the mode of ignorance». One remembers only what was experienced, and not what was not experienced, for experience and remembrance are invariably limited to a single subject. It is appropriate to say that the asleep living entity is understood neither as the body nor as being devoid of the sense of ‘I ‘during deep sleep, but it is rather different from the body and the sense of ‘I’.

### Text 10

**Перевод:**

**This feeling of ‘I is not the cause of worldly life, for it is connected with one’s pure svarūpa. Rather, knowledge of the ‘I ‘as such actually gives liberation from worldly life, since it removes the false ego. If the sense of ‘I ‘were a mere bodily designation (upadhi), people would run away even from discussions on the topic, knowing that ‘upon attaining liberation, I will vanish’. Those who aspire for liberation entertain the will that, “May I become powerful, become free from all distress, and enjoy inexhaustible happiness.” Fearing the destruction of one’s self at the time of liberation, people would not even be inclined to hear and talk about it. Nor can it be said that, “Although I will vanish, some consciousness will remain existing, and that is actually the soul.” The reason is that no inclination is seen towards such a goal, and there is no evidence of it either. Would the evidence of this be the Vedas or a witness? The first case cannot be, for those who say that the self is destroyed do not accept the Vedas. The second case is also not possible, for the witness would have vanished at the time of liberation. It will be later established that the ‘I ‘is the witness. Therefore, it is well corroborated that the ‘I ‘is the knower itself, the individual soul.**

**Комментарий:**

Having thus demonstrated that the sense of 7 ‘is the soul, the author now refutes the idea that the sense of 7 ‘is the cause of transmigration and shows that it is actually the cause of liberation. The feeling that 7 am a jiva ‘is characterized by the feeling that 7 am atomic in size and consist of consciousness and bliss; I am blissful, a knower devoid of material false ego and senses’. The word ‘tatah ‘means ‘from worldly life’.

The words ‘tad-virodhitvat ‘mean ‘because it removes the material false ego, such as the concepts that I am a brāhmaṇa, I am fair complexioned, I have senses, I am ignorant, etc’. Pointing out the flaw in the opposite view, the author says, “if the sense, etc». The meaning is that if the sense of ‘I‘ characterized by the mentioned feeling that ‘I am a jiva, ‘’I am atomic, ‘and so on, were a material designation produced by the mahat-tattva, then people would run away from discussions about liberation. If the ‘I’ would be destroyed at liberation, being that same ‘I’, one who aspires for liberation would not be inclined towards such means to attain liberation. The opponent argues that although the ‘I’ is destroyed, some consciousness devoid of attributes will remain existing, just as a pure gem when the dirt has been removed. “There is no evidence of it,” i.e., of such consciousness devoid of attributes. The word ‘tadanim ‘means cat the time of liberation’. “Since you accept that the witness ‘I’ vanishes, there cannot be any witness at all, for according to your view, only pure consciousness devoid of attributes remains at the state of liberation. ”

### Text 11

**Перевод:**

**Therefore, even someone who has realised consciousness verbalizes such words in first person: tad dhaitat pasyann rsir vamadevah pratipede ‘ham manur abhavam suryas ca (Bṛhad-āraṇyaka Upaniṣad, 1.4.10), “Realizing the Supreme, the sage Vamadeva declared, ‘I became Manu and the sun-god’». Further examples are also seen in the scriptures: prano’smi prajnatma (Kausttaki-brāhmaṇa Upaniṣad, 3-2), Indra said, “I am the life air, the consciousness, the soul.” nasto mohah smṛtir labdha tvat-prasadan mayacyuta, sthito’smi gata-sandehah karisye vacanam tava (Bhagavad-gita, 18.73), Arjuna said, “O Acyuta, by Your kindness, my illusion is now dispelled. Now I am determined, am free from doubts, and have recovered my memory. I will act according to Your words.” The words of Para-brahman, Who is to be sought after by liberated souls, are also seen: brahma va idam agra asit tad atmanam avaid aham brahmasmi (Bṛhad-āraṇyaka Upaniṣad, 1.4.10), “Before creation, all this was Brahman, Who knew oneself as ‘I am Brahman’». seyarh devataiksata hantaham imas tisro devatah (Chāndogya Upaniṣad, 6.3.2), “That same Supreme God thought, ‘Let Me enter into these three divine elements (fire, water, and earth)’». aham sarvasya prabhavah (Bhagavad-gita, 10.8), “I am the source of everything.” aham evasam evagre (Śrimad-Bhāgavatam, 2.9.33), “I alone existed before creation.”**

**Комментарий:**

Having thus demonstrated that the sense of ‘I ‘belongs to the pure soul, here the author teaches the truth according to the realization of the wise. “Therefore, ” i.e., “because the sense of ‘I ‘belongs to the pure soul. ” The words ‘evam vyaharah ‘means ‘in whose speech the sense of I is prevalent’. According to the Amara-kośa (1.51), the words ‘vyaharah, ‘‘uktih, ‘‘lapitam, ‘‘bhasitam, ‘‘vacanam, ‘and ‘vacah ‘are synonyms of ‘speech’. The text ‘tad dhaitat ‘is from the Bṛhad-āraṇyaka Upaniṣad (1.4.10). Realizing (pasyan) the svarūpa of Brahman (etat) as sustaining and pervading everything, the sage Vamadeva spoke (pratipede). What did he say ? “I became Manu and the sun-god, ” i.e., “Brahman itself is Manu, the sun-god, and the whole universe. Brahman is the cause of my sustenance and pervades me. ” The same should also be understood in the following sentences of that passage. The sentence ‘prano’smi prajnatma ‘is from the Kausltaki-brāhmaṇa Upaniṣad (3.2) and was spoken by Indra. His devotion to the Supreme as described in the Kenopanisad is well-known. Indra’s antagonistic behaviour towards the Supreme Lord is according to His own will in order to enhance His pastimes. Nevertheless, Indra asks forgiveness for acting in a way unbefitting a servant. This is the opinion of the commentators. The verse ‘nasto mohah ‘is from the Bhagavad-gita (18.73) and was spoken by Arjuna. The word ‘ca ‘in the next sentence of this paragraph means that the words of the Supreme are also included. The text ‘brahma va ‘is from the Bṛhad-āraṇyaka Upaniṣad (1.4.10). The text’seyam devataiksata ‘is from the Chāndogya Upaniṣad (6.3.2). That same (sa) Supreme Soul (devata) thought (aiksata), “Let Me enter into these (imah) three (tisrah) divine elements — fire, water, and earth — along with my potencies and the individual soul and then create name and form». This is the connection with the next sentence. The rest is clear. The texts starting with ‘brahma va ‘are reflections of the Supreme Lord Himself with the sense of ‘I’. He must think so because this sense is connected with His svarūpa. According to those who know the truth, the words ‘before creation ‘mean ‘before the material false ego was brought forth’.

### Text 12

**Перевод:**

**This being so demonstrated, according to the verse ‘bhumir apo ‘nalo vayuh, ‘ the false ego (ahankara) situated within the body should be comprehended as having been mentioned as such because it causes the feeling of ‘I ‘within something other than the soul — the material body. Therefore, the etymological explanation of the word ‘ahankara’ should be considered after adding the affix ‘cvi’.  These two kinds of self-consciousness are demonstrated in the śruti ’sukham aham ‘ and the smṛti ’sanne yad indriya-gane, ‘ because the material designation in the form of egotism is dormant, and because of the thought of one’s real self with the concept of ‘I ‘when one feels that ‘I slept happily ‘and when one recollects oneself. Therefore, even when one deliberates that ‘I did not know myself, ‘there is no observation of the material egotism, which is the object of such ignorance; however, there is observation of the real ‘I, ‘which is the witness of such ignorance. Thus, the soul remains as the witness of ignorance during deep sleep. This is certainly the way you (the opponent) explain it. To be a witness actually means to be a direct knower, as stated by Panini (5.2.91): saksad drastari samjnayam, “The affix ‘ini ‘is applied to the word ’saksat ‘in a noun in the sense of ‘direct observer’». This witness is the same ‘I ‘demonstrated by the perception that ‘I know directly’. This ‘I ‘remains existing even during liberation. Otherwise, nobody would be inclined to attain liberation, as previously mentioned.**

**Комментарий:**

Someone may argue, “The false ego mentioned in the verse ‘bhumir apah ‘is part of the material body made of twenty-four elements. The described sense of ‘Г connected with one’s svarūpa is just a concoction, for there is no evidence of this. ” The author responds to this argument in the present paragraph. The meaning here is as follows: The primary meaning expressed by the word ‘asmat ‘is the 7, ‘which is non-different from the self. On the other hand, the sense of 7 ‘that is part of the material body is expressed by an indeclinable word ending in ‘ma ‘(aham). Thus, there is a difference between the two things being expressed and the two words expressing them. This ‘V that is part of the material body is produced by the mahat-tattva and is secondary, for it causes the concept of self within the body, which is not the self, by thoughts such as 7 am dark complexioned, ‘7 am deaf, ‘etc. This is expressed by the word ‘ahankara ‘(false ego), to which the affix ‘cvi 9 is applied. The author then gives evidence of these two concepts of ‘V by quoting the scriptures. During the waking state, the jiva endowed with sense organs capable of perceiving sense objects is subject to transformations. During sleep, being endowed with the false ego, which is the material cause of all the sense organs, the jiva enjoys sense objects in the form of subtle impressions, and is thus subject to transformations. During deep sleep (prasupte), being situated in the core of the heart, when all the sense organs (indriya-gane) and the false ego (ahami) of the jiva vanish (sanne) within the Supreme Soul, it becomes free from transformations (kūtasthah). Why? It is said ‘asayam rte, ‘because in that state the subtle body (asayam), which is the cause of transformations, is absent. Someone may argue, “This is not perceived so. ” It is said ‘tad-anusmṛtir nah, ‘we (nah), masters of yoga, recollect (anusmṛtih) the immutable soul (tat), the witness during deep sleep, whose svarūpa is happiness and consciousness. The idea is that because one’s subtle body has not yet been destroyed by spiritual knowledge, one awakens from deep sleep and is again united with the subtle body, and hence not yet liberated. Then, in two sentences, the author explains the two kinds of 7’. The meaning is that in the sentences 7 slept happily’ and ‘We recollect’, the sense of ‘I’ expressed by the word ‘asmat ‘is different from the one asleep. “Therefore”, i.e., “as the two kinds of ‘I’ have been demonstrated”. During the waking state, one’s reflection is like this: having being questioned, someone ignorant about his caste says, “I did not know myself”. Here, there is no observation of the feeling of ‘I ‘pertaining to the gross and subtle body, which are the object of the ignorance in the form of the material body. However, there is observation of the ‘I’ that is the witness of the ignorance in the form of the material body. Then, the author shows that even in the opponent’s view, the soul is not devoid of attributes during deep sleep. Rather, it is actually the knower, the ‘I,’ since it is the direct observer of the ignorance in the form of the material body, which is different from the soul. The word ’saksi ‘(witness) is formed by applying the affix ‘ini ‘after the word ’saksat ‘(directly). It is applied to a noun in the sense of one who directly observes the contention of two disputing parties without taking anyone’s side. This is the meaning of the quoted aphorism. The rest is clear.

### Text 13

**Перевод:**

**The understanding that the concept of ‘I ‘pertains to the soul is also accepted in the Viṣṇu Purāṇa: yo bhavan yan nimittam va yad agamana-karanam, tat sarvam kathyatam vidvan mahyam susrūsave tvayā (2.13.79), “Who are you? By what means did you come here and for what reason? О learned one, I am eager to hear, so please tell me everything.” Being thus asked by Rahugana, Bharata then explained that the word ‘Г cannot be expressed: sruyatam ко ‘ham ity etad vaktum bhupa na sakyate (2.13.80), “Listen, О king. It is not possible to use the word ‘I ‘to say who I am.” Rahugana submits that the word ‘I ‘can be spoken in the sense of the soul: atmany esa na dosaya sabdo ‘ham iti yo dvija (2.13.85), “O brāhmaṇa, there is no fault in using the word ‘I ‘in the sense of the soul.” Bharata concurs again: sabdo ‘ham iti dosaya natmany esa tathaiva tad (2.13.86), “It is true that there is no fault in using the word ‘I ‘in the sense of the soul.” Yet he replies that the word ‘I’ should not be used in the sense of what is not the soul, such as the tongue, and so on: anatmany atma-vijnānam sabdo va bhranti-lakṣaṇāh, “However, to employ the word ‘I ‘in the sense of something that is not the soul, understanding it as the self, is a symptom of delusion.”**

**Комментарий:**

Here the author clearly explains that the sense of ‘I ‘pertains to the soul. It should be understood that the quoted verses are from the second part of the Viṣṇu Purāṇa One should know that Bharata first rejects the use of the word ‘V apprehending the defect of considering the ‘V as independent. The word ‘vaktum ‘means ‘to use’. The word ‘tat ‘refers to the understanding of the word ‘I’. The idea is that there is no fault in using the word ‘aham ‘in the sense of the soul, for the soul is actually expressed by the word ‘asmat’.

### Text 14

**Перевод:**

**Someone may argue, “If the sense of ‘Iis the soul, then how would the subsequent statements in that scripture be coherent? As it is stated: yady anyo’sti parah ко ‘pi mattah parthiva-sattama, tadaiso ‘ham ayam canyo vaktum evam apisyate, yada samasta-dehesu puman eko vyavasthitah, tada hi ко bhavan so ‘ham ity etad viphalam vacah (2.13-86-87), ‘O best of kings, if there is someone else besides me, then it is valid to say ‘I am such-and-such’. Yet when there is only one Person situated within all bodies, then ‘Who are you? ‘and ‘I am such-and-such ‘are purposeless words’». This is not as you think. In fact, the concept of the independence of the soul as the ‘I ‘is also refuted by these subsequent verses. The word ‘I ‘indeed places independence on the self in instances like ‘I know, ‘‘I eat, ‘‘I go’. Such independence is not possible in the individual soul, because it is dependent on the Supreme Soul. Hence, only the Supreme Soul is worthy of this concept of independence, and not the jiva. Since their sustenance, activities, and so on are dependent upon the Supreme Soul, the individual soul is not separated from Him. Nor is the sense of ‘I ‘undermined in this way, for it is neither given up nor contradicted when one thinks ‘I am dependent on God, ‘and so forth. This being so, the meaning of these verses is the following: “Besides my only shelter (mattah), God, if there is anyone (parah) else (anyah) who judges oneself independent — either the ‘I ‘or the soul — then it would be possible to say that ‘I exist separated from Him’. ” Yet it is not so, therefore he says: ‘yada samasta-dehesu, etc’. “When (yada) the Supreme independent Person (puman) is situated (vyavasthitah) everywhere in His own position, your determinative question ‘Who are you? ‘among many equals, and my response ‘I am such-and-such, ‘are purposeless. This being so, the intended meaning in the verse’sruyatam ко ‘bhavan ‘is similar. Therefore, the jiva’s egotism as independent is refuted in these verses, and not the sense of ‘I’. If that were the case, the word ‘I’ spoken by Bharata would be contradictory.**

**Комментарий:**

After raising a doubt regarding the established fact that the sense of ‘I ‘pertains to the soul, the author corroborates the same conclusion. The word ‘mattah ‘means ‘other than me (Bharata), the single soul without a second’. The words ‘parthiva-sattama ‘mean ‘O Rahugana, best of kings’. The word ‘tena ‘means ‘by the subsequent verses’. The word ’svarthe ‘means ‘the I in the sense of the individual soul’. The word ‘ahanat ‘means ‘because it is not given up’. “In His own position, ” i.e., as the maintainer, pervader, and supporter. The compound ’svokta ‘means ’spoken by Bharata’.

### Text 15

**Перевод:**

**Also at the end of the same chapter, after refuting the idea that the ‘I ‘is the body, the pure ‘I ‘is established: samastavayavebhyas tvam prthag bhupa vyavasthitah, ко ‘ham ity eva nipunam bhutva cintaya parthiva (2.13.103), “O king, you are different from all these limbs. Having become completely situated in knowledge, you should think, ‘Who am I ? ‘” The meaning is that the concept that ‘I am a powerful king ‘is just delusion. Real knowledge is the realization that ‘I am a soul, different from the body and consist of happiness and consciousness’. He further says: evam vyavasthite tattve mayaham iti bhasitum, prthak-karana-nispadyam sakyate nrpa te katham (2.13.104), “Being thus situated in the truth, how can you utter concepts that are brought about by the false ego, such as ‘I ‘or ‘by me? ‘” The idea here is that being situated in the truth of the soul as different from the gross and subtle body, how can one say ‘This enemy has been killed by me, the king, and I will also kill that one? ‘This concept is brought about by the material false ego.**

**Комментарий:**
