If the material world were false, sentences like ‘yato va imani bhūtani jayante ‘would be as meaningless as sentences like ‘this is the son of a barren woman, ‘and hence they would be unauthoritative and tantamount to Buddhism. Moreover, the Supreme Lord‘s qualities, such as mercy and His purifying others from sin, would not exist. The Lord‘s mercy is meant for the afflicted and fallen, who are other than Him, and not for Himself. Therefore, in such a view, sentences about His mercy and so forth would not be possible. Having aversion towards pure dualism — although it is free from flaws — and desiring independence, since there are flaws in both of those monistic theories, such people remain on the edge, being afraid of the characteristics adopted by becoming disciples of the dualists. They should be chastised by the Tattvavadis. Therefore, such unintelligent people are to be ignored.

### Text 31

*vidya-rūpam bhusanam me pradaya khyatim ninye tena yo mam udarah \|*  
*sri-govindah svapna-nirdista-bhasyo radha-bandhur bandhurangah sa jiyat \|\| 31 \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**He Who, after giving me the jewel of knowledge, made me renowned for this, Who is Śri Radha’s beloved, and Who, in a dream, ordered me to write a commentary on the Brahma-sūtra, may that magnanimous Lord Govinda of lovely limbs reveal His excellence everywhere.**

**Комментарий:**

Having thus composed this introduction to the Govinda-bhāṣya, revealing how he received the Supreme Lord‘s mercy to compose that commentary, the author at the end writes an auspicious verse declaring His supreme excellence. Having given me (me pradaya) a jewel-like (bhūsanam) knowledge (vidya), Lord Govinda made me attain (ninye) reputation (khyatim) because of this knowledge (tena). Someone may ask, “Why did he do so?” The author says, “Because He is magnanimous (udarah).” According to the Amara-kośa (3-3.192), the word ‘udara ‘is used in the sense of ‘generous ‘and ‘magnanimous’. May He reveal His excellence everywhere (jiyat). What is He like ? He is the beloved (bandhuh) of Śri Radha, the daughter of King Vrsabhanu. All His limbs are lovely (bandhurangah). It was He Who, in a dream (svapna), three times gave an order (nirdista) to compose a Brahma-sūtra commentary (bhāṣya). This should be understood here: Upon being questioned regarding the conclusive meaning of the Brahma-sūtra that the son of Nanda Maharaja is the Supreme Lord Himself, the author became very dejected because the conclusion was not taken as such. Unable to tolerate his dejection, the Supreme Lord, Ṡyāmasundara, wearing yellow garments, a sacred thread, an Urdhva-puṇḍra tilaka, and braided hair, appeared in his dream and ordered him three times. In the first time, He ordered, "Do it. "In the second time, He ordered, “Do it. You will have it. ” In the third time, He ordered, “Comment on the Brahma-sūtra. Your commentary will be fully accomplished». Therefore, the author says, “He who told me three times to write a commentary.” Although this should not be disclosed, he has mentioned it to glorify the Lord‘s mercy and to remove the misconception that he, Vidyābhūṣaṇa, is the author.

### Text 32

*yad brahma-sutresu vibhati bhāṣyam*  
*kṛṣṇatmakam vyakta-nava-prameyam \|*  
*tasyopavesaya suvarna-pitham*  
*siddhānta-ratnaṁ na bhavet kim etat \|\| 32 \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**The Govinda-bhāṣya, whose essence is Lord Kṛṣṇaand which reveals nine prameyas, shines among the Vedānta aphorisms. Why should this Siddhānta-ratnaṁ not be a golden throne on which to place it?**

**Комментарий:**

Praising the Govinda-bhāṣya, here the author says that this introduction supports it. The compound ‘kṛṣṇatmakam ‘means ‘that in which Kṛṣṇais the essence to be described’. Alternatively, by this compound it is stated that the commentary is worshipable like Kṛṣṇa. The compound ‘vyakta-nava-prameyam ‘means ‘that in which the nine prameyas (objects of knowledge) starting with Lord Hari’s supremacy are revealed. By this, the author expressed that he is a follower of Madhvācārya. The word ‘upavesaya ‘means ‘for easy support, ‘ because without all the reasoning and śruti sentences mentioned here, the Govinda-bhāṣya would be unsupported. The word ’suvarna ‘means ‘golden’. Alternatively, it means ‘beautiful words’. The compound’siddhānta-ratnaṁ ‘means ‘that in which the philosophical conclusions are jewels’. Alternatively, it means a throne (pitham) named ‘Siddhānta-ratnaṁ’.

### Text 33

*siddhānta-ratna-kiranat kila bhāṣya-candrad*  
*advaita-sarvara-harad amala-prakāśat \|*  
*drstam na kim kim iha tattvam udagradhibhih*  
*sriman-murari-caranambuja-bhakti-bhagbhih \|\| 33 \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Through the pristine light of this Siddhānta-ratnaṁ — a ray of the moon-like Govinda-bhāṣya dispelling the darkness of Advaitavāda — what truth is not visible to those highly intelligent and devoted to the lotus feet of the charming Murāri, the son of Nanda Maharaja?**

**Комментарий:**

The Govinda-bhāṣya gives the highest benefit to those who aspire to know the truth. Yet it reveals the truth when it is read by those who have devotion for Lord Hari and the spiritual master, and not when read with a desire for scholarship or for making a living. This is the idea of what the author says here. This introduction called Siddhānta-ratnaṁ is like moon-rays (kirana) that shower nectar. The non-dualist (advaita) theory, according to which there is only one consciousness devoid of attributes, is darkness (sarvara) itself The word ‘harat ‘means ‘dispelling that darkness’. The word ‘amala ‘means ‘bright’. The word ‘prakāśah ‘means ‘halo of light, ‘characterized by the direct perception of objects. What (kith kim) truth (tattvam), either the svarūpa of the jiva, the svarūpa of God, the svarūpa of His abode, the svarūpa ofbhakti, the svarūpa ofprakṛti, and so on, is not seen through this light ? The meaning is that everything is seen. The name ‘Murāri ‘refers to the Supreme Lord Himself, the son of Nanda Maharaja. Alternatively, it means Śri Rasikānanda Murāri, who belonged to the fourth generation ofācāryas preceding the author.

### Text 34

*vijayante sri-radha-damodara-pada-pankaja-dyutayah \|*  
*yabhih sakrd uditabhir vinirmito me mahan modah \|\| 34\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**All glories to the splendour of Śri Rādhā-Dāmodara ’s lotus feet, which upon being seen suddenly gave me great joy.**

**Комментарий:**

Here at the end, the author presents an auspicious verse about the excellence of his mantra-guru. Śri Rādhā-Dāmodara , the author’s mantra-guru, is the crest-jewel of the dynasties ofbrāhmaṇasfrom Kanyakubja, the greatest and foremost scholar. The splendour of His lotus feet is praised here. To elucidate his greatness, the author says’sakrt ‘(suddenly). The word ‘modah ‘means the joy caused by understanding the Supreme, by being an object of mercy among the congregation of saints, and by being a member of the king’s court. Alternatively, the splendour of the lotus feet of Śri Radha and Śri Kṛṣṇagive the joy caused by exclusive devotion for Them.

### Text 35

*vedanta-danta-hrdayai racitam mayaitat*  
*samgrhya yukti-nicayam mita-bhasitena \|*  
*pitambarasya karunavarunalayasya*  
*karunyatah krtam udetu mude budhanam \|\| 35 \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Having collected the writings of those whose minds were controlled by studying the Upaniṣads, by the mercy of Śri Pitambara dāsa, who is an ocean of compassion, I have compiled this treatise with brief words which are full of logic. May it give joy to the learned.**

**Комментарий:**

Showing that this treatise was composed on the basis of the logic established by ancient ācāryas and is authoritative, here the author speaks about the mercy received from the preceptor who taught him the scriptures. “Having collected (samgrhya) the writings (racitam) of the previous ācāryas, who were detached and whose minds were controlled by having studied the Upaniṣads and realised their meaning (vedanta-danta-hrdayaih), I have (maya) compiled (krtam) this (etat) Bhāṣya-piṭhakam with brief words (mita-bhasitena)». This indicates the unrefuted logic and deep meaning of this treatise. “This was composed by the mercy of my vidya-guru, SriPitambara dāsa, who knows all scriptures, is the crest-jewel among renunciants, and is very famous for his celibacy. ” This indicates his worship of his guru from the Madhva-sampradāya. The words ‘karunavarunalayasya ‘mean that he is an ocean of mercy. Alternatively, ‘Pitambara ‘is Lord Govinda, the son of Nanda Maharaja.

### Text 36

*anandatirtha-plutam acyutam me*  
*caitanya-bhasvat-prabhayatiphullam \|*  
*ceto'ravindam priyata-marandam*  
*pibaty alih sacchavi tattvavadah \|\| 36 \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Absorbed in Tattvavada, my bee-like mind drinks a beautiful imperishable lotus flower that was spread by Madhvācārya, became fully blossomed by the effulgent rays of Caitanya Mahāprabhu, and is filled with the nectar of love for Kṛṣṇa.**

**Комментарий:**

**Thus ends the eighth chapter of the introduction to the Govinda-bhāṣya, named Siddhānta-ratnaṁ, whose topic is the ascertainment of the ultimate goal of life.**

Here the author states that he is fixed in the philosophy of Lord KṛṣṇaCaitanya, Who had been initiated in the line of Śri Madhvācārya. Tattvavada is the philosophical conclusion of Madhvācārya, according to which everything is real and there is nothing false. "This philosophy itself is my bee-like mind that drinks a lotus flower, i.e., my mind is fully occupied with this philosophy. ” What is that lotus flower like ? It is immersed up to the stalk (plutam) in bliss (ananda), which is like a reservoir of holy water resorted to by sages (tirtha). Alternatively, this also means ’spread (plutam) by the sage Madhva (Ananda Tirtha)’. That flower was fully blossomed (atiphullam) by the effulgent rays (bhasvat-prabha) of the knowledge (caitanya) sprung from the Tattvavada treatises. Alternatively, that flower was blossomed (atiphullam) by the meditation on the bodily splendour of the sun-like Lord Caitanya, the son of Mother Sact, and it is filled with the nectar (marandam) of love (priyata) for Lord Hari. The compound’sacchavi \* means ‘having beauty.9

In this treatise there are eight chapters, whose titles and order are as follows: Pancajanya, Kaumodaki, Sudarśana, Tārkṣya, Vāmana, Trivikrama, Nandaka, and Padma. The chapters should be understood to be named as such due to their being similar to each of these.

A certain sadhu has composed this commentary on this pleasant introduction to the Govinda-bhāṣya, which establishes his Lord, Hari. May the devotees, who are an ocean of mercy, repeatedly examine it with faith and then explain it to others.

Thus, the eighth chapter, entitled ‘Padma, ‘has been explained in this commentary on the introduction to the Govinda-bhāṣya.

hareh prapake sva-prabhoh pithakeyyam priye sadhuna samvyadhayi prabandhah daya-sindhavah sadhavah sraddhayainam muhur lokayadhvam tatah sodhayadhvam

## Baladeva Vidyābhūṣaṇa Project

This publication is part of the Baladeva Vidyābhūṣaṇa Project, whose main objectives are as follows:

1\. Search for lost manuscripts (such as the complete commentaries on the ten Upaniṣads and the Six Sandarbhas of Jiva Gosvāmi).

2\. Digitally preserve manuscripts currently existing in different libraries.

3\. Digitally preserve all editions of VidyabhQsana’s books.

4\. Digitally preserve articles written about Vidyābhūṣaṇa.

5\. Type the texts in digital Unicode, which can be converted into Devanāgari, Bengali characters, etc.

6\. Prepare a critical edition of each work.

7\. Translate all the works into English.

8\. Publish all translations with the original Devanāgari text.

9\. Investigate and verify existent biographical data on Vidyābhūṣaṇa.

10\. Publish a comprehensive biographical work.
